देवीमाहात्म्यम् · Study Edition
ContentsCh. 2 →Source & notesSacred texts

Prathama Carita · Chapter 1 of 13 · 104 mantras (1.1–1.104)

मधुकैटभवधः

madhukaiṭabhavadhaḥ

The Slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha · Complete with Noted Variants

Introduction

Summary
Mārkaṇḍeya promises the story of Sāvarṇi, the eighth Manu (1.1–3). King Suratha, betrayed by his ministers, rides alone into the forest and reaches the hermitage of the sage Medhas (1.4–11). Still brooding over his lost city, elephant and treasury (1.12–16), he meets Samādhi, a merchant thrown out by his own wife and sons, who cannot stop worrying about them either (1.17–34). Together they ask Medhas why their minds cling to what has rejected them, though they see the fault (1.35–45). Medhas answers that every creature has knowledge, yet all are bound by mamatā, and that the cause is Mahāmāyā, who deludes even the wise, who is the knowledge that liberates and the cause of bondage (1.46–58). Asked who she is (1.59–62), he tells how, at the end of an age, the demons Madhu and Kaiṭabha, born from the wax of Viṣṇu’s ears while he slept, rose to kill Brahmā; Brahmā praised the Goddess as Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā (1.63–87); she left Viṣṇu’s body, he woke, fought them for five thousand years, and, when the deluded demons offered him a boon, chose their death and slew them on his thighs, where the flood had not reached (1.88–104).
Forms of the Devī
Mahāmāyā, the power that binds and releases (1.55–58); Yoganidrā, the sleep of Viṣṇu (1.54, 1.66–71); Tāmasī, the dark Goddess who leaves Viṣṇu’s body (1.89); and, in Brahmā’s hymn, Svāhā, Svadhā and the syllable (1.73–74), the night of dissolution — Kālarātri, Mahārātri, Moharātri (1.79) — and the mild and the terrible at once (1.80–82).
Significance of the events
The frame story poses the question the whole text answers: why knowing is not enough to be free. The first battle is not fought by the Goddess at all: her act is to withdraw from Viṣṇu, so that the god who sustains the world can wake. The demons are destroyed by the same delusion that held the king and the merchant: they offer their enemy a boon.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
Narrative perfects (उवाच, बभूव, ददर्श) and aorists (अभूत्, अद्राक्षीत्); long sentences carried over verse boundaries in the dialogue (1.12–16, 1.22–24, 1.51–55); genitives of the speaker with verbs of hearing (गदतो मम, 1.2); gerundives (कार्यः, 1.54); passives of causatives (सम्मोह्यते, 1.55); and, in Brahmā’s hymn, the second person: त्वम् with nominative predicates, vocatives, and feminine stems in -ā, -ī and -ti.
The teaching (as distinct from the narrative)
Literally, a king and a merchant are consoled by a sage, and a god wakes to kill two demons. As teaching, the chapter says that attachment (mamatā) is not a private weakness but the working of the power that keeps the world in being; that the same power, as knowledge (vidyā), is the cause of liberation (1.57); and that she is not reached by argument but by praise and surrender — Brahmā, helpless on the lotus, does not fight; he praises.

Themes

  • Mamatā and moha: knowing and still clinging
  • Mahāmāyā as bondage and as liberation
  • Yoganidrā: the Goddess within Viṣṇu
  • Praise (stuti) as the act that turns events
  • The frame of the Manvantara: grace inside history
Recited before the chapter (liturgical, from the Gita Press pāṭha)

These lines belong to the recitation, not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras. They are given here in Sanskrit and IAST only.

Viniyoga

ॐ प्रथमचरित्रस्य ब्रह्मा ऋषिः, महाकाली देवता, गायत्री छन्दः, नन्दा शक्तिः, रक्तदन्तिका बीजम्, अग्निस्तत्त्वम्, ऋग्वेदः स्वरूपम्, श्रीमहाकालीप्रीत्यर्थे प्रथमचरित्रजपे विनियोगः।

ॐ prathamacaritrasya brahmā ṛṣiḥ, mahākālī devatā, gāyatrī chandaḥ, nandā śaktiḥ, raktadantikā bījam, agnistattvam, ṛgvedaḥ svarūpam, śrīmahākālīprītyarthe prathamacaritrajape viniyogaḥ|

Dhyāna

ॐ खड्गं चक्रगदेषुचापपरिघाञ्छूलं भुशुण्डीं शिरः
शङ्खं संदधतीं करैस्त्रिनयनां सर्वाङ्गभूषावृताम्।
नीलाश्मद्युतिमास्यपाददशकां सेवे महाकालिकां
यामस्तौत्स्वपिते हरौ कमलजो हन्तुं मधुं कैटभम्॥

ॐ khaḍgaṃ cakragadeṣucāpaparighāñchūlaṃ bhuśuṇḍīṃ śiraḥ
śaṅkhaṃ saṃdadhatīṃ karaistrinayanāṃ sarvāṅgabhūṣāvṛtām|
nīlāśmadyutimāsyapādadaśakāṃ seve mahākālikāṃ
yāmastautsvapite harau kamalajo hantuṃ madhuṃ kaiṭabham||

Invocation

ॐ नमश्चण्डिकायै

ॐ namaścaṇḍikāyai

1.1Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ॐ ऐं मार्कण्डेय उवाच ॥ १ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मार्कण्डेयः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedmārkaṇḍeya uvāca ||

Separatedmārkaṇḍeyaḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मार्कण्डेयःmārkaṇḍeyaḥMārkaṇḍeya, the sagem. nom. sg. of मार्कण्डेय (taddhita: ‘descendant of Mṛkaṇḍu’); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मार्कण्डेय उवाच mārkaṇḍeya uvāca → मार्कण्डेयः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -aḥ before a vowel other than a: the visarga becomes y, the y is dropped, and the two vowels stand apart (मार्कण्डेय | उवाच). The gap is not closed again.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच्: reduplication उ-वाच with samprasāraṇa of the reduplicated syllable (व → उ). The perfect is the narrative past of the Purāṇas: ‘(he) said’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

मार्कण्डेयः उवाच।

mārkaṇḍeyaḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralMārkaṇḍeya said:

ReadableMārkaṇḍeya said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe speaker label that opens the Devī Māhātmya. In the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa the sage Mārkaṇḍeya is answering his pupil Krauṣṭuki’s questions about the ages of the Manus; the whole of the Devī Māhātmya is a story told inside that answer.

Traditional interpretationThe Gita Press pāṭha counts every uvāca line as a mantra, which is how the text reaches seven hundred (the chapter colophons count them separately: 57 uvāca mantras in all). In recitation this first mantra carries the bījas ॐ ऐं, which belong to the pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa.

1.2Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सावर्णिः सूर्यतनयो यो मनुः कथ्यतेऽष्टमः।
निशामय तदुत्पत्तिं विस्तराद् गदतो मम ॥ २ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सावर्णिः | सूर्यतनयः | यः | मनुः | कथ्यते | अष्टमःनिशामय | तदुत्पत्तिम् | विस्तरात् | गदतः | मम

3IAST

As recitedsāvarṇiḥ sūryatanayo yo manuḥ kathyate'ṣṭamaḥ |
niśāmaya tadutpattiṃ vistarād gadato mama ||

Separatedsāvarṇiḥ | sūryatanayaḥ | yaḥ | manuḥ | kathyate | aṣṭamaḥ
niśāmaya | tadutpattim | vistarāt | gadataḥ | mama

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सावर्णिःsāvarṇiḥSāvarṇim. nom. sg. of सावर्णि, a metronymic: ‘son of Savarṇā’ (Chāyā, the Sun’s consort)
सूर्यतनयःsūryatanayaḥthe son of the Sunm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to सावर्णिः
यःyaḥwhorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.
मनुःmanuḥManu, the progenitor who presides over an agem. nom. sg. of मनु (u-stem); predicate of कथ्यते
कथ्यतेkathyateis called, is spoken of√कथ् (curādi, 10, ‘to tell’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
अष्टमःaṣṭamaḥthe eighthordinal of अष्टन्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies मनुः
निशामयniśāmayahear, listen toनि + √शम् (causative stem निशामय- ‘to perceive’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तदुत्पत्तिम्tadutpattimhis originf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of निशामय
विस्तरात्vistarātin detail, at lengthm. abl. sg. of विस्तर, used adverbially
गदतःgadataḥtelling, as (I) tellpresent participle (śatṛ) of √गद् ‘to speak’, m. gen. sg.; agrees with मम
ममmamaof me, from mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; ‘hear from me’ (the speaker heard is in the genitive)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सूर्यतनयो यो sūryatanayo yo → सूर्यतनयः + यः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o: the visarga (as रु) turns to u, and a + u gives o. The same change gives यो मनुः and गदतो मम.
  • कथ्यतेऽष्टमः kathyate'ṣṭamaḥ → कथ्यते + अष्टमः
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    A word-final e before initial a: the a is absorbed into the e. The avagraha (ऽ) marks the lost a.
  • विस्तराद् गदतो vistarād gadato → विस्तरात् + गदतः
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a voiced sound (here g). The Gita Press writes the d with a virāma and a space.
  • तदुत्पत्तिं tadutpattiṃ → तद् + उत्पत्तिम्
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The pronoun stem tat ends in d before the vowel of उत्पत्ति. The final m of the compound becomes anusvāra before the next word’s v (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सूर्यतनयः sūryatanayaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सूर्य + तनय · Vigraha: सूर्यस्य तनयः sūryasya tanayaḥ
    Literally “son of the Sun”; in the verse: Sāvarṇi, whose father is Sūrya
  • तदुत्पत्तिम् tadutpattim — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: तद् + उत्पत्ति · Vigraha: तस्य उत्पत्तिः (ताम्) tasya utpattiḥ (tām)
    Literally “the origin of him”; in the verse: how Sāvarṇi came to be — the story that follows

C. Morphology

  • कथ्यते kathyate — Passive present: stem कथ्य- (√कथ् + yak) with the ātmanepada ending -ते. ‘Is called’; the Manu is the grammatical subject.
  • निशामय niśāmaya — Imperative of the causative stem of नि + √शम्, a Purāṇic idiom for ‘listen’ (literally ‘perceive’).
  • गदतः gadataḥ — Present participle गदत् (√गद् + śatṛ), genitive singular: ‘of (me) telling’. With verbs of hearing the source is put in the genitive.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यः सूर्यतनयः सावर्णिः अष्टमः मनुः कथ्यते, तदुत्पत्तिं विस्तरात् गदतः मम निशामय।

yaḥ sūryatanayaḥ sāvarṇiḥ aṣṭamaḥ manuḥ kathyate, tadutpattiṃ vistarāt gadataḥ mama niśāmaya |

A relative clause (यः … कथ्यते) names the subject; the main clause is the imperative निशामय with its object तदुत्पत्तिम्, and गदतः मम gives the speaker.

6Translation

LiteralSāvarṇi, the son of the Sun, who is called the eighth Manu — hear his origin from me as I tell it in detail.

ReadableSāvarṇi, the Sun’s son, is spoken of as the eighth Manu. Listen as I tell you at length how he came to be.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMārkaṇḍeya announces his subject: the origin of Sāvarṇi, the eighth Manu. The frame closes in the last mantra of the text (13.29), where King Suratha is promised rebirth from the Sun as Sāvarṇi. The glory of the Goddess is told as the answer to a question about a Manu.

Traditional interpretationThe chapter colophons place the text in the Purāṇa’s account of the Sāvarṇika manvantara (सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे). Each Manu presides over one age of the world; the eighth age is still to come.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the Goddess first appears inside an ordinary story of loss and succession. Grace, in this text, works through history, not around it.

1.3Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

महामायानुभावेन यथा मन्वन्तराधिपः।
स बभूव महाभागः सावर्णिस्तनयो रवेः ॥ ३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

महामायानुभावेन | यथा | मन्वन्तराधिपःसः | बभूव | महाभागः | सावर्णिः | तनयः | रवेः

3IAST

As recitedmahāmāyānubhāvena yathā manvantarādhipaḥ |
sa babhūva mahābhāgaḥ sāvarṇistanayo raveḥ ||

Separatedmahāmāyānubhāvena | yathā | manvantarādhipaḥ
saḥ | babhūva | mahābhāgaḥ | sāvarṇiḥ | tanayaḥ | raveḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
महामायानुभावेनmahāmāyānubhāvenaby the power of Mahāmāyām. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of बभूव
यथाyathāhow, in what wayindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
मन्वन्तराधिपःmanvantarādhipaḥlord of a manvantaram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate nominative with बभूव
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.
बभूवbabhūvabecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
महाभागःmahābhāgaḥthe greatly fortunatem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सावर्णिः
सावर्णिःsāvarṇiḥSāvarṇim. nom. sg.; subject
तनयःtanayaḥsonm. nom. sg.; in apposition to सावर्णिः
रवेःraveḥof Ravi, the Sunm. gen. sg. of रवि (i-stem)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • महामायानुभावेन mahāmāyānubhāvena → महामाया + अनुभावेन
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a: two like vowels merge into one long ā.
  • मन्वन्तराधिपः manvantarādhipaḥ → मनु + अन्तर + अधिपः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ and savarṇa-dīrgha) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    u before a vowel becomes v (मनु + अन्तर → मन्वन्तर); a + a → ā (अन्तर + अधिप → अन्तराधिप).
  • स बभूव sa babhūva → सः + बभूव
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः (and एषः) is dropped before any consonant.
  • सावर्णिस्तनयो sāvarṇistanayo → सावर्णिः + तनयः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; then तनयः before the voiced r of रवेः becomes तनयो (हशि च, 6.1.114).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महामायानुभावेन mahāmāyānubhāvena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member महामाया is itself a karmadhāraya: महती चासौ माया च)
    Members: महामाया + अनुभाव · Vigraha: महामायायाः अनुभावः, तेन mahāmāyāyāḥ anubhāvaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the might of the Great Māyā”; in the verse: by the Goddess’s grace — the first time the text names her
  • मन्वन्तराधिपः manvantarādhipaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (मन्वन्तर = मनोः अन्तरम्, also ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa)
    Members: मन्वन्तर + अधिप · Vigraha: मन्वन्तरस्य अधिपः manvantarasya adhipaḥ
    Literally “overlord of a Manu-interval”; in the verse: the Manu who presides over an age
  • महाभागः mahābhāgaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + भाग · Vigraha: महान् भागः यस्य सः mahān bhāgaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose share (fortune) is great”; in the verse: illustrious, blessed — a standard epithet

C. Morphology

  • बभूव babhūva — Perfect of √भू with the reduplicated syllable ब (bh → b). With a predicate nominative it means ‘became’: स … मन्वन्तराधिपः बभूव.

D. Anvaya — prose order

रवेः तनयः महाभागः सः सावर्णिः महामायानुभावेन यथा मन्वन्तराधिपः बभूव, (तत् शृणु)।

raveḥ tanayaḥ mahābhāgaḥ saḥ sāvarṇiḥ mahāmāyānubhāvena yathā manvantarādhipaḥ babhūva, (tat śṛṇu) |

A dependent clause of manner (यथा … बभूव) still governed by निशामय in 1.2: ‘(hear) how he became …’.

6Translation

LiteralHow, by the power of Mahāmāyā, that greatly fortunate Sāvarṇi, son of Ravi, became lord of a manvantara —

ReadableHear how, by the grace of Mahāmāyā, that blessed Sāvarṇi, son of the Sun, came to rule an age of the world.

The verse continues the sentence of 1.2; ‘Hear’ is supplied from निशामय there.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe cause of Sāvarṇi’s rise is stated at once: the power (अनुभाव) of Mahāmāyā. The story of Suratha that follows (1.4 onward) explains how.

Traditional interpretationMahāmāyā is the name under which the first carita knows the Goddess: the power that binds beings in attachment (1.55–58) and the power that releases them. The same name returns in Medhas’s teaching (1.55) and in Brahmā’s hymn.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names her Mahāmāyā (name 215, महामाया). The shared name is a shared idea in Śākta thought; the two texts remain separate works.

1.4Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स्वारोचिषेऽन्तरे पूर्वं चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः।
सुरथो नाम राजाभूत्समस्ते क्षितिमण्डले ॥ ४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

स्वारोचिषे | अन्तरे | पूर्वम् | चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवःसुरथः | नाम | राजा | अभूत् | समस्ते | क्षितिमण्डले

3IAST

As recitedsvārociṣe'ntare pūrvaṃ caitravaṃśasamudbhavaḥ |
suratho nāma rājābhūtsamaste kṣitimaṇḍale ||

Separatedsvārociṣe | antare | pūrvam | caitravaṃśasamudbhavaḥ
surathaḥ | nāma | rājā | abhūt | samaste | kṣitimaṇḍale

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
स्वारोचिषेsvārociṣein the Svārociṣa (age)n. loc. sg. of स्वारोचिष (taddhita from स्वरोचिस्, the second Manu); qualifies अन्तरे
अन्तरेantarein the interval, in the manvantaran. loc. sg. of अन्तर; here short for मन्वन्तरे
पूर्वम्pūrvamformerly, long agoindeclinable (adverbial acc. of पूर्व)
चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवःcaitravaṃśasamudbhavaḥborn in the line of Caitram. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुरथः
सुरथःsurathaḥSuratham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
नामnāmaby name, namedindeclinable
राजाrājāa kingm. nom. sg. of राजन्; predicate
अभूत्abhūtwas√भू, luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
समस्तेsamasteover the wholen. loc. sg. of समस्त; qualifies क्षितिमण्डले
क्षितिमण्डलेkṣitimaṇḍaleearth’s circle, the whole earthn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वारोचिषेऽन्तरे svārociṣe'ntare → स्वारोचिषे + अन्तरे
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).
  • सुरथो नाम suratho nāma → सुरथः + नाम
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o.
  • राजाभूत् rājābhūt → राजा + अभूत्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • राजाभूत्समस्ते rājābhūtsamaste → राजा + अभूत् + समस्ते
    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)
    After राजा + अभूत् → राजाभूत्, the final t of अभूत् stays unvoiced before the unvoiced s; the words are only written together.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः caitravaṃśasamudbhavaḥ — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (चैत्रवंश)
    Members: चैत्र + वंश + समुद्भव · Vigraha: चैत्रस्य वंशः चैत्रवंशः; चैत्रवंशे समुद्भवः यस्य सः caitrasya vaṃśaḥ caitravaṃśaḥ; caitravaṃśe samudbhavaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “whose birth is in Caitra’s line”; in the verse: a king of the Caitra dynasty
    Alternative: Also read as a saptamī tatpuruṣa, चैत्रवंशे समुद्भवः, ‘one born in Caitra’s line’; the sense is the same.
  • क्षितिमण्डले kṣitimaṇḍale — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: क्षिति + मण्डल · Vigraha: क्षितेः मण्डलम्, तस्मिन् kṣiteḥ maṇḍalam, tasmin
    Literally “in the circle of the earth”; in the verse: over the whole earth

C. Morphology

  • अभूत् abhūt — Root aorist of √भू with augment a-: ‘was, came to be’. Used alongside the perfect for narrative past.
  • स्वारोचिषे svārociṣe — Taddhita derivative: स्वारोचिष ‘belonging to Svarocis’, i.e. the age of the second Manu, Svārociṣa.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पूर्वं स्वारोचिषे अन्तरे चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः सुरथः नाम राजा समस्ते क्षितिमण्डले अभूत्।

pūrvaṃ svārociṣe antare caitravaṃśasamudbhavaḥ surathaḥ nāma rājā samaste kṣitimaṇḍale abhūt |

Time (पूर्वं स्वारोचिषे अन्तरे), subject with its epithet (चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः सुरथः नाम राजा), place (समस्ते क्षितिमण्डले), verb (अभूत्).

6Translation

LiteralFormerly, in the Svārociṣa interval, there was over the whole circle of the earth a king named Suratha, born in Caitra’s line.

ReadableLong ago, in the age of Svārociṣa Manu, there lived a king named Suratha, of the line of Caitra, who ruled the whole earth.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSuratha is introduced: a universal king of an earlier age. The story will take everything from him, in order (1.5–9).

Traditional interpretationSuratha is the king who, at the end of the text, receives his kingdom back and is promised birth as Sāvarṇi (13.25–29). His companion, the merchant Samādhi, will ask for knowledge instead. The tradition reads the pair as the two goals the Goddess grants: enjoyment (bhoga) and liberation (mokṣa).

1.5Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्य पालयतः सम्यक् प्रजाः पुत्रानिवौरसान्।
बभूवुः शत्रवो भूपाः कोलाविध्वंसिनस्तदा ॥ ५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्य | पालयतः | सम्यक् | प्रजाः | पुत्रान् | इव | औरसान्बभूवुः | शत्रवः | भूपाः | कोलाविध्वंसिनः | तदा

3IAST

As recitedtasya pālayataḥ samyak prajāḥ putrānivaurasān |
babhūvuḥ śatravo bhūpāḥ kolāvidhvaṃsinastadā ||

Separatedtasya | pālayataḥ | samyak | prajāḥ | putrān | iva | aurasān
babhūvuḥ | śatravaḥ | bhūpāḥ | kolāvidhvaṃsinaḥ | tadā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तस्यtasyaof him (Suratha)pronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; with पालयतः
पालयतःpālayataḥprotecting, while (he) was rulingpresent participle (śatṛ) of √पाल् (curādi, 10, stem पालय- ‘to protect’), m. gen. sg.; agrees with तस्य
सम्यक्samyakrightly, wellindeclinable (adverb of manner)
प्रजाःprajāḥthe subjects, his peoplef. acc. pl. of प्रजा; object of पालयतः
पुत्रान्putrānsonsm. acc. pl. of पुत्र; the standard of comparison
इवivalike, asindeclinable (particle of comparison)
औरसान्aurasānhis own (lit. ‘born of the breast’, legitimate)m. acc. pl. of औरस (taddhita from उरस् ‘breast’); qualifies पुत्रान्
बभूवुःbabhūvuḥbecame, arose (as)√भू (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana
शत्रवःśatravaḥenemiesm. nom. pl. of शत्रु (u-stem); predicate with बभूवुः
भूपाःbhūpāḥkings (lit. ‘protectors of the earth’)m. nom. pl. of भूप (upapada compound भू + प); subject
कोलाविध्वंसिनःkolāvidhvaṃsinaḥthe Kolāvidhvaṃsins, lit. ‘destroyers of Kolā’m. nom. pl. of कोलाविध्वंसिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भूपाः
तदाtadāthen, at that timeindeclinable (adverb of time)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पुत्रानिवौरसान् putrānivaurasān → पुत्रान् + इव + औरसान्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + au → au: इव and औरसान् fuse into इवौरसान्. The final n of पुत्रान् is unchanged before the vowel; the three words are only written together.
  • शत्रवो भूपाः śatravo bhūpāḥ → शत्रवः + भूपाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant (bh) becomes -o.
  • कोलाविध्वंसिनस्तदा kolāvidhvaṃsinastadā → कोलाविध्वंसिनः + तदा
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कोलाविध्वंसिनः kolāvidhvaṃsinaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (the genitive of the object with an agent noun in -in)
    Members: कोला + विध्वंसिन् · Vigraha: कोलायाः विध्वंसिनः kolāyāḥ vidhvaṃsinaḥ
    Literally “destroyers of Kolā”; in the verse: a group of kings so called; most translators keep it as a name
    Alternative: It may be a proper name of a people or dynasty (the Kolāvidhvaṃsins) rather than a description. The text uses it again in 1.6 as if a known name, which favours the name; the analysis of the compound is the same either way.
  • भूपाः bhūpāḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: भू + प · Vigraha: भुवं पान्ति इति भूपाः bhuvaṃ pānti iti bhūpāḥ
    Literally “earth-protectors”; in the verse: kings

C. Morphology

  • पालयतः pālayataḥ — Present participle of the curādi stem पालय- (+ śatṛ → पालयत्), genitive singular. With तस्य it gives the circumstance: ‘while he was ruling’. The genitive can be read simply as possessive (‘his enemies’) or as the genitive absolute of disregard (‘though he ruled well, kings became his enemies’); the narrative supports both.
  • बभूवुः babhūvuḥ — Perfect of √भू, third person plural (-उः). With a predicate nominative, ‘became’: भूपाः शत्रवः बभूवुः ‘kings became (his) enemies’.
  • औरसान् aurasān — Taddhita in -a with vṛddhi of the first syllable: उरस् ‘breast’ → औरस ‘born of the breast’, i.e. a son of one’s own body, as opposed to an adopted son.

D. Anvaya — prose order

प्रजाः औरसान् पुत्रान् इव सम्यक् पालयतः तस्य तदा कोलाविध्वंसिनः भूपाः शत्रवः बभूवुः।

prajāḥ aurasān putrān iva samyak pālayataḥ tasya tadā kolāvidhvaṃsinaḥ bhūpāḥ śatravaḥ babhūvuḥ |

The genitive phrase (तस्य पालयतः …) frames the clause; the subject is भूपाः with its epithet कोलाविध्वंसिनः, शत्रवः is the predicate, बभूवुः the verb.

6Translation

LiteralOf him, as he protected his subjects rightly like his own sons, the kings, the Kolāvidhvaṃsins, then became enemies.

ReadableWhile he ruled his people justly, as though they were his own children, the kings called the Kolāvidhvaṃsins rose against him as enemies.

‘Rose against him’ renders बभूवुः शत्रवः (‘became enemies’) with the genitive तस्य.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first loss begins. Suratha is a good king — he rules his subjects ‘like his own sons’ — and still enemies arise. The text gives no fault on his side as the cause.

Traditional interpretationRuling one’s people as one’s own children is the classical ideal of kingship in the epics and Purāṇas; the verse states it in a single simile and moves on.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: कोलाविध्वंसिनः: whether a proper name of a people or dynasty or a description (‘destroyers of Kolā’), and what Kolā refers to (a city is often assumed), cannot be settled from the text.

1.6Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्य तैरभवद् युद्धमतिप्रबलदण्डिनः।
न्यूनैरपि स तैर्युद्धे कोलाविध्वंसिभिर्जितः ॥ ६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्य | तैः | अभवत् | युद्धम् | अतिप्रबलदण्डिनःन्यूनैः | अपि | सः | तैः | युद्धे | कोलाविध्वंसिभिः | जितः

3IAST

As recitedtasya tairabhavad yuddhamatiprabaladaṇḍinaḥ |
nyūnairapi sa tairyuddhe kolāvidhvaṃsibhirjitaḥ ||

Separatedtasya | taiḥ | abhavat | yuddham | atiprabaladaṇḍinaḥ
nyūnaiḥ | api | saḥ | taiḥ | yuddhe | kolāvidhvaṃsibhiḥ | jitaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तस्यtasyahis (Suratha’s)pronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; with अतिप्रबलदण्डिनः
तैःtaiḥwith thempronoun तद्, m. instr. pl. (instrumental of association: ‘a battle with them’)
अभवत्abhavatthere was, took place√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
युद्धम्yuddhama battlen. nom. sg. of युद्ध; subject of अभवत्
अतिप्रबलदण्डिनःatiprabaladaṇḍinaḥwho wielded a very mighty force (daṇḍa: the rod of rule, an army)m. gen. sg. of अतिप्रबलदण्डिन्; compound (see Samāsa); agrees with तस्य
न्यूनैःnyūnaiḥinferior (in strength)m. instr. pl. of न्यून; qualifies तैः … कोलाविध्वंसिभिः
अपिapieven, thoughindeclinable (particle: concessive ‘even’)
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of जितः
तैःtaiḥby thempronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; agent
युद्धेyuddhein battlen. loc. sg. of युद्ध
कोलाविध्वंसिभिःkolāvidhvaṃsibhiḥby the Kolāvidhvaṃsinsm. instr. pl. of कोलाविध्वंसिन्; compound (as in 1.5); agent of जितः
जितःjitaḥwas defeatedpast passive participle (kta) of √जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तैरभवद् tairabhavad → तैः + अभवत्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā (here ai), the visarga stands as r before a vowel or voiced consonant. The same gives न्यूनैरपि, तैर्युद्धे and कोलाविध्वंसिभिर्जितः.
  • अभवद् युद्धम् abhavad yuddham → अभवत् + युद्धम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced y; the Gita Press writes the d with virāma and a space.
  • स तैर्युद्धे sa tairyuddhe → सः + तैः + युद्धे
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before the consonant t.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिप्रबलदण्डिनः atiprabaladaṇḍinaḥ — karmadhāraya (अतिप्रबलदण्ड) with the possessive taddhita -in; अतिप्रबल is itself a prādi tatpuruṣa (अतिशयेन प्रबलः)
    Members: अतिप्रबल + दण्ड + -इन् · Vigraha: अतिप्रबलः दण्डः अतिप्रबलदण्डः; सः अस्य अस्ति इति अतिप्रबलदण्डी, तस्य atiprabalaḥ daṇḍaḥ atiprabaladaṇḍaḥ; saḥ asya asti iti atiprabaladaṇḍī, tasya
    Literally “possessing an exceedingly strong rod”; in the verse: a king whose army and power of punishment were very great
    Alternative: दण्ड can mean the army or the king’s power to punish (daṇḍanīti); both fit, and the contrast with न्यूनैः (‘weaker’) favours military strength.

C. Morphology

  • जितः jitaḥ — Past passive participle of √जि with the auxiliary ‘was’ understood: सः … जितः ‘he (was) defeated’. The agent is in the instrumental (कोलाविध्वंसिभिः).
  • अभवत् abhavat — Imperfect of √भू with augment a-: ‘(a battle) took place’. The narrative moves between imperfect, aorist and perfect without difference of sense.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अतिप्रबलदण्डिनः तस्य तैः युद्धम् अभवत्। न्यूनैः अपि तैः कोलाविध्वंसिभिः सः युद्धे जितः।

atiprabaladaṇḍinaḥ tasya taiḥ yuddham abhavat | nyūnaiḥ api taiḥ kolāvidhvaṃsibhiḥ saḥ yuddhe jitaḥ |

Two sentences: a battle took place (युद्धम् अभवत्); he was defeated (सः जितः), with the agent and its concessive qualifier न्यूनैः अपि.

6Translation

LiteralA battle took place between him, who had a very strong force, and them; even though they were weaker, he was defeated in battle by those Kolāvidhvaṃsins.

ReadableWar broke out between them and the king, mighty as his forces were; and though they were the weaker side, the Kolāvidhvaṃsins defeated him in battle.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe paradox is in अपि: the strong king loses to the weaker side. The verse does not explain it; the following verses show his own ministers turning against him.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: strength on paper does not decide the outcome. The story strips Suratha of power that was never as secure as it looked.

1.7Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततः स्वपुरमायातो निजदेशाधिपोऽभवत्।
आक्रान्तः स महाभागस्तैस्तदा प्रबलारिभिः ॥ ७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | स्वपुरम् | आयातः | निजदेशाधिपः | अभवत्आक्रान्तः | सः | महाभागः | तैः | तदा | प्रबलारिभिः

3IAST

As recitedtataḥ svapuramāyāto nijadeśādhipo'bhavat |
ākrāntaḥ sa mahābhāgastaistadā prabalāribhiḥ ||

Separatedtataḥ | svapuram | āyātaḥ | nijadeśādhipaḥ | abhavat
ākrāntaḥ | saḥ | mahābhāgaḥ | taiḥ | tadā | prabalāribhiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthen, thereuponindeclinable (adverb, from तद् + tasil)
स्वपुरम्svapuramto his own cityn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); goal of आयातः
आयातःāyātaḥhaving come (back)past participle (kta) of आ + √या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’; with आ ‘to come’), m. nom. sg.; active sense
निजदेशाधिपःnijadeśādhipaḥthe ruler of his own country (only)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with अभवत्
अभवत्abhavatbecame, was√भू (bhvādi, 1P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
आक्रान्तःākrāntaḥwas attacked, overrunpast passive participle (kta) of आ + √क्रम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to step’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.
महाभागःmahābhāgaḥthe illustrious (king)m. nom. sg.; compound (bahuvrīhi, as in 1.3); qualifies सः
तैःtaiḥby thempronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; agent
तदाtadāthen, at that timeindeclinable (adverb of time)
प्रबलारिभिःprabalāribhiḥby powerful enemiesm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to तैः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • आयातो निज āyāto nija → आयातः + निजदेशाधिपः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o.
  • निजदेशाधिपोऽभवत् nijadeśādhipo'bhavat → निजदेशाधिपः + अभवत्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a- becomes -o, and the following a is absorbed (avagraha). Inside the compound, देश + अधिप → देशाधिप by अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101).
  • आक्रान्तः स महाभागस्तैस्तदा ākrāntaḥ sa mahābhāgastaistadā → आक्रान्तः + सः + महाभागः + तैः + तदा
    visarga sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    सः loses its visarga before m; महाभागः and तैः each turn their visarga to s before t.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्वपुरम् svapuram — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: स्व + पुर · Vigraha: स्वस्य पुरम् svasya puram
    Literally “the city of oneself”; in the verse: his own capital
    Alternative: Also analysed as a karmadhāraya, स्वं च तत् पुरं च, ‘his own city’; the sense is the same.
  • निजदेशाधिपः nijadeśādhipaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya
    Members: निजदेश + अधिप · Vigraha: निजः देशः निजदेशः (karmadhāraya); निजदेशस्य अधिपः nijaḥ deśaḥ nijadeśaḥ (karmadhāraya); nijadeśasya adhipaḥ
    Literally “overlord of his own country”; in the verse: the universal king of 1.4 is now master only of his home territory
  • प्रबलारिभिः prabalāribhiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: प्रबल + अरि · Vigraha: प्रबलाः अरयः, तैः prabalāḥ arayaḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by strong enemies”; in the verse: by enemies now grown powerful

C. Morphology

  • आयातः āyātaḥ — With verbs of motion the kta participle is active: आयातः ‘(he) having come’, not ‘having been reached’. Its goal stands in the accusative (स्वपुरम्).
  • आक्रान्तः ākrāntaḥ — Here the kta is passive, with the agent in the instrumental (तैः … प्रबलारिभिः): ‘he was overrun by them’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः स्वपुरम् आयातः (सः) निजदेशाधिपः अभवत्। तदा सः महाभागः तैः प्रबलारिभिः आक्रान्तः।

tataḥ svapuram āyātaḥ (saḥ) nijadeśādhipaḥ abhavat | tadā saḥ mahābhāgaḥ taiḥ prabalāribhiḥ ākrāntaḥ |

First sentence: participle + predicate nominative + verb. Second: passive participle as verb with an instrumental agent.

6Translation

LiteralThen, having come to his own city, he became ruler of his own country; then that illustrious one was attacked by them, the powerful enemies.

ReadableThen he withdrew to his own city and ruled only his own land; and even there that noble king was assailed by those enemies, now grown strong.

‘Only’ and ‘even there’ make explicit the contrast with 1.4 (‘over the whole earth’), which the compound निजदेशाधिपः implies.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second loss: the emperor of 1.4 is reduced to his home territory, and the enemies follow him there.

Traditional interpretationThe steps of Suratha’s fall — empire, country, city, treasury, army — are told in order, so that by 1.9 nothing royal is left to him but the name महाभाग.

1.8Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अमात्यैर्बलिभिर्दुष्टैर्दुर्बलस्य दुरात्मभिः।
कोशो बलं चापहृतं तत्रापि स्वपुरे ततः ॥ ८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अमात्यैः | बलिभिः | दुष्टैः | दुर्बलस्य | दुरात्मभिःकोशः | बलम् | च | अपहृतम् | तत्र | अपि | स्वपुरे | ततः

3IAST

As recitedamātyairbalibhirduṣṭairdurbalasya durātmabhiḥ |
kośo balaṃ cāpahṛtaṃ tatrāpi svapure tataḥ ||

Separatedamātyaiḥ | balibhiḥ | duṣṭaiḥ | durbalasya | durātmabhiḥ
kośaḥ | balam | ca | apahṛtam | tatra | api | svapure | tataḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अमात्यैःamātyaiḥby (his) ministersm. instr. pl. of अमात्य; agent of अपहृतम्
बलिभिःbalibhiḥpowerfulm. instr. pl. of बलिन् (‘possessing strength’); qualifies अमात्यैः
दुष्टैःduṣṭaiḥcorrupt, wickedpast participle (kta) of √दुष् (divādi, 4P ‘to be spoiled’), used as an adjective, m. instr. pl.; qualifies अमात्यैः
दुर्बलस्यdurbalasyaof the weakened (king)m. gen. sg. of दुर्बल; compound (see Samāsa); possessor of कोशः and बलम्
दुरात्मभिःdurātmabhiḥevil-mindedm. instr. pl. of दुरात्मन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अमात्यैः
कोशःkośaḥthe treasurym. nom. sg. of कोश; subject
बलम्balamthe armyn. nom. sg. of बल; subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अपहृतम्apahṛtamwas carried off, seizedpast passive participle (kta) of अप + √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take’), n. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)
स्वपुरेsvapurein his own cityn. loc. sg.; compound (as in 1.7); in apposition to तत्र
ततःtataḥthen, after thatindeclinable (adverb)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अमात्यैर्बलिभिर्दुष्टैर्दुर्बलस्य amātyairbalibhirduṣṭairdurbalasya → अमात्यैः + बलिभिः + दुष्टैः + दुर्बलस्य
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai and i the visarga stands as r before the voiced b and d: three junctions in a row, which give the line its rolling sound.
  • कोशो बलं kośo balaṃ → कोशः + बलम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced b becomes -o.
  • चापहृतं cāpahṛtaṃ → च + अपहृतम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. The final m of अपहृतम् becomes anusvāra before t (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • तत्रापि tatrāpi → तत्र + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दुर्बलस्य durbalasya — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुस् + बल · Vigraha: दुष्टं (अल्पं) बलं यस्य सः, तस्य duṣṭaṃ (alpaṃ) balaṃ yasya saḥ, tasya
    Literally “of one whose strength is poor”; in the verse: of the king, now weak
  • दुरात्मभिः durātmabhiḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुस् + आत्मन् · Vigraha: दुष्टः आत्मा येषां ते, तैः duṣṭaḥ ātmā yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those whose selves are bad”; in the verse: by the wicked-hearted ministers

C. Morphology

  • अपहृतम् apahṛtam — Passive participle as verb, neuter singular although it has a masculine (कोशः) and a neuter (बलम्) subject: it agrees with the nearer one, बलम्, or takes the neuter for a mixed pair. ‘The treasury and the army were seized.’
  • बलिभिः balibhiḥ — Instrumental plural of बलिन्, an -in stem (बल + possessive -in): ‘having strength’. Contrast दुर्बलस्य in the same line: the ministers are strong, the king weak.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः तत्र स्वपुरे अपि बलिभिः दुष्टैः दुरात्मभिः अमात्यैः दुर्बलस्य (तस्य) कोशः बलं च अपहृतम्।

tataḥ tatra svapure api balibhiḥ duṣṭaiḥ durātmabhiḥ amātyaiḥ durbalasya (tasya) kośaḥ balaṃ ca apahṛtam |

The passive participle अपहृतम् is the verb; its subjects are कोशः बलं च, its agent the ministers with three adjectives, and दुर्बलस्य names whose treasury.

6Translation

LiteralThen, even there in his own city, the treasury and the army of the weakened one were carried off by his powerful, corrupt, evil-minded ministers.

ReadableThen, in his own city too, his powerful ministers — corrupt and wicked men — seized the weakened king’s treasury and army.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe third loss comes from inside: his own ministers take the treasury (कोश) and the army (बल), the two supports of kingship. The king is now called दुर्बल ‘weak’, the ministers बलिन् ‘strong’.

Traditional interpretationTreasury and army are two of the classical ‘limbs’ of the state (rājyāṅga); Suratha will speak of his attachment to the limbs of his kingdom in 1.41.

1.9Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततो मृगयाव्याजेन हृतस्वाम्यः स भूपतिः।
एकाकी हयमारुह्य जगाम गहनं वनम् ॥ ९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | मृगयाव्याजेन | हृतस्वाम्यः | सः | भूपतिःएकाकी | हयम् | आरुह्य | जगाम | गहनम् | वनम्

3IAST

As recitedtato mṛgayāvyājena hṛtasvāmyaḥ sa bhūpatiḥ |
ekākī hayamāruhya jagāma gahanaṃ vanam ||

Separatedtataḥ | mṛgayāvyājena | hṛtasvāmyaḥ | saḥ | bhūpatiḥ
ekākī | hayam | āruhya | jagāma | gahanam | vanam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb)
मृगयाव्याजेनmṛgayāvyājenaunder the pretext of huntingm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
हृतस्वाम्यःhṛtasvāmyaḥrobbed of his sovereigntym. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भूपतिः
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; with भूपतिः
भूपतिःbhūpatiḥking (lit. ‘lord of the earth’)m. nom. sg. of भूपति; subject
एकाकीekākīalonem. nom. sg. of एकाकिन्; predicative adjective
हयम्hayama horsem. acc. sg. of हय; object of आरुह्य
आरुह्यāruhyahaving mountedabsolutive (lyap) of आ + √रुह् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to climb’)
जगामjagāmawent√गम् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
गहनम्gahanamdense, deepn. acc. sg. of गहन; qualifies वनम्
वनम्vanamforestn. acc. sg. of वन; goal of जगाम

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततो मृगया tato mṛgayā → ततः + मृगयाव्याजेन
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o.
  • स भूपतिः sa bhūpatiḥ → सः + भूपतिः
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant.
  • गहनं वनम् gahanaṃ vanam → गहनम् + वनम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मृगयाव्याजेन mṛgayāvyājena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मृगया + व्याज · Vigraha: मृगयायाः व्याजः, तेन mṛgayāyāḥ vyājaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the pretext of the hunt”; in the verse: on the excuse of going hunting
  • हृतस्वाम्यः hṛtasvāmyaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: हृत + स्वाम्य · Vigraha: हृतं स्वाम्यं यस्य सः hṛtaṃ svāmyaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose lordship has been taken”; in the verse: the dispossessed king
  • भूपतिः bhūpatiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: भू + पति · Vigraha: भुवः पतिः bhuvaḥ patiḥ
    Literally “lord of the earth”; in the verse: king — said with irony of a king who has lost his land

C. Morphology

  • आरुह्य āruhya — Absolutive with a prefix, so -य (lyap) instead of -त्वा: आ + √रुह् + ल्यप् → आरुह्य ‘having mounted’. Its subject is the subject of the main verb जगाम.
  • एकाकी ekākī — Nominative singular of एकाकिन् ‘solitary’ (एक + -आकिन्); n-stems in -in lose the n in the nominative and lengthen the i.
  • जगाम jagāma — Perfect of √गम्, with reduplication ज- (g → j) and vṛddhi of the root vowel in the third singular.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः हृतस्वाम्यः सः भूपतिः मृगयाव्याजेन एकाकी हयम् आरुह्य गहनं वनं जगाम।

tataḥ hṛtasvāmyaḥ saḥ bhūpatiḥ mṛgayāvyājena ekākī hayam āruhya gahanaṃ vanaṃ jagāma |

Subject with its bahuvrīhi epithet, the manner (मृगयाव्याजेन, एकाकी), the absolutive with its object, then the main verb with its goal.

6Translation

LiteralThen that king, robbed of his sovereignty, on the pretext of hunting, alone, having mounted a horse, went to a dense forest.

ReadableStripped of his kingdom, the king rode out alone on horseback under the pretext of a hunt, and went into a deep forest.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last step: the king leaves alone, keeping up appearances (‘as if to hunt’). The forest is where the story can turn.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the pretext matters. Even in defeat Suratha is guarding how he is seen — the attachment to position that he will confess in 1.12–16.

1.10Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स तत्राश्रममद्राक्षीद् द्विजवर्यस्य मेधसः।
प्रशान्तश्वापदाकीर्णं मुनिशिष्योपशोभितम् ॥ १० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सः | तत्र | आश्रमम् | अद्राक्षीत् | द्विजवर्यस्य | मेधसःप्रशान्तश्वापदाकीर्णम् | मुनिशिष्योपशोभितम्

3IAST

As recitedsa tatrāśramamadrākṣīd dvijavaryasya medhasaḥ |
praśāntaśvāpadākīrṇaṃ muniśiṣyopaśobhitam ||

Separatedsaḥ | tatra | āśramam | adrākṣīt | dvijavaryasya | medhasaḥ
praśāntaśvāpadākīrṇam | muniśiṣyopaśobhitam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सःsaḥhe (the king)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject
तत्रtatrathere (in the forest)indeclinable (adverb of place)
आश्रमम्āśramama hermitagem. acc. sg. of आश्रम; object of अद्राक्षीत्
अद्राक्षीत्adrākṣītsaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’), luṅ (s-aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
द्विजवर्यस्यdvijavaryasyaof the best of the twice-bornm. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मेधसः
मेधसःmedhasaḥof Medhas (the sage)m. gen. sg. of मेधस् (s-stem), proper name; possessor of आश्रमम्
प्रशान्तश्वापदाकीर्णम्praśāntaśvāpadākīrṇamfilled with wild beasts grown peacefulm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies आश्रमम्
मुनिशिष्योपशोभितम्muniśiṣyopaśobhitamgraced by the sage’s disciplesm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies आश्रमम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स तत्राश्रमम् sa tatrāśramam → सः + तत्र + आश्रमम्
    visarga sandhi (सः) and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    सः drops its visarga before t; तत्र + आश्रमम्: a + ā → ā.
  • अद्राक्षीद् द्विज adrākṣīd dvija → अद्राक्षीत् + द्विजवर्यस्य
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced d; printed with virāma and a space.
  • श्वापदाकीर्णं śvāpadākīrṇaṃ → श्वापद + आकीर्णम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā. The final m becomes anusvāra before the m of मुनि- (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • शिष्योपशोभितम् śiṣyopaśobhitam → शिष्य + उपशोभितम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • द्विजवर्यस्य dvijavaryasya — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: द्विज + वर्य · Vigraha: द्विजेषु वर्यः, तस्य dvijeṣu varyaḥ, tasya
    Literally “of the choicest among the twice-born”; in the verse: of that eminent brahmin
    Alternative: Equally read as ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa द्विजानां वर्यः; the sense is the same.
  • प्रशान्तश्वापदाकीर्णम् praśāntaśvāpadākīrṇam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya
    Members: प्रशान्तश्वापद + आकीर्ण · Vigraha: प्रशान्ताः श्वापदाः प्रशान्तश्वापदाः (karmadhāraya); प्रशान्तश्वापदैः आकीर्णम् praśāntāḥ śvāpadāḥ praśāntaśvāpadāḥ (karmadhāraya); praśāntaśvāpadaiḥ ākīrṇam
    Literally “strewn with calmed beasts of prey”; in the verse: a hermitage where even predators had become peaceful
  • मुनिशिष्योपशोभितम् muniśiṣyopaśobhitam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मुनिशिष्य + उपशोभित · Vigraha: मुनेः शिष्याः मुनिशिष्याः (ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa); मुनिशिष्यैः उपशोभितम् muneḥ śiṣyāḥ muniśiṣyāḥ (ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa); muniśiṣyaiḥ upaśobhitam
    Literally “adorned by the sage’s pupils”; in the verse: made beautiful by the presence of Medhas’s disciples
    Alternative: मुनिशिष्य can also be a dvandva, मुनयश्च शिष्याश्च ‘sages and disciples’; the singular sage of the verse (द्विजवर्यस्य मेधसः) favours ‘the sage’s disciples’.

C. Morphology

  • अद्राक्षीत् adrākṣīt — s-aorist of √दृश्: augment अ-; the root takes an inserted a (दृश् → द्रश्), lengthened in this aorist (द्राश्); ś becomes k before the aorist s, which turns to ṣ (द्राक्ष्); ending -ईत्. The present uses the suppletive stem पश्य-; the aorist is built on the root itself.
  • मेधसः medhasaḥ — Genitive singular of the s-stem मेधस्; the same form could be nominative/accusative plural of a different stem, but the sense here is ‘of Medhas’. The name means ‘intelligence, wisdom’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सः तत्र द्विजवर्यस्य मेधसः प्रशान्तश्वापदाकीर्णं मुनिशिष्योपशोभितम् आश्रमम् अद्राक्षीत्।

saḥ tatra dvijavaryasya medhasaḥ praśāntaśvāpadākīrṇaṃ muniśiṣyopaśobhitam āśramam adrākṣīt |

Subject, place, the owner of the hermitage (genitive), two compound adjectives of the object, the object, and the verb.

6Translation

LiteralThere he saw the hermitage of Medhas, best of the twice-born, filled with beasts of prey grown peaceful and graced by the sage’s disciples.

ReadableThere he came upon the hermitage of Medhas, foremost of brahmins — a place where even the wild beasts had grown gentle, graced by the sage’s disciples.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe turning point of the frame story. In the forest the king finds Medhas, who will become his teacher; the rest of the Devī Māhātmya is Medhas’s answer to his question (1.40 onward).

Traditional interpretationPredators living peacefully around a sage is a common sign, in epic and Purāṇic description, of the power of an ascetic’s calm. The setting shows what Suratha does not yet have.

1.11Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्थौ कंचित्स कालं च मुनिना तेन सत्कृतः।
इतश्चेतश्च विचरंस्तस्मिन्मुनिवराश्रमे ॥ ११ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्थौ | कंचित् | सः | कालम् | च | मुनिना | तेन | सत्कृतःइतः | च | इतः | च | विचरन् | तस्मिन् | मुनिवराश्रमे

3IAST

As recitedtasthau kaṃcitsa kālaṃ ca muninā tena satkṛtaḥ |
itaścetaśca vicaraṃstasminmunivarāśrame ||

Separatedtasthau | kaṃcit | saḥ | kālam | ca | muninā | tena | satkṛtaḥ
itaḥ | ca | itaḥ | ca | vicaran | tasmin | munivarāśrame

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तस्थौtasthaustayed√स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand, stay’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
कंचित्kaṃcitsomeindefinite pronoun किम् + चित्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies कालम्
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject
कालम्kālamtimem. acc. sg. of काल; accusative of duration
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
मुनिनाmunināby the sagem. instr. sg. of मुनि; agent of सत्कृतः
तेनtenaby that (sage)pronoun तद्, m. instr. sg.; with मुनिना
सत्कृतःsatkṛtaḥhonoured, received with hospitalitypast passive participle (kta) of सत् + √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. sg.; qualifies सः
इतःitaḥhere (lit. ‘from here’)indeclinable (adverb, इदम् + tasil)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
इतःitaḥthere (lit. ‘from here’)indeclinable (adverb)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); इतश्चेतश्च ‘hither and thither’
विचरन्vicaranwandering aboutpresent participle (śatṛ) of वि + √चर् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to move’), m. nom. sg.; qualifies सः
तस्मिन्tasminin thatpronoun तद्, m. loc. sg.; with मुनिवराश्रमे
मुनिवराश्रमेmunivarāśramein the hermitage of the excellent sagem. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कंचित्स कालं kaṃcitsa kālaṃ → कंचित् + सः + कालम्
    visarga sandhi (सः); no change of t · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); खरि च (8.4.55)
    सः loses its visarga before k. The t of कंचित् stays unvoiced before the unvoiced s; the words are only written together.
  • इतश्चेतश्च itaścetaśca → इतः + च + इतः + च
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (guṇa) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga before c becomes s, then palatal ś next to c; च + इतः → चेतः (a + i → e).
  • विचरंस्तस्मिन् vicaraṃstasmin → विचरन् + तस्मिन्
    consonant sandhi (n before t) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)
    Final n before t becomes ṃs: विचरन् → विचरंस्.
  • मुनिवराश्रमे munivarāśrame → मुनिवर + आश्रमे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मुनिवराश्रमे munivarāśrame — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मुनिवर + आश्रम · Vigraha: मुनिषु वरः मुनिवरः (saptamī tatpuruṣa); मुनिवरस्य आश्रमः, तस्मिन् muniṣu varaḥ munivaraḥ (saptamī tatpuruṣa); munivarasya āśramaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the hermitage of the best among sages”; in the verse: in Medhas’s hermitage

C. Morphology

  • तस्थौ tasthau — Perfect of √स्था: reduplication त- (the s of the cluster is dropped, the stop kept and deaspirated) and the ending -औ of ā-roots in the first and third singular.
  • कंचित् kaṃcit — Accusative of the interrogative किम् with the indefinite particle -चित्: कम् + चित् ‘some (one)’. With कालम् it is an accusative of duration: ‘for some time’.
  • विचरन् vicaran — Present participle of वि + √चर्, nominative singular masculine (stem विचरत्): ‘while wandering’, simultaneous with तस्थौ.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तेन मुनिना सत्कृतः सः तस्मिन् मुनिवराश्रमे इतः च इतः च विचरन् कंचित् कालं तस्थौ च।

tena muninā satkṛtaḥ saḥ tasmin munivarāśrame itaḥ ca itaḥ ca vicaran kaṃcit kālaṃ tasthau ca |

Main verb तस्थौ with an accusative of time; the participles सत्कृतः and विचरन् describe the subject. च joins this to the previous verse’s action.

6Translation

LiteralAnd he stayed for some time, honoured by that sage, wandering here and there in that hermitage of the excellent sage.

ReadableWelcomed with honour by the sage, he stayed there for a while, wandering here and there about the great sage’s hermitage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSuratha is received as a guest and stays. He has not yet asked anything; his wandering ‘here and there’ prepares the restless thoughts of the next verses.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the body is at rest in a peaceful place, the mind is not. Outer refuge does not by itself settle inner turmoil.

1.12Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सोऽचिन्तयत्तदा तत्र ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनः।
मत्पूर्वैः पालितं पूर्वं मया हीनं पुरं हि तत् ॥ १२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सः | अचिन्तयत् | तदा | तत्र | ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनःमत्पूर्वैः | पालितम् | पूर्वम् | मया | हीनम् | पुरम् | हि | तत्

3IAST

As recitedso'cintayattadā tatra mamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥ |
matpūrvaiḥ pālitaṃ pūrvaṃ mayā hīnaṃ puraṃ hi tat ||

Separatedsaḥ | acintayat | tadā | tatra | mamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥ
matpūrvaiḥ | pālitam | pūrvam | mayā | hīnam | puram | hi | tat

सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject
अचिन्तयत्acintayatthought, reflected√चिन्त् (curādi, 10P ‘to think’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनःmamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥhis mind drawn away by the sense of ‘mine’m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सः
मत्पूर्वैःmatpūrvaiḥby my forefathersm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of पालितम्
पालितम्pālitamprotected, ruledpast passive participle (kta) of √पाल् (curādi, 10 ‘to protect’), n. nom. sg.; qualifies पुरम्

हीनम्hīnambereft, deprived (of me)past participle (kta) of √हा (juhotyādi, 3P ‘to leave’), n. nom. sg.; qualifies पुरम्

हिhiindeed, nowindeclinable (emphatic particle)
तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; with पुरम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सोऽचिन्तयत् so'cintayat → सः + अचिन्तयत्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Before a vowel the special rule for सः (6.1.132) does not apply; सः behaves like any -aḥ: so + a → so’ with avagraha.
  • ममत्वाकृष्ट mamatvākṛṣṭa → ममत्व + आकृष्ट
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • मत्पूर्वैः matpūrvaiḥ → मद् + पूर्वैः
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (devoicing) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    The compound stem मद् (‘I, my’) ends in t before the unvoiced p.
  • पालितं पूर्वं मया हीनं पुरं pālitaṃ pūrvaṃ mayā hīnaṃ puraṃ → पालितम् + पूर्वम् + मया + हीनम् + पुरम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, four times in one line.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनः mamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥ — bahuvrīhi (its first part ममत्वाकृष्ट is a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa: ममत्वेन आकृष्टा)
    Members: ममत्व + आकृष्ट + चेतना · Vigraha: ममत्वेन आकृष्टा चेतना यस्य सः mamatvena ākṛṣṭā cetanā yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose mind is pulled by mine-ness”; in the verse: possessed by the thought ‘this is mine’
    Alternative: चेतन (n.) ‘mind’ may be the last member instead of चेतना (f.); the bahuvrīhi ends in -चेतनः either way.
  • मत्पूर्वैः matpūrvaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मद् + पूर्व · Vigraha: मम पूर्वे, तैः mama pūrve, taiḥ
    Literally “by my predecessors”; in the verse: by my ancestors, the earlier kings of my line

C. Morphology

  • ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनः mamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥ — ममत्व is the genitive मम ‘of me’ made into an abstract noun with -त्व: ‘mine-ness’, possessiveness. The word returns in 1.41 and 1.44 (ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौ), where the king and the merchant both confess it.
  • हीनम् hīnam — Participle of √हा ‘to leave’ (हीन ‘left, lacking’). With an instrumental it means ‘deprived of’: मया हीनम् ‘deprived of me’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तदा तत्र ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनः सः अचिन्तयत् — पूर्वं मत्पूर्वैः पालितं मया हीनं तत् पुरं हि …

tadā tatra mamatvākṛṣṭacetanaḥ saḥ acintayat — pūrvaṃ matpūrvaiḥ pālitaṃ mayā hīnaṃ tat puraṃ hi …

The first line introduces the king’s thought; the second line begins it (the thought runs to 1.16), with पुरम् the subject of पाल्यते in 1.13.

6Translation

LiteralThen, there, he, his mind drawn by possessiveness, thought: ‘That city, protected formerly by my forefathers, now deprived of me —

ReadableThen, his mind still held by the sense of ‘mine’, he brooded: ‘That city my forefathers once protected, now bereft of me —

‘Now’ is supplied for the contrast with पूर्वम्; the sentence is completed in 1.13.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe cause of Suratha’s grief is named at once: ममत्व, the feeling of ‘mine’. His thoughts (1.12–16) are a list of things he no longer owns — city, elephant, servants, treasury.

Traditional interpretationममत्व is the theme of the frame story. At the end the merchant asks the Goddess for the knowledge that removes ‘mine’ and ‘I’ (13.18: ममेत्यहमिति … सङ्गविच्युतिकारकम्), while the king asks for his kingdom (13.17).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess as free of and destroying this very thing: names 164 निर्ममा and 165 ममताहन्त्री. The two texts are distinct; the shared idea is the possessiveness the Goddess removes.

1.13Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मद्भृत्यैस्तैरसद्वृत्तैर्धर्मतः पाल्यते न वा।
न जाने स प्रधानो मे शूरहस्ती सदामदः ॥ १३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मद्भृत्यैः | तैः | असद्वृत्तैः | धर्मतः | पाल्यते | न | वान | जाने | सः | प्रधानः | मे | शूरहस्ती | सदामदः

3IAST

As recitedmadbhṛtyaistairasadvṛttairdharmataḥ pālyate na vā |
na jāne sa pradhāno me śūrahastī sadāmadaḥ ||

Separatedmadbhṛtyaiḥ | taiḥ | asadvṛttaiḥ | dharmataḥ | pālyate | na | vā
na | jāne | saḥ | pradhānaḥ | me | śūrahastī | sadāmadaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मद्भृत्यैःmadbhṛtyaiḥby my servantsm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of पाल्यते
तैःtaiḥby thosepronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; with मद्भृत्यैः
असद्वृत्तैःasadvṛttaiḥof bad conductm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मद्भृत्यैः
धर्मतःdharmataḥaccording to dharma, rightlyindeclinable (धर्म + tasil, adverb)
पाल्यतेpālyateis (it) protected√पाल् (curādi, 10), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
वाvāorindeclinable (disjunctive); न वा ‘or not’
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
जानेjāneI know√ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U ‘to know’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; with शूरहस्ती
प्रधानःpradhānaḥchiefm. nom. sg. of प्रधान; qualifies शूरहस्ती
मेmemyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg. (enclitic form)
शूरहस्तीśūrahastīwar-elephant (lit. ‘valiant elephant’)m. nom. sg. of शूरहस्तिन्; compound (see Samāsa); subject of यातः … उपलप्स्यते in 1.14
सदामदःsadāmadaḥalways in rut (musth)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शूरहस्ती

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मद्भृत्यैस्तैरसद्वृत्तैर्धर्मतः madbhṛtyaistairasadvṛttairdharmataḥ → मद्भृत्यैः + तैः + असद्वृत्तैः + धर्मतः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga before t becomes s (भृत्यैस्तैः); after ai it stands as r before a vowel (तैरसद्) and before the voiced dh (वृत्तैर्धर्मतः).
  • मद्भृत्यै madbhṛtyai → मद् + भृत्यैः
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The stem मद् keeps its voiced d before bh; the same in असद्वृत्तैः (असत् + वृत्त).
  • स प्रधानो मे sa pradhāno me → सः + प्रधानः + मे
    visarga sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    सः loses its visarga before p; प्रधानः before the voiced m becomes प्रधानो.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मद्भृत्यैः madbhṛtyaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मद् + भृत्य · Vigraha: मम भृत्याः, तैः mama bhṛtyāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by my servants”; in the verse: by the officers he left behind — the ministers of 1.8
  • असद्वृत्तैः asadvṛttaiḥ — bahuvrīhi (असत् is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न सत्)
    Members: असत् + वृत्त · Vigraha: असत् वृत्तं येषां ते, तैः asat vṛttaṃ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those whose conduct is not good”; in the verse: by men of bad character
  • शूरहस्ती śūrahastī — karmadhāraya
    Members: शूर + हस्तिन् · Vigraha: शूरः हस्ती śūraḥ hastī
    Literally “a valiant elephant”; in the verse: a battle-elephant
  • सदामदः sadāmadaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सदा + मद · Vigraha: सदा मदः यस्य सः sadā madaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose rut is constant”; in the verse: an elephant always in musth — the sign of a prized war-elephant

C. Morphology

  • पाल्यते pālyate — Passive present of the curādi root √पाल्: the causative-type -अय- is dropped before the passive -य-, giving पाल्य-ते. The subject is पुरम् from 1.12.
  • जाने jāne — Present ātmanepada first singular of √ज्ञा (kryādi): the weak stem जानी- loses its ī before a vowel ending, जान् + ए → जाने. न जाने ‘I do not know’ introduces the next worry, which runs into 1.14.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तत् पुरं) तैः असद्वृत्तैः मद्भृत्यैः धर्मतः पाल्यते न वा (इति) न जाने। सः मे प्रधानः सदामदः शूरहस्ती …

(tat puraṃ) taiḥ asadvṛttaiḥ madbhṛtyaiḥ dharmataḥ pālyate na vā (iti) na jāne | saḥ me pradhānaḥ sadāmadaḥ śūrahastī …

न जाने governs the question पाल्यते न वा; the second line begins a new subject (the elephant), whose verbs are in 1.14.

6Translation

Literal‘— whether it is protected according to dharma by those servants of mine of bad conduct, or not, I do not know. That chief war-elephant of mine, always in rut —

Readableis it being ruled justly by those faithless servants of mine, or not? I cannot know. And my chief war-elephant, ever in rut —

न जाने is attached to the first question in the literal version; the readable one lets it stand between the two worries, as the line does.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo worries: whether his city is ruled rightly, and what has become of his favourite elephant. The king still thinks as an owner — ‘my servants’, ‘my elephant’ (मे).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the grief is real but oddly specific. Loss often attaches itself to one cherished thing — here an elephant — that stands for the whole of what was taken.

1.14Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मम वैरिवशं यातः कान् भोगानुपलप्स्यते।
ये ममानुगता नित्यं प्रसादधनभोजनैः ॥ १४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मम | वैरिवशम् | यातः | कान् | भोगान् | उपलप्स्यतेये | मम | अनुगताः | नित्यम् | प्रसादधनभोजनैः

3IAST

As recitedmama vairivaśaṃ yātaḥ kān bhogānupalapsyate |
ye mamānugatā nityaṃ prasādadhanabhojanaiḥ ||

Separatedmama | vairivaśam | yātaḥ | kān | bhogān | upalapsyate
ye | mama | anugatāḥ | nityam | prasādadhanabhojanaiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; with वैरि- (‘my enemies’)
वैरिवशम्vairivaśaminto the power of (my) enemiesm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); goal of यातः
यातःyātaḥhaving gonepast participle (kta) of √या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), m. nom. sg.; active sense; qualifies the elephant of 1.13
कान्kānwhat (kind of)interrogative pronoun किम्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies भोगान्
भोगान्bhogāncomforts, enjoyments (fodder and care)m. acc. pl. of भोग; object of उपलप्स्यते
उपलप्स्यतेupalapsyatewill it getउप + √लभ् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to obtain’), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
येyethose whorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. pl.; correlative to ते in 1.15
ममmamamefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; with अनुगताः (‘followers of me’)
अनुगताःanugatāḥfollowed, were attached (to me)past participle (kta) of अनु + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. pl.; active sense
नित्यम्nityamalwaysindeclinable (adverbial acc. of नित्य)
प्रसादधनभोजनैःprasādadhanabhojanaiḥthrough favours, wealth and mealsn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of means

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वैरिवशं यातः vairivaśaṃ yātaḥ → वैरिवशम् + यातः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • यातः कान् yātaḥ kān → यातः + कान्
    visarga before k · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before the unvoiced k the visarga stays a visarga. The final n of कान् is then unchanged before the bh of भोगान्.
  • ममानुगता नित्यं mamānugatā nityaṃ → मम + अनुगताः + नित्यम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    मम + अनुगताः: a + a → ā. Then -āḥ before the voiced n: the visarga becomes y, and y is lost before a consonant, leaving अनुगता.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वैरिवशम् vairivaśam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: वैरिन् + वश · Vigraha: वैरिणां वशः, तम् vairiṇāṃ vaśaḥ, tam
    Literally “the control of enemies”; in the verse: into enemy hands
    Alternative: मम qualifies only the first member (‘my enemies’ control’). Strictly a word outside a compound should not qualify a member inside it, but such constructions are common in epic and Purāṇic verse. मम could also go with यातः loosely (‘gone from me’), but ‘my enemies’ is the natural reading.
  • प्रसादधनभोजनैः prasādadhanabhojanaiḥ — dvandva (itaretara)
    Members: प्रसाद + धन + भोजन · Vigraha: प्रसादः च धनं च भोजनं च, तैः prasādaḥ ca dhanaṃ ca bhojanaṃ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by favour, wealth and food”; in the verse: by the patronage, gifts and meals the king gave them

C. Morphology

  • उपलप्स्यते upalapsyate — Simple future of उप + √लभ् (ātmanepada root): लभ् + स्य → लप्स्य- (bh → p before s), with the ending -ते. ‘Will it obtain?’
  • यातः yātaḥ — With a verb of motion the kta is active: ‘having gone (into the enemies’ power)’. The subject is the elephant of 1.13.
  • अनुगताः anugatāḥ — Active kta of अनु + √गम्, nominative plural: ‘(those who) followed (me)’. The relative clause ये … अनुगताः is answered by ते in 1.15.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः शूरहस्ती) मम वैरिवशं यातः कान् भोगान् उपलप्स्यते? ये प्रसादधनभोजनैः नित्यं मम अनुगताः, (ते …)

(saḥ śūrahastī) mama vairivaśaṃ yātaḥ kān bhogān upalapsyate? ye prasādadhanabhojanaiḥ nityaṃ mama anugatāḥ, (te …)

The first line completes the elephant sentence of 1.13; the second opens a relative clause whose main clause (ते … कुर्वन्ति) is in 1.15.

6Translation

Literal‘— having gone into the power of my enemies, what enjoyments will it obtain? Those who always followed me, through favours, wealth and meals —

Readablenow fallen into my enemies’ hands, what comforts will it find? And those who always followed me, won by my favour, my gifts and my table —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king pities his elephant, then turns to his retainers, who were bound to him by what he gave: favour, money and food. The next verse says where their loyalty has gone.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: loyalty bought with favour, wealth and food follows the giver of those things, not the person. Suratha half sees this, and still grieves.

1.15Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अनुवृत्तिं ध्रुवं तेऽद्य कुर्वन्त्यन्यमहीभृताम्।
असम्यग्व्ययशीलैस्तैः कुर्वद्भिः सततं व्ययम् ॥ १५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अनुवृत्तिम् | ध्रुवम् | ते | अद्य | कुर्वन्ति | अन्यमहीभृताम्असम्यग्व्ययशीलैः | तैः | कुर्वद्भिः | सततम् | व्ययम्

3IAST

As recitedanuvṛttiṃ dhruvaṃ te'dya kurvantyanyamahībhṛtām |
asamyagvyayaśīlaistaiḥ kurvadbhiḥ satataṃ vyayam ||

Separatedanuvṛttim | dhruvam | te | adya | kurvanti | anyamahībhṛtām
asamyagvyayaśīlaiḥ | taiḥ | kurvadbhiḥ | satatam | vyayam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अनुवृत्तिम्anuvṛttimservice, compliance (lit. ‘following after’)f. acc. sg. of अनुवृत्ति; object of कुर्वन्ति
ध्रुवम्dhruvamsurelyindeclinable (adverbial acc. of ध्रुव)
तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; correlative of ये in 1.14
अद्यadyanow, todayindeclinable (adverb of time)
कुर्वन्तिkurvantido, render√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
अन्यमहीभृताम्anyamahībhṛtāmof other kingsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); dependent on अनुवृत्तिम्
असम्यग्व्ययशीलैःasamyagvyayaśīlaiḥgiven to improper spendingm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तैः
तैःtaiḥby them (the ministers)pronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; instrumental of cause, construed with गमिष्यति in 1.16
कुर्वद्भिःkurvadbhiḥmakingpresent participle (śatṛ) of √कृ, m. instr. pl.; agrees with तैः
सततम्satatamconstantlyindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत)
व्ययम्vyayamexpenditurem. acc. sg. of व्यय; object of कुर्वद्भिः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तेऽद्य te'dya → ते + अद्य
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • कुर्वन्त्यन्य kurvantyanya → कुर्वन्ति + अन्यमहीभृताम्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.
  • असम्यग्व्यय asamyagvyaya → असम्यक् + व्यय
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final k becomes g before the voiced v.
  • व्ययशीलैस्तैः vyayaśīlaistaiḥ → असम्यग्व्ययशीलैः + तैः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अन्यमहीभृताम् anyamahībhṛtām — karmadhāraya (महीभृत् is an upapada tatpuruṣa: महीं बिभर्ति इति)
    Members: अन्य + महीभृत् · Vigraha: अन्ये महीभृतः, तेषाम् anye mahībhṛtaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of other earth-bearers”; in the verse: of other kings — his rivals
  • असम्यग्व्ययशीलैः asamyagvyayaśīlaiḥ — bahuvrīhi (असम्यक् is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न सम्यक्)
    Members: असम्यक् + व्यय + शील · Vigraha: असम्यक् व्ययः शीलं येषां ते, तैः asamyak vyayaḥ śīlaṃ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those whose habit is improper spending”; in the verse: by spendthrift, dishonest managers of the treasury

C. Morphology

  • कुर्वन्ति kurvanti — Present third plural of √कृ (tanādi), weak stem कुरु- with u → v before the vowel ending: कुर्व्-अन्ति.
  • कुर्वद्भिः kurvadbhiḥ — Present participle कुर्वत् (√कृ + śatṛ), instrumental plural: the t becomes d before bh. It agrees with तैः: ‘by them, making (expense)’.
  • ध्रुवम् dhruvam — The adjective ध्रुव ‘fixed, certain’ in the neuter accusative, used as an adverb: ‘surely’. The king is guessing, but with conviction.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ते अद्य ध्रुवम् अन्यमहीभृताम् अनुवृत्तिं कुर्वन्ति। सततं व्ययं कुर्वद्भिः असम्यग्व्ययशीलैः तैः (1.16: …कोशः क्षयं गमिष्यति)।

te adya dhruvam anyamahībhṛtām anuvṛttiṃ kurvanti | satataṃ vyayaṃ kurvadbhiḥ asamyagvyayaśīlaiḥ taiḥ (1.16: …kośaḥ kṣayaṃ gamiṣyati) |

The first line completes the relative clause of 1.14 (ये … ते). The second line is a string of instrumentals whose verb, गमिष्यति, comes in 1.16.

6Translation

Literal‘— they surely now render service to other kings. By those given to improper spending, constantly making expenditure —

Readablesurely they now pay court to other kings. And by those wastrels, spending without pause —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king imagines his former followers serving his rivals, and his ministers squandering his treasury. Both thoughts are guesses; he has no news.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: anxiety supplies certainties (ध्रुवम्, ‘surely’) that the mind has no way of checking. Suratha suffers over events he can only imagine.

1.16Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

संचितः सोऽतिदुःखेन क्षयं कोशो गमिष्यति।
एतच्चान्यच्च सततं चिन्तयामास पार्थिवः ॥ १६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

संचितः | सः | अतिदुःखेन | क्षयम् | कोशः | गमिष्यतिएतत् | च | अन्यत् | च | सततम् | चिन्तयामास | पार्थिवः

3IAST

As recitedsaṃcitaḥ so'tiduḥkhena kṣayaṃ kośo gamiṣyati |
etaccānyacca satataṃ cintayāmāsa pārthivaḥ ||

Separatedsaṃcitaḥ | saḥ | atiduḥkhena | kṣayam | kośaḥ | gamiṣyati
etat | ca | anyat | ca | satatam | cintayāmāsa | pārthivaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
संचितःsaṃcitaḥamassed, accumulatedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √चि (svādi, 5U ‘to gather’), m. nom. sg.; qualifies कोशः
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; with कोशः
अतिदुःखेनatiduḥkhenawith great hardshipn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
क्षयम्kṣayamto ruin, to exhaustionm. acc. sg. of क्षय; goal of गमिष्यति
कोशःkośaḥthe treasurym. nom. sg. of कोश; subject
गमिष्यतिgamiṣyatiwill go√गम् (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. acc. sg.; object of चिन्तयामास
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अन्यत्anyatother (things)pronominal adjective अन्य, n. acc. sg.; object of चिन्तयामास
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); एतच्च अन्यच्च ‘this and more’
सततम्satatamconstantlyindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत)
चिन्तयामासcintayāmāsathought over, brooded on√चिन्त् (curādi, 10P), liṭ (periphrastic perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
पार्थिवःpārthivaḥthe kingm. nom. sg. of पार्थिव (taddhita from पृथिवी: ‘lord of the earth’); subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सोऽतिदुःखेन so'tiduḥkhena → सः + अतिदुःखेन
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    सः before a: so’ with avagraha (as in 1.12).
  • कोशो गमिष्यति kośo gamiṣyati → कोशः + गमिष्यति
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced g becomes -o.
  • एतच्चान्यच्च etaccānyacca → एतत् + च + अन्यत् + च
    consonant sandhi (t → c) and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Final t before c becomes c (एतच्च, अन्यच्च); च + अन्यत् → चान्यत्.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिदुःखेन atiduḥkhena — prādi tatpuruṣa
    Members: अति + दुःख · Vigraha: अतिशयितं दुःखम्, तेन atiśayitaṃ duḥkham, tena
    Literally “by excessive pain”; in the verse: with great labour and hardship

C. Morphology

  • चिन्तयामास cintayāmāsa — Periphrastic perfect: roots with a derived stem (here the curādi चिन्तय-) form the perfect with -आम् + the perfect of √अस् (आस): चिन्तयाम् + आस. Compare अचिन्तयत् (imperfect) in 1.12, which opened the same train of thought.
  • गमिष्यति gamiṣyati — Simple future of √गम् with connecting -इ-: गम् + इ + ष्य + ति. With क्षयम् ‘will go to ruin’.
  • संचितः saṃcitaḥ — Participle of सम् + √चि: ‘gathered together’. Printed with anusvāra for the nasal of सम् before c.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तैः) अतिदुःखेन संचितः सः कोशः क्षयं गमिष्यति। एतत् च अन्यत् च पार्थिवः सततं चिन्तयामास।

(taiḥ) atiduḥkhena saṃcitaḥ saḥ kośaḥ kṣayaṃ gamiṣyati | etat ca anyat ca pārthivaḥ satataṃ cintayāmāsa |

The first line completes the sentence begun in 1.15b (‘through them … the treasury will go to ruin’); the second closes the king’s thoughts (1.12–16) and is the narrator’s.

6Translation

Literal‘— that treasury, amassed with great hardship, will go to ruin.’ This and other things the king pondered constantly.

Readablethe treasury I gathered with such toil will be drained away.’ These and other thoughts filled the king’s mind without rest.

‘I’ is supplied: the Sanskrit says only ‘amassed with great hardship’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last of the king’s worries is the treasury; the narrator then closes the passage: he thought of ‘this and other things’ ceaselessly. No इति marks the end of the thought; the change of verb (चिन्तयामास) does.

Traditional interpretationSuratha’s monologue moves through city, elephant, retainers and treasury — the things of a kingdom. Medhas’s later teaching treats all of them as objects of ममत्व held in place by Mahāmāyā.

1.17Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तत्र विप्राश्रमाभ्याशे वैश्यमेकं ददर्श सः।
स पृष्टस्तेन कस्त्वं भो हेतुश्चागमनेऽत्र कः ॥ १७ ॥

3IAST

As recitedtatra viprāśramābhyāśe vaiśyamekaṃ dadarśa saḥ |
sa pṛṣṭastena kastvaṃ bho hetuścāgamane'tra kaḥ ||

saḥ | pṛṣṭaḥ | tena | kaḥ | tvam | bhoḥ | hetuḥ | ca | āgamane | atra | kaḥ

सःsaḥhe (the merchant)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; subject of पृष्टः
पृष्टःpṛṣṭaḥwas askedpast passive participle (kta) of √प्रच्छ् (tudādi, 6P ‘to ask’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
