5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विप्राश्रमाभ्याशे viprāśramābhyāśe → विप्र + आश्रम + अभ्याशे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha, twice) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā and a + a → ā.
  • पृष्टस्तेन कस्त्वं pṛṣṭastena kastvaṃ → पृष्टः + तेन + कः + त्वम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, twice.
  • भो हेतु bho hetu → भोः + हेतुः
    visarga sandhi (भोः) · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    The visarga of भोः becomes y before a voiced sound, and y is lost before a consonant: भो हेतुः.
  • हेतुश्चागमनेऽत्र hetuścāgamane'tra → हेतुः + च + आगमने + अत्र
    visarga sandhi, savarṇa-dīrgha and pūrvarūpa · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Visarga before c → ś; च + आ → चा; आगमने + अत्र → आगमनेऽत्र.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विप्राश्रमाभ्याशे viprāśramābhyāśe — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विप्राश्रम + अभ्याश · Vigraha: विप्रस्य आश्रमः विप्राश्रमः; विप्राश्रमस्य अभ्याशः, तस्मिन् viprasya āśramaḥ viprāśramaḥ; viprāśramasya abhyāśaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the vicinity of the brahmin’s hermitage”; in the verse: near Medhas’s hermitage

C. Morphology

  • पृष्टः pṛṣṭaḥ — Passive participle of √प्रच्छ्: the root becomes पृच्छ्/प्रश्- and with -त gives पृष्ट (ś + t → ṣṭ). Verbs of asking take the person asked as the subject in the passive: ‘he was asked’.
  • ददर्श dadarśa — Perfect of √दृश् with reduplication द- and guṇa of the root vowel (दृश् → दर्श्). Compare the aorist अद्राक्षीत् in 1.10.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तत्र विप्राश्रमाभ्याशे सः एकं वैश्यं ददर्श। सः तेन पृष्टः — भोः, त्वं कः? अत्र आगमने च हेतुः कः?

tatra viprāśramābhyāśe saḥ ekaṃ vaiśyaṃ dadarśa | saḥ tena pṛṣṭaḥ — bhoḥ, tvaṃ kaḥ? atra āgamane ca hetuḥ kaḥ?

Two narrative clauses (the king saw; the merchant was asked) and the direct question, which continues into 1.18.

6Translation

LiteralThere, near the brahmin’s hermitage, he saw a certain merchant. He was asked by him: ‘Who are you, sir? And what is the reason for your coming here?

ReadableThere, near the sage’s hermitage, he saw a merchant, and asked him: ‘Who are you, friend? What has brought you here?

The passive (‘he was asked by him’) is turned into an active sentence.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second figure of the frame story appears: a merchant, met by chance near the hermitage. The king, absorbed in his own losses until now, notices another man’s grief and speaks first.

Traditional interpretationThe meeting of the deposed king and the disowned merchant pairs two kinds of loss — power and family. The tradition reads the two as the seekers of enjoyment and of liberation, as their final requests in chapter 13 show.

1.18Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सशोक इव कस्मात्त्वं दुर्मना इव लक्ष्यसे।
इत्याकर्ण्य वचस्तस्य भूपतेः प्रणयोदितम् ॥ १८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सशोकः | इव | कस्मात् | त्वम् | दुर्मनाः | इव | लक्ष्यसेइति | आकर्ण्य | वचः | तस्य | भूपतेः | प्रणयोदितम्

3IAST

As recitedsaśoka iva kasmāttvaṃ durmanā iva lakṣyase |
ityākarṇya vacastasya bhūpateḥ praṇayoditam ||

Separatedsaśokaḥ | iva | kasmāt | tvam | durmanāḥ | iva | lakṣyase
iti | ākarṇya | vacaḥ | tasya | bhūpateḥ | praṇayoditam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सशोकःsaśokaḥsorrowfulm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with लक्ष्यसे
इवivaas if, seeminglyindeclinable (particle of comparison; here ‘as it were’)
कस्मात्kasmātwhy (lit. ‘from what’)interrogative pronoun किम्, n. abl. sg., used adverbially
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
दुर्मनाःdurmanāḥdejected, troubled in mindm. nom. sg. of दुर्मनस्; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with लक्ष्यसे
इवivaas ifindeclinable (particle)
लक्ष्यसेlakṣyasedo you appear, are you seen√लक्ष् (curādi, 10 ‘to mark, observe’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (quotative particle)
आकर्ण्यākarṇyahaving heardabsolutive (lyap) of आ + कर्णय- (denominative from कर्ण ‘ear’, curādi type: ‘to give ear to’)
वचःvacaḥthe wordsn. acc. sg. of वचस्; object of आकर्ण्य
तस्यtasyaof thatpronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; with भूपतेः
भूपतेःbhūpateḥof the kingm. gen. sg. of भूपति (i-stem)
प्रणयोदितम्praṇayoditamspoken in friendshipn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वचः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सशोक इव saśoka iva → सशोकः + इव
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -aḥ before a vowel other than a: the visarga becomes y, the y is dropped, and the vowels stand apart (as in 1.1).
  • दुर्मना इव durmanā iva → दुर्मनाः + इव
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y and is dropped; the hiatus remains.
  • इत्याकर्ण्य ityākarṇya → इति + आकर्ण्य
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before ā becomes y.
  • वचस्तस्य vacastasya → वचः + तस्य
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • प्रणयोदितम् praṇayoditam → प्रणय + उदितम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सशोकः saśokaḥ — bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi; सह becomes स-)
    Members: सह + शोक · Vigraha: शोकेन सह वर्तते इति śokena saha vartate iti
    Literally “being with sorrow”; in the verse: grief-stricken
  • दुर्मनाः durmanāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुस् + मनस् · Vigraha: दुष्टं (खिन्नं) मनः यस्य सः duṣṭaṃ (khinnaṃ) manaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “one whose mind is ill”; in the verse: downcast, heavy-hearted
  • प्रणयोदितम् praṇayoditam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: प्रणय + उदित · Vigraha: प्रणयेन उदितम् praṇayena uditam
    Literally “spoken with affection”; in the verse: words said kindly, as to a friend

C. Morphology

  • लक्ष्यसे lakṣyase — Passive present, second singular, of √लक्ष्: ‘you are observed (as)’, i.e. ‘you look’. The predicates सशोकः and दुर्मनाः stand in the nominative with it.
  • दुर्मनाः durmanāḥ — Nominative singular masculine of the s-stem दुर्मनस्: the vowel before the final s is lengthened (-अस् → -आः).
  • आकर्ण्य ākarṇya — Absolutive of a denominative verb formed from कर्ण ‘ear’; with the prefix आ it takes -य (lyap), and the stem’s -अय- is dropped: आकर्णय- → आकर्ण्य.

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वं कस्मात् सशोकः इव दुर्मनाः इव लक्ष्यसे? इति तस्य भूपतेः प्रणयोदितं वचः आकर्ण्य, (1.19: सः वैश्यः … प्रत्युवाच)।

tvaṃ kasmāt saśokaḥ iva durmanāḥ iva lakṣyase? iti tasya bhūpateḥ praṇayoditaṃ vacaḥ ākarṇya, (1.19: saḥ vaiśyaḥ … pratyuvāca) |

The first line ends the king’s question; इति closes it. The absolutive आकर्ण्य has as its subject the merchant of 1.19.

6Translation

Literal‘Why do you appear as if sorrowful, as if downcast?’ Having heard thus the words of that king, spoken in friendship —

ReadableWhy do you look so sorrowful, so heavy-hearted?’ Hearing these kindly words of the king,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king’s question ends, and the narrator notes its tone: it was spoken with प्रणय, warmth or friendliness. The repeated इव (‘as if’) is courteous; the king does not presume to name the stranger’s grief.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: for the first time since 1.5 Suratha’s attention leaves his own losses. Concern for another’s sorrow opens the conversation that leads to the teaching.

1.19Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

प्रत्युवाच स तं वैश्यः प्रश्रयावनतो नृपम् ॥ १९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

प्रत्युवाच | सः | तम् | वैश्यः | प्रश्रयावनतः | नृपम्

3IAST

As recitedpratyuvāca sa taṃ vaiśyaḥ praśrayāvanato nṛpam ||

Separatedpratyuvāca | saḥ | tam | vaiśyaḥ | praśrayāvanataḥ | nṛpam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
प्रत्युवाचpratyuvācarepliedप्रति + √वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
सःsaḥthatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; with वैश्यः
तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; with नृपम्
वैश्यःvaiśyaḥmerchantm. nom. sg. of वैश्य; subject
प्रश्रयावनतःpraśrayāvanataḥbowing in humilitym. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वैश्यः
नृपम्nṛpamthe kingm. acc. sg. of नृप; the person addressed

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रत्युवाच pratyuvāca → प्रति + उवाच
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) between prefix and verb · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    The prefix प्रति before the u of उवाच becomes प्रत्य्-.
  • स तं वैश्यः sa taṃ vaiśyaḥ → सः + तम् + वैश्यः
    visarga sandhi (सः) and anusvāra · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    सः loses its visarga before t; तम् becomes तं before v.
  • प्रश्रयावनतो नृपम् praśrayāvanato nṛpam → प्रश्रयावनतः + नृपम्
    visarga sandhi; savarṇa-dīrgha inside the compound · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    -aḥ before the voiced n → -o; प्रश्रय + अवनत → प्रश्रयावनत.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रश्रयावनतः praśrayāvanataḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: प्रश्रय + अवनत · Vigraha: प्रश्रयेण अवनतः praśrayeṇa avanataḥ
    Literally “bent down with modesty”; in the verse: bowing respectfully

C. Morphology

  • प्रत्युवाच pratyuvāca — Perfect of √वच् (उवाच, as in 1.1) with the prefix प्रति: ‘spoke back, replied’. Verbs of speaking take the person addressed in the accusative (तं नृपम्).
  • प्रश्रयावनतः praśrayāvanataḥ — अवनत is the kta of अव + √नम् ‘to bend’ (the nasal of नम् is lost before -त); active sense, ‘bowed’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सः वैश्यः प्रश्रयावनतः तं नृपं प्रत्युवाच।

saḥ vaiśyaḥ praśrayāvanataḥ taṃ nṛpaṃ pratyuvāca |

Subject with its participle, the person addressed, and the verb. It completes the sentence of 1.18b (आकर्ण्य … प्रत्युवाच).

6Translation

Literal— that merchant, bowed with humility, replied to that king.

Readablethe merchant bowed respectfully and answered him.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA half-śloka that closes the narrative of the meeting. The merchant answers with deference to a man he recognises as a king; his own story follows after the speaker label (1.20).

1.20Uvāca · speaker

वैश्य उवाच ॥ २० ॥

वैश्यः | उवाच

उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

  • वैश्य उवाच vaiśya uvāca → वैश्यः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -aḥ before a vowel other than a: the visarga becomes y, the y is dropped, and the vowels stand apart (as in मार्कण्डेय उवाच, 1.1).

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच्, as in 1.1: ‘(he) said’.

ReadableThe merchant said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSpeaker label: the merchant now answers the king’s questions of 1.17–18 (who he is, and why he has come). Mārkaṇḍeya is still the narrator of the whole exchange.

1.21Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

समाधिर्नाम वैश्योऽहमुत्पन्नो धनिनां कुले ॥ २१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

समाधिः | नाम | वैश्यः | अहम् | उत्पन्नः | धनिनाम् | कुले

3IAST

As recitedsamādhirnāma vaiśyo'hamutpanno dhanināṃ kule ||

Separatedsamādhiḥ | nāma | vaiśyaḥ | aham | utpannaḥ | dhaninām | kule

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
समाधिःsamādhiḥSamādhi (lexically ‘concentration, composure’)m. nom. sg. of समाधि (i-stem), proper name; in apposition to अहम्
नामnāmaby nameindeclinable
वैश्यःvaiśyaḥa merchantm. nom. sg. of वैश्य; predicate
अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
उत्पन्नःutpannaḥbornpast participle (kta) of उत् + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to go’; with उत् ‘to arise’), m. nom. sg.; active sense
धनिनाम्dhanināmof wealthy menm. gen. pl. of धनिन् (‘possessing wealth’); with कुले

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समाधिर्नाम samādhirnāma → समाधिः + नाम
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga stands as r before the voiced n.
  • वैश्योऽहमुत्पन्नो vaiśyo'hamutpanno → वैश्यः + अहम् + उत्पन्नः
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a- → -o’; the m of अहम् is simply written together with the following vowel; उत्पन्नः before the voiced dh becomes उत्पन्नो (हशि च, 6.1.114).

C. Morphology

  • उत्पन्नः utpannaḥ — kta of उत् + √पद् ‘to arise’: the root’s final d and the t of the suffix become -न्न- (पद् + त → पन्न). With an intransitive verb the participle is active: ‘born, arisen’.
  • धनिनाम् dhaninām — Genitive plural of the -in stem धनिन् ‘having wealth’ (धन + possessive -in), like बलिन् in 1.8.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अहं समाधिः नाम वैश्यः धनिनां कुले उत्पन्नः।

ahaṃ samādhiḥ nāma vaiśyaḥ dhanināṃ kule utpannaḥ |

Name and class (समाधिः नाम वैश्यः), subject (अहम्), and a participle phrase giving his birth.

6Translation

LiteralI am a merchant named Samādhi, born in a family of wealthy men.

ReadableI am a merchant named Samādhi, born into a wealthy family.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant answers the first question (‘who are you?’): his name, his class, his family. The next verses answer the second (‘why have you come?’).

Traditional interpretationThe name समाधि — ‘composure, absorption’ — is often noticed as apt: he is the one who at the end asks the Goddess for knowledge rather than wealth (13.18), and receives it (13.25).

1.22Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पुत्रदारैर्निरस्तश्च धनलोभादसाधुभिः।

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पुत्रदारैर्निरस्तश्च putradārairnirastaśca → पुत्रदारैः + निरस्तः + च
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    After ai the visarga stands as r before the voiced n; निरस्तः before c becomes निरस्तश्.
  • धनलोभादसाधुभिः dhanalobhādasādhubhiḥ → धनलोभात् + असाधुभिः
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.
  • धनैर्दारैः पुत्रैरादाय dhanairdāraiḥ putrairādāya → धनैः + दारैः + पुत्रैः + आदाय
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    After ai the visarga stands as r before the voiced d and before the vowel ā; before the unvoiced p (दारैः पुत्रैः) it remains a visarga.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पुत्रदारैः putradāraiḥ — dvandva (itaretara)
    Members: पुत्र + दार · Vigraha: पुत्राः च दाराः च, तैः putrāḥ ca dārāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by sons and wife”; in the verse: by his own family
  • धनलोभात् dhanalobhāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: धन + लोभ · Vigraha: धनस्य लोभः, तस्मात् dhanasya lobhaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from greed of wealth”; in the verse: because they coveted his money

C. Morphology

  • विहीनः vihīnaḥ — Participle of वि + √हा ‘to leave’: ‘left without, deprived of’, construed with the instrumental (धनैः दारैः पुत्रैः).
  • आदाय ādāya — Absolutive of आ + √दा ‘to take’. Its logical subject is not the speaker but those who wronged him — the sons and wife (or the kinsmen of 1.23): ‘having taken my wealth, (they cast me out)’. Such a shift of subject is common in epic narrative.
  • दारैः dāraiḥ — दार ‘wife’ is masculine and used in the plural for a single wife — a fixed idiom.

D. Anvaya — prose order

धनलोभात् असाधुभिः पुत्रदारैः (अहं) निरस्तः च, धनैः दारैः पुत्रैः च विहीनः; (ते) मे धनम् आदाय (1.23: आप्तबन्धुभिः च निरस्तः) …

dhanalobhāt asādhubhiḥ putradāraiḥ (ahaṃ) nirastaḥ ca, dhanaiḥ dāraiḥ putraiḥ ca vihīnaḥ; (te) me dhanam ādāya (1.23: āptabandhubhiḥ ca nirastaḥ) …

Two predicates of an implied अहम् (निरस्तः, विहीनः); the absolutive phrase आदाय मे धनम् belongs with the agents who cast him out and runs into 1.23.

6Translation

LiteralAnd I have been cast out by my wicked sons and wife out of greed for wealth, and am deprived of wealth, wife and sons; (they,) having taken my wealth —

ReadableMy wicked sons and wife, greedy for my wealth, have driven me out. I am left without money, wife or sons; they have taken all I had,

‘All I had’ renders मे धनम् ‘my wealth’; the subject ‘they’ of आदाय is supplied.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant’s answer to ‘why have you come?’: his own family has thrown him out to take his wealth. His loss mirrors the king’s — Suratha was betrayed by ministers, Samādhi by sons and wife.

Traditional interpretationUnlike the king, the merchant names his betrayers as his nearest kin; the point is made sharper in 1.31–34, where he confesses that his heart still goes out to them.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The construction of the second line admits two readings: (a) धनैः दारैः पुत्रैः all depend on विहीनः (‘deprived of wealth, wife and sons’), with आदाय मे धनम् referring to the relatives; (b) विहीनः धनैः only, with दारैः पुत्रैः as the agents of आदाय (‘deprived of wealth, my wife and sons having taken it’). Reading (a) is followed here as the more natural word order.

1.23Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वनमभ्यागतो दुःखी निरस्तश्चाप्तबन्धुभिः।
सोऽहं न वेद्मि पुत्राणां कुशलाकुशलात्मिकाम् ॥ २३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वनम् | अभ्यागतः | दुःखी | निरस्तः | च | आप्तबन्धुभिःसः | अहम् | न | वेद्मि | पुत्राणाम् | कुशलाकुशलात्मिकाम्

3IAST

As recitedvanamabhyāgato duḥkhī nirastaścāptabandhubhiḥ |
so'haṃ na vedmi putrāṇāṃ kuśalākuśalātmikām ||

Separatedvanam | abhyāgataḥ | duḥkhī | nirastaḥ | ca | āptabandhubhiḥ
saḥ | aham | na | vedmi | putrāṇām | kuśalākuśalātmikām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
वनम्vanamto the forestn. acc. sg. of वन; goal of अभ्यागतः
अभ्यागतःabhyāgataḥhave comepast participle (kta) of अभि + आ + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. sg.; active sense
दुःखीduḥkhīin sorrowm. nom. sg. of दुःखिन् (‘having sorrow’); predicative
निरस्तःnirastaḥcast outpast passive participle (kta) of निर् + √अस्, m. nom. sg.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
आप्तबन्धुभिःāptabandhubhiḥby trusted kinsmenm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of निरस्तः
सःsaḥthat (very)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; with अहम् (‘I, such as I am’)
अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
वेद्मिvedmiI know√विद् (adādi, 2P ‘to know’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वनमभ्यागतो दुःखी vanamabhyāgato duḥkhī → वनम् + अभ्यागतः + दुःखी
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    The m of वनम् is written together with the vowel; -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.
  • निरस्तश्चाप्तबन्धुभिः nirastaścāptabandhubhiḥ → निरस्तः + च + आप्तबन्धुभिः
    visarga sandhi and savarṇa-dīrgha · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c → ś; च + आ → चा.
  • सोऽहं so'haṃ → सः + अहम्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    सः before a → so’ (as in 1.12).
  • कुशलाकुशलात्मिकाम् kuśalākuśalātmikām → कुशल + अकुशल + आत्मिका
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha, twice) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā and a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • आप्तबन्धुभिः āptabandhubhiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: आप्त + बन्धु · Vigraha: आप्ताः बन्धवः, तैः āptāḥ bandhavaḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by trusted relatives”; in the verse: by the kinsmen he relied on
  • कुशलाकुशलात्मिकाम् kuśalākuśalātmikām — bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -क) containing a dvandva
    Members: कुशलाकुशल + आत्मन् (+ क) · Vigraha: कुशलं च अकुशलं च कुशलाकुशले (dvandva; अकुशल is a nañ tatpuruṣa); कुशलाकुशले आत्मा (स्वरूपम्) यस्याः सा, ताम् kuśalaṃ ca akuśalaṃ ca kuśalākuśale (dvandva; akuśala is a nañ tatpuruṣa); kuśalākuśale ātmā (svarūpam) yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally “whose nature is well-being and ill-being”; in the verse: news, whether good or bad

C. Morphology

  • वेद्मि vedmi — Present first singular of √विद् (adādi), with guṇa (विद् → वेद्) before the strong ending -मि. न वेद्मि governs प्रवृत्तिम् in 1.24.
  • अभ्यागतः abhyāgataḥ — Active kta of a verb of motion with two prefixes, अभि + आ: ‘(I) have come’ (to the forest).
  • कुशलाकुशलात्मिकाम् kuśalākuśalātmikām — आत्मक ‘having the nature of, consisting of’ (आत्मन् + -क) makes its feminine in -इका: आत्मिका. It agrees with प्रवृत्तिम् (f.) in the next verse.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अहं) दुःखी आप्तबन्धुभिः च निरस्तः वनम् अभ्यागतः। सः अहम् अत्र संस्थितः पुत्राणां … कुशलाकुशलात्मिकां (प्रवृत्तिं) न वेद्मि (1.24)।

(ahaṃ) duḥkhī āptabandhubhiḥ ca nirastaḥ vanam abhyāgataḥ | saḥ aham atra saṃsthitaḥ putrāṇāṃ … kuśalākuśalātmikāṃ (pravṛttiṃ) na vedmi (1.24) |

The first line ends the account of his expulsion; the second begins a sentence completed in 1.24 (न वेद्मि … प्रवृत्तिम्).

6Translation

Literal— and cast out by trusted kinsmen, I have come, sorrowful, to the forest. I, such as I am, do not know the (news), of good or ill, of my sons,

Readableand, cast off by the kinsmen I trusted, I have come to this forest in grief. Now I do not know whether things go well or ill with my sons,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant’s second answer: he came to the forest because his own people drove him out. Yet his first thought is not of his wrong but of their welfare, which he cannot learn.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: he has been thrown out and robbed, and still worries whether those who did it are well. The text shows the bond outliving the betrayal before it names it as a puzzle (1.31–34).

1.24Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

प्रवृत्तिं स्वजनानां च दाराणां चात्र संस्थितः।
किं नु तेषां गृहे क्षेममक्षेमं किं नु साम्प्रतम् ॥ २४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

प्रवृत्तिम् | स्वजनानाम् | च | दाराणाम् | च | अत्र | संस्थितःकिम् | नु | तेषाम् | गृहे | क्षेमम् | अक्षेमम् | किम् | नु | साम्प्रतम्

3IAST

As recitedpravṛttiṃ svajanānāṃ ca dārāṇāṃ cātra saṃsthitaḥ |
kiṃ nu teṣāṃ gṛhe kṣemamakṣemaṃ kiṃ nu sāmpratam ||

Separatedpravṛttim | svajanānām | ca | dārāṇām | ca | atra | saṃsthitaḥ
kim | nu | teṣām | gṛhe | kṣemam | akṣemam | kim | nu | sāmpratam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
प्रवृत्तिम्pravṛttimnews, tidingsf. acc. sg. of प्रवृत्ति; object of न वेद्मि (1.23)
स्वजनानाम्svajanānāmof my own peoplem. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); with प्रवृत्तिम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
दाराणाम्dārāṇāmof my wifem. gen. pl. of दार (plural for one wife); with प्रवृत्तिम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अत्रatrahereindeclinable (adverb of place)
संस्थितःsaṃsthitaḥstaying, situatedpast participle (kta) of सम् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P), m. nom. sg.; active sense; qualifies (अहम्)
किम्kimis it (that)indeclinable (interrogative particle)
नुnuI wonderindeclinable (particle strengthening a question)
तेषाम्teṣāmtheirpronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.
गृहेgṛhein the house, at homen. loc. sg. of गृह
क्षेमम्kṣemamwelfare, well-beingn. nom. sg. of क्षेम
अक्षेमम्akṣemamill-being, misfortunen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
किम्kimor is itindeclinable (interrogative particle; किम् … किम् ‘whether … or’)
नुnuI wonderindeclinable (particle)
साम्प्रतम्sāmpratamnow, at presentindeclinable (adverb of time)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चात्र cātra → च + अत्र
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • किं नु तेषां गृहे क्षेममक्षेमं किं kiṃ nu teṣāṃ gṛhe kṣemamakṣemaṃ kiṃ → किम् + नु + तेषाम् + गृहे + क्षेमम् + अक्षेमम् + किम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra (किं, तेषां, अक्षेमं); before the vowel of अक्षेमम् the m of क्षेमम् stays m and is written with it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्वजनानाम् svajanānām — karmadhāraya
    Members: स्व + जन · Vigraha: स्वाः जनाः, तेषाम् svāḥ janāḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of one’s own people”; in the verse: of his relatives
    Alternative: Also read as ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, स्वस्य जनाः ‘one’s own people’; the sense is the same.
  • अक्षेमम् akṣemam — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + क्षेम · Vigraha: न क्षेमम् na kṣemam
    Literally “not-welfare”; in the verse: harm, misfortune

C. Morphology

  • संस्थितः saṃsthitaḥ — kta of सम् + √स्था (स्था → स्थि before -त), active: ‘(I,) remaining (here)’. It gives the reason he has no news.
  • किम् kim — किम् … किम् as particles: ‘is it … or is it …?’, a double question. The particle नु adds anxious wondering.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अत्र संस्थितः (अहं) पुत्राणां स्वजनानां च दाराणां च प्रवृत्तिं न वेद्मि (1.23)। तेषां गृहे साम्प्रतं क्षेमं किं नु, अक्षेमं किं नु?

atra saṃsthitaḥ (ahaṃ) putrāṇāṃ svajanānāṃ ca dārāṇāṃ ca pravṛttiṃ na vedmi (1.23) | teṣāṃ gṛhe sāmprataṃ kṣemaṃ kiṃ nu, akṣemaṃ kiṃ nu?

The first line completes न वेद्मि of 1.23 with its object; the second is a double question (किं नु … किं नु).

6Translation

Literal— (the news) of my own people and of my wife, staying here. Is it, I wonder, welfare in their house, or ill-fare, at present?

Readableor with my kin and my wife, for I am here, far away. Is all well in their home now, or is it not?

‘Far away’ draws out अत्र संस्थितः (‘staying here’), which explains why he has no news.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sentence of 1.23 ends: he has no tidings of sons, kin or wife because he is here in the forest. He asks twice, with किं नु, whether they are well.

1.25Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कथं ते किं नु सद्वृत्ता दुर्वृत्ताः किं नु मे सुताः ॥ २५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कथम् | ते | किम् | नु | सद्वृत्ताः | दुर्वृत्ताः | किम् | नु | मे | सुताः

3IAST

As recitedkathaṃ te kiṃ nu sadvṛttā durvṛttāḥ kiṃ nu me sutāḥ ||

Separatedkatham | te | kim | nu | sadvṛttāḥ | durvṛttāḥ | kim | nu | me | sutāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कथम्kathamhow (are)indeclinable (interrogative adverb)
तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.
किम्kimare (they)indeclinable (interrogative particle)
नुnuI wonderindeclinable (particle)
सद्वृत्ताःsadvṛttāḥof good conductm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of सुताः
दुर्वृत्ताःdurvṛttāḥof bad conductm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of सुताः
किम्kimor (are they)indeclinable (interrogative particle)
नुnuI wonderindeclinable (particle)
मेmemyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg. (enclitic)
सुताःsutāḥsonsm. nom. pl. of सुत; subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सद्वृत्ता दुर्वृत्ताः sadvṛttā durvṛttāḥ → सद्वृत्ताः + दुर्वृत्ताः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before the voiced d: the visarga becomes y, and y is lost before a consonant.
  • सद्वृत्ता sadvṛttā → सत् + वृत्त
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of सत् becomes d before the voiced v. (The digital source marked the half-form d with a joiner, removed as an encoding hint; the letters are unchanged.)
  • दुर्वृत्ताः किं durvṛttāḥ kiṃ → दुर्वृत्ताः + किम्
    visarga before k · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before the unvoiced k the visarga remains. Inside दुर्वृत्त, the prefix दुस् shows r before the voiced v (ससजुषो रुः, 8.2.66).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सद्वृत्ताः sadvṛttāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सत् + वृत्त · Vigraha: सत् वृत्तं येषां ते sat vṛttaṃ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose conduct is good”; in the verse: well-behaved
  • दुर्वृत्ताः durvṛttāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुस् + वृत्त · Vigraha: दुष्टं वृत्तं येषां ते duṣṭaṃ vṛttaṃ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose conduct is bad”; in the verse: ill-behaved, gone astray

C. Morphology

  • कथम् katham — कथम् ‘how?’ asks after their condition in general (‘how are they?’); the following किं नु … किं नु asks the narrower question of their conduct.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ते कथम्? मे सुताः सद्वृत्ताः किं नु, दुर्वृत्ताः किं नु?

te katham? me sutāḥ sadvṛttāḥ kiṃ nu, durvṛttāḥ kiṃ nu?

A short question (कथं ते) and a double question with किं नु … किं नु.

6Translation

LiteralHow are they? Are my sons, I wonder, of good conduct, or of bad conduct?

ReadableHow are they? Are my sons living rightly now, or have they gone astray?

‘Now’ is supplied from साम्प्रतम् in 1.24.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant ends his answer with anxious questions about the very sons who robbed him. The king will ask next (1.27–28) why he still cares for them.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: he does not ask whether they regret what they did, only whether they are good. Attachment here takes the form of hope for those who caused the hurt.

1.26Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

राजोवाच ॥ २६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

राजा | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedrājovāca ||

Separatedrājā | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
राजाrājāthe king (Suratha)m. nom. sg. of राजन् (n-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • राजोवाच rājovāca → राजा + उवाच
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Final ā before initial u: the two vowels fuse into o, and the words are written as one.

C. Morphology

  • राजा rājā — Nominative singular of the n-stem राजन्: the final n is dropped and the vowel lengthened (राजन् → राजा).

D. Anvaya — prose order

राजा उवाच।

rājā uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe king said:

ReadableThe king said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSuratha answers the merchant Samādhi, who has just told him (1.21–25) how his wife and sons took his wealth and drove him out, and how he still worries about them. The two exiles have met in the forest near the sage Medhas’s hermitage (1.17).

1.27Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

यैर्निरस्तो भवाँल्लुब्धैः पुत्रदारादिभिर्धनैः ॥ २७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यैः | निरस्तः | भवान् | लुब्धैः | पुत्रदारादिभिः | धनैः

3IAST

As recitedyairnirasto bhavāँllubdhaiḥ putradārādibhirdhanaiḥ ||

Separatedyaiḥ | nirastaḥ | bhavān | lubdhaiḥ | putradārādibhiḥ | dhanaiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
यैःyaiḥby whomrelative pronoun यद्, m. instr. pl.; agent of the passive participle निरस्तः; correlative तेषु in 1.28
निरस्तःnirastaḥcast out, driven awayनिर् + √अस् (divādi, 4P ‘to throw’) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. sg.; predicate of भवान्
भवान्bhavānyou, sirm. nom. sg. of भवत्, the respectful pronoun of address (construed with the third person)
लुब्धैःlubdhaiḥgreedy√लुभ् (divādi, 4P ‘to covet’) + क्त, used as an adjective, m. instr. pl.; qualifies पुत्रदारादिभिः
पुत्रदारादिभिःputradārādibhiḥby sons, wife and the restm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to यैः
धनैःdhanaiḥlit. ‘by riches’; here ‘for the sake of wealth’n. instr. pl. of धन; instrumental of cause (hetu)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यैर्निरस्तो yairnirasto → यैः + निरस्तः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a voiced consonant: यैः → यैर् before n. The same change gives पुत्रदारादिभिर्धनैः.
  • निरस्तो भवाँल्लुब्धैः nirasto bhavāँllubdhaiḥ → निरस्तः + भवान् + लुब्धैः
    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (n + l) · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); तोर्लि (8.4.60)
    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o. Then final n before l becomes a nasalised l: भवान् लुब्धैः → भवाँल्लुब्धैः. The Gita Press writes the nasal colour with candrabindu (ँ) on the vowel before the double l.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पुत्रदारादिभिः putradārādibhiḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (पुत्रदार)
    Members: पुत्र + दार + आदि · Vigraha: पुत्राश्च दाराश्च पुत्रदाराः; पुत्रदाराः आदयः येषां ते, तैः putrāśca dārāśca putradārāḥ; putradārāḥ ādayaḥ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “those of whom sons and wife are the first”; in the verse: sons, wife and the other members of the household

C. Morphology

  • निरस्तः nirastaḥ — Past passive participle of निर् + √अस् ‘throw’: ‘thrown out’. With the instrumental यैः it makes a passive clause: ‘by whom you were cast out’.
  • भवान् bhavān — The polite ‘you’: nominative of भवत्, a pronoun that takes the verb in the third person. Its final n meets the l of लुब्धैः and becomes a nasalised l (भवाँल्लुब्धैः).
  • धनैः dhanaiḥ — Instrumental of cause: ‘because of (your) wealth’. The plural is idiomatic for ‘riches’. See Uncertain.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यैः लुब्धैः पुत्रदारादिभिः धनैः (हेतुभिः) भवान् निरस्तः, (तेषु …)

yaiḥ lubdhaiḥ putradārādibhiḥ dhanaiḥ (hetubhiḥ) bhavān nirastaḥ, (teṣu …)

A relative clause only: its correlative तेषु and the main verb come in the next half-verse (1.28).

6Translation

LiteralThose greedy ones — sons, wife and the rest — by whom you were cast out on account of wealth,

ReadableThose greedy ones — your sons, your wife and the rest — who drove you out for the sake of your wealth:

The sentence is completed in 1.28.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king restates Samādhi’s own account (1.22: ‘cast out by sons and wife out of greed for wealth’). The question it leads to comes in the next half-verse.

Traditional interpretationRead with 1.12–14, the question is ironic: Suratha has just been worrying, in the same way, about the kingdom, servants and elephant that were taken from him. He asks the merchant what he cannot yet answer for himself; he admits as much in 1.41–45.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: धनैः: the instrumental plural is read here as cause (‘on account of wealth’). Some translators take it loosely as ‘(deprived) of your wealth’, echoing 1.22 (पुत्रैरादाय मे धनम्). The reading itself is not in doubt.

1.28Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तेषु किं भवतः स्नेहमनुबध्नाति मानसम् ॥ २८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तेषु | किम् | भवतः | स्नेहम् | अनुबध्नाति | मानसम्

3IAST

As recitedteṣu kiṃ bhavataḥ snehamanubadhnāti mānasam ||

Separatedteṣu | kim | bhavataḥ | sneham | anubadhnāti | mānasam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तेषुteṣutoward thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl. (locative of the object of feeling); correlative of यैः in 1.27
किम्kimwhyindeclinable (interrogative adverb, the n. acc. of किम्)
भवतःbhavataḥyourm. gen. sg. of भवत् (respectful ‘you’); qualifies मानसम्
स्नेहम्snehamaffection, attachmentm. acc. sg. of स्नेह; object of अनुबध्नाति
अनुबध्नातिanubadhnātibinds on, keeps formingअनु + √बन्ध् (kryādi, 9P ‘to bind’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
मानसम्mānasamthe mindn. nom. sg. of मानस; subject of अनुबध्नाति

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • किं भवतः kiṃ bhavataḥ → किम् + भवतः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.
  • स्नेहमनुबध्नाति snehamanubadhnāti → स्नेहम् + अनुबध्नाति
    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written together with the next word.

C. Morphology

  • अनुबध्नाति anubadhnāti — Present of the ninth class: the root बन्ध् loses its nasal and takes the class sign ना (बध्ना-ति). With अनु: ‘to bind to, to attach’; स्नेहम् अनुबध्नाति ‘forms an attachment’.
  • मानसम् mānasam — Taddhita from मनस् ‘mind’: मानस ‘belonging to the mind’, used as a noun for the mind itself.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भवतः मानसं तेषु स्नेहं किम् अनुबध्नाति?

bhavataḥ mānasaṃ teṣu snehaṃ kim anubadhnāti?

The main clause of the question begun in 1.27: subject मानसम्, object स्नेहम्, the locative तेषु for the persons loved.

6Translation

Literal— why does your mind bind affection to them?

Readablewhy does your mind still hold to them in affection?

‘Still’ renders the force of the question in context; it is not a separate word.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king’s question: why love those who have wronged you? It is the question of the whole first carita, and the merchant will answer that he does not know (1.31–34).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the question is easier to ask of another than of oneself. The text lets the king hear his own trouble in a stranger’s voice before he can name it.

1.30Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवमेतद्यथा प्राह भवानस्मद्गतं वचः ॥ ३० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | एतत् | यथा | प्राह | भवान् | अस्मद्गतम् | वचः

3IAST

As recitedevametadyathā prāha bhavānasmadgataṃ vacaḥ ||

Separatedevam | etat | yathā | prāha | bhavān | asmadgatam | vacaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamso, just soindeclinable (adverb of manner); predicate of एतत्
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
यथाyathāasindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
प्राहprāhasays, has saidप्र + आह; आह is the form substituted for √ब्रू (adādi, 2U ‘to speak’) in the present (laṭ), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada, with perfect-type endings
भवान्bhavānyou, sirm. nom. sg. of भवत् (respectful ‘you’, with a third-person verb); subject of प्राह
अस्मद्गतम्asmadgatamconcerning men. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वचः
वचःvacaḥword, what was saidn. acc. sg. of वचस् (s-stem); object of प्राह

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवमेतद्यथा evametadyathā → एवम् + एतत् + यथा
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final m stays m before the vowel e and is written joined; the final t of एतत् becomes d before the voiced y.
  • भवानस्मद्गतं bhavānasmadgataṃ → भवान् + अस्मद्गतम्
    no change (n before a vowel) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply
    Final n is doubled before a vowel only after a short vowel; after the long ā of भवान् it simply joins the next word.
  • अस्मद्गतं वचः asmadgataṃ vacaḥ → अस्मद्गतम् + वचः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अस्मद्गतम् asmadgatam — dvitīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: अस्मद् + गत · Vigraha: अस्मान् गतम् asmān gatam
    Literally “gone to us”; in the verse: about me, concerning my case
    Alternative: Also glossed अस्मद्विषयकम् ‘having us as its subject’; the sense is the same. The plural stem अस्मद् is used of the one speaker, a usage the grammarians allow for the first person.

C. Morphology

  • प्राह prāha — आह looks like a perfect but is, for Pāṇini, a present: the first five present endings of √ब्रू are optionally replaced by perfect-type endings with आह for the root. With प्र: ‘says, declares’.
  • भवान् bhavān — Nominative of भवत्, the pronoun of respect: ‘you’ in meaning, third person in grammar (प्राह, not a second-person form).

D. Anvaya — prose order

भवान् अस्मद्गतं वचः यथा प्राह, एतत् एवम् (अस्ति)।

bhavān asmadgataṃ vacaḥ yathā prāha, etat evam (asti) |

A relative clause of manner (यथा … प्राह) and a nominal main clause with the verb ‘is’ understood: एतत् एवम्.

6Translation

LiteralThis is so, as you, sir, have spoken the words concerning me.

ReadableIt is just as you say about me.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSamādhi grants the king’s point at once: those he loves have treated him cruelly. What follows (1.31–34) is not a defence of them but a confession that his mind will not obey what he knows.

1.31Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

किं करोमि न बध्नाति मम निष्ठुरतां मनः।
यैः संत्यज्य पितृस्नेहं धनलुब्धैर्निराकृतः ॥ ३१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

किम् | करोमि | न | बध्नाति | मम | निष्ठुरताम् | मनःयैः | संत्यज्य | पितृस्नेहम् | धनलुब्धैः | निराकृतः

3IAST

As recitedkiṃ karomi na badhnāti mama niṣṭhuratāṃ manaḥ |
yaiḥ saṃtyajya pitṛsnehaṃ dhanalubdhairnirākṛtaḥ ||

Separatedkim | karomi | na | badhnāti | mama | niṣṭhuratām | manaḥ
yaiḥ | saṃtyajya | pitṛsneham | dhanalubdhaiḥ | nirākṛtaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
किम्kimwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, n. acc. sg.; object of करोमि
करोमिkaromiam I to do, can I do√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
बध्नातिbadhnātilit. ‘binds’; here ‘takes on, assumes’√बन्ध् (kryādi, 9P ‘to bind’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; with मनः
निष्ठुरताम्niṣṭhuratāmhardnessf. acc. sg. of निष्ठुरता (abstract noun from निष्ठुर ‘hard, harsh’); object of बध्नाति
मनःmanaḥmind, heartn. nom. sg. of मनस् (s-stem); subject of बध्नाति
यैःyaiḥby whomrelative pronoun यद्, m. instr. pl.; agent of निराकृतः; correlative तेषु in 1.32
संत्यज्यsaṃtyajyahaving cast offसम् + √त्यज् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to leave’) + ल्यप् (absolutive of a prefixed verb)
पितृस्नेहम्pitṛsnehamlove for a fatherm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of संत्यज्य
धनलुब्धैःdhanalubdhaiḥgreedy for wealthm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies यैः
निराकृतःnirākṛtaḥspurned, driven awayनिर् + आ + √कृ (tanādi, 8U) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. sg.; ‘I’ (अहम्) is understood

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • किं करोमि kiṃ karomi → किम् + करोमि
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; the same in निष्ठुरतां मनः and पितृस्नेहं धन-.
  • धनलुब्धैर्निराकृतः dhanalubdhairnirākṛtaḥ → धनलुब्धैः + निराकृतः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced n.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पितृस्नेहम् pitṛsneham — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (objective genitive)
    Members: पितृ + स्नेह · Vigraha: पितुः स्नेहः, तम् pituḥ snehaḥ, tam
    Literally “love of a father”; in the verse: the love owed to a father — the sons’ love for him
    Alternative: Also read as a saptamī tatpuruṣa, पितरि स्नेहः ‘love toward the father’; the sense is the same. Read as ‘a father’s love (for his sons)’ it would not fit: the sons are the ones who cast it off.
  • धनलुब्धैः dhanalubdhaiḥ — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: धन + लुब्ध · Vigraha: धने लुब्धाः, तैः dhane lubdhāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “greedy in (the matter of) wealth”; in the verse: covetous of his money
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, धनस्य लुब्धाः; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • करोमि karomi — Present of √कृ (stem करो- before the first-person ending -मि). In a question the present is deliberative: ‘what am I to do?’
  • संत्यज्य saṃtyajya — Absolutive with ल्यप् (-य), the form used when the verb has a prefix (contrast त्यक्त्वा). Its subject is the agent of the main verb, here the kin (यैः).
  • निष्ठुरताम् niṣṭhuratām — Abstract noun in -ता (taddhita तल्) from निष्ठुर ‘hard’. मनः निष्ठुरतां न बध्नाति: ‘the mind does not take on hardness’ — the merchant cannot make himself stop caring.

D. Anvaya — prose order

किं करोमि? मम मनः निष्ठुरतां न बध्नाति। यैः धनलुब्धैः पितृस्नेहं संत्यज्य (अहं) निराकृतः, …

kiṃ karomi? mama manaḥ niṣṭhuratāṃ na badhnāti | yaiḥ dhanalubdhaiḥ pitṛsnehaṃ saṃtyajya (ahaṃ) nirākṛtaḥ, …

A question, then a statement; the second line opens a relative clause (यैः … निराकृतः) whose correlative तेषु and main clause come in 1.32.

6Translation

LiteralWhat am I to do? My mind does not take on hardness. Those greedy for wealth by whom, having cast off love for a father, I was spurned —

ReadableWhat can I do? My heart will not grow hard. Those who, greedy for my wealth, threw off the love they owed their father and drove me out —

‘my’ with wealth and ‘their’ with father are supplied from the context; the sentence continues in 1.32.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant answers with a helpless question. He knows how he was treated and cannot stop loving: his mind refuses the hardness that reason would justify.

Traditional interpretationThe verb बन्ध् ‘to bind’ is the word the text will use for saṃsāra itself (संसारबन्ध, 1.58). Here it is turned around: the mind will not bind itself to hardness, because it is already bound by affection.

1.32Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पतिस्वजनहार्दं च हार्दि तेष्वेव मे मनः।
किमेतन्नाभिजानामि जानन्नपि महामते ॥ ३२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पतिस्वजनहार्दम्patisvajanahārdamaffection for husband and kinsmann. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second object of संत्यज्य in 1.31
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
हार्दिhārdiloving, full of affectionn. nom. sg. of the adjective हार्दिन् (in-stem); predicate of मनः
तेषुteṣutoward thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl.; correlative of यैः in 1.31
एवevastill, those very (people)indeclinable (emphatic particle), stressing तेषु
मेmemyenclitic form of अस्मद्, gen. sg.
मनःmanaḥmind, heartn. nom. sg. of मनस्; subject
किम्kimwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, n. nom. sg.; predicate of एतत्
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
अभिजानामिabhijānāmiI understand, I see intoअभि + √ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U ‘to know’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
जानन्jānanknowing√ज्ञा + शतृ (present participle), m. nom. sg.; agrees with the understood ‘I’
अपिapieven thoughindeclinable (concessive particle, with the participle)
महामतेmahāmateO wise onem. voc. sg. of महामति; compound (see Samāsa); addressed to the king

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तेष्वेव teṣveva → तेषु + एव
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v.
  • किमेतन्नाभिजानामि kimetannābhijānāmi → किम् + एतत् + न + अभिजानामि
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation); vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Final t before the nasal n becomes n; then न + अभि- gives नाभि-. The m of किम् simply joins the vowel of एतत्.
  • जानन्नपि jānannapi → जानन् + अपि
    consonant sandhi (doubling of n) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32)
    A final n after a short vowel is doubled before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पतिस्वजनहार्दम् patisvajanahārdam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (objective genitive) containing an itaretara dvandva (पतिस्वजन)
    Members: पति + स्वजन + हार्द · Vigraha: पतिश्च स्वजनश्च पतिस्वजनौ; पतिस्वजनयोः हार्दम्, तत् patiśca svajanaśca patisvajanau; patisvajanayoḥ hārdam, tat
    Literally “affection of (= toward) husband and kinsman”; in the verse: the love a wife owes her husband and kin owe a kinsman — which they too threw off
    Alternative: स्वजन is itself a karmadhāraya, स्वः जनः ‘one’s own person, a kinsman’. The dvandva may also be taken as plural (पतिस्वजनाः).
  • महामते mahāmate — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + मति · Vigraha: महती मतिः यस्य सः, तत्सम्बोधने mahatī matiḥ yasya saḥ, tatsambodhane
    Literally “one whose understanding is great”; in the verse: a respectful address to the king

C. Morphology

  • हार्दि hārdi — Neuter nominative of हार्दिन् ‘having हार्द (affection)’, agreeing with मनः. The masculine nominative of the same word, हार्दी, comes in 1.43.
  • जानन् jānan — Present participle of √ज्ञा (stem जानत्), nominative singular masculine. With अपि it is concessive: ‘though knowing’.
  • अभिजानामि abhijānāmi — The prefix अभि sharpens ‘know’ to ‘understand, recognise’: the merchant knows the facts (जानन्) but cannot see into them (न अभिजानामि).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(यैः) पतिस्वजनहार्दं च (संत्यज्य अहं निराकृतः), तेषु एव मे मनः हार्दि (अस्ति)। महामते, जानन् अपि एतत् किं (इति) न अभिजानामि।

(yaiḥ) patisvajanahārdaṃ ca (saṃtyajya ahaṃ nirākṛtaḥ), teṣu eva me manaḥ hārdi (asti) | mahāmate, jānan api etat kiṃ (iti) na abhijānāmi |

The first half closes the relative sentence of 1.31: पतिस्वजनहार्दम् is a second object of संत्यज्य, and तेषु … हार्दि is the main clause. The second half is a new sentence: an indirect question (किम् एतत्) governed by न अभिजानामि.

6Translation

Literal— and affection for husband and kinsman: toward those very ones my mind is loving. What this is I do not understand, though knowing, O great-minded one.

Readablewho threw off all feeling for husband and kinsman — toward them, still, my heart is full of love. I know all this, wise king, and yet I cannot understand it.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant states the paradox the sage will be asked to explain: he knows (जानन्) and yet does not understand (न अभिजानामि). The facts are clear to him; his heart does not follow them.

Traditional interpretationThis is the condition Medhas will name in 1.55: Mahāmāyā draws away the minds even of those who know (ज्ञानिनामपि चेतांसि). Knowledge of the facts is not freedom from delusion.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the text separates information from understanding. The merchant is not ignorant; he is attached — and he is honest enough to say that the two are different.

1.33Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

यत्प्रेमप्रवणं चित्तं विगुणेष्वपि बन्धुषु।
तेषां कृते मे निःश्वासो दौर्मनस्यं च जायते ॥ ३३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यत् | प्रेमप्रवणम् | चित्तम् | विगुणेषु | अपि | बन्धुषुतेषाम् | कृते | मे | निःश्वासः | दौर्मनस्यम् | च | जायते

3IAST

As recitedyatpremapravaṇaṃ cittaṃ viguṇeṣvapi bandhuṣu |
teṣāṃ kṛte me niḥśvāso daurmanasyaṃ ca jāyate ||

Separatedyat | premapravaṇam | cittam | viguṇeṣu | api | bandhuṣu
teṣām | kṛte | me | niḥśvāsaḥ | daurmanasyam | ca | jāyate

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
यत्yatthat (namely)indeclinable use of यद् (n.), a conjunction introducing what एतत् in 1.32 refers to
प्रेमप्रवणम्premapravaṇaminclined to loven. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of चित्तम्
चित्तम्cittamthe mind, the heartn. nom. sg. of चित्त; subject
विगुणेषुviguṇeṣuworthless, without virtuem. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies बन्धुषु
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)
बन्धुषुbandhuṣutoward kinsfolkm. loc. pl. of बन्धु (u-stem); locative of the object of feeling
तेषाम्teṣāmtheirpronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.; governed by कृते
कृतेkṛtefor the sake of, on account ofindeclinable (the loc. sg. of कृत, used as a postposition with the genitive)
मेmein me, myenclitic form of अस्मद्, gen. sg.
निःश्वासःniḥśvāsaḥsighingm. nom. sg. of निःश्वास (निस् + √श्वस् + घञ्); subject of जायते
दौर्मनस्यम्daurmanasyamdejection, heaviness of heartn. nom. sg. of दौर्मनस्य (abstract noun from दुर्मनस्); second subject of जायते
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
जायतेjāyatearises√जन् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to be born’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विगुणेष्वपि viguṇeṣvapi → विगुणेषु + अपि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v.
  • निःश्वासो दौर्मनस्यं niḥśvāso daurmanasyaṃ → निःश्वासः + दौर्मनस्यम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o. The final m of दौर्मनस्यम् becomes anusvāra before च (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • यत्प्रेमप्रवणं yatpremapravaṇaṃ → यत् + प्रेमप्रवणम्
    no change (t before an unvoiced stop) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    The final t stays unvoiced before p; the two words are only written together.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रेमप्रवणम् premapravaṇam — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: प्रेमन् + प्रवण · Vigraha: प्रेम्णि प्रवणम् premṇi pravaṇam
    Literally “sloping toward love”; in the verse: naturally given to love
    Alternative: Also read as a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, प्रेम्णा प्रवणम् ‘bent by love’; the sense is close. प्रेमन् drops its final n as the first member of a compound.
  • विगुणेषु viguṇeṣu — bahuvrīhi (prādi: the prefix वि stands for विगत)
    Members: वि + गुण · Vigraha: विगताः गुणाः येभ्यः ते, तेषु vigatāḥ guṇāḥ yebhyaḥ te, teṣu
    Literally “from whom virtues have gone”; in the verse: worthless, who have behaved without virtue

C. Morphology

  • जायते jāyate — Present of √जन् in the fourth class (stem जाय-), ātmanepada. It is singular with two subjects joined by च (निःश्वासः, दौर्मनस्यम्), agreeing with the nearer or taking the pair as one experience.
  • दौर्मनस्यम् daurmanasyam — Abstract noun with vṛddhi of the first syllable (taddhita ष्यञ्): दुर्मनस् ‘ill-minded, dejected’ → दौर्मनस्य ‘dejection’.
  • कृते kṛte — Locative of कृत ‘done’, fixed as a postposition: तेषां कृते ‘for their sake, on their account’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(एतत् किम्,) यत् चित्तं विगुणेषु अपि बन्धुषु प्रेमप्रवणम् (अस्ति), तेषां कृते मे निःश्वासः दौर्मनस्यं च जायते?

(etat kim,) yat cittaṃ viguṇeṣu api bandhuṣu premapravaṇam (asti), teṣāṃ kṛte me niḥśvāsaḥ daurmanasyaṃ ca jāyate?

A यत् clause (‘that …’) spelling out the एतत् of 1.32: two statements, the first nominal (verb ‘is’ understood), the second with जायते.

6Translation

Literal— that the mind is inclined to love even toward worthless kinsfolk; that on their account sighing and dejection arise in me.

Readable— that my heart leans in love toward kinsfolk who have shown no virtue, and that I sigh and grieve for their sake.

‘my’ with heart is supplied from मे in the second line; the verse explains the ‘this’ (एतत्) of 1.32.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant names what he cannot understand: love that persists toward the unworthy, and the sighing and gloom it brings. The symptoms are bodily (निःश्वास) and mental (दौर्मनस्य).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the verse does not call the love wrong. It reports, without judgement, that love does not wait for its object to deserve it — which is both the merchant’s pain and, later in the text, the shape of the Goddess’s own compassion.

1.34Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

करोमि किं यन्न मनस्तेष्वप्रीतिषु निष्ठुरम् ॥ ३४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

करोमि | किम् | यत् | न | मनः | तेषु | अप्रीतिषु | निष्ठुरम्

3IAST

As recitedkaromi kiṃ yanna manasteṣvaprītiṣu niṣṭhuram ||

Separatedkaromi | kim | yat | na | manaḥ | teṣu | aprītiṣu | niṣṭhuram

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
करोमिkaromiam I to do√कृ (tanādi, 8U), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
किम्kimwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, n. acc. sg.; object of करोमि
यत्yatsince, seeing thatindeclinable use of यद् (n.), a causal conjunction
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
मनःmanaḥthe mind, the heartn. nom. sg. of मनस्; subject
तेषुteṣutoward thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl.
अप्रीतिषुaprītiṣuwho are without lovem. loc. pl. of अप्रीति (as an adjective); compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तेषु
निष्ठुरम्niṣṭhuramhardn. nom. sg. of निष्ठुर; predicate of मनः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यन्न yanna → यत् + न
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal n becomes n.
  • मनस्तेष्वप्रीतिषु manasteṣvaprītiṣu → मनः + तेषु + अप्रीतिषु
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Visarga before t becomes s; final u before a becomes v.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अप्रीतिषु aprītiṣu — bahuvrīhi (nañ-bahuvrīhi)
    Members: अ + प्रीति · Vigraha: न विद्यते प्रीतिः येषां ते, तेषु na vidyate prītiḥ yeṣāṃ te, teṣu
    Literally “those for whom there is no love”; in the verse: the unloving kin
    Alternative: Also possible as a nañ tatpuruṣa noun, न प्रीतिः ‘absence of love’, in the locative plural: ‘amid (their) unkindnesses’. The bahuvrīhi fits better, since the word stands beside तेषु as its qualifier.

C. Morphology

  • करोमि karomi — Deliberative present, as in 1.31: करोमि किम् here answers किं करोमि there, so the merchant’s reply begins and ends with the same helpless question.
  • निष्ठुरम् niṣṭhuram — The adjective whose abstract noun (निष्ठुरता) appeared in 1.31; here neuter, agreeing with मनः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

किं करोमि, यत् मनः तेषु अप्रीतिषु निष्ठुरं न (भवति)?

kiṃ karomi, yat manaḥ teṣu aprītiṣu niṣṭhuraṃ na (bhavati)?

A question with a causal यत् clause; the verb ‘is’ (भवति) is understood.

6Translation

LiteralWhat am I to do, since the mind is not hard toward those loveless ones?

ReadableWhat am I to do? My heart will not harden against those who have no love for me.

‘for me’ makes explicit whom the kin do not love; the compound says only ‘without love’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe merchant closes his reply where he began (1.31): ‘What can I do?’ The repetition marks the end of his speech and of his own resources; the next step is to go to the sage.

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    As in 1.1: the y that replaces the visarga before a vowel is dropped, and the vowels stand apart.

मार्कण्डेयः उवाच।

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralMārkaṇḍeya said:

ReadableMārkaṇḍeya said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe frame narrator, Mārkaṇḍeya, speaking to his pupil Krauṣṭuki, takes up the story again: the conversation of the king and the merchant is over, and he tells how the two went together to the sage Medhas.

1.36Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततस्तौ सहितौ विप्र तं मुनिं समुपस्थितौ ॥ ३६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | तौ | सहितौ | विप्र | तम् | मुनिम् | समुपस्थितौ

3IAST

As recitedtatastau sahitau vipra taṃ muniṃ samupasthitau ||

Separatedtataḥ | tau | sahitau | vipra | tam | munim | samupasthitau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time, the ablative of तद् fixed as a particle)
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. nom. du.; subject
सहितौsahitautogetheradjective सहित ‘accompanied, joined’, m. nom. du.; qualifies तौ
विप्रvipraO brahminm. voc. sg. of विप्र; Mārkaṇḍeya addresses his listener
तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies मुनिम्
मुनिम्munimsagem. acc. sg. of मुनि (i-stem); object of समुपस्थितौ
समुपस्थितौsamupasthitauapproachedसम् + उप + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’) + क्त (past participle in active sense), m. nom. du.; predicate of तौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततस्तौ tatastau → ततः + तौ
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • तं मुनिं समुपस्थितौ taṃ muniṃ samupasthitau → तम् + मुनिम् + समुपस्थितौ
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra, twice.

C. Morphology

  • समुपस्थितौ samupasthitau — Past participle of √स्था with सम् + उप. With verbs of motion and standing the क्त participle is active and may take an object: तं मुनिं समुपस्थितौ ‘(they) approached the sage’. It serves as the finite verb.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) विप्र, ततः सहितौ तौ तं मुनिं समुपस्थितौ।

(he) vipra, tataḥ sahitau tau taṃ muniṃ samupasthitau |

Subject (तौ, with सहितौ), object (तं मुनिम्), and the participle समुपस्थितौ as the verb; विप्र is a vocative aside. The two are named in 1.37.

6Translation

LiteralThen, O brahmin, those two together approached that sage —

ReadableThen, O brahmin, the two went together to the sage:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king and the merchant go together to Medhas. The vocative विप्र is Mārkaṇḍeya’s address to his listener in the frame, the brahmin Krauṣṭuki — a reminder that the whole story is being told to him.

1.37Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सःsaḥthat, hepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies पार्थिवसत्तमः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
पार्थिवसत्तमःpārthivasattamaḥthe best of kingsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving made√कृ (tanādi, 8U) + क्त्वा (absolutive)
तुtuand, thenindeclinable (particle marking the next step of the narrative)
तौtauthe twopronoun तद्, m. nom. du.; subject of कृत्वा and of चक्रतुः in 1.38
यथान्यायम्yathānyāyamas was properindeclinable; compound (see Samāsa)
यथार्हम्yathārhamas was fittingindeclinable; compound (see Samāsa)
तेनtenawith him (the sage)pronoun तद्, m. instr. sg.; instrumental of association
संविदम्saṃvidamlit. ‘mutual understanding’; here ‘an exchange of greetings’f. acc. sg. of संविद् (consonant stem); object of कृत्वा

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समाधिर्नाम samādhirnāma → समाधिः + नाम
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced n.
  • वैश्योऽसौ vaiśyo'sau → वैश्यः + असौ
    visarga sandhi with pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • स च sa ca → सः + च
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant.
  • यथान्यायं यथार्हं yathānyāyaṃ yathārhaṃ → यथा + न्यायम्; यथा + अर्हम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); anusvāra · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    ā + a → ā in यथार्हम्; each final m becomes anusvāra before the next consonant.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पार्थिवसत्तमः pārthivasattamaḥ — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पार्थिव + सत्तम · Vigraha: पार्थिवेषु सत्तमः pārthiveṣu sattamaḥ
    Literally “the best among kings”; in the verse: the noble king Suratha
    Alternative: Superlatives of selection are not compounded as genitives (‘best of kings’); the grammarians resolve them with the locative of the group.
  • यथान्यायम् yathānyāyam — avyayībhāva
    Members: यथा + न्याय · Vigraha: न्यायम् अनतिक्रम्य nyāyam anatikramya
    Literally “not going beyond what is proper”; in the verse: in due form
  • यथार्हम् yathārham — avyayībhāva
    Members: यथा + अर्ह · Vigraha: अर्हम् अनतिक्रम्य arham anatikramya
    Literally “not going beyond what is fitting”; in the verse: as befitted the sage

C. Morphology

  • असौ asau — Nominative singular masculine (and feminine) of अदस् ‘that (yonder)’, the pronoun of distance.
  • कृत्वा kṛtvā — Absolutive of √कृ (unprefixed, so -त्वा): ‘having made’. Its subject तौ is the subject of the main verb चक्रतुः in 1.38.
  • संविदम् saṃvidam — Accusative of संविद्, from सम् + √विद् ‘to know together’: agreement, mutual understanding; with कृ ‘to come to terms, exchange courtesies’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

समाधिः नाम असौ वैश्यः सः पार्थिवसत्तमः च। तौ तु तेन यथान्यायं यथार्हं संविदं कृत्वा, (उपविष्टौ …)

samādhiḥ nāma asau vaiśyaḥ saḥ pārthivasattamaḥ ca | tau tu tena yathānyāyaṃ yathārhaṃ saṃvidaṃ kṛtvā, (upaviṣṭau …)

The first line names the two (in apposition to तौ of 1.36). The second line opens a new sentence with an absolutive clause; its main verb (चक्रतुः) is in 1.38.

6Translation

Literal— that merchant named Samādhi and that best of kings. And the two, having made with him, as was proper and as was fitting, an exchange of greetings,

Readablethe merchant Samādhi and the noble king. When they had greeted him with due courtesy, as was fitting,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe two exiles are named side by side and come before the sage in due form. The courtesies are not skipped: the teaching that follows begins with the proper approach of the pupil.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the merchant’s name, Samādhi, is the word for meditative absorption. The text does not comment on it, but at its end (13.15ff) it is he who asks for knowledge rather than a kingdom.

1.38Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

उपविष्टौ कथाः काश्चिच्चक्रतुर्वैश्यपार्थिवौ ॥ ३८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

उपविष्टौ | कथाः | काश्चित् | चक्रतुः | वैश्यपार्थिवौ

3IAST

As recitedupaviṣṭau kathāḥ kāściccakraturvaiśyapārthivau ||

Separatedupaviṣṭau | kathāḥ | kāścit | cakratuḥ | vaiśyapārthivau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
उपविष्टौupaviṣṭauseated, having sat downउप + √विश् (tudādi, 6P ‘to enter’) + क्त (past participle in active sense), m. nom. du.
कथाःkathāḥtalks, conversationf. acc. pl. of कथा; object of चक्रतुः
काश्चित्kāścitsomeindefinite pronoun किम् + चित्, f. acc. pl.; qualifies कथाः
चक्रतुःcakratuḥ(they two) made, held√कृ (tanādi, 8U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, parasmaipada
वैश्यपार्थिवौvaiśyapārthivauthe merchant and the kingm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • काश्चिच्चक्रतुर् kāściccakratur → काश्चित् + चक्रतुः
    consonant sandhi (palatalisation); visarga sandhi · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Final t before c becomes c. The visarga of चक्रतुः after u is sounded r before the voiced v of वैश्य-.
  • काश्चिच् kāścic → काः + चित् (before च-)
    visarga sandhi inside the indefinite pronoun · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    The visarga of काः becomes s before c, and the s becomes ś next to the palatal: काः + चित् → काश्चित्, whose t then becomes c before चक्रतुः.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वैश्यपार्थिवौ vaiśyapārthivau — itaretara dvandva
    Members: वैश्य + पार्थिव · Vigraha: वैश्यश्च पार्थिवश्च vaiśyaśca pārthivaśca
    Literally “the merchant and the king”; in the verse: Samādhi and Suratha, named as a pair

C. Morphology

  • चक्रतुः cakratuḥ — Perfect of √कृ, third person dual: reduplication च (k → c) + क्र + the dual ending -अतुः.
  • उपविष्टौ upaviṣṭau — Past participle of उप + √विश् ‘to sit down’ (श् + त → ष्ट), active in sense: ‘having seated themselves’.
  • काश्चित् kāścit — The indefinite pronoun: a form of किम् with the particle चित् (‘some’). It is one word and declines in its first part (कश्चित्, काश्चित्, केचित्).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तौ) वैश्यपार्थिवौ उपविष्टौ (सन्तौ) काश्चित् कथाः चक्रतुः।

(tau) vaiśyapārthivau upaviṣṭau (santau) kāścit kathāḥ cakratuḥ |

The main clause of the sentence begun in 1.37: subject वैश्यपार्थिवौ, participle उपविष्टौ, object कथाः with काश्चित्, verb चक्रतुः.

6Translation

Literal— seated, the merchant and the king held some talks.

Readablethe merchant and the king sat down and spoke with him a while.

‘with him’ is carried over from तेन in 1.37.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe scene is set for the teaching: the two are seated before the sage and the conversation begins. The king’s question follows.
