1.40Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भगवंस्त्वामहं प्रष्टुमिच्छाम्येकं वदस्व तत् ॥ ४० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भगवन् | त्वाम् | अहम् | प्रष्टुम् | इच्छामि | एकम् | वदस्व | तत्

3IAST

As recitedbhagavaṃstvāmahaṃ praṣṭumicchāmyekaṃ vadasva tat ||

Separatedbhagavan | tvām | aham | praṣṭum | icchāmi | ekam | vadasva | tat

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भगवन्bhagavanO revered onem. voc. sg. of भगवत्; addressed to the sage
त्वाम्tvāmyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of प्रष्टुम्
अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of इच्छामि
प्रष्टुम्praṣṭumto ask√प्रच्छ् (tudādi, 6P ‘to ask’) + तुमुन् (infinitive)
इच्छामिicchāmiI wish√इष् (tudādi, 6P ‘to wish’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एकम्ekamone thingn. acc. sg. of एक; second object of प्रष्टुम् (verbs of asking take two accusatives)
वदस्वvadasvatell√वद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to speak’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; object of वदस्व

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भगवंस्त्वामहं bhagavaṃstvāmahaṃ → भगवन् + त्वाम् + अहम्
    consonant sandhi (n before t) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)
    Final n before t becomes ṃs (anusvāra + s): भगवन् त्वाम् → भगवंस्त्वाम्. The m of त्वाम् joins the vowel of अहम्.
  • प्रष्टुमिच्छाम्येकं praṣṭumicchāmyekaṃ → प्रष्टुम् + इच्छामि + एकम्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before e becomes y; the m of प्रष्टुम् joins the following vowel.
  • एकं वदस्व ekaṃ vadasva → एकम् + वदस्व
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • प्रष्टुम् praṣṭum — Infinitive of √प्रच्छ्: the root-final छ् becomes ष् before त्, which becomes ट् (प्रच्छ् + तुम् → प्रष्टुम्).
  • वदस्व vadasva — Imperative ātmanepada of √वद्, a root normally parasmaipada. Pāṇini allows the ātmanepada of वद् in certain senses, among them speaking with knowledge or expertise; here it suits a request for instruction. It is also common in epic usage.
  • भगवन् bhagavan — Vocative of भगवत् (‘possessed of fortune, revered’), the -vat stem: nominative भगवान्, vocative भगवन्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भगवन्, अहं त्वाम् एकं प्रष्टुम् इच्छामि; तत् वदस्व।

bhagavan, ahaṃ tvām ekaṃ praṣṭum icchāmi; tat vadasva |

Two clauses: the wish to ask (इच्छामि with the infinitive प्रष्टुम् and its two objects त्वाम्, एकम्), and the imperative वदस्व with तत्.

6Translation

LiteralRevered one, I wish to ask you one thing; tell (me) that.

ReadableRevered sir, there is one thing I wish to ask you. Please explain it to me.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king formally asks to put a question. After the approach (1.36–38) comes the request, and then the question itself (1.41–45).

Traditional interpretationThe shape is that of a classical teaching dialogue: a pupil who has come with due courtesy asks one question, and the teacher’s answer becomes the text. The rest of the Devī Māhātmya unfolds as Medhas’s answer.

1.41Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दुःखाय यन्मे मनसः स्वचित्तायत्ततां विना।
ममत्वं गतराज्यस्य राज्याङ्गेष्वखिलेष्वपि ॥ ४१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दुःखाय | यत् | मे | मनसः | स्वचित्तायत्तताम् | विनाममत्वम् | गतराज्यस्य | राज्याङ्गेषु | अखिलेषु | अपि

3IAST

As recitedduḥkhāya yanme manasaḥ svacittāyattatāṃ vinā |
mamatvaṃ gatarājyasya rājyāṅgeṣvakhileṣvapi ||

Separatedduḥkhāya | yat | me | manasaḥ | svacittāyattatām | vinā
mamatvam | gatarājyasya | rājyāṅgeṣu | akhileṣu | api

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दुःखायduḥkhāyato the grief (of), so as to painn. dat. sg. of दुःख; dative of result
यत्yatthatindeclinable use of यद् (n.), a conjunction; the clause it opens is taken up by एतत् in 1.42
मेmeof me, in meenclitic form of अस्मद्, gen. sg.; possessor of ममत्वम्, and qualified by गतराज्यस्य
मनसःmanasaḥof the mindn. gen. sg. of मनस्; with दुःखाय
स्वचित्तायत्तताम्svacittāyattatāmdependence on one’s own mind, being under one’s own controlf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); governed by विना
विनाvināwithout, apart fromindeclinable (postposition, with the accusative)
ममत्वम्mamatvamthe sense of ‘mine’, possessivenessn. nom. sg. of ममत्व (abstract noun from मम ‘mine’); subject, with ‘is’ understood
गतराज्यस्यgatarājyasyawhose kingdom is gonem. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); agrees with मे
राज्याङ्गेषुrājyāṅgeṣutoward the limbs of the kingdomn. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the object of ममत्व
अखिलेषुakhileṣualladjective अखिल, n. loc. pl.; qualifies राज्याङ्गेषु
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यन्मे yanme → यत् + मे
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal m becomes n.
  • राज्याङ्गेष्वखिलेष्वपि rājyāṅgeṣvakhileṣvapi → राज्याङ्गेषु + अखिलेषु + अपि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a becomes v, twice.
  • स्वचित्तायत्ततां svacittāyattatāṃ → स्वचित्त + आयत्तताम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā. The same joining inside राज्याङ्गेषु (राज्य + अङ्ग).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्वचित्तायत्तताम् svacittāyattatām — saptamī tatpuruṣa (स्वचित्तायत्त) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (स्वचित्त), made abstract with -ता
    Members: स्व + चित्त + आयत्त + ता · Vigraha: स्वस्य चित्तं स्वचित्तम्; स्वचित्ते आयत्तः स्वचित्तायत्तः; तस्य भावः स्वचित्तायत्तता, ताम् svasya cittaṃ svacittam; svacitte āyattaḥ svacittāyattaḥ; tasya bhāvaḥ svacittāyattatā, tām
    Literally “the state of being dependent on one’s own mind”; in the verse: self-control; विना ‘without’ it means ‘beyond my control, against my will’
  • गतराज्यस्य gatarājyasya — bahuvrīhi
    Members: गत + राज्य · Vigraha: गतं राज्यं यस्य सः, तस्य gataṃ rājyaṃ yasya saḥ, tasya
    Literally “of one whose kingdom has gone”; in the verse: of me, the dispossessed king
  • राज्याङ्गेषु rājyāṅgeṣu — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: राज्य + अङ्ग · Vigraha: राज्यस्य अङ्गानि, तेषु rājyasya aṅgāni, teṣu
    Literally “in the limbs of the kingdom”; in the verse: every part of the realm he once ruled

C. Morphology

  • ममत्वम् mamatvam — A noun built on an inflected form: the genitive मम ‘of me, mine’ + the abstract suffix -त्व. ‘Mine-ness’: the feeling that something belongs to me. The same idea returns as ममता in 1.53.
  • दुःखाय duḥkhāya — Dative of result: ‘for the grief of (my) mind’, i.e. so that it pains me.
  • स्वचित्तायत्तताम् svacittāyattatām — Abstract noun in -ता on a compound: the whole phrase ‘dependent on one’s own mind’ is turned into a state, then governed by विना.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत् गतराज्यस्य मे अखिलेषु अपि राज्याङ्गेषु ममत्वं (अस्ति), (मम) मनसः दुःखाय, स्वचित्तायत्ततां विना, …

yat gatarājyasya me akhileṣu api rājyāṅgeṣu mamatvaṃ (asti), (mama) manasaḥ duḥkhāya, svacittāyattatāṃ vinā, …

A यत् clause (‘that …’), nominal, with ‘is’ understood; its subject is ममत्वम्. It is taken up by किम् एतत् in 1.42.

6Translation

LiteralThat — to the grief of my mind, without (its) being under the control of my own mind — there is in me, whose kingdom is gone, a sense of ‘mine’ even toward all the limbs of the kingdom,

ReadableMy kingdom is gone, yet I still feel that every part of it is mine — against my own will, and to my mind’s distress,

The Sanskrit is a ‘that …’ clause completed by ‘what is this?’ in 1.42; the translation makes it a statement. ‘Against my own will’ renders स्वचित्तायत्ततां विना.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningNow the king speaks of himself. He asked the merchant why he still loved those who had wronged him (1.27–28); here he admits the same bondage: he has lost his kingdom and cannot stop feeling it is his.

Traditional interpretation‘Limbs of the kingdom’ is a term of Indian political thought, where a state has seven limbs: the king, ministers, territory, fort, treasury, army and ally. The king’s earlier worries (1.12–14) touch several of them: his city, his servants, his elephant, his treasury.

1.42Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

जानतोऽपि यथाज्ञस्य किमेतन्मुनिसत्तम।
अयं च निकृतः पुत्रैर्दारैर्भृत्यैस्तथोज्झितः ॥ ४२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

जानतः | अपि | यथा | अज्ञस्य | किम् | एतत् | मुनिसत्तमअयम् | च | निकृतः | पुत्रैः | दारैः | भृत्यैः | तथा | उज्झितः

3IAST

As recitedjānato'pi yathājñasya kimetanmunisattama |
ayaṃ ca nikṛtaḥ putrairdārairbhṛtyaistathojjhitaḥ ||

Separatedjānataḥ | api | yathā | ajñasya | kim | etat | munisattama
ayam | ca | nikṛtaḥ | putraiḥ | dāraiḥ | bhṛtyaiḥ | tathā | ujjhitaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
जानतःjānataḥknowing√ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U) + शतृ (present participle), m. gen. sg.; agrees with मे in 1.41
अपिapieven thoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
यथाyathāas, just likeindeclinable (adverb of comparison)
अज्ञस्यajñasyaof one who does not knowm. gen. sg. of अज्ञ; compound (see Samāsa)
किम्kimwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, n. nom. sg.; predicate
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject; refers back to the यत् clause of 1.41
मुनिसत्तमmunisattamaO best of sagesm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
अयम्ayamthis man (the merchant)demonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
निकृतःnikṛtaḥwronged, humiliatedनि + √कृ (tanādi, 8U) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. sg.
पुत्रैःputraiḥby sonsm. instr. pl. of पुत्र; agent
दारैःdāraiḥby his wifem. instr. pl. of दार (always masculine plural, ‘wife’); agent
भृत्यैःbhṛtyaiḥby servantsm. instr. pl. of भृत्य; agent
तथाtathālikewise, and alsoindeclinable (adverb)
उज्झितःujjhitaḥabandoned√उज्झ् (tudādi, 6P ‘to forsake’) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • जानतोऽपि jānato'pi → जानतः + अपि
    visarga sandhi with pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • यथाज्ञस्य yathājñasya → यथा + अज्ञस्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. Read as two words: ‘as of one who does not know’.
  • किमेतन्मुनिसत्तम kimetanmunisattama → किम् + एतत् + मुनिसत्तम
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before m becomes n.
  • पुत्रैर्दारैर्भृत्यैस्तथोज्झितः putrairdārairbhṛtyaistathojjhitaḥ → पुत्रैः + दारैः + भृत्यैः + तथा + उज्झितः
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (guṇa) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aiḥ is sounded -air before the voiced d and bh, and becomes -ais before t; तथा + उज्झितः gives तथोज्झितः.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अज्ञस्य ajñasya — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + ज्ञ · Vigraha: न ज्ञः अज्ञः, तस्य na jñaḥ ajñaḥ, tasya
    Literally “not a knower”; in the verse: an ignorant man; ज्ञ ‘knowing’ is itself a kṛt noun from √ज्ञा
  • मुनिसत्तम munisattama — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मुनि + सत्तम · Vigraha: मुनिषु सत्तमः, तत्सम्बोधने muniṣu sattamaḥ, tatsambodhane
    Literally “best among sages”; in the verse: a respectful address to Medhas (compare पार्थिवसत्तमः, 1.37)

C. Morphology

  • जानतः jānataḥ — Genitive of the present participle जानत्, agreeing with मे in 1.41: ‘of me, though knowing’. It echoes the merchant’s जानन्नपि (1.32).
  • दारैः dāraiḥ — दार ‘wife’ is masculine and used only in the plural; one wife is meant.
  • निकृतः nikṛtaḥ — Past participle of नि + √कृ ‘to bring down, to wrong’; with उज्झितः the two participles describe the merchant, the verb ‘is’ understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) मुनिसत्तम, जानतः अपि (मे) अज्ञस्य यथा (ममत्वम्) — एतत् किम्? अयं च पुत्रैः दारैः भृत्यैः निकृतः तथा उज्झितः, …

(he) munisattama, jānataḥ api (me) ajñasya yathā (mamatvam) — etat kim? ayaṃ ca putraiḥ dāraiḥ bhṛtyaiḥ nikṛtaḥ tathā ujjhitaḥ, …

The first line closes the king’s own case (‘though knowing, as if not knowing’) and asks ‘what is this?’. The second opens the merchant’s case, continued in 1.43.

6Translation

Literal— (in me,) though knowing, as in one who does not know: what is this, O best of sages? And this man has been wronged by sons, wife and servants, and likewise abandoned,

Readablethough I know better, as if I did not. What is this, best of sages? And this man too has been wronged and cast off by his sons, his wife and his servants,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king states his own paradox in the words of the merchant: knowing, yet behaving like one who does not know (compare जानन्नपि, 1.32). Then he presents the merchant’s case beside his own.

1.43Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स्वजनेन च संत्यक्तस्तेषु हार्दी तथाप्यति।
एवमेष तथाहं च द्वावप्यत्यन्तदुःखितौ ॥ ४३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

स्वजनेन | च | संत्यक्तः | तेषु | हार्दी | तथा | अपि | अतिएवम् | एषः | तथा | अहम् | च | द्वौ | अपि | अत्यन्तदुःखितौ

3IAST

As recitedsvajanena ca saṃtyaktasteṣu hārdī tathāpyati |
evameṣa tathāhaṃ ca dvāvapyatyantaduḥkhitau ||

Separatedsvajanena | ca | saṃtyaktaḥ | teṣu | hārdī | tathā | api | ati
evam | eṣaḥ | tathā | aham | ca | dvau | api | atyantaduḥkhitau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
स्वजनेनsvajanenaby his own people, his kinm. instr. sg. of स्वजन (used collectively); agent
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
संत्यक्तःsaṃtyaktaḥdesertedसम् + √त्यज् (bhvādi, 1P) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. sg.
तेषुteṣutoward thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl.
हार्दीhārdīaffectionate, lovingm. nom. sg. of the adjective हार्दिन्; predicate of the merchant
तथाtathāso (with अपि: even so)indeclinable (adverb)
अपिapieven (with तथा: nevertheless)indeclinable (particle)
अतिatiexceedinglyindeclinable, here used as a free adverb
एवम्evamthusindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एषःeṣaḥthis mandemonstrative pronoun एतद्, m. nom. sg.
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb)
अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
द्वौdvautwonumeral द्वि, m. nom. du.
अपिapiboth (with द्वौ)indeclinable (particle of inclusion)
अत्यन्तदुःखितौatyantaduḥkhitaudeeply afflictedm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of द्वौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संत्यक्तस्तेषु saṃtyaktasteṣu → संत्यक्तः + तेषु
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • तथाप्यति tathāpyati → तथा + अपि + अति
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha; yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + a → ā (तथापि); then i before a becomes y (अप्यति).
  • एवमेष तथाहं evameṣa tathāhaṃ → एवम् + एषः + तथा + अहम्
    visarga sandhi (special rule for एषः); vowel sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    The visarga of एषः is dropped before the consonant t; तथा + अहम् → तथाहम्.
  • द्वावप्यत्यन्तदुःखितौ dvāvapyatyantaduḥkhitau → द्वौ + अपि + अत्यन्तदुःखितौ
    vowel sandhi (ayādi; yaṇ) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    au before a becomes āv (द्वावपि); i before a becomes y (अप्यत्यन्त-).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अत्यन्तदुःखितौ atyantaduḥkhitau — karmadhāraya (with an adverbial first member)
    Members: अत्यन्त + दुःखित · Vigraha: अत्यन्तं दुःखितौ atyantaṃ duḥkhitau
    Literally “endlessly pained”; in the verse: in the deepest distress
    Alternative: अत्यन्त is itself a prādi compound, अन्तम् अतिक्रान्तम् ‘going beyond the end’, used as an adverb.
  • स्वजनेन svajanena — karmadhāraya
    Members: स्व + जन · Vigraha: स्वः जनः, तेन svaḥ janaḥ, tena
    Literally “one’s own people”; in the verse: the merchant’s kinsfolk

C. Morphology

  • हार्दी hārdī — Masculine nominative of हार्दिन् (in-stem: the n drops and the i lengthens), the word used in the neuter (हार्दि) in 1.32 of the merchant’s mind.
  • अति ati — Normally a prefix (अति-), here a free adverb ‘exceedingly’, a usage found in epic and Purāṇic verse; it goes with हार्दी.
  • अत्यन्तदुःखितौ atyantaduḥkhitau — दुःखित ‘pained’ is दुःख + the taddhita -इत (‘in whom grief has arisen’), dual to match द्वौ.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अयं) स्वजनेन च संत्यक्तः, तथा अपि तेषु अति हार्दी। एवम् एषः तथा अहं च द्वौ अपि अत्यन्तदुःखितौ, …

(ayaṃ) svajanena ca saṃtyaktaḥ, tathā api teṣu ati hārdī | evam eṣaḥ tathā ahaṃ ca dvau api atyantaduḥkhitau, …

The first line finishes the merchant’s case: participle संत्यक्तः, then a concessive clause with हार्दी. The second sums up both men; the nominal predicate अत्यन्तदुःखितौ continues into 1.44.

6Translation

Literaland deserted by his kin; even so he is exceedingly affectionate toward them. Thus this man and likewise I — both of us are deeply afflicted,

Readableand deserted by his kin; yet he loves them dearly. So he and I alike are both in deep distress,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king sets the two cases side by side and draws the sum: both men are in great pain, and for the same reason. The next verse names it.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: तथाप्यति is split तथा अपि अति, with अति as a free adverb qualifying हार्दी. This is a point of interpretation, not of reading: the uncommon free adverb अति could also be taken loosely with the whole clause (‘even so, too much’); either way he loves them excessively.

1.44Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दृष्टदोषेऽपि विषये ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौ।
तत्किमेतन्महाभाग यन्मोहो ज्ञानिनोरपि ॥ ४४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दृष्टदोषे | अपि | विषये | ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौतत् | किम् | एतत् | महाभाग | यत् | मोहः | ज्ञानिनोः | अपि

3IAST

As reciteddṛṣṭadoṣe'pi viṣaye mamatvākṛṣṭamānasau |
tatkimetanmahābhāga yanmoho jñāninorapi ||

Separateddṛṣṭadoṣe | api | viṣaye | mamatvākṛṣṭamānasau
tat | kim | etat | mahābhāga | yat | mohaḥ | jñāninoḥ | api

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दृष्टदोषेdṛṣṭadoṣewhose fault has been seenm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies विषये
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)
विषयेviṣayein the object (of attachment), worldly thingsm. loc. sg. of विषय; locative of the object of feeling
ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौmamatvākṛṣṭamānasauwhose minds are dragged by the sense of ‘mine’m. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); agrees with द्वौ in 1.43
तत्tatso, thereforeindeclinable use of तद् (n.), a particle of inference
किम्kimwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, n. nom. sg.; predicate
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
महाभागmahābhāgaO blessed onem. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); addressed to the sage
यत्yatthatindeclinable use of यद् (n.), a conjunction explaining एतत्
मोहःmohaḥdelusion, bewildermentm. nom. sg. of मोह; subject (‘is’ understood)
ज्ञानिनोःjñāninoḥof (us) two who knowm. gen. du. of ज्ञानिन् (in-stem); possessor of मोहः
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • दृष्टदोषेऽपि dṛṣṭadoṣe'pi → दृष्टदोषे + अपि
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Final e before a: the a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • किमेतन्महाभाग kimetanmahābhāga → किम् + एतत् + महाभाग
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before m becomes n; the same in यन्मोहो.
  • मोहो ज्ञानिनोरपि moho jñāninorapi → मोहः + ज्ञानिनोः + अपि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced j becomes -o; after o the visarga of ज्ञानिनोः is sounded r before the vowel of अपि.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दृष्टदोषे dṛṣṭadoṣe — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दृष्ट + दोष · Vigraha: दृष्टः दोषः यस्मिन् सः, तस्मिन् dṛṣṭaḥ doṣaḥ yasmin saḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in which the fault has been seen”; in the verse: a thing whose flaw is already plain
  • ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौ mamatvākṛṣṭamānasau — bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (ममत्वाकृष्ट)
    Members: ममत्व + आकृष्ट + मानस · Vigraha: ममत्वेन आकृष्टं ममत्वाकृष्टम्; ममत्वाकृष्टं मानसं ययोः तौ mamatvena ākṛṣṭaṃ mamatvākṛṣṭam; mamatvākṛṣṭaṃ mānasaṃ yayoḥ tau
    Literally “the two whose minds are drawn by mine-ness”; in the verse: pulled by possessiveness against their judgement
  • महाभाग mahābhāga — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + भाग · Vigraha: महान् भागः यस्य सः, तत्सम्बोधने mahān bhāgaḥ yasya saḥ, tatsambodhane
    Literally “one whose portion is great”; in the verse: a respectful address (as in 1.3)

C. Morphology

  • ज्ञानिनोः jñāninoḥ — Genitive dual of ज्ञानिन् ‘one who has knowledge’ (the same form serves as locative dual). ‘Of us two who know’: the king includes himself and the merchant.
  • ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौ mamatvākṛṣṭamānasau — आकृष्ट is the past participle of आ + √कृष् ‘to drag’. Compare 1.12, where the king alone was ममत्वाकृष्टचेतनः; now both men are.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(आवां) दृष्टदोषे अपि विषये ममत्वाकृष्टमानसौ (स्वः)। तत्, महाभाग, एतत् किम्, यत् ज्ञानिनोः अपि (आवयोः) मोहः (भवति)?

(āvāṃ) dṛṣṭadoṣe api viṣaye mamatvākṛṣṭamānasau (svaḥ) | tat, mahābhāga, etat kim, yat jñāninoḥ api (āvayoḥ) mohaḥ (bhavati)?

The first line completes the description of the two (from 1.43); the second asks the question proper, with a यत् clause explaining एतत्.

6Translation

Literal— our minds dragged by possessiveness toward an object even though its fault has been seen. So what is this, O blessed one, that there is delusion even in (us) two who know?

Readableour minds dragged by the sense of ‘mine’ toward things whose faults we have plainly seen. Then what is this, blessed one — that even we, who know, are deluded?

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe question of the first carita, in its full form: why does delusion (मोह) persist in those who know? The king and the merchant both see the fault in what they cling to, and cling still.

Traditional interpretationThe sage’s answer turns on two words of this verse. He takes up विषय at once (1.47–48), and he names the source of the मोह of ‘even those who know’ as Mahāmāyā (1.55, ज्ञानिनामपि चेतांसि).

1.45Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ममास्य च भवत्येषा विवेकान्धस्य मूढता ॥ ४५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मम | अस्य | च | भवति | एषा | विवेकान्धस्य | मूढता

3IAST

As recitedmamāsya ca bhavatyeṣā vivekāndhasya mūḍhatā ||

Separatedmama | asya | ca | bhavati | eṣā | vivekāndhasya | mūḍhatā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ममmamaof me, in mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.
अस्यasyaof him (this man)demonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. gen. sg.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
भवतिbhavatiis, comes about√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be, become’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एषाeṣāthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies मूढता
विवेकान्धस्यvivekāndhasyablind in discernmentm. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); agrees with अस्य and is meant of both
मूढताmūḍhatābewilderment, follyf. nom. sg. of मूढता (abstract noun from मूढ ‘bewildered’); subject of भवति

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ममास्य mamāsya → मम + अस्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • भवत्येषा bhavatyeṣā → भवति + एषा
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before e becomes y.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विवेकान्धस्य vivekāndhasya — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विवेक + अन्ध · Vigraha: विवेके अन्धः, तस्य viveke andhaḥ, tasya
    Literally “blind in (the matter of) discernment”; in the verse: unable to judge rightly, though not unknowing

C. Morphology

  • विवेकान्धस्य vivekāndhasya — Singular, agreeing with the nearer genitive अस्य, but the sense applies to both speakers: ‘of me and of him, (each) blind in discernment’.
  • मूढता mūḍhatā — Abstract noun in -ता from मूढ, the past participle of √मुह् ‘to be bewildered’ — the same root as मोह (1.44).

D. Anvaya — prose order

मम अस्य च विवेकान्धस्य एषा मूढता भवति।

mama asya ca vivekāndhasya eṣā mūḍhatā bhavati |

A simple sentence completing the question of 1.44: subject मूढता with एषा, possessors मम अस्य च, verb भवति.

6Translation

Literal— this bewilderment comes to me and to him, blind in discernment.

Readable— this bewilderment, in him and in me, blind as we are to discernment.

‘as we are’ renders the singular विवेकान्धस्य applied to both.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king’s question ends. He distinguishes two things: they have knowledge (ज्ञान, 1.44) but lack discernment (विवेक), and so they are bewildered. The sage begins his answer in 1.47.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the king’s diagnosis is precise. Information is not the problem; the faculty that weighs it and lets go is. The rest of the chapter traces that failure to a power larger than the two men.

1.46Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ ४६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe sage (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel and joins it: ऋषिः उवाच → ऋषिरुवाच.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe sage said:

ReadableThe sage said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas, the sage whose hermitage Suratha found (1.10), begins his answer to the question of 1.40–45. From here the ṛṣi is the narrator of the Goddess’s deeds; his answer runs to the end of the text.

1.47Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ज्ञानमस्ति समस्तस्य जन्तोर्विषयगोचरे ॥ ४७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ज्ञानम् | अस्ति | समस्तस्य | जन्तोः | विषयगोचरे

3IAST

As recitedjñānamasti samastasya jantorviṣayagocare ||

Separatedjñānam | asti | samastasya | jantoḥ | viṣayagocare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ज्ञानम्jñānamknowledgen. nom. sg. of ज्ञान; subject of अस्ति
अस्तिastithere is, (every creature) has√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
समस्तस्यsamastasyaevery, alladjective समस्त, m. gen. sg.; qualifies जन्तोः
जन्तोःjantoḥof a creature, of a living beingm. gen. sg. of जन्तु (u-stem); genitive of possession with अस्ति
विषयगोचरेviṣayagocarein the range of sense-objectsm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ज्ञानमस्ति jñānamasti → ज्ञानम् + अस्ति
    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written joined.
  • जन्तोर्विषयगोचरे jantorviṣayagocare → जन्तोः + विषयगोचरे
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After o (not a/ā) the visarga is sounded r before the voiced v.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विषयगोचरे viṣayagocare — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विषय + गोचर · Vigraha: विषयाणां गोचरः, तस्मिन् viṣayāṇāṃ gocaraḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the pasture of sense-objects”; in the verse: in the sphere of what the senses grasp
    Alternative: Also read as a karmadhāraya, विषयः एव गोचरः ‘the field that is the object’; the sense is the same. गोचर is literally ‘where cattle graze’, then any range or province.

C. Morphology

  • अस्ति asti — With a genitive, ‘to be’ means ‘to have’: समस्तस्य जन्तोः ज्ञानम् अस्ति ‘every creature has knowledge’.
  • जन्तोः jantoḥ — Genitive singular of an u-stem: guṇa of the stem vowel + -ः (जन्तु → जन्तोः).

D. Anvaya — prose order

समस्तस्य जन्तोः विषयगोचरे ज्ञानम् अस्ति।

samastasya jantoḥ viṣayagocare jñānam asti |

A simple existential sentence: possessor in the genitive, sphere in the locative, subject ज्ञानम्, verb अस्ति.

6Translation

LiteralThere is knowledge of every creature in the range of sense-objects.

ReadableEvery living creature has knowledge in the realm of the senses.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas begins by granting the king’s premise in a limited way: yes, there is knowledge — but the knowledge every creature has, knowledge of sense-objects.

Traditional interpretationThe word picks up the king’s विषय (1.44). The text will distinguish this ordinary knowing from the knowledge that frees, which it calls the supreme विद्या and identifies with the Goddess herself (1.57).

1.48Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विषयश्च महाभाग याति चैवं पृथक् पृथक्।
दिवान्धाः प्राणिनः केचिद्रात्रावन्धास्तथापरे ॥ ४८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विषयः | च | महाभाग | याति | च | एवम् | पृथक् | पृथक्दिवा | अन्धाः | प्राणिनः | केचित् | रात्रौ | अन्धाः | तथा | अपरे

3IAST

As recitedviṣayaśca mahābhāga yāti caivaṃ pṛthak pṛthak |
divāndhāḥ prāṇinaḥ kecidrātrāvandhāstathāpare ||

Separatedviṣayaḥ | ca | mahābhāga | yāti | ca | evam | pṛthak | pṛthak
divā | andhāḥ | prāṇinaḥ | kecit | rātrau | andhāḥ | tathā | apare

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विषयःviṣayaḥthe sense-object, the world of objectsm. nom. sg. of विषय; subject of याति
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction, linking to 1.47)
महाभागmahābhāgaO blessed onem. voc. sg.; compound (bahuvrīhi, as in 1.44); addressed to the king
यातिyātigoes, reaches (each creature)√या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
पृथक्pṛthakseparatelyindeclinable (adverb)
पृथक्pṛthak(repeated:) each in its own wayindeclinable; repetition (āmreḍita) for distribution
दिवाdivāby dayindeclinable (adverb of time)
अन्धाःandhāḥblindadjective अन्ध, m. nom. pl.; predicate of प्राणिनः
प्राणिनःprāṇinaḥcreatures, living beingsm. nom. pl. of प्राणिन् (‘having breath’); subject
केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun किम् + चित्, m. nom. pl.; qualifies प्राणिनः
रात्रौrātrauat nightf. loc. sg. of रात्रि (i-stem); locative of time
अन्धाःandhāḥblindadjective अन्ध, m. nom. pl.; predicate of अपरे
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb)
अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विषयश्च viṣayaśca → विषयः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to the palatal c becomes ś.
  • चैवं caivaṃ → च + एवम्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai. The final m becomes anusvāra before p (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • दिवान्धाः divāndhāḥ → दिवा + अन्धाः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. Taken here as two words, to match रात्रौ अन्धाः in the same line; दिवान्ध can also be read as one word (it is a name of the owl).
  • केचिद्रात्रावन्धास्तथापरे kecidrātrāvandhāstathāpare → केचित् + रात्रौ + अन्धाः + तथा + अपरे
    consonant, vowel and visarga sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    t → d before the voiced r; au → āv before a; āḥ → ās before t; ā + a → ā.

C. Morphology

  • पृथक् pṛthak — Repeated (पृथक् पृथक्) to express distribution: ‘severally, each in its own way’.
  • अपरे apare — Nominative plural masculine in -e: अपर follows the pronominal declension (like सर्वे).
  • प्राणिनः prāṇinaḥ — Nominative plural of the in-stem प्राणिन् ‘one who has breath (प्राण)’, a general word for living beings.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) महाभाग, विषयः च एवं पृथक् पृथक् याति च। केचित् प्राणिनः दिवा अन्धाः, तथा अपरे रात्रौ अन्धाः।

(he) mahābhāga, viṣayaḥ ca evaṃ pṛthak pṛthak yāti ca | kecit prāṇinaḥ divā andhāḥ, tathā apare rātrau andhāḥ |

A general statement (the object reaches each being differently), then two examples in nominal sentences (the verb ‘are’ understood).

6Translation

LiteralAnd the sense-object, O blessed one, reaches (creatures) thus, severally. Some creatures are blind by day; likewise others are blind at night.

ReadableAnd the world of objects, blessed one, reaches each creature in its own way. Some creatures are blind by day, others blind by night,

‘creatures’ in the first sentence is supplied; the Sanskrit says only that the object ‘goes’ severally.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas illustrates: knowledge of objects is not uniform. What a creature can know depends on its nature — some see only by night, some only by day. The text names no species.

Traditional interpretationThe point prepares the next step: if knowing objects were what made a being wise, the animals would be wise too (1.49–50). The knowledge the king has is real but of this ordinary kind.

1.49Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

केचिद्दिवा तथा रात्रौ प्राणिनस्तुल्यदृष्टयः।
ज्ञानिनो मनुजाः सत्यं किं तु ते न हि केवलम् ॥ ४९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

केचित् | दिवा | तथा | रात्रौ | प्राणिनः | तुल्यदृष्टयःज्ञानिनः | मनुजाः | सत्यम् | किम् | तु | ते | न | हि | केवलम्

3IAST

As recitedkeciddivā tathā rātrau prāṇinastulyadṛṣṭayaḥ |
jñānino manujāḥ satyaṃ kiṃ tu te na hi kevalam ||

Separatedkecit | divā | tathā | rātrau | prāṇinaḥ | tulyadṛṣṭayaḥ
jñāninaḥ | manujāḥ | satyam | kim | tu | te | na | hi | kevalam

4Word by word

केचित्kecitsomeindefinite pronoun किम् + चित्, m. nom. pl.; qualifies प्राणिनः
दिवाdivāby dayindeclinable (adverb of time)
तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (adverb)
रात्रौrātrauat nightf. loc. sg. of रात्रि; locative of time
प्राणिनःprāṇinaḥcreaturesm. nom. pl. of प्राणिन्; subject
तुल्यदृष्टयःtulyadṛṣṭayaḥof equal sightm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of प्राणिनः
ज्ञानिनःjñāninaḥpossessed of knowledgem. nom. pl. of ज्ञानिन्; predicate of मनुजाः
मनुजाःmanujāḥhuman beingsm. nom. pl. of मनुज; compound (see Samāsa); subject
सत्यम्satyamtruly, it is trueindeclinable (adverbial n. acc. of सत्य)

तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; refers to मनुजाः

हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
केवलम्kevalamalone, the only onesindeclinable (adverbial n. acc. of केवल ‘only’)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • केचिद्दिवा keciddivā → केचित् + दिवा
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced d.
  • प्राणिनस्तुल्यदृष्टयः prāṇinastulyadṛṣṭayaḥ → प्राणिनः + तुल्यदृष्टयः
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • ज्ञानिनो मनुजाः jñānino manujāḥ → ज्ञानिनः + मनुजाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o.
  • सत्यं किं satyaṃ kiṃ → सत्यम् + किम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, also in किं तु.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • तुल्यदृष्टयः tulyadṛṣṭayaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: तुल्य + दृष्टि · Vigraha: तुल्या दृष्टिः येषां ते tulyā dṛṣṭiḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose sight is equal”; in the verse: who see alike by day and night
  • मनुजाः manujāḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa (ज from √जन् ‘to be born’)
    Members: मनु + ज · Vigraha: मनोः जाताः manoḥ jātāḥ
    Literally “born of Manu”; in the verse: human beings

C. Morphology

  • सत्यम् satyam — Accusative neuter of सत्य used as an adverb of concession: ‘true, (men) are knowers’ — answered by किं तु ‘but’.
  • केवलम् kevalam — Adverbial accusative of केवल ‘alone’: न हि केवलम् ‘not (they) alone, indeed’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

केचित् प्राणिनः दिवा तथा रात्रौ तुल्यदृष्टयः। मनुजाः सत्यं ज्ञानिनः, किं तु ते केवलं न हि (ज्ञानिनः)।

kecit prāṇinaḥ divā tathā rātrau tulyadṛṣṭayaḥ | manujāḥ satyaṃ jñāninaḥ, kiṃ tu te kevalaṃ na hi (jñāninaḥ) |

Three nominal clauses with ‘are’ understood: the third example of creatures, a concession about men, and its correction.

6Translation

LiteralSome creatures are of equal sight by day and likewise at night. Men are truly possessed of knowledge; but they indeed are not the only ones.

Readableand some see alike by day and by night. Human beings have knowledge, it is true — but they are not alone in having it.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas completes the list (blind by day, blind by night, seeing in both) and turns it on the king’s claim to be a ‘knower’: humans do know, but knowledge of this kind is shared with all creatures.

1.50Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

यतो हि ज्ञानिनः सर्वे पशुपक्षिमृगादयः।
ज्ञानं च तन्मनुष्याणां यत्तेषां मृगपक्षिणाम् ॥ ५० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यतः | हि | ज्ञानिनः | सर्वे | पशुपक्षिमृगादयःज्ञानम् | च | तत् | मनुष्याणाम् | यत् | तेषाम् | मृगपक्षिणाम्

3IAST

As recitedyato hi jñāninaḥ sarve paśupakṣimṛgādayaḥ |

हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यतो हि yato hi → यतः + हि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced h becomes -o.
  • तन्मनुष्याणां tanmanuṣyāṇāṃ → तत् + मनुष्याणाम्
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before m becomes n.
  • यत्तेषां yatteṣāṃ → यत् + तेषाम्
    no change (t before t) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    The final t stays t before the unvoiced t; the words are written together.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पशुपक्षिमृगादयः paśupakṣimṛgādayaḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (पशुपक्षिमृग)
    Members: पशु + पक्षिन् + मृग + आदि · Vigraha: पशवश्च पक्षिणश्च मृगाश्च पशुपक्षिमृगाः; पशुपक्षिमृगाः आदयः येषां ते paśavaśca pakṣiṇaśca mṛgāśca paśupakṣimṛgāḥ; paśupakṣimṛgāḥ ādayaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those of whom cattle, birds and wild beasts are the first”; in the verse: all the animals
  • मृगपक्षिणाम् mṛgapakṣiṇām — itaretara dvandva
    Members: मृग + पक्षिन् · Vigraha: मृगाश्च पक्षिणश्च, तेषाम् mṛgāśca pakṣiṇaśca, teṣām
    Literally “of beasts and birds”; in the verse: of the animals just named

C. Morphology

  • पशुपक्षिमृगादयः paśupakṣimṛgādayaḥ — पक्षिन् ‘bird’ drops its final n inside the compound (पक्षि-). पशु is the domestic animal, मृग the wild one.
  • सर्वे sarve — Pronominal nominative plural in -e (सर्व, like अपरे in 1.48).

D. Anvaya — prose order

यतः हि पशुपक्षिमृगादयः सर्वे ज्ञानिनः (सन्ति)। मनुष्याणां च तत् ज्ञानम् (अस्ति), यत् तेषां मृगपक्षिणाम् (अस्ति)।

yataḥ hi paśupakṣimṛgādayaḥ sarve jñāninaḥ (santi) | manuṣyāṇāṃ ca tat jñānam (asti), yat teṣāṃ mṛgapakṣiṇām (asti) |

A causal clause (यतः …) and a relative pair (तत् … यत्): men’s knowledge is the same as that which belongs to beasts and birds.

6Translation

LiteralFor all — cattle, birds, wild beasts and the rest — are possessed of knowledge. And men have that knowledge which those beasts and birds (have);

Readablefor every creature — cattle, birds, wild beasts and the rest — has knowledge. And the knowledge men have is the same as that of beasts and birds,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe argument reaches its point: as far as knowledge of objects goes, humans and animals are on a level. The king’s ‘knowing’ (1.42, 1.44) is of this kind, and so it cannot by itself free him.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the sage is not belittling the king. He is moving the question from how much one knows to what one is bound by — the subject of the next three verses.

1.51Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मनुष्याणां च यत्तेषां तुल्यमन्यत्तथोभयोः।
ज्ञानेऽपि सति पश्यैतान् पतङ्गाञ्छावचञ्चुषु ॥ ५१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मनुष्याणाम् | च | यत् | तेषाम् | तुल्यम् | अन्यत् | तथा | उभयोःज्ञाने | अपि | सति | पश्य | एतान् | पतङ्गान् | शावचञ्चुषु

3IAST

As recitedmanuṣyāṇāṃ ca yatteṣāṃ tulyamanyattathobhayoḥ |
jñāne'pi sati paśyaitān pataṅgāñchāvacañcuṣu ||

Separatedmanuṣyāṇām | ca | yat | teṣām | tulyam | anyat | tathā | ubhayoḥ
jñāne | api | sati | paśya | etān | pataṅgān | śāvacañcuṣu

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मनुष्याणाम्manuṣyāṇāmof human beingsm. gen. pl. of मनुष्य; genitive of possession
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
यत्yatwhat, that whichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg. (‘knowledge’ understood from 1.50)
तेषाम्teṣāmof them (the animals)pronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.
तुल्यम्tulyamequal, alikeadjective तुल्य, n. nom. sg.; predicate of अन्यत्
अन्यत्anyatthe rest, everything elsepronominal adjective अन्य, n. nom. sg. (pronominal ending -त्); subject
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb)
उभयोःubhayoḥfor bothpronominal adjective उभ, m. gen. du.; with तुल्यम्
ज्ञानेjñāneknowledgen. loc. sg. of ज्ञान; in the locative absolute with सति
अपिapieven thoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
सतिsatibeing (there)√अस् (adādi, 2P) + शतृ (present participle सत्), n. loc. sg.; locative absolute with ज्ञाने
पश्यpaśyalook at, see√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P; present stem पश्य-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एतान्etānthesedemonstrative pronoun एतद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies पतङ्गान्
पतङ्गान्pataṅgānbirdsm. acc. pl. of पतङ्ग (‘flying one’: a bird, also a winged insect); object of पश्य
शावचञ्चुषुśāvacañcuṣuinto the beaks of their youngf. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the goal, with कणमोक्ष- in 1.52

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तुल्यमन्यत्तथोभयोः tulyamanyattathobhayoḥ → तुल्यम् + अन्यत् + तथा + उभयोः
    consonant and vowel sandhi · खरि च (8.4.55); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    The m of तुल्यम् joins the vowel; t stays t before t; ā + u → o (तथोभयोः).
  • ज्ञानेऽपि jñāne'pi → ज्ञाने + अपि
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Final e before a: the a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • पश्यैतान् paśyaitān → पश्य + एतान्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.
  • पतङ्गाञ्छावचञ्चुषु pataṅgāñchāvacañcuṣu → पतङ्गान् + शावचञ्चुषु
    consonant sandhi (n before ś) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)
    Final n before ś becomes the palatal ñ, and the ś becomes ch. In the full derivation a t is optionally inserted after the n, made palatal, and then lost; the Gita Press writes the result ञ्छ (compare the variant note on 3.22). Without the change the words would be written पतङ्गान् शाव-.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शावचञ्चुषु śāvacañcuṣu — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शाव + चञ्चु · Vigraha: शावानां चञ्चवः, तासु śāvānāṃ cañcavaḥ, tāsu
    Literally “in the beaks of the young”; in the verse: into their nestlings’ open beaks

C. Morphology

  • सति sati — Locative of the present participle of √अस् ‘to be’: ज्ञाने सति ‘knowledge being (present)’, a locative absolute; with अपि it is concessive: ‘even though they have knowledge’.
  • पश्य paśya — √दृश् has no present of its own; the stem पश्य- serves for the present system. Imperative 2nd sg.: ‘look!’
  • अन्यत् anyat — Neuter nominative of the pronominal adjective अन्य, with -त् as in तत्, यत्: ‘the other (things)’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

मनुष्याणां च यत् (ज्ञानम्), (तत्) तेषाम् (अपि अस्ति); अन्यत् (च) तथा उभयोः तुल्यम्। ज्ञाने सति अपि, एतान् पतङ्गान् पश्य, शावचञ्चुषु (कणमोक्षादृतान् …)

manuṣyāṇāṃ ca yat (jñānam), (tat) teṣām (api asti); anyat (ca) tathā ubhayoḥ tulyam | jñāne sati api, etān pataṅgān paśya, śāvacañcuṣu (kaṇamokṣādṛtān …)

The first line: a relative clause with its correlative understood, then a nominal clause. The second line: a concessive locative absolute and the imperative पश्य with its object पतङ्गान्, described further in 1.52.

6Translation

LiteralAnd what is men’s is theirs; and the rest is likewise equal for both. Even though there is knowledge, look at these birds — into the beaks of their young

Readableand what men have, they have; in all else, too, the two are alike. Yet, for all their knowledge, look at these birds — into the beaks of their young

The sentence runs on into 1.52: the birds are intent on dropping grain into those beaks.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas closes the comparison (humans and animals are alike in knowledge and in the rest) and points to an example. The demonstrative एतान् with the imperative पश्य suggests birds within sight at the hermitage.

Traditional interpretationThe text does not say what ‘the rest’ (अन्यत्) is; the point is only that, in the ordinary life of the senses, humans and animals are on one level.

1.52Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कणमोक्षादृतान्मोहात् पीड्यमानानपि क्षुधा।
मानुषा मनुजव्याघ्र साभिलाषाः सुतान् प्रति ॥ ५२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कणमोक्षादृतान् | मोहात् | पीड्यमानान् | अपि | क्षुधामानुषाः | मनुजव्याघ्र | साभिलाषाः | सुतान् | प्रति

3IAST

As recitedkaṇamokṣādṛtānmohāt pīḍyamānānapi kṣudhā |
mānuṣā manujavyāghra sābhilāṣāḥ sutān prati ||

Separatedkaṇamokṣādṛtān | mohāt | pīḍyamānān | api | kṣudhā
mānuṣāḥ | manujavyāghra | sābhilāṣāḥ | sutān | prati

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कणमोक्षादृतान्kaṇamokṣādṛtānintent on letting fall grains (of food)m. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies पतङ्गान् in 1.51
मोहात्mohātout of delusionm. abl. sg. of मोह; ablative of cause
पीड्यमानान्pīḍyamānānbeing tormented√पीड् (curādi, 10 ‘to press, pain’) + यक् + शानच् (passive present participle), m. acc. pl.; qualifies पतङ्गान्
अपिapieven thoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
क्षुधाkṣudhāby hungerf. instr. sg. of क्षुध् (consonant stem); agent of पीड्यमानान्
मानुषाःmānuṣāḥhuman beingsm. nom. pl. of मानुष; subject
मनुजव्याघ्रmanujavyāghraO tiger among menm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); addressed to the king
साभिलाषाःsābhilāṣāḥfull of longingm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of मानुषाः
सुतान्sutānsons, childrenm. acc. pl. of सुत; governed by प्रति
प्रतिpratitowardindeclinable (postposition with the accusative)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कणमोक्षादृतान् kaṇamokṣādṛtān → कणमोक्ष + आदृतान्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • पीड्यमानानपि pīḍyamānānapi → पीड्यमानान् + अपि
    no change (n before a vowel) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply
    After the long ā the final n is not doubled; it simply joins the vowel.
  • मानुषा मनुजव्याघ्र mānuṣā manujavyāghra → मानुषाः + मनुजव्याघ्र
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga becomes y, and the y is dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कणमोक्षादृतान् kaṇamokṣādṛtān — saptamī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कणमोक्ष)
    Members: कण + मोक्ष + आदृत · Vigraha: कणानां मोक्षः कणमोक्षः; कणमोक्षे आदृताः, तान् kaṇānāṃ mokṣaḥ kaṇamokṣaḥ; kaṇamokṣe ādṛtāḥ, tān
    Literally “devoted to the release of grains”; in the verse: intent on dropping food into their young’s beaks
  • मनुजव्याघ्र manujavyāghra — karmadhāraya (upamita: the standard of comparison stands second)
    Members: मनुज + व्याघ्र · Vigraha: मनुजः व्याघ्रः इव, तत्सम्बोधने manujaḥ vyāghraḥ iva, tatsambodhane
    Literally “a man (who is) a tiger”; in the verse: a heroic address to the king
    Alternative: Also resolved as a saptamī tatpuruṣa, मनुजेषु व्याघ्रः ‘a tiger among men’; the sense is the same.
  • साभिलाषाः sābhilāṣāḥ — bahuvrīhi (with सह, shortened to स)
    Members: स (सह) + अभिलाष · Vigraha: अभिलाषेण सह वर्तन्ते ये ते abhilāṣeṇa saha vartante ye te
    Literally “those who are with longing”; in the verse: full of desire, yearning

C. Morphology

  • पीड्यमानान् pīḍyamānān — Passive present participle: passive stem पीड्य- (yak) + -मान. ‘Being tormented’, with the agent क्षुधा in the instrumental.
  • मोहात् mohāt — Ablative of cause: the birds act ‘from delusion’, not from calculation — the contrast with लोभात् (‘from greed’) in 1.53.
  • प्रति prati — As a postposition (karmapravacanīya) प्रति takes the accusative: सुतान् प्रति ‘toward (their) sons’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(पतङ्गान्) मोहात् कणमोक्षादृतान्, क्षुधा पीड्यमानान् अपि (पश्य)। (हे) मनुजव्याघ्र, मानुषाः सुतान् प्रति साभिलाषाः (भवन्ति), …

(pataṅgān) mohāt kaṇamokṣādṛtān, kṣudhā pīḍyamānān api (paśya) | (he) manujavyāghra, mānuṣāḥ sutān prati sābhilāṣāḥ (bhavanti), …

The first line completes the description of the birds (objects of पश्य in 1.51). The second begins a new statement about humans; its cause comes in 1.53.

6Translation

Literal— intent, out of delusion, on letting fall grains, though tormented by hunger. Human beings, O tiger among men, are full of longing toward their sons

Readablethey drop grain into them out of sheer delusion, though racked by hunger themselves. Men, tiger among men, long for sons

‘themselves’ makes plain that the hunger is the parent birds’ own. The second line runs on into 1.53.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe birds feed their young while starving themselves, and the sage calls the motive मोह, delusion. Then he turns to humans, who also long for their children.

Traditional interpretationThe two cases are not the same: the birds act from blind delusion, humans (1.53) from greed for a return. Both are forms of the attachment the king asked about, and both, Medhas says next, are the work of Mahāmāyā (1.54).

1.53Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

लोभात्प्रत्युपकाराय नन्वेतान् किं न पश्यसि।
तथापि ममतावर्त्ते मोहगर्ते निपातिताः ॥ ५३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

लोभात् | प्रत्युपकाराय | ननु | एतान् | किम् | न | पश्यसितथा | अपि | ममतावर्त्ते | मोहगर्ते | निपातिताः

3IAST

As recitedlobhātpratyupakārāya nanvetān kiṃ na paśyasi |
tathāpi mamatāvartte mohagarte nipātitāḥ ||

Separatedlobhāt | pratyupakārāya | nanu | etān | kim | na | paśyasi
tathā | api | mamatāvartte | mohagarte | nipātitāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
लोभात्lobhātout of greedm. abl. sg. of लोभ; ablative of cause
प्रत्युपकारायpratyupakārāyafor a return of the favourm. dat. sg. of प्रत्युपकार; dative of purpose
ननुnanusurelyindeclinable (particle introducing a question that expects assent)
एतान्etānthese (people)demonstrative pronoun एतद्, m. acc. pl.; object of पश्यसि
किम्kim(do you) …?indeclinable (interrogative particle)
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
पश्यसिpaśyasiyou see√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P; present stem पश्य-), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तथाtathāso (with अपि: even so)indeclinable (adverb)
अपिapieven (with तथा: nevertheless)indeclinable (particle)
ममतावर्त्तेmamatāvartteinto the whirlpool of ‘mine’m. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); locative of the goal
मोहगर्तेmohagarteinto the pit of delusionm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ममतावर्त्ते
निपातिताःnipātitāḥcast down, made to fallनि + √पत् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to fall’), causative (णिच्) + क्त (past passive participle), m. nom. pl.; ‘they’ (all these beings) understood

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • लोभात्प्रत्युपकाराय lobhātpratyupakārāya → लोभात् + प्रत्युपकाराय
    no change (t before an unvoiced stop) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    The final t stays unvoiced before p; the words are only written together. Inside प्रत्युपकार, प्रति + उप- gives प्रत्युप- (इको यणचि, 6.1.77).
  • नन्वेतान् nanvetān → ननु + एतान्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before e becomes v.
  • तथापि tathāpi → तथा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The same joining inside ममतावर्त्ते (ममता + आवर्त्त).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • ममतावर्त्ते mamatāvartte — karmadhāraya (a metaphor, rūpaka: the first member is identified with the second)
    Members: ममता + आवर्त्त · Vigraha: ममता एव आवर्त्तः, तस्मिन् mamatā eva āvarttaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the whirlpool that is mine-ness”; in the verse: caught in the circling pull of possessiveness
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, ममतायाः आवर्त्तः ‘the whirlpool of mine-ness’; the metaphorical reading fits the paired image मोहगर्ते.
  • मोहगर्ते mohagarte — karmadhāraya (rūpaka)
    Members: मोह + गर्त · Vigraha: मोहः एव गर्तः, तस्मिन् mohaḥ eva gartaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the pit that is delusion”; in the verse: fallen into bewilderment as into a pit
    Alternative: Also ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, मोहस्य गर्तः; as above.

C. Morphology

  • निपातिताः nipātitāḥ — Past passive participle of the causative of नि + √पत्: ‘made to fall, thrown down’. The causer is named in the next verse: by the power of Mahāmāyā (1.54).
  • ममतावर्त्ते mamatāvartte — ममता (मम + -ता) is the same idea as ममत्व in 1.41 and 1.44. आवर्त्त ‘whirlpool’ (आ + √वृत् ‘to turn’) is printed with a doubled t after r, a spelling the grammarians permit; it is the same word as आवर्त.
  • प्रत्युपकाराय pratyupakārāya — प्रत्युपकार ‘a service in return’ (प्रति + उपकार); the dative gives the purpose: parents cherish children ‘for the sake of a return’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(मानुषाः) लोभात् प्रत्युपकाराय (सुतान् प्रति साभिलाषाः)। ननु एतान् किं न पश्यसि? तथा अपि (एते) ममतावर्त्ते मोहगर्ते निपातिताः (महामायाप्रभावेण, 1.54)।

(mānuṣāḥ) lobhāt pratyupakārāya (sutān prati sābhilāṣāḥ) | nanu etān kiṃ na paśyasi? tathā api (ete) mamatāvartte mohagarte nipātitāḥ (mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa, 1.54) |

The first half finishes 1.52 (why men long for sons) and asks a rhetorical question. The second begins the sentence completed in 1.54: the beings are thrown into mamatā and moha by the power of Mahāmāyā.

6Translation

Literal— out of greed, for a return of the favour. Surely you see these? Even so, (they are) cast down into the whirlpool of mine-ness, into the pit of delusion,

Readableout of greed, hoping to be repaid. Do you not see them? And still they are thrown into the whirlpool of ‘mine’, into the pit of delusion —

The sentence is completed in 1.54: ‘by the power of Mahāmāyā, who keeps the world of saṃsāra in being’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningHuman love for children is mixed with self-interest: greed for a return. Yet humans and birds alike, knowing as they are, are thrown into the same whirlpool of possessiveness and pit of delusion. The next verse names who throws them.

Traditional interpretationThe two images answer the king’s words: his ममत्व (1.41, 1.44) and मोह (1.44) return as the whirlpool and the pit. The sage’s answer is that this is not a private failure of the king and the merchant but the working of Mahāmāyā (1.54–56), who is also the cause of release (1.57).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāThe Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess from the other side of the same pair: ममताहन्त्री (165), ‘destroyer of mine-ness’, and मोहनाशिनी (163), ‘remover of delusion’. Here in the Devī Māhātmya the same power that casts beings into mamatā and moha is the one that frees them when she is gracious (1.57).

1.54Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

महामायाप्रभावेण संसारस्थितिकारिणा।
तन्नात्र विस्मयः कार्यो योगनिद्रा जगत्पतेः ॥ ५४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

महामायाप्रभावेण | संसारस्थितिकारिणातत् | न | अत्र | विस्मयः | कार्यः | योगनिद्रा | जगत्पतेः

3IAST

As recitedmahāmāyāprabhāveṇa saṃsārasthitikāriṇā |
tannātra vismayaḥ kāryo yoganidrā jagatpateḥ ||

Separatedmahāmāyāprabhāveṇa | saṃsārasthitikāriṇā
tat | na | atra | vismayaḥ | kāryaḥ | yoganidrā | jagatpateḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
महामायाप्रभावेणmahāmāyāprabhāveṇaby the power of Mahāmāyām. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of निपातिताः in 1.53
संसारस्थितिकारिणाsaṃsārasthitikāriṇāwhich brings about the continuance of saṃsāram. instr. sg. of the in-stem संसारस्थितिकारिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महामायाप्रभावेण
तत्tatthereforepronoun तद्, n. acc. sg. used as an indeclinable of consequence (‘so, hence’)
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
अत्रatrahere, in this matterindeclinable (adverb of place, from इदम् + tral)
विस्मयःvismayaḥwonder, astonishmentm. nom. sg. of विस्मय (वि + √स्मि); subject of the gerundive
कार्यःkāryaḥto be felt; lit. ‘to be made’gerundive (kṛtya, ṇyat) of √कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do’), m. nom. sg.; agrees with विस्मयः
योगनिद्राyoganidrāthe Yoganidrā, the yogic sleepf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of एषा in 1.55
जगत्पतेःjagatpateḥof the lord of the world (Viṣṇu)m. gen. sg. of the i-stem जगत्पति; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तन्नात्र tannātra → तत् + न + अत्र
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    The final t of तत् becomes the nasal n before the nasal n of न (optional, but usual); then the a of न and the a of अत्र merge into ā.
  • विस्मयः कार्यो vismayaḥ kāryo → विस्मयः + कार्यः
    visarga sandhi (no change) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before an unvoiced k the visarga simply stays. Contrast the next junction.
  • कार्यो योगनिद्रा kāryo yoganidrā → कार्यः + योगनिद्रा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.
  • महामायाप्रभावेण mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa → महामाया + प्रभावेन
    internal retroflexion (ṇatva) inside a compound · the n of an ending becomes ṇ after r in the same word when no dental or palatal intervenes (ṇatva)
    The r of प्र- turns the instrumental ending -ena into -eṇa; the same happens in संसारस्थितिकारिणा (r of कारिन्).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महामायाप्रभावेण mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (महामाया is itself a karmadhāraya: महती चासौ माया च)
    Members: महामाया + प्रभाव · Vigraha: महामायायाः प्रभावः, तेन mahāmāyāyāḥ prabhāvaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the might of the Great Māyā”; in the verse: by the Goddess’s own power — the answer to the question of 1.44–45
  • संसारस्थितिकारिणा saṃsārasthitikāriṇā — upapada tatpuruṣa (the agent noun कारिन्, kṛt -in, after its object) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (संसारस्थिति)
    Members: संसार + स्थिति + कारिन् · Vigraha: संसारस्य स्थितिः संसारस्थितिः; तां करोति इति संसारस्थितिकारी, तेन saṃsārasya sthitiḥ saṃsārasthitiḥ; tāṃ karoti iti saṃsārasthitikārī, tena
    Literally “making the standing of saṃsāra”; in the verse: which keeps the round of birth and death going
  • योगनिद्रा yoganidrā — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: the first member identified with the second)
    Members: योग + निद्रा · Vigraha: योगः एव निद्रा yogaḥ eva nidrā
    Literally “the sleep that is yoga”; in the verse: Viṣṇu’s sleep between ages, which is not ignorance but withdrawn absorption
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, योगस्य निद्रा, ‘the sleep of yoga’; the sense is the same.
  • जगत्पतेः jagatpateḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + पति · Vigraha: जगतः पतिः, तस्य jagataḥ patiḥ, tasya
    Literally “of the lord of the world”; in the verse: of Viṣṇu (named as Hari in 1.55)

C. Morphology

  • कार्यः kāryaḥ — Gerundive of √कृ with ṇyat: कृ + य → कार्य (vṛddhi of the root vowel). With न it expresses what ought not to be: विस्मयः न कार्यः ‘wonder is not to be made’ = ‘do not wonder’.
  • संसारस्थितिकारिणा saṃsārasthitikāriṇā — Instrumental singular of an -in stem: कारिन् + आ → कारिणा (n → ṇ after r). It agrees with प्रभावेण, not with महामाया: it is the power that keeps saṃsāra in place.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ते) संसारस्थितिकारिणा महामायाप्रभावेण (ममतावर्ते मोहगर्ते निपातिताः)। तत् अत्र विस्मयः न कार्यः। (एषा) जगत्पतेः योगनिद्रा …

(te) saṃsārasthitikāriṇā mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa (mamatāvarte mohagarte nipātitāḥ) | tat atra vismayaḥ na kāryaḥ | (eṣā) jagatpateḥ yoganidrā …

The first line completes the sentence of 1.53 (‘they are thrown into the whirlpool of mine-ness … by the power of Mahāmāyā’). Then a short main clause with a gerundive (विस्मयः न कार्यः), and the opening of a new sentence that 1.55 completes (योगनिद्रा जगत्पतेः … एषा).

6Translation

Literal(They are thrown down) by the power of Mahāmāyā, which makes the continuance of saṃsāra. Therefore no wonder is to be felt in this: the yogic sleep of the lord of the world —

ReadableIt is the power of Mahāmāyā, who keeps the round of worldly existence turning, that casts them down. So there is nothing to wonder at here. She who is the yogic sleep of the Lord of the world —

The first line ends the sentence of 1.53; the last words begin the sentence completed in 1.55.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sage Medhas answers the king’s question (1.44–45: why are we attached when we see the fault?). Creatures who know better are still bound to their own, as the birds feeding their young show (1.51–53); the cause is the power of Mahāmāyā, so it is no cause for surprise. In the digital text a footnote asterisk of the printed book followed संसारस्थितिकारिणा; it has been removed (corrections.json, encoding).

Traditional interpretationThe answer turns a psychological question into a theological one: attachment is not merely a private failure but the working of the power that keeps the world in being. The same Goddess is introduced in the same breath as Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā, the name under which Brahmā will praise her (1.69–71).

1.55Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

महामाया हरेश्चैषा तया सम्मोह्यते जगत्।
ज्ञानिनामपि चेतांसि देवी भगवती हि सा ॥ ५५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

महामाया | हरेः | च | एषा | तया | सम्मोह्यते | जगत्ज्ञानिनाम् | अपि | चेतांसि | देवी | भगवती | हि | सा

3IAST

As recitedmahāmāyā hareścaiṣā tayā sammohyate jagat |
jñānināmapi cetāṃsi devī bhagavatī hi sā ||

Separatedmahāmāyā | hareḥ | ca | eṣā | tayā | sammohyate | jagat
jñāninām | api | cetāṃsi | devī | bhagavatī | hi | sā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
महामायाmahāmāyāthe Great Māyāf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of एषा
हरेःhareḥof Hari (Viṣṇu)m. gen. sg. of हरि (i-stem)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins हरेः महामाया to जगत्पतेः योगनिद्रा (1.54)
एषाeṣāshe, this onepronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
तयाtayāby herpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; agent of the passive सम्मोह्यते
सम्मोह्यतेsammohyateis deluded, is made to be bewilderedसम् + √मुह् (divādi 4P ‘to be bewildered’), causative stem मोहय-, karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
जगत्jagatthe worldn. nom. sg. of जगत्; subject of the passive
ज्ञानिनाम्jñānināmof the wise, of those who knowm. gen. pl. of ज्ञानिन् (ज्ञान + ini, ‘possessing knowledge’); depends on चेतांसि
अपिapievenindeclinable (emphatic particle)
चेतांसिcetāṃsithe mindsn. acc. pl. of चेतस् (s-stem); object of आकृष्य and प्रयच्छति in 1.56
देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; predicate of सा
भगवतीbhagavatīthe Blessed One, the Ladyf. nom. sg. of भगवती (भगवत् + ṅīp); qualifies देवी
हिhifor, indeedindeclinable (particle of reason and emphasis)
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हरेश्चैषा hareścaiṣā → हरेः + च + एषा
    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś (हरेश्च). Then a + e → ai (चैषा).
  • ज्ञानिनामपि jñānināmapi → ज्ञानिनाम् + अपि
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m becomes anusvāra only before a consonant; before the vowel of अपि it is simply written together with it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महामाया mahāmāyā — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + माया · Vigraha: महती चासौ माया च mahatī cāsau māyā ca
    Literally “the great illusion-power”; in the verse: the Goddess as the power that veils and binds
    Alternative: As a name it can also be taken as a bahuvrīhi, महती माया यस्याः सा ‘she whose māyā is great’; the karmadhāraya fits the identification with Yoganidrā better.

C. Morphology

  • सम्मोह्यते sammohyate — Passive of a causative: मोहय- loses its causative -ay- before the passive -ya- (मोह् + य + ते). The world is the subject; तया names the agent: ‘the world is deluded by her’.
  • चेतांसि cetāṃsi — Neuter plural of चेतस्: the s-stems insert a nasal and lengthen the vowel in the nominative and accusative plural (चेतांसि, like मनांसि). Here accusative, looking forward to the verbs of 1.56.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(या) जगत्पतेः योगनिद्रा, एषा हरेः महामाया च; तया जगत् सम्मोह्यते। सा हि भगवती देवी ज्ञानिनाम् अपि चेतांसि (बलात् आकृष्य मोहाय प्रयच्छति)।

(yā) jagatpateḥ yoganidrā, eṣā hareḥ mahāmāyā ca; tayā jagat sammohyate | sā hi bhagavatī devī jñāninām api cetāṃsi (balāt ākṛṣya mohāya prayacchati) |

The first line identifies the Yoganidrā of 1.54 with Hari’s Mahāmāyā and states her work with a passive (तया सम्मोह्यते जगत्). The second line begins a sentence completed in 1.56: चेतांसि is the object of आकृष्य … प्रयच्छति there, and देवी भगवती हि सा is a parenthesis giving the reason.

6Translation

Literal— and this one is the Mahāmāyā of Hari; by her the world is deluded. The minds even of the wise — for she is the Goddess, the Blessed One —

Readable— she is Hari’s Great Māyā, and by her the whole world is held in delusion. Even the minds of the wise — for she is the Goddess, the Blessed Lady —

The sentence is carried over from 1.54 and into 1.56, where the verbs governing चेतांसि stand.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas names the power: Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā is the same as Hari’s Mahāmāyā, and the whole world is deluded by her. Even the wise are not exempt — the very point the king and the merchant found puzzling about themselves (1.44).

Traditional interpretationThe verse joins two roles that the chapter keeps together: the sleep that holds Viṣṇu (the story of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, 1.66 onward) and the delusion that holds all beings. In the Śākta reading the god who sustains the world is himself subject to her, which is why his waking depends on her withdrawal (1.86–89).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has both names: 215, महामाया, and 339, विष्णुमाया. The Devī Māhātmya itself will later praise her as Viṣṇumāyā in the hymn of chapter 5; the two works remain distinct.

1.56Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

बलादाकृष्य मोहाय महामाया प्रयच्छति।
तया विसृज्यते विश्वं जगदेतच्चराचरम् ॥ ५६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

बलात् | आकृष्य | मोहाय | महामाया | प्रयच्छतितया | विसृज्यते | विश्वम् | जगत् | एतत् | चराचरम्

3IAST

As recitedbalādākṛṣya mohāya mahāmāyā prayacchati |
tayā visṛjyate viśvaṃ jagadetaccarācaram ||

Separatedbalāt | ākṛṣya | mohāya | mahāmāyā | prayacchati
tayā | visṛjyate | viśvam | jagat | etat | carācaram

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
बलात्balātby force, forciblyn. abl. sg. of बल, used adverbially
आकृष्यākṛṣyahaving dragged, having drawn awayabsolutive (lyap) of आ + √कृष् (bhvādi 1P ‘to drag, draw’)
मोहायmohāyato delusionm. dat. sg. of मोह; dative of the end to which (with प्रयच्छति ‘hands over to’)
महामायाmahāmāyāMahāmāyāf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 1.55); subject
प्रयच्छतिprayacchatihands over, deliversप्र + √दा (दाण्, bhvādi 1P ‘to give’, with the substitute stem यच्छ-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तयाtayāby herpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; agent of the passive
विसृज्यतेvisṛjyateis brought forth, is emitted (created)वि + √सृज् (tudādi 6P ‘to let go, emit’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
विश्वम्viśvamentire, the wholepronominal adjective विश्व, n. nom. sg.; qualifies जगत्
जगत्jagatworldn. nom. sg. of जगत्; subject of विसृज्यते
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies जगत्
चराचरम्carācarammoving and unmovingn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies जगत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • बलादाकृष्य balādākṛṣya → बलात् + आकृष्य
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.
  • जगदेतच्चराचरम् jagadetaccarācaram → जगत् + एतत् + चराचरम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing; palatal assimilation) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    The t of जगत् is voiced to d before the vowel of एतत्; the t of एतत् becomes c next to the c of चराचरम्.
  • विश्वं जगद् viśvaṃ jagad → विश्वम् + जगत्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • चराचरम् carācaram — dvandva (samāhāra, neuter singular), used here as an attribute of जगत्
    Members: चर + अचर · Vigraha: चरं च अचरं च caraṃ ca acaraṃ ca
    Literally “the moving and the unmoving”; in the verse: all beings, animate and inanimate
    Alternative: Inside the dvandva, अचर is a nañ tatpuruṣa (न चरम्). Some take चराचर as an itaretara dvandva (चराः च अचराः च); as an adjective of the neuter singular जगत् the samāhāra form fits.

C. Morphology

  • प्रयच्छति prayacchati — √दा in the sense ‘give’ (दाण्) takes the stem यच्छ- in the present system: प्र-यच्छ-ति. With the dative मोहाय: ‘hands (them) over to delusion’.
  • आकृष्य ākṛṣya — Absolutive with a prefix takes -य (lyap) instead of -त्वा: आ-कृष्-य ‘having dragged’. Its subject is the subject of the main verb, Mahāmāyā.
  • विसृज्यते visṛjyate — Passive present of वि + √सृज्: ‘is emitted’, the Purāṇic word for creation as outflow (visarga).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सा) महामाया ज्ञानिनाम् अपि चेतांसि बलात् आकृष्य मोहाय प्रयच्छति। तया एतत् विश्वं चराचरं जगत् विसृज्यते।

(sā) mahāmāyā jñāninām api cetāṃsi balāt ākṛṣya mohāya prayacchati | tayā etat viśvaṃ carācaraṃ jagat visṛjyate |

The first line completes the sentence begun in 1.55 (object चेतांसि); the second is a passive sentence with तया as agent.

6Translation

Literalhaving dragged (them) by force, Mahāmāyā hands (them) over to delusion. By her is emitted this entire world, moving and unmoving.

Readable— Mahāmāyā seizes them by force and gives them over to delusion. It is she who brings forth this whole world of the moving and the still.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo works of the one Goddess are named: she forcibly binds even the wise in delusion, and she emits the entire world. Delusion and creation are put side by side as acts of the same power.

Traditional interpretationThe force (बलात्) is stressed: knowledge alone does not free a person from attachment, which is why the next verse turns to her grace (प्रसन्ना). The tradition reads the sequence 1.55–58 as the theological core of Medhas’s teaching, to which the three caritas are the illustration.

1.57Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सैषा प्रसन्ना वरदा नृणां भवति मुक्तये।
सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता सनातनी ॥ ५७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सा | एषा | प्रसन्ना | वरदा | नृणाम् | भवति | मुक्तयेसा | विद्या | परमा | मुक्तेः | हेतुभूता | सनातनी

3IAST

As recitedsaiṣā prasannā varadā nṛṇāṃ bhavati muktaye |
sā vidyā paramā mukterhetubhūtā sanātanī ||

Separatedsā | eṣā | prasannā | varadā | nṛṇām | bhavati | muktaye
sā | vidyā | paramā | mukteḥ | hetubhūtā | sanātanī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
एषाeṣāthis very onepronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; with सा: ‘she, this same one’
प्रसन्नाprasannāwhen pleased, graciouspast participle (kta) of प्र + √सद् (bhvādi 1P ‘to settle, be clear’), f. nom. sg.; conditional sense (‘being pleased’)
वरदाvaradāboon-giving, bestowing favourf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with भवति
नृणाम्nṛṇāmto men, for human beingsm. gen. pl. of नृ (also written नॄणाम्); genitive of the beneficiary
भवतिbhavatibecomes√भू (bhvādi 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
मुक्तयेmuktayefor liberationf. dat. sg. of मुक्ति; dative of purpose
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
विद्याvidyāknowledgef. nom. sg. of विद्या; predicate
परमाparamāsupremef. nom. sg. of परम; qualifies विद्या
मुक्तेःmukteḥof liberationf. gen. sg. of मुक्ति; depends on हेतुभूता
हेतुभूताhetubhūtābeing the causef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of सा
सनातनीsanātanīeternalf. nom. sg. of सनातन (feminine in -ī); qualifies सा

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सैषा saiṣā → सा + एषा
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • नृणां भवति nṛṇāṃ bhavati → नृणाम् + भवति
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता mukterhetubhūtā → मुक्तेः + हेतुभूता
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after a vowel other than a/ā, before a voiced sound, appears as r.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वरदा varadā — upapada tatpuruṣa (√दा with the kṛt affix -a after its object)
    Members: वर + दा · Vigraha: वरं ददाति इति varaṃ dadāti iti
    Literally “giving a boon”; in the verse: gracious, granting what is sought
  • हेतुभूता hetubhūtā — karmadhāraya-type compound with भूत ‘being’ as final member
    Members: हेतु + भूत · Vigraha: हेतुः भूता hetuḥ bhūtā
    Literally “become the cause”; in the verse: who is the cause (of liberation)
    Alternative: Grammarians also class such -भूत compounds as supsupā (‘nominal with nominal’) compounds; the sense ‘serving as the cause’ is unaffected.

C. Morphology

  • प्रसन्ना prasannā — Past participle of प्र + √सद् with the -na variant of the kta affix (सद् + न → सन्न): ‘settled, clear’, hence ‘pleased, gracious’.
  • नृणाम् nṛṇām — Genitive plural of the ṛ-stem नृ: नृ + नाम् → नृणाम् (n → ṇ after ṛ). The long form नॄणाम् is equally correct.
  • सनातनी sanātanī — Feminine of सनातन ‘eternal’ formed with ṅīp (-ī) rather than -ā.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सा एषा प्रसन्ना (सती) नृणां मुक्तये वरदा भवति। सा परमा विद्या, सनातनी, मुक्तेः हेतुभूता (च)।

sā eṣā prasannā (satī) nṛṇāṃ muktaye varadā bhavati | sā paramā vidyā, sanātanī, mukteḥ hetubhūtā (ca) |

Two clauses: a verbal one (सा … वरदा भवति) with a dative of purpose, and a nominal one (सा विद्या परमा … हेतुभूता सनातनी) that 1.58 continues.

6Translation

LiteralShe, this one, when pleased, becomes boon-giving to men for liberation. She is the supreme knowledge, the eternal cause of liberation,

ReadableAnd when she is pleased, this same Goddess grants human beings the boon of liberation. She is the supreme knowledge, the eternal cause of freedom —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe power that binds (1.55–56) is the same power that frees. When she is gracious she grants liberation; as supreme knowledge she is its eternal cause.

Traditional interpretationThe Śākta tradition reads this verse with 1.58 as the key to the text: bondage and release both come from her, so release is sought by turning to her, not by escaping her. The rest of the Devī Māhātmya — and Suratha’s and Samādhi’s boons in chapter 13 — is the working out of प्रसन्ना वरदा.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names her 402, विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी, ‘whose form is both knowledge and ignorance’, and 331, वरदा. The pairing of binding and freeing in one Goddess is common Śākta ground; the texts are separate.

1.58Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

संसारबन्धहेतुश्च सैव सर्वेश्वरेश्वरी ॥ ५८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

संसारबन्धहेतुः | च | सा | एव | सर्वेश्वरेश्वरी

3IAST

As recitedsaṃsārabandhahetuśca saiva sarveśvareśvarī ||

Separatedsaṃsārabandhahetuḥ | ca | sā | eva | sarveśvareśvarī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
संसारबन्धहेतुःsaṃsārabandhahetuḥthe cause of the bondage of saṃsāram. nom. sg. of the u-stem हेतु; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of सा (the noun keeps its own masculine gender)
चcaand alsoindeclinable (conjunction); joins this predicate to मुक्तेः हेतुभूता (1.57)
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
एवevaalone, she and no otherindeclinable (restrictive particle)
सर्वेश्वरेश्वरीsarveśvareśvarīthe sovereign over all sovereignsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हेतुश्च hetuśca → हेतुः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → s → ś.
  • सैव saiva → सा + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • सर्वेश्वरेश्वरी sarveśvareśvarī → सर्व + ईश्वर + ईश्वरी
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa, twice) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e at both seams.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • संसारबन्धहेतुः saṃsārabandhahetuḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (संसारबन्ध, avadhāraṇa: ‘the bondage that is saṃsāra’)
    Members: संसारबन्ध + हेतु · Vigraha: संसार एव बन्धः संसारबन्धः; तस्य हेतुः saṃsāra eva bandhaḥ saṃsārabandhaḥ; tasya hetuḥ
    Literally “cause of the saṃsāra-bondage”; in the verse: she is the reason beings are bound to the round of births
    Alternative: संसारबन्ध can also be a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, संसारस्य बन्धः ‘the bondage of (belonging to) saṃsāra’; the meaning hardly differs.
  • सर्वेश्वरेश्वरी sarveśvareśvarī — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सर्वेश्वर + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: सर्वेषाम् ईश्वराः सर्वेश्वराः; तेषाम् ईश्वरी sarveṣām īśvarāḥ sarveśvarāḥ; teṣām īśvarī
    Literally “ruler of the rulers of all”; in the verse: sovereign even over the gods who rule the world
    Alternative: Or सर्वस्य ईश्वरः सर्वेश्वरः (the Lord of all, i.e. Viṣṇu or Śiva), तस्य ईश्वरी ‘ruler of the Lord of all’. Either way she stands above the highest god — which fits a verse spoken of Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā.

C. Morphology

  • संसारबन्धहेतुः saṃsārabandhahetuḥ — A feminine subject with a masculine predicate noun: हेतु is a masculine noun and does not change gender to agree, as in English ‘she is the cause’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सा एव संसारबन्धहेतुः च, सर्वेश्वरेश्वरी (च)।

sā eva saṃsārabandhahetuḥ ca, sarveśvareśvarī (ca) |

A nominal sentence continuing 1.57: सा (subject) with two predicates.

6Translation

Literaland the cause of the bondage of saṃsāra; she alone is the sovereign of the sovereigns of all.

Readableand the cause of bondage to worldly existence. She alone is sovereign over all who rule.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe teaching closes on its paradox: the cause of liberation (1.57) is also the cause of bondage, and she alone (सैव) is both. She is therefore above every ruling power, including the gods.

Traditional interpretationWith this half-verse Medhas’s answer to the king is complete, and the king’s next question (1.60–62) follows from it: who is this Goddess? The three caritas are told as the answer.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: what holds us and what frees us may not be two different things. The same capacity for attachment that binds a parent to a child (the birds of 1.51–52) is, turned rightly, the capacity for devotion.

राजोवाच ॥ ५९ ॥

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + u → o.

C. Morphology

  • राजा rājā — Nominative singular of the n-stem राजन्: the final n is dropped and the vowel lengthened (राजा).

D. Anvaya — prose order

राजा उवाच।

rājā uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe king said:

ReadableThe king said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningKing Suratha speaks to the sage Medhas, in his hermitage, having heard that a Goddess called Mahāmāyā both binds and frees (1.54–58). His question (1.60–62) opens the first carita’s story.

1.60Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भगवन् का हि सा देवी महामायेति यां भवान् ॥ ६० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भगवन् | का | हि | सा | देवी | महामाया | इति | याम् | भवान्

3IAST

As recitedbhagavan kā hi sā devī mahāmāyeti yāṃ bhavān ||

Separatedbhagavan | kā | hi | sā | devī | mahāmāyā | iti | yām | bhavān

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भगवन्bhagavanO blessed one, revered sirm. voc. sg. of भगवत्
काkāwhointerrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.
हिhiindeed, then (emphasising the question)indeclinable (emphatic particle)
साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
देवीdevīGoddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of the question
महामायाmahāmāyāMahāmāyāf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 1.55); the name quoted by इति
