याम्yāmwhomrelative pronoun यद्, f. acc. sg.; object of ब्रवीति (1.61)
भवान्bhavānyour honour, youhonorific pronoun भवत्, m. nom. sg.; subject of ब्रवीति (1.61)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • महामायेति mahāmāyeti → महामाया + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.
  • यां भवान् yāṃ bhavān → याम् + भवान्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • भवान् bhavān — The polite pronoun भवत् takes a third-person verb: भवान् … ब्रवीति (1.61) ‘your honour says’, not ‘you say’ in the second person.
  • भगवन् bhagavan — Vocative of the -vat stem भगवत्: भगवन् (nominative भगवान्).

D. Anvaya — prose order

भगवन्, यां भवान् महामाया इति (ब्रवीति), सा देवी का हि?

bhagavan, yāṃ bhavān mahāmāyā iti (bravīti), sā devī kā hi?

A direct question (का हि सा देवी) with a relative clause (यां भवान् … ब्रवीति) whose verb stands at the start of 1.61.

6Translation

LiteralO blessed one, who indeed is that Goddess whom your honour (calls) ‘Mahāmāyā’ —

ReadableRevered sir, who is this Goddess whom you call Mahāmāyā?

The verb of the relative clause, ब्रवीति, stands at the beginning of 1.61; the readable translation draws it back.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king takes up the name the sage used (1.55–56) and asks who she is. The question is the hinge of the whole text: everything that follows, through chapter 13, is Medhas’s answer.

Traditional interpretationSuratha’s questions in 1.60–62 — who she is, how she arose, what she does, her power, her nature, her origin — are traditionally read as the plan of the Devī Māhātmya: the three caritas show her arising and her deeds, and the hymns show her nature.

1.61Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ब्रवीति कथमुत्पन्ना सा कर्मास्याश्च किं द्विज।
यत्प्रभावा च सा देवी यत्स्वरूपा यदुद्भवा ॥ ६१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ब्रवीति | कथम् | उत्पन्ना | सा | कर्म | अस्याः | च | किम् | द्विजयत्प्रभावा | च | सा | देवी | यत्स्वरूपा | यदुद्भवा

3IAST

As recitedbravīti kathamutpannā sā karmāsyāśca kiṃ dvija |
yatprabhāvā ca sā devī yatsvarūpā yadudbhavā ||

Separatedbravīti | katham | utpannā | sā | karma | asyāḥ | ca | kim | dvija
yatprabhāvā | ca | sā | devī | yatsvarūpā | yadudbhavā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ब्रवीतिbravīti(you) call, speak of√ब्रू (adādi 2U ‘to speak’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; its subject is भवान् (1.60)
कथम्kathamhowindeclinable (interrogative adverb)
उत्पन्नाutpannāarisen, bornpast participle (kta) of उद् + √पद् (divādi 4Ā ‘to go’; with उद् ‘to arise’), f. nom. sg.; predicate of सा
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.
कर्मkarmadeed, workn. nom. sg. of कर्मन्
अस्याःasyāḥherpronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

द्विजdvijaO twice-born (brāhmaṇa)m. voc. sg. of द्विज
यत्प्रभावाyatprabhāvāof what powerf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of सा देवी (indirect question)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
देवीdevīGoddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject
यत्स्वरूपाyatsvarūpāof what naturef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
यदुद्भवाyadudbhavāof what origin, arising from whatf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कथमुत्पन्ना kathamutpannā → कथम् + उत्पन्ना
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m stays m and is written together with the following vowel.
  • कर्मास्याश्च karmāsyāśca → कर्म + अस्याः + च
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    a + a → ā; then the visarga of अस्याः before c becomes ś.
  • यदुद्भवा yadudbhavā → यत् + उद्भवा
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of the pronoun stem becomes d before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • यत्प्रभावा yatprabhāvā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: यद् + प्रभाव · Vigraha: यः प्रभावः यस्याः सा yaḥ prabhāvaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose power is which”; in the verse: what her power is
    Alternative: The relative pronoun here does the work of an indirect interrogative (‘of what power’); all three compounds are resumed by तत्सर्वम् in 1.62.
  • यत्स्वरूपा yatsvarūpā — bahuvrīhi (स्वरूप is itself a karmadhāraya: स्वम् रूपम्)
    Members: यद् + स्वरूप · Vigraha: यत् स्वरूपं यस्याः सा yat svarūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose own form is which”; in the verse: what her true nature is
  • यदुद्भवा yadudbhavā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: यद् + उद्भव · Vigraha: यतः उद्भवः यस्याः सा yataḥ udbhavaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose arising is from which”; in the verse: where she comes from
    Alternative: Or यः उद्भवः यस्याः ‘she whose origin is which’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • ब्रवीति bravīti — √ब्रू inserts ī before consonant-initial strong endings: ब्रव् + ई + ति. Third person because the subject is the honorific भवान् (1.60).
  • उत्पन्ना utpannā — Past participle of उद् + √पद् with the -na affix (पद् + न → पन्न; d + t → tt, ud-panna → utpanna). Used as a finite past: ‘how did she arise?’

D. Anvaya — prose order

(यां भवान् महामाया इति) ब्रवीति। द्विज, सा कथम् उत्पन्ना? अस्याः कर्म च किम्? सा देवी यत्प्रभावा, यत्स्वरूपा, यदुद्भवा च, …

(yāṃ bhavān mahāmāyā iti) bravīti | dvija, sā katham utpannā? asyāḥ karma ca kim? sā devī yatprabhāvā, yatsvarūpā, yadudbhavā ca, …

The first word completes the relative clause of 1.60. Two direct questions follow (कथम् … किम्), then three indirect questions in compound form, resumed by तत्सर्वम् in 1.62.

6Translation

Literal— calls (Mahāmāyā): how did she arise, and what is her deed, O twice-born? And of what power that Goddess is, of what nature, of what origin —

ReadableHow did she come to be, O brāhmaṇa, and what are her deeds? What is her power, what her true nature, and from where does she arise?

कर्म is singular (‘deed’); the readable version renders it as the collective ‘deeds’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king asks six things: how she arose, what she does, her power, her nature, her origin — and, in 1.62, he asks to hear it all. He addresses Medhas as द्विज, a brāhmaṇa.

Traditional interpretationThe sage’s answer (1.64–66) qualifies the first question at once: she is eternal, and is said to ‘arise’ only when she becomes manifest for the gods’ work. The questions about power and nature are answered less by definition than by the stories and the hymns.

1.62Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तत्सर्वं श्रोतुमिच्छामि त्वत्तो ब्रह्मविदां वर ॥ ६२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तत् | सर्वम् | श्रोतुम् | इच्छामि | त्वत्तः | ब्रह्मविदाम् | वर

3IAST

As recitedtatsarvaṃ śrotumicchāmi tvatto brahmavidāṃ vara ||

Separatedtat | sarvam | śrotum | icchāmi | tvattaḥ | brahmavidām | vara

इच्छामिicchāmiI wish√इष् (tudādi 6P ‘to wish’, present stem इच्छ-), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
त्वत्तःtvattaḥfrom youpronoun युष्मद् with the ablative suffix -tas (त्वत् + तसिल्); indeclinable ablative
ब्रह्मविदाम्brahmavidāmamong the knowers of Brahmanm. gen. pl. of ब्रह्मविद्; compound (see Samāsa); genitive of the group (nirdhāraṇa) with वर
वरvaraO bestm. voc. sg. of वर

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तत्सर्वं tatsarvaṃ → तत् + सर्वम्
    no change (unvoiced before unvoiced); anusvāra · खरि च (8.4.55); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The t stays unvoiced before s; the final m of सर्वम् becomes anusvāra before the consonant of श्रोतुम्.
  • श्रोतुमिच्छामि śrotumicchāmi → श्रोतुम् + इच्छामि
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m is written together with the following vowel.
  • त्वत्तो ब्रह्मविदां tvatto brahmavidāṃ → त्वत्तः + ब्रह्मविदाम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced b becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • ब्रह्मविदाम् brahmavidām — upapada tatpuruṣa (√विद् with the zero kṛt affix kvip after its object)
    Members: ब्रह्मन् + विद् · Vigraha: ब्रह्म विदन्ति इति ब्रह्मविदः, तेषाम् brahma vidanti iti brahmavidaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of the Brahman-knowers”; in the verse: among those who know the ultimate reality

C. Morphology

  • श्रोतुम् śrotum — Infinitive in -tum with guṇa of the root vowel (श्रु → श्रो-तुम्), complement of इच्छामि: ‘I wish to hear’.
  • त्वत्तः tvattaḥ — The -tas ablative of the pronoun: त्वत्तः ‘from you’ (equivalent to त्वत्), regular with verbs of hearing and learning.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ब्रह्मविदां वर, तत् सर्वं त्वत्तः श्रोतुम् इच्छामि।

brahmavidāṃ vara, tat sarvaṃ tvattaḥ śrotum icchāmi |

6Translation

LiteralAll that I wish to hear from you, O best of the knowers of Brahman.

ReadableAll this I long to hear from you, O best of those who know Brahman.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe king’s request closes: he wants to hear all of it from the sage, whom he honours as the best of Brahman-knowers. Medhas begins his answer at once (1.63).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the king who has lost his kingdom does not ask for it back here; he asks to understand. The text lets the desire to know come before the desire to regain.

1Original śloka

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ ६३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas answers King Suratha (with the merchant Samādhi listening) in his hermitage. His answer runs from the Goddess’s eternity (1.64) into the first story, the slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha.

1.64Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नित्यैव सा जगन्मूर्तिस्तया सर्वमिदं ततम् ॥ ६४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नित्या | एव | सा | जगन्मूर्तिः | तया | सर्वम् | इदम् | ततम्

3IAST

As recitednityaiva sā jaganmūrtistayā sarvamidaṃ tatam ||

Separatednityā | eva | sā | jaganmūrtiḥ | tayā | sarvam | idam | tatam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नित्याnityāeternalf. nom. sg. of नित्य; predicate of सा
एवevatruly, in fact (she is eternal — not born)indeclinable (restrictive particle)
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
जगन्मूर्तिःjaganmūrtiḥshe whose form is the worldf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second predicate
तयाtayāby herpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; agent with ततम्
सर्वम्sarvamallpronominal adjective सर्व, n. nom. sg.; qualifies इदम्
इदम्idamthispronoun इदम्, n. nom. sg.; subject
ततम्tatampervaded, spread throughpast participle (kta) of √तन् (tanādi 8U ‘to stretch, extend’), n. nom. sg.; predicate

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नित्यैव nityaiva → नित्या + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • जगन्मूर्तिस्तया jaganmūrtistayā → जगन्मूर्तिः + तया
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • जगन्मूर्ति jaganmūrti → जगत् + मूर्ति
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    t before the nasal m becomes the nasal n.
  • सर्वमिदं ततम् sarvamidaṃ tatam → सर्वम् + इदम् + ततम्
    final m before vowel and before consonant · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    सर्वम् keeps its m before the vowel of इदम्; इदम् shows anusvāra before the t of ततम्.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • जगन्मूर्तिः jaganmūrtiḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: जगत् + मूर्ति · Vigraha: जगत् मूर्तिः यस्याः सा jagat mūrtiḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose embodied form is the world”; in the verse: the world is her body
    Alternative: Also possible as a karmadhāraya, जगत् एव मूर्तिः ‘the world-form’, predicated of her; the bahuvrīhi suits a feminine subject described by a compound.

C. Morphology

  • ततम् tatam — Past participle of √तन्: the root-final nasal is dropped before -ta (तन् + त → तत). ‘Stretched out, pervaded’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सा नित्या एव, जगन्मूर्तिः; तया इदं सर्वं ततम्।

sā nityā eva, jaganmūrtiḥ; tayā idaṃ sarvaṃ tatam |

Two nominal sentences: a description of her (नित्या एव … जगन्मूर्तिः) and a passive statement with a participle as predicate (तया … ततम्).

6Translation

LiteralShe is eternal indeed, having the world as her form; by her all this is pervaded.

ReadableShe is eternal; the world is her very form, and by her all this is pervaded.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sage’s first answer corrects the king’s first question (‘how did she arise?’, 1.61): she is eternal and has no birth. The world is her form, and all of it is filled by her.

Traditional interpretationThe phrase तया सर्वमिदं ततम् echoes the Bhagavad Gītā’s language for the pervading Absolute (येन सर्वमिदं ततम्, 2.17; मया ततमिदं सर्वम्, 9.4). The Devī Māhātmya applies to the Goddess what the Gītā says of the imperishable and of Kṛṣṇa.

1.65Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तथापि तत्समुत्पत्तिर्बहुधा श्रूयतां मम।
देवानां कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थमाविर्भवति सा यदा ॥ ६५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तथा | अपि | तत्समुत्पत्तिः | बहुधा | श्रूयताम् | ममदेवानाम् | कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थम् | आविर्भवति | सा | यदा

3IAST

As recitedtathāpi tatsamutpattirbahudhā śrūyatāṃ mama |
devānāṃ kāryasiddhyarthamāvirbhavati sā yadā ||

Separatedtathā | api | tatsamutpattiḥ | bahudhā | śrūyatām | mama
devānām | kāryasiddhyartham | āvirbhavati | sā | yadā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तथाtathāso, thusindeclinable (adverb of manner); with अपि: ‘even so, nevertheless’
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle)
तत्समुत्पत्तिःtatsamutpattiḥher arisingf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of श्रूयताम्
बहुधाbahudhāin many ways, many timesindeclinable (adverb with the suffix -dhā)
श्रूयताम्śrūyatāmlet it be heard√श्रु (bhvādi 1P ‘to hear’ in the Dhātupāṭha, with the class-5 present शृणोति), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
ममmamafrom mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; the speaker heard
देवानाम्devānāmof the godsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on the first member of कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थम्
कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थम्kāryasiddhyarthamfor the accomplishment of the purposecompound (see Samāsa), n. acc. sg. used adverbially
आविर्भवतिāvirbhavatibecomes manifest, appearsआविस् (gati) + √भू (bhvādi 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
यदाyadāwhenindeclinable (relative adverb of time); correlative of तदा in 1.66

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तथापि tathāpi → तथा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • तत्समुत्पत्तिर्बहुधा tatsamutpattirbahudhā → तत्समुत्पत्तिः + बहुधा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before the voiced b, appears as r.
  • कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थमाविर्भवति kāryasiddhyarthamāvirbhavati → कार्यसिद्धि + अर्थम् + आविर्भवति
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ); final m before a vowel · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    The i of सिद्धि becomes y before अर्थ (सिद्ध्यर्थ); the final m of the compound is written together with the vowel of आविर्भवति.
  • आविर्भवति āvirbhavati → आविस् + भवति
    visarga (s) sandhi before a voiced consonant · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of the gati आविस् becomes r before the voiced bh.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • तत्समुत्पत्तिः tatsamutpattiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: तद् + समुत्पत्ति · Vigraha: तस्याः समुत्पत्तिः tasyāḥ samutpattiḥ
    Literally “the arising of her”; in the verse: the occasions of her appearing
    Alternative: The pronoun stem तद् serves for every gender inside a compound; here it stands for the feminine तस्याः.
  • कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थम् kāryasiddhyartham — caturthī tatpuruṣa (with अर्थ, always compounded; used adverbially in the accusative)
    Members: कार्यसिद्धि + अर्थ · Vigraha: कार्यसिद्धये इदम् kāryasiddhaye idam
    Literally “for the sake of task-accomplishment”; in the verse: to bring about what the gods need done
    Alternative: The first member कार्यसिद्धि is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, कार्यस्य सिद्धिः; देवानाम् outside the compound depends on कार्य inside it.

C. Morphology

  • श्रूयताम् śrūyatām — Passive imperative: श्रू-य-ताम् (the root vowel lengthened before the passive -ya). The courteous Sanskrit way of saying ‘listen’: ‘let (it) be heard (from me)’.
  • आविर्भवति āvirbhavati — आविस् ‘openly, visibly’ is a gati that combines with √भू and √कृ: आविर्भवति ‘becomes visible, appears’. It describes manifestation, not birth — the key to the sage’s answer.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तथा अपि मम (मत्तः) तत्समुत्पत्तिः बहुधा श्रूयताम्। यदा सा देवानां कार्यसिद्ध्यर्थम् आविर्भवति, (तदा …)

tathā api mama (mattaḥ) tatsamutpattiḥ bahudhā śrūyatām | yadā sā devānāṃ kāryasiddhyartham āvirbhavati, (tadā …)

A passive imperative sentence, then a यदा clause whose main clause (तदा …) is in 1.66.

6Translation

LiteralEven so, let her arising, in many ways, be heard from me. When she becomes manifest for the accomplishment of the purpose of the gods —

ReadableEven so, hear from me how she comes forth, again and again. When she becomes manifest to accomplish the purpose of the gods,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAlthough she is eternal, she has many ‘arisings’, and the sage will tell them. Each is a manifestation (आविर्भाव) for the gods’ need — the pattern of all three caritas.

Traditional interpretationThe three caritas — the slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, of Mahiṣa, and of Śumbha and Niśumbha — are traditionally read as three such manifestations. The Gita Press pāṭha assigns them the presiding forms Mahākālī, Mahālakṣmī and Mahāsarasvatī; that assignment is liturgical tradition, not stated in this verse.

1.66Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

उत्पन्नेति तदा लोके सा नित्याप्यभिधीयते।
योगनिद्रां यदा विष्णुर्जगत्येकार्णवीकृते ॥ ६६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

उत्पन्ना | इति | तदा | लोके | सा | नित्या | अपि | अभिधीयतेयोगनिद्राम् | यदा | विष्णुः | जगति | एकार्णवीकृते

3IAST

As recitedutpanneti tadā loke sā nityāpyabhidhīyate |
yoganidrāṃ yadā viṣṇurjagatyekārṇavīkṛte ||

Separatedutpannā | iti | tadā | loke | sā | nityā | api | abhidhīyate
yoganidrām | yadā | viṣṇuḥ | jagati | ekārṇavīkṛte

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
उत्पन्नाutpannāborn, arisenpast participle (kta) of उद् + √पद्, f. nom. sg.; the word quoted by इति
इतिiti(as) ‘…’indeclinable (quotation marker)
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (correlative adverb of time, answering यदा in 1.65)
लोकेlokein the world, among peoplem. loc. sg. of लोक
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
नित्याnityāeternalf. nom. sg. of नित्य; concessive with अपि
अपिapithoughindeclinable (concessive particle)
अभिधीयतेabhidhīyateis called, is spoken ofअभि + √धा (juhotyādi 3U ‘to place’; with अभि ‘to name’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
योगनिद्राम्yoganidrāmthe Yoganidrā, yogic sleepf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 1.54); object of अभजत् (1.67)

एकार्णवीकृतेekārṇavīkṛtehaving been made a single oceanpast participle (kta) of √कृ with the cvi formation एकार्णवी-, n. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); locative absolute

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • उत्पन्नेति utpanneti → उत्पन्ना + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.
  • नित्याप्यभिधीयते nityāpyabhidhīyate → नित्या + अपि + अभिधीयते
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha; yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + a → ā; then the i of अपि becomes y before the a of अभिधीयते.
  • विष्णुर्जगत्येकार्णवीकृते viṣṇurjagatyekārṇavīkṛte → विष्णुः + जगति + एकार्णवीकृते
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Visarga after u before the voiced j appears as r; the i of जगति becomes y before e.
  • योगनिद्रां यदा yoganidrāṃ yadā → योगनिद्राम् + यदा
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • एकार्णवीकृते ekārṇavīkṛte — gati tatpuruṣa with a cvi formation (a thing made into what it was not: the final a of एकार्णव becomes ī)
    Members: एकार्णव + (cvi) + कृत · Vigraha: अनेकार्णवम् एकार्णवं कृतम् इति एकार्णवीकृतम्, तस्मिन् anekārṇavam ekārṇavaṃ kṛtam iti ekārṇavīkṛtam, tasmin
    Literally “made into one ocean”; in the verse: when the whole world was flooded into a single sea at the dissolution
    Alternative: The inner member एकार्णव is a karmadhāraya, एकः च असौ अर्णवः च ‘one ocean’, with savarṇa-dīrgha (एक + अर्णव).

C. Morphology

  • एकार्णवीकृते ekārṇavīkṛte — cvi construction: a noun + ī + a form of √कृ/√भू means ‘make (become) what was not before’ — एकार्णवी-कृत ‘made one ocean’. In the locative with जगति it is a locative absolute: ‘when the world had been made one ocean’.
  • अभिधीयते abhidhīyate — Passive of अभि + √धा: the root vowel becomes ī before the passive -ya (धी-य-ते). ‘Is named’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(यदा सा … आविर्भवति,) तदा सा नित्या अपि लोके उत्पन्ना इति अभिधीयते। (कल्पान्ते) जगति एकार्णवीकृते यदा विष्णुः योगनिद्राम् (अभजत्), …

(yadā sā … āvirbhavati,) tadā sā nityā api loke utpannā iti abhidhīyate | (kalpānte) jagati ekārṇavīkṛte yadā viṣṇuḥ yoganidrām (abhajat), …

The first line is the main clause answering यदा in 1.65 (तदा … अभिधीयते). The second line opens a new यदा clause whose verb अभजत् and main clause (तदा …) are in 1.67.

6Translation

Literal— then in the world she, though eternal, is called ‘born’. When Viṣṇu, the world having been made one ocean, (resorted to) Yoganidrā —

Readablethen, though eternal, she is said in the world to be ‘born’. At the end of an age, when the world had become a single ocean and Viṣṇu entered his yogic sleep,

‘At the end of an age’ is drawn from कल्पान्ते in 1.67, and ‘entered’ renders अभजत् there.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe answer to ‘how did she arise?’ is completed: her ‘birth’ is a way of speaking about her appearing. The second line begins the first story, set at the dissolution, when the world is one ocean and Viṣṇu lies in yogic sleep.

Traditional interpretationThe distinction between eternal being and occasional manifestation governs the whole text: the Goddess of the three caritas is not three goddesses but one, appearing as the need arises. The idea runs parallel to Bhagavad Gītā 4.6–8 (अजोऽपि सन्नव्ययात्मा …), where the Lord, though unborn, comes into being from age to age for the protection of the good.

1.67Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

आस्तीर्य शेषमभजत्कल्पान्ते भगवान् प्रभुः।
तदा द्वावसुरौ घोरौ विख्यातौ मधुकैटभौ ॥ ६७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

आस्तीर्य | शेषम् | अभजत् | कल्पान्ते | भगवान् | प्रभुःतदा | द्वौ | असुरौ | घोरौ | विख्यातौ | मधुकैटभौ

3IAST

As recitedāstīrya śeṣamabhajatkalpānte bhagavān prabhuḥ |
tadā dvāvasurau ghorau vikhyātau madhukaiṭabhau ||

Separatedāstīrya | śeṣam | abhajat | kalpānte | bhagavān | prabhuḥ
tadā | dvau | asurau | ghorau | vikhyātau | madhukaiṭabhau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
आस्तीर्यāstīryahaving spread out (as his bed)absolutive (lyap) of आ + √स्तॄ (kryādi 9U ‘to spread, strew’)
शेषम्śeṣamŚeṣa, the serpentm. acc. sg. of शेष; object of आस्तीर्य
अभजत्abhajatresorted to, entered√भज् (bhvādi 1U ‘to share, resort to’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; object योगनिद्राम् (1.66)
कल्पान्तेkalpānteat the end of the kalpa (world-age)m. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
भगवान्bhagavānthe Blessed Onem. nom. sg. of भगवत्; qualifies विष्णुः (1.66)
प्रभुःprabhuḥthe Lordm. nom. sg. of प्रभु (u-stem); qualifies विष्णुः
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (correlative adverb of time, answering यदा in 1.66)
द्वौdvautwonumeral द्वि, m. nom. du.
असुरौasurauasuras, demonsm. nom. du. of असुर; subject (verb उद्यतौ in 1.68)
घोरौghorauterriblem. nom. du. of घोर; qualifies असुरौ
विख्यातौvikhyātaurenownedpast participle (kta) of वि + √ख्या (adādi 2P ‘to be known’), m. nom. du.
मधुकैटभौmadhukaiṭabhauMadhu and Kaiṭabham. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to असुरौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शेषमभजत्कल्पान्ते śeṣamabhajatkalpānte → शेषम् + अभजत् + कल्पान्ते
    final m before a vowel; t before an unvoiced stop · खरि च (8.4.55)
    शेषम् keeps its m before the vowel of अभजत्; the t of अभजत् stays unvoiced before k and is written together.
  • कल्पान्ते kalpānte → कल्प + अन्ते
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • द्वावसुरौ dvāvasurau → द्वौ + असुरौ
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv: dvāv-asurau.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कल्पान्ते kalpānte — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: कल्प + अन्त · Vigraha: कल्पस्य अन्तः, तस्मिन् kalpasya antaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “at the end of the kalpa”; in the verse: at the dissolution, when one day of Brahmā closes
  • मधुकैटभौ madhukaiṭabhau — itaretara dvandva (dual)
    Members: मधु + कैटभ · Vigraha: मधुः च कैटभः च madhuḥ ca kaiṭabhaḥ ca
    Literally “Madhu and Kaiṭabha”; in the verse: the two asuras of the first carita

C. Morphology

  • आस्तीर्य āstīrya — Absolutive of आ + √स्तॄ: the root ṝ becomes īr before -ya (स्तॄ → स्तीर्). ‘Having spread out Śeṣa’ — the serpent as Viṣṇu’s couch.
  • अभजत् abhajat — Imperfect of √भज्: augment a- + bhaja- + t. Its object योगनिद्राम् stands in the previous verse: ‘he took to yogic sleep’.
  • द्वौ dvau — The numeral द्वि is declined only in the dual: m. nom. द्वौ.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(यदा) भगवान् प्रभुः विष्णुः कल्पान्ते (जगति एकार्णवीकृते) शेषम् आस्तीर्य योगनिद्राम् अभजत्, तदा घोरौ विख्यातौ मधुकैटभौ द्वौ असुरौ (विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ ब्रह्माणं हन्तुम् उद्यतौ)।

(yadā) bhagavān prabhuḥ viṣṇuḥ kalpānte (jagati ekārṇavīkṛte) śeṣam āstīrya yoganidrām abhajat, tadā ghorau vikhyātau madhukaiṭabhau dvau asurau (viṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau brahmāṇaṃ hantum udyatau) |

The first line ends the यदा clause begun in 1.66; the second begins the main clause (तदा …), whose predicate उद्यतौ is in 1.68.

6Translation

Literal— having spread out Śeṣa, the Blessed Lord resorted (to it) at the end of the kalpa; then two terrible asuras, the renowned Madhu and Kaiṭabha,

Readablespreading out the serpent Śeṣa as his couch — then two dreadful asuras, the notorious Madhu and Kaiṭabha,

The readable version keeps the verse’s order across 1.66–68; ‘the Blessed Lord’ and ‘at the end of the kalpa’ are rendered in 1.66’s readable line.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe scene is set: at the dissolution, Viṣṇu lies on the serpent Śeṣa in yogic sleep on the single ocean. Two asuras, Madhu and Kaiṭabha, appear.

Traditional interpretationŚeṣa (‘the remainder’) is the serpent who remains when all else is dissolved. The first carita is traditionally presided over by Mahākālī in the Gita Press pāṭha; that is liturgical tradition, not stated in the text.

1.68Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ हन्तुं ब्रह्माणमुद्यतौ।
स नाभिकमले विष्णोः स्थितो ब्रह्मा प्रजापतिः ॥ ६८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ | हन्तुम् | ब्रह्माणम् | उद्यतौसः | नाभिकमले | विष्णोः | स्थितः | ब्रह्मा | प्रजापतिः

3IAST

As recitedviṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau hantuṃ brahmāṇamudyatau |
sa nābhikamale viṣṇoḥ sthito brahmā prajāpatiḥ ||

Separatedviṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau | hantum | brahmāṇam | udyatau
saḥ | nābhikamale | viṣṇoḥ | sthitaḥ | brahmā | prajāpatiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौviṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtausprung from the wax of Viṣṇu’s earsm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies असुरौ (1.67)
हन्तुम्hantumto killinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’)
ब्रह्माणम्brahmāṇamBrahmām. acc. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; object of हन्तुम्
उद्यतौudyatauwere intent, set aboutpast participle (kta) of उद् + √यम् (bhvādi 1P ‘to hold up’; with उद् ‘to strive’), m. nom. du.; predicate
सःsaḥhe, thatpronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies ब्रह्मा
नाभिकमलेnābhikamaleon the navel-lotusn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
विष्णोःviṣṇoḥof Viṣṇum. gen. sg. of विष्णु; depends on नाभि within नाभिकमले
स्थितःsthitaḥseated, stationedpast participle (kta) of √स्था (bhvādi 1P ‘to stand’), m. nom. sg.
ब्रह्माbrahmāBrahmām. nom. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; subject of तुष्टाव (1.69)
प्रजापतिःprajāpatiḥthe lord of creaturesm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ब्रह्मा

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हन्तुं ब्रह्माणमुद्यतौ hantuṃ brahmāṇamudyatau → हन्तुम् + ब्रह्माणम् + उद्यतौ
    final m before consonant and before vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    हन्तुम् shows anusvāra before b; ब्रह्माणम् keeps its m before the vowel of उद्यतौ.
  • स नाभिकमले sa nābhikamale → सः + नाभिकमले
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant.
  • स्थितो ब्रह्मा sthito brahmā → स्थितः + ब्रह्मा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced b becomes -o.
  • विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ viṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau → विष्णुकर्णमल + उद्भूतौ
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ viṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau — pañcamī tatpuruṣa containing two ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (विष्णुकर्ण, विष्णुकर्णमल)
    Members: विष्णुकर्णमल + उद्भूत · Vigraha: विष्णोः कर्णौ विष्णुकर्णौ; तयोः मलम् विष्णुकर्णमलम्; तस्मात् उद्भूतौ viṣṇoḥ karṇau viṣṇukarṇau; tayoḥ malam viṣṇukarṇamalam; tasmāt udbhūtau
    Literally “arisen from the dirt of Viṣṇu’s ears”; in the verse: born of the wax in the sleeping Viṣṇu’s ears
  • नाभिकमले nābhikamale — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: नाभि + कमल · Vigraha: नाभेः कमलम्, तस्मिन् nābheḥ kamalam, tasmin
    Literally “on the lotus of the navel”; in the verse: on the lotus that grows from Viṣṇu’s navel
    Alternative: Or saptamī tatpuruṣa, नाभौ कमलम् ‘the lotus at the navel’; the sense is the same. विष्णोः outside the compound depends on its first member.
  • प्रजापतिः prajāpatiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: प्रजा + पति · Vigraha: प्रजानां पतिः prajānāṃ patiḥ
    Literally “lord of offspring”; in the verse: the creator, lord of all creatures

C. Morphology

  • उद्यतौ udyatau — Past participle of उद् + √यम्: the nasal is dropped before -ta (यम् + त → यत). Used as a finite verb: ‘the two were ready (to kill)’, with the infinitive हन्तुम्.
  • ब्रह्माणम् brahmāṇam — Accusative of the masculine n-stem ब्रह्मन् (the god Brahmā): strong stem ब्रह्माण्-. The neuter ब्रह्मन् (Brahman) is a different word.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तौ असुरौ) विष्णुकर्णमलोद्भूतौ ब्रह्माणं हन्तुम् उद्यतौ। विष्णोः नाभिकमले स्थितः सः प्रजापतिः ब्रह्मा (तौ असुरौ … दृष्ट्वा … तुष्टाव)।

(tau asurau) viṣṇukarṇamalodbhūtau brahmāṇaṃ hantum udyatau | viṣṇoḥ nābhikamale sthitaḥ saḥ prajāpatiḥ brahmā (tau asurau … dṛṣṭvā … tuṣṭāva) |

The first line completes the main clause of 1.67. The second introduces the subject (सः … ब्रह्मा प्रजापतिः) of a sentence whose verb, तुष्टाव, is in 1.69.

6Translation

Literal— sprung from the wax of Viṣṇu’s ears, were intent on killing Brahmā. He, Brahmā, the lord of creatures, stationed on the navel-lotus of Viṣṇu,

Readableborn from the wax of Viṣṇu’s ears, rose up to kill Brahmā. Brahmā, lord of creatures, seated on the lotus springing from Viṣṇu’s navel,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asuras are born from the sleeping Viṣṇu’s own ear-wax and set out to kill Brahmā, who sits on the lotus from Viṣṇu’s navel. The creator is threatened by what comes from the sustainer while the sustainer sleeps.

Traditional interpretationThe tradition reads the image closely: danger arises from Viṣṇu himself while he is held by Yoganidrā, and Brahmā cannot wake him directly. He must turn to the power that holds Viṣṇu — the Goddess.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: what threatens creation is born of the sleeper’s own neglect. The remedy the story offers is not force but praise — attention turned to the power at work.

1.69Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दृष्ट्वा तावसुरौ चोग्रौ प्रसुप्तं च जनार्दनम्।
तुष्टाव योगनिद्रां तामेकाग्रहृदयस्थितः ॥ ६९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दृष्ट्वा | तौ | असुरौ | च | उग्रौ | प्रसुप्तम् | च | जनार्दनम्तुष्टाव | योगनिद्राम् | ताम् | एकाग्रहृदयस्थितः

3IAST

As reciteddṛṣṭvā tāvasurau cograu prasuptaṃ ca janārdanam |
tuṣṭāva yoganidrāṃ tāmekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥ ||

Separateddṛṣṭvā | tau | asurau | ca | ugrau | prasuptam | ca | janārdanam
tuṣṭāva | yoganidrām | tām | ekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’)
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. acc. du.; qualifies असुरौ
असुरौasurauasurasm. acc. du. of असुर; object of दृष्ट्वा
चcaboth … (and)indeclinable (conjunction; the first of a च … च pair)
उग्रौugraufiercem. acc. du. of उग्र; qualifies असुरौ
प्रसुप्तम्prasuptamfast asleeppast participle (kta) of प्र + √स्वप् (adādi 2P ‘to sleep’), m. acc. sg.; qualifies जनार्दनम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction; the second of the pair)
जनार्दनम्janārdanamJanārdana (Viṣṇu)m. acc. sg. of जनार्दन; compound (see Samāsa); object of दृष्ट्वा
तुष्टावtuṣṭāvapraised√स्तु (adādi 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
योगनिद्राम्yoganidrāmYoganidrāf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 1.54); object of तुष्टाव
ताम्tāmher, that (Goddess)pronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; qualifies योगनिद्राम्
एकाग्रहृदयस्थितःekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥsteadied in a one-pointed heartm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ब्रह्मा (1.68)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तावसुरौ tāvasurau → तौ + असुरौ
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv.
  • चोग्रौ cograu → च + उग्रौ
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • प्रसुप्तं च prasuptaṃ ca → प्रसुप्तम् + च
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra (pronounced as the palatal nasal before c: अनुस्वारस्य ययि परसवर्णः, 8.4.58, वा पदान्तस्य, 8.4.59).
  • तामेकाग्रहृदयस्थितः tāmekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥ → ताम् + एकाग्रहृदयस्थितः
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m is written together with the following vowel. Inside the compound, एक + अग्र → एकाग्र (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • एकाग्रहृदयस्थितः ekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥ — saptamī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (एकाग्रहृदय) whose first member एकाग्र is a bahuvrīhi
    Members: एकाग्रहृदय + स्थित · Vigraha: एकम् अग्रं यस्य तत् एकाग्रम्; एकाग्रं हृदयम् एकाग्रहृदयम्; तस्मिन् स्थितः ekam agraṃ yasya tat ekāgram; ekāgraṃ hṛdayam ekāgrahṛdayam; tasmin sthitaḥ
    Literally “standing in a heart that has one point”; in the verse: with his whole mind gathered on her
    Alternative: Or tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, एकाग्रहृदयेन स्थितः ‘standing (there) with a concentrated heart’; the sense — Brahmā’s focused devotion — is the same.
  • जनार्दनम् janārdanam — upapada tatpuruṣa (traditional etymology; अर्दन from √अर्द् ‘to afflict, stir’)
    Members: जन + अर्दन · Vigraha: जनान् अर्दयति इति janān ardayati iti
    Literally “he who afflicts (wicked) people”; in the verse: a standard name of Viṣṇu
    Alternative: Traditional etymologies differ (also ‘he whom people entreat’, from √अर्द् ‘to ask’); the name is used here simply for Viṣṇu.

C. Morphology

  • तुष्टाव tuṣṭāva — Perfect of √स्तु: the reduplicated syllable keeps the stop (tu-), and the s of the root becomes ṣ, drawing t into ṭ (tu-ṣṭāva); the root vowel takes vṛddhi in the 3rd singular. The verb that frames the whole hymn (1.73–87).
  • दृष्ट्वा dṛṣṭvā — Absolutive of √दृश्: दृश् + त्वा → दृष्ट्वा (ś → ṣ, t → ṭ). Its subject is Brahmā, the subject of तुष्टाव.
  • प्रसुप्तम् prasuptam — Past participle of प्र + √स्वप् with samprasāraṇa (स्वप् → सुप्): ‘deeply asleep’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः ब्रह्मा) तौ उग्रौ असुरौ च प्रसुप्तं जनार्दनं च दृष्ट्वा एकाग्रहृदयस्थितः तां योगनिद्रां तुष्टाव।

(saḥ brahmā) tau ugrau asurau ca prasuptaṃ janārdanaṃ ca dṛṣṭvā ekāgrahṛdayasthitaḥ tāṃ yoganidrāṃ tuṣṭāva |

The subject (ब्रह्मा) comes from 1.68; an absolutive phrase with two objects joined by च … च, then the main verb with its object.

6Translation

Literalhaving seen both those two fierce asuras and Janārdana fast asleep, praised that Yoganidrā, steadied in a one-pointed heart —

Readableseeing the two fierce asuras and Janārdana deep in sleep, with his heart wholly gathered, began to praise Yoganidrā —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā sees the danger and sees that Viṣṇu cannot help while asleep. With a one-pointed heart he praises Yoganidrā, the power holding Viṣṇu — the hymn of 1.73–87.

Traditional interpretationThe narrative logic is precise: to wake Viṣṇu, Brahmā addresses not Viṣṇu but the sleep that holds him. In the Śākta reading even the highest god’s activity depends on her release.

1.70Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विबोधनार्थाय हरेर्हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम्।
विश्वेश्वरीं जगद्धात्रीं स्थितिसंहारकारिणीम् ॥ ७० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विबोधनार्थाय | हरेः | हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम्विश्वेश्वरीम् | जगद्धात्रीम् | स्थितिसंहारकारिणीम्

3IAST

As recitedvibodhanārthāya harerharinetrakṛtālayām |
viśveśvarīṃ jagaddhātrīṃ sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīm ||

Separatedvibodhanārthāya | hareḥ | harinetrakṛtālayām
viśveśvarīm | jagaddhātrīm | sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīm

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विबोधनार्थायvibodhanārthāyafor the sake of awakeningcompound (see Samāsa), m. dat. sg.; dative of purpose
हरेःhareḥof Harim. gen. sg. of हरि; objective genitive with विबोधन (‘the waking of Hari’)
हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम्harinetrakṛtālayāmwho had made her dwelling in Hari’s eyesf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies योगनिद्राम् (1.69)
विश्वेश्वरीम्viśveśvarīmthe sovereign of the universef. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to योगनिद्राम्
जगद्धात्रीम्jagaddhātrīmthe supporter (nurse) of the worldf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition
स्थितिसंहारकारिणीम्sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīmwho brings about preservation and dissolutionf. acc. sg. of -कारिणी (in-stem + ṅīp); compound (see Samāsa); in apposition

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हरेर्हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम् harerharinetrakṛtālayām → हरेः + हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after e, before the voiced h, appears as r.
  • विबोधनार्थाय vibodhanārthāya → विबोधन + अर्थाय
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā; likewise कृत + आलय → कृतालय.
  • विश्वेश्वरीं viśveśvarīṃ → विश्व + ईश्वरीम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa); anusvāra · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    a + ī → e; the final m becomes anusvāra before the j of जगद्धात्रीम्.
  • जगद्धात्रीं jagaddhātrīṃ → जगत् + धात्रीम्
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of जगत् is voiced to d before the voiced dh.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विबोधनार्थाय vibodhanārthāya — caturthī tatpuruṣa (with अर्थ, always compounded)
    Members: विबोधन + अर्थ · Vigraha: विबोधनाय इदम् vibodhanāya idam
    Literally “for the purpose of awakening”; in the verse: so that Hari might wake
  • हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम् harinetrakṛtālayām — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (हरिनेत्र)
    Members: हरिनेत्र + कृत + आलय · Vigraha: हरेः नेत्रे हरिनेत्रे; हरिनेत्रयोः कृतः आलयः यया सा, ताम् hareḥ netre harinetre; harinetrayoḥ kṛtaḥ ālayaḥ yayā sā, tām
    Literally “by whom a dwelling has been made in Hari’s eyes”; in the verse: she who, as sleep, rests on Viṣṇu’s eyelids
  • विश्वेश्वरीम् viśveśvarīm — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विश्व + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: विश्वस्य ईश्वरी, ताम् viśvasya īśvarī, tām
    Literally “ruler of the universe”; in the verse: sovereign of all that is
  • जगद्धात्रीम् jagaddhātrīm — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + धात्री · Vigraha: जगतः धात्री, ताम् jagataḥ dhātrī, tām
    Literally “supporter of the world”; in the verse: the one who upholds and nourishes the world (धात्री also ‘nurse, mother’)
  • स्थितिसंहारकारिणीम् sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīm — ṣaṣṭhī (upapada-type) tatpuruṣa containing an itaretara dvandva (स्थितिसंहार)
    Members: स्थिति + संहार + कारिणी · Vigraha: स्थितिः च संहारः च स्थितिसंहारौ; तयोः कारिणी, ताम् sthitiḥ ca saṃhāraḥ ca sthitisaṃhārau; tayoḥ kāriṇī, tām
    Literally “maker of maintenance and destruction”; in the verse: who keeps the world in being and withdraws it

C. Morphology

  • जगद्धात्रीम् jagaddhātrīm — धात्री is the feminine of the agent noun धातृ (√धा + tṛc), formed with ṅīp: ‘she who places, supports’.
  • स्थितिसंहारकारिणीम् sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīm — Feminine of the -in agent noun कारिन् (+ ī): कारिणी, accusative कारिणीम्. Compare the masculine instrumental कारिणा in 1.54.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(ब्रह्मा) हरेः विबोधनार्थाय हरिनेत्रकृतालयां विश्वेश्वरीं जगद्धात्रीं स्थितिसंहारकारिणीं (तां योगनिद्रां तुष्टाव)।

(brahmā) hareḥ vibodhanārthāya harinetrakṛtālayāṃ viśveśvarīṃ jagaddhātrīṃ sthitisaṃhārakāriṇīṃ (tāṃ yoganidrāṃ tuṣṭāva) |

A string of accusative epithets in apposition to योगनिद्राम् (1.69), with a dative of purpose; the verb is तुष्टाव (1.69).

6Translation

Literalfor the sake of the awakening of Hari — (her) who had made her abode in Hari’s eyes, the sovereign of the universe, the supporter of the world, the maker of maintenance and dissolution,

Readableto wake Hari: she who dwelt in Hari’s eyes, sovereign of the universe, sustainer of the world, who preserves it and dissolves it —

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe purpose of Brahmā’s praise is stated — to wake Hari — and the Goddess is described by her place (resting in Viṣṇu’s eyes, as sleep) and her cosmic functions: sovereign, supporter, preserver and dissolver of the world.

Traditional interpretationThe epithets already give her the works usually divided among the gods: preservation (Viṣṇu’s) and dissolution (Rudra’s). The hymn will add creation (1.75–77), so that all three belong to her.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same epithet, name 935, जगद्धात्री, and lists the three functions as separate names: 264, सृष्टिकर्त्री; 266, गोप्त्री; 268, संहारिणी.

1.71Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

निद्रां भगवतीं विष्णोरतुलां तेजसः प्रभुः ॥ ७१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

निद्राम् | भगवतीम् | विष्णोः | अतुलाम् | तेजसः | प्रभुः

3IAST

As recitednidrāṃ bhagavatīṃ viṣṇoratulāṃ tejasaḥ prabhuḥ ||

Separatednidrām | bhagavatīm | viṣṇoḥ | atulām | tejasaḥ | prabhuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
निद्राम्nidrāmSleepf. acc. sg. of निद्रा; in apposition to योगनिद्राम् (1.69)
भगवतीम्bhagavatīmthe Blessed Onef. acc. sg. of भगवती; qualifies निद्राम्
विष्णोःviṣṇoḥof Viṣṇum. gen. sg. of विष्णु; depends on निद्राम्
अतुलाम्atulāmincomparablef. acc. sg. of अतुल (nañ compound: न तुला यस्य); qualifies निद्राम्
तेजसःtejasaḥof radiance, of splendourn. gen. sg. of तेजस् (s-stem); depends on प्रभुः
प्रभुःprabhuḥthe lord (Brahmā)m. nom. sg. of प्रभु; subject (= ब्रह्मा of 1.68)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विष्णोरतुलां viṣṇoratulāṃ → विष्णोः + अतुलाम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after o (not a/ā), before a vowel, appears as r.
  • निद्रां भगवतीं nidrāṃ bhagavatīṃ → निद्राम् + भगवतीम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra (also in भगवतीं विष्णोः and अतुलां तेजसः).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतुलाम् atulām — bahuvrīhi (nañ-bahuvrīhi)
    Members: न + तुला · Vigraha: न विद्यते तुला यस्याः सा, ताम् na vidyate tulā yasyāḥ sā, tām
    Literally “she who has no equal measure”; in the verse: beyond compare

C. Morphology

  • तेजसः tejasaḥ — Genitive singular of the s-stem तेजस्; its construction is open (see flags): with प्रभुः ‘lord of radiance’, or with अतुलाम् ‘unequalled in radiance’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तेजसः प्रभुः (ब्रह्मा) विष्णोः अतुलां भगवतीं निद्रां (तुष्टाव)।

tejasaḥ prabhuḥ (brahmā) viṣṇoḥ atulāṃ bhagavatīṃ nidrāṃ (tuṣṭāva) |

The half-verse closes the sentence begun in 1.68: more accusatives in apposition to योगनिद्राम्, and the nominative प्रभुः restating the subject.

6Translation

Literal— the Blessed Sleep of Viṣṇu, the incomparable — (so) the lord of radiance (praised her).

Readablethe incomparable Lady, the Sleep of Viṣṇu. So Brahmā, lord of splendour, praised her.

‘Brahmā’ and ‘praised her’ are supplied from 1.68–69 to close the sentence.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last epithets: she is Viṣṇu’s Sleep, the Blessed One, beyond compare. The nominative प्रभुः restates the subject, Brahmā, before the hymn begins.

Traditional interpretationThe Goddess is named here by her plainest name, निद्रा ‘sleep’, with the honorific भगवती: what holds the highest god is not a lesser power but the Blessed One herself.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The construction of तेजसः is open. Taken here with प्रभुः, ‘the lord of radiance’, an epithet of Brahmā (the subject); it can also be read with अतुलाम्, ‘(her) unequalled in radiance’, with प्रभुः simply ‘the Lord’. Both are grammatical; the sense of the verse is not affected.

1.72Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ब्रह्मोवाच ॥ ७२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ब्रह्मा | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedbrahmovāca ||

Separatedbrahmā | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ब्रह्माbrahmāBrahmām. nom. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ब्रह्मोवाच brahmovāca → ब्रह्मा + उवाच
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + u → o.

C. Morphology

  • ब्रह्मा brahmā — Nominative of the masculine n-stem ब्रह्मन् (the god): the n is dropped and the vowel lengthened.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ब्रह्मा उवाच।

brahmā uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralBrahmā said:

ReadableBrahmā said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningWithin the sage Medhas’s narration, Brahmā on the navel-lotus speaks to Yoganidrā. His hymn (1.73–87) is the first of the four great hymns of the Devī Māhātmya.

1.73Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

त्वं स्वाहा त्वं स्वधा त्वं हि वषट्कारः स्वरात्मिका ॥ ७३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

त्वम् | स्वाहा | त्वम् | स्वधा | त्वम् | हि | वषट्कारः | स्वरात्मिका

3IAST

As recitedtvaṃ svāhā tvaṃ svadhā tvaṃ hi vaṣaṭkāraḥ svarātmikā ||

Separatedtvam | svāhā | tvam | svadhā | tvam | hi | vaṣaṭkāraḥ | svarātmikā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
स्वाहाsvāhāSvāhā, the call with which offerings are given to the godsf. nom. sg. of स्वाहा (otherwise an indeclinable exclamation, here a noun personified); predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
स्वधाsvadhāSvadhā, the call with which offerings are given to the ancestorsf. nom. sg. of स्वधा; predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
वषट्कारःvaṣaṭkāraḥthe exclamation vaṣaṭm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate (masculine noun with a feminine subject)
स्वरात्मिकाsvarātmikāwhose very self is sound (accent, tone)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वं स्वाहा त्वं स्वधा त्वं हि tvaṃ svāhā tvaṃ svadhā tvaṃ hi → त्वम् + स्वाहा + त्वम् + स्वधा + त्वम् + हि
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Each final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • वषट्कारः स्वरात्मिका vaṣaṭkāraḥ svarātmikā → वषट्कारः + स्वरात्मिका
    visarga sandhi (optional retention before a sibilant) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before s the visarga may stay (as printed) or become s; the Gita Press keeps the visarga.
  • स्वरात्मिका svarātmikā → स्वर + आत्मिका
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वषट्कारः vaṣaṭkāraḥ — tatpuruṣa with कार ‘making, utterance’ after a quoted sound (as in ओंकार)
    Members: वषट् + कार · Vigraha: वषट् इति कारः (उच्चारणम्) vaṣaṭ iti kāraḥ (uccāraṇam)
    Literally “the making of ‘vaṣaṭ’”; in the verse: the cry vaṣaṭ with which the hotṛ priest signals the pouring of the oblation
  • स्वरात्मिका svarātmikā — bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -ka; feminine -ikā)
    Members: स्वर + आत्मन् (+ ka) · Vigraha: स्वरः आत्मा यस्याः सा svaraḥ ātmā yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose self is svara”; in the verse: she whose nature is sacred sound — the accent of the Veda, or the vowel that makes speech audible
    Alternative: स्वर can mean the Vedic accent (udātta, anudātta, svarita), a vowel, or a musical note; the verse does not say which. The Vedic sense suits the ritual words before it.

C. Morphology

  • स्वरात्मिका svarātmikā — Bahuvrīhis ending in आत्मन् add -ka; in the feminine the a before -kā becomes i: आत्मक → आत्मिका. The same formation recurs as मात्रात्मिका in 1.74.
  • वषट्कारः vaṣaṭkāraḥ — A masculine noun predicated of the Goddess: in identity statements (‘you are X’) the predicate noun keeps its own gender.

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वं स्वाहा, त्वं स्वधा, त्वं हि स्वरात्मिका वषट्कारः।

tvaṃ svāhā, tvaṃ svadhā, tvaṃ hi svarātmikā vaṣaṭkāraḥ |

Three identity statements with त्वम् repeated; स्वरात्मिका qualifies the subject.

6Translation

LiteralYou are Svāhā, you are Svadhā, you indeed are the vaṣaṭ-call, you whose self is sound.

ReadableYou are Svāhā, you are Svadhā; you are the sacred cry Vaṣaṭ — your very self is the sound of the Veda.

‘Of the Veda’ interprets स्वर (see Samāsa).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā opens by identifying the Goddess with the three ritual calls of Vedic sacrifice: svāhā (offering to the gods), svadhā (offering to the ancestors) and vaṣaṭ (the hotṛ’s call at the oblation). She is the very sound through which offering reaches its recipient.

Traditional interpretationThe hymn begins with sacrificial speech because in the Vedic view the sacrifice sustains the world. To call her svāhā and svadhā is to make her the power by which gods and ancestors are fed — the support of both worlds. In Purāṇic myth Svāhā and Svadhā are also personified as goddesses, consorts of Agni and of the ancestors.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same pair as consecutive names: 535, स्वाहा, and 536, स्वधा.

1.74Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सुधा त्वमक्षरे नित्ये त्रिधा मात्रात्मिका स्थिता।
अर्धमात्रास्थिता नित्या यानुच्चार्या विशेषतः ॥ ७४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सुधा | त्वम् | अक्षरे | नित्ये | त्रिधा | मात्रात्मिका | स्थिताअर्धमात्रास्थिता | नित्या | या | अनुच्चार्या | विशेषतः

3IAST

As recitedsudhā tvamakṣare nitye tridhā mātrātmikā sthitā |
ardhamātrāsthitā nityā yānuccāryā viśeṣataḥ ||

Separatedsudhā | tvam | akṣare | nitye | tridhā | mātrātmikā | sthitā
ardhamātrāsthitā | nityā | yā | anuccāryā | viśeṣataḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सुधाsudhānectar, the drink of immortalityf. nom. sg. of सुधा; predicate
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
अक्षरेakṣarein the imperishable (syllable, Om)n. loc. sg. of अक्षर; alt. f. voc. sg. of अक्षरा ‘O Imperishable One’ (see flags)
नित्येnityeeternaln. loc. sg. of नित्य, qualifying अक्षरे; alt. f. voc. sg. of नित्या ‘O Eternal One’
त्रिधाtridhāthreefold, in three waysindeclinable (adverb with the suffix -dhā)
मात्रात्मिकाmātrātmikāwhose self is the morae (a, u, m)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम्
स्थिताsthitāabiding, presentpast participle (kta) of √स्था (bhvādi 1P ‘to stand’), f. nom. sg.; predicate
अर्धमात्रास्थिताardhamātrāsthitāabiding in the half-moraf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of त्वम्
नित्याnityāeternalf. nom. sg. of नित्य; qualifies त्वम् (or the half-mora)
याyāwho, whichrelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.
अनुच्चार्याanuccāryānot to be uttered, unpronounceablef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); gerundive (ṇyat) of उद् + √चर् (causative)
विशेषतःviśeṣataḥdistinctly, as a separate soundindeclinable (abl. sense with the suffix -tas, from विशेष)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वमक्षरे tvamakṣare → त्वम् + अक्षरे
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m is written together with the following vowel.
  • मात्रात्मिका mātrātmikā → मात्रा + आत्मिका
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā.
  • यानुच्चार्या yānuccāryā → या + अनुच्चार्या
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • उच्चार्या uccāryā → उत् + चार्या (in अनुच्चार्या)
    consonant sandhi inside the word (palatal assimilation) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    The t of the prefix उद्/उत् becomes c before c: उच्चार्य. The negative prefix takes the form अन्- before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मात्रात्मिका mātrātmikā — bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -ka; feminine -ikā)
    Members: मात्रा + आत्मन् (+ ka) · Vigraha: मात्राः आत्मा यस्याः सा mātrāḥ ātmā yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose self is the measures”; in the verse: she who exists as the three sounds a, u and m of Om
  • अर्धमात्रास्थिता ardhamātrāsthitā — saptamī tatpuruṣa (अर्धमात्रा is itself a tatpuruṣa: मात्रायाः अर्धम् ‘half of a mora’)
    Members: अर्धमात्रा + स्थित · Vigraha: अर्धमात्रायां स्थिता ardhamātrāyāṃ sthitā
    Literally “standing in the half-mora”; in the verse: she who is present in the faint resonance that closes Om
    Alternative: The printed text joins अर्धमात्रास्थिता; it can also be divided as two words, अर्धमात्रा स्थिता ‘(you) abide as the half-mora’, and then या refers to the half-mora. The compound is kept as printed.
  • अनुच्चार्या anuccāryā — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: न + उच्चार्य · Vigraha: न उच्चार्या na uccāryā
    Literally “not to be uttered”; in the verse: beyond articulation

C. Morphology

  • अनुच्चार्या anuccāryā — Gerundive of the causative of उद् + √चर् (उच्चारयति ‘pronounces’): उच्चार्य ‘to be pronounced’; with the negative अन्- ‘not pronounceable’.
  • अक्षरे akṣare — Ambiguous form: n. loc. sg. of अक्षर (‘in the imperishable syllable’) or f. voc. sg. of अक्षरा (‘O Imperishable!’). The locative is chosen because it gives स्थिता its place and matches the half-mora of the next line.
  • त्रिधा tridhā — The suffix -धा makes adverbs of manner from numerals: त्रिधा ‘in three ways’, बहुधा ‘in many ways’ (1.65).

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वं सुधा। त्वं नित्ये अक्षरे त्रिधा मात्रात्मिका स्थिता। (त्वं) या विशेषतः अनुच्चार्या, नित्या, अर्धमात्रास्थिता (असि)।

tvaṃ sudhā | tvaṃ nitye akṣare tridhā mātrātmikā sthitā | (tvaṃ) yā viśeṣataḥ anuccāryā, nityā, ardhamātrāsthitā (asi) |

A short identity (सुधा त्वम्), a participial predicate with a locative of place, and a second predicate expanded by a relative clause (या … विशेषतः).

6Translation

LiteralYou are nectar; in the eternal imperishable (syllable) you abide threefold, having the morae as your self; you abide in the half-mora, eternal, (you) who cannot be uttered distinctly.

ReadableYou are the nectar of immortality. In the eternal, imperishable syllable you dwell as its three sounds; and you dwell in the half-measure beyond them, eternal, which no voice can utter as a separate sound.

‘Syllable’ and ‘its three sounds’ interpret अक्षर and मात्रा as the syllable Om and its parts a, u, m, the standard sense in this context.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is identified with the sacred syllable: she abides in its three morae (a, u, m) and in the half-mora — the fading resonance after m — which cannot be pronounced as a distinct sound. She is also सुधा, the nectar of immortality.

Traditional interpretationThe analysis of Om into three measures and a fourth beyond utterance is Upaniṣadic: the Māṇḍūkya Upaniṣad treats a, u and m as the three states of waking, dream and deep sleep and the ‘measureless fourth’ (अमात्रः चतुर्थः) as the self beyond them. Brahmā places the Goddess both in the three and in what lies beyond them.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the hymn begins with words that can be spoken — svāhā, svadhā, vaṣaṭ — and moves at once to the part of the sacred sound that cannot be spoken. Praise here is a way of approaching what speech cannot hold.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: अक्षरे नित्ये may be locative (‘in the eternal imperishable [syllable]’, chosen here) or two feminine vocatives (‘O Imperishable, O Eternal One’); both are grammatical and both are found in translations. अर्धमात्रास्थिता is printed as one word; a division into अर्धमात्रा स्थिता is equally possible.

1.75Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

त्वमेव संध्या सावित्री त्वं देवि जननी परा।
त्वयैतद्धार्यते विश्वं त्वयैतत्सृज्यते जगत् ॥ ७५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

त्वम् | एव | संध्या | सावित्री | त्वम् | देवि | जननी | परात्वया | एतत् | धार्यते | विश्वम् | त्वया | एतत् | सृज्यते | जगत्

3IAST

As recitedtvameva saṃdhyā sāvitrī tvaṃ devi jananī parā |
tvayaitaddhāryate viśvaṃ tvayaitatsṛjyate jagat ||

Separatedtvam | eva | saṃdhyā | sāvitrī | tvam | devi | jananī | parā
tvayā | etat | dhāryate | viśvam | tvayā | etat | sṛjyate | jagat

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
एवevaalone, you and no otherindeclinable (restrictive particle)
संध्याsaṃdhyāSandhyā, the twilight (and its rite)f. nom. sg. of संध्या; predicate
सावित्रीsāvitrīSāvitrī, the sacred verse to Savitṛf. nom. sg. of सावित्री (taddhita from सवितृ); predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
जननीjananīmother, she who gives birthf. nom. sg. of जननी (√जन् + lyuṭ, feminine -ī); predicate
पराparāsupremef. nom. sg. of पर; qualifies जननी
त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of the passive
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies विश्वम्
धार्यतेdhāryateis upheld, is borne√धृ (bhvādi 1U ‘to hold’), causative stem धारय-, karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
विश्वम्viśvamuniversen. nom. sg. of विश्व; subject of धार्यते
त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies जगत्
सृज्यतेsṛjyateis created, is brought forth√सृज् (tudādi 6P ‘to emit’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
जगत्jagatworldn. nom. sg. of जगत्; subject of सृज्यते

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वमेव tvameva → त्वम् + एव
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m is written together with the following vowel.
  • त्वयैतद्धार्यते tvayaitaddhāryate → त्वया + एतत् + धार्यते
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); consonant sandhi (voicing) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    ā + e → ai; the t of एतत् is voiced to d before the voiced dh.
  • त्वयैतत्सृज्यते tvayaitatsṛjyate → त्वया + एतत् + सृज्यते
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); no change before an unvoiced sibilant · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); खरि च (8.4.55)
    ā + e → ai; the t stays unvoiced before s.
  • विश्वं त्वयै viśvaṃ tvayai → विश्वम् + त्वया
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • धार्यते dhāryate — Passive of the causative of √धृ (धारयति ‘holds up, sustains’): the causative -ay- drops before the passive -ya (धार्-य-ते). ‘Is sustained’.
  • सावित्री sāvitrī — Taddhita in -ī from सवितृ: ‘belonging to Savitṛ’, the name of the Gāyatrī verse addressed to the Sun-god Savitṛ.
  • जननी jananī — Feminine agent noun from √जन् with -ana and -ī: ‘she who gives birth’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वम् एव संध्या, सावित्री। देवि, त्वं परा जननी। त्वया एतत् विश्वं धार्यते, त्वया एतत् जगत् सृज्यते।

tvam eva saṃdhyā, sāvitrī | devi, tvaṃ parā jananī | tvayā etat viśvaṃ dhāryate, tvayā etat jagat sṛjyate |

Identity statements (त्वम् … संध्या सावित्री; त्वम् … जननी परा), then two parallel passive sentences with त्वया as agent.

6Translation

LiteralYou alone are Sandhyā and Sāvitrī; you, O Goddess, are the supreme mother. By you this universe is upheld; by you this world is created.

ReadableYou alone are the sacred twilight and the Sāvitrī verse; you, O Goddess, are the supreme Mother. By you this universe is sustained, and by you this world is brought forth.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā continues identifying her with the forms of sacred worship — the twilight rite (Sandhyā) and the Sāvitrī verse recited in it — and then names her the supreme Mother, by whom the world is created and sustained.

Traditional interpretationSandhyā and Sāvitrī belong together: the Sāvitrī (Gāyatrī) verse addressed to Savitṛ is recited at the twilight worship. To call the Goddess both is to make the daily Vedic observance itself her form. The verse then passes from ritual to cosmology: the one invoked at dawn and dusk is the mother of the world.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 422, सन्ध्या; 699, सावित्री; and 823, जननी.

1.76Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

त्वयैतत्पाल्यते देवि त्वमत्स्यन्ते च सर्वदा।
विसृष्टौ सृष्टिरूपा त्वं स्थितिरूपा च पालने ॥ ७६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

त्वया | एतत् | पाल्यते | देवि | त्वम् | अत्सि | अन्ते | च | सर्वदाविसृष्टौ | सृष्टिरूपा | त्वम् | स्थितिरूपा | च | पालने

3IAST

As recitedtvayaitatpālyate devi tvamatsyante ca sarvadā |
visṛṣṭau sṛṣṭirūpā tvaṃ sthitirūpā ca pālane ||

Separatedtvayā | etat | pālyate | devi | tvam | atsi | ante | ca | sarvadā
visṛṣṭau | sṛṣṭirūpā | tvam | sthitirūpā | ca | pālane

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of the passive
एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject of पाल्यते
पाल्यतेpālyateis protected, is maintained√पाल् (curādi 10 ‘to protect’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of अत्सि
अत्सिatsidevour, consume√अद् (adādi 2P ‘to eat’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अन्तेanteat the endm. loc. sg. of अन्त; locative of time
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
सर्वदाsarvadāalways, at every (end)indeclinable (adverb of time, सर्व + -dā)
विसृष्टौvisṛṣṭauin creation, at the emission (of the world)f. loc. sg. of विसृष्टि (वि + √सृज् + ktin)
सृष्टिरूपाsṛṣṭirūpāhaving the form of creationf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of त्वम्
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
स्थितिरूपाsthitirūpāhaving the form of preservationf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
पालनेpālanein protecting, in maintenancen. loc. sg. of पालन (√पाल् + lyuṭ)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वयैतत्पाल्यते tvayaitatpālyate → त्वया + एतत् + पाल्यते
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); no change before an unvoiced stop · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); खरि च (8.4.55)
    ā + e → ai; the t stays unvoiced before p.
  • त्वमत्स्यन्ते tvamatsyante → त्वम् + अत्सि + अन्ते
    final m before a vowel; vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    त्वम् keeps its m before the a of अत्सि; the i of अत्सि becomes y before the a of अन्ते.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सृष्टिरूपा sṛṣṭirūpā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सृष्टि + रूप · Vigraha: सृष्टिः रूपं यस्याः सा sṛṣṭiḥ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose form is creation”; in the verse: you take the form of creation when the world is made
  • स्थितिरूपा sthitirūpā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: स्थिति + रूप · Vigraha: स्थितिः रूपं यस्याः सा sthitiḥ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose form is continuance”; in the verse: you take the form of preservation while the world endures

C. Morphology

  • अत्सि atsi — Present 2nd singular of √अद्, a root-class (adādi) verb: अद् + सि, the d unvoiced to t before s (खरि च, 8.4.55). ‘You eat’ — the image of dissolution as the world swallowed back.
  • पाल्यते pālyate — Passive of the curādi root √पाल् (पालयति): the -aya- of the present drops before the passive -ya (पाल्-य-ते).

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, त्वया एतत् पाल्यते; अन्ते च सर्वदा त्वम् (एतत्) अत्सि। विसृष्टौ त्वं सृष्टिरूपा, पालने च स्थितिरूपा, …

devi, tvayā etat pālyate; ante ca sarvadā tvam (etat) atsi | visṛṣṭau tvaṃ sṛṣṭirūpā, pālane ca sthitirūpā, …

A passive sentence, an active one with an understood object (एतत्), and a series of nominal predicates with locatives of occasion, continued in 1.77 (संहृतिरूपा).

6Translation

LiteralBy you this is protected, O Goddess, and you devour (it) at the end, always. In creation you have the form of creation, and the form of preservation in protecting,

ReadableBy you, O Goddess, it is protected, and at the end of every age you consume it. When the world is brought forth, you are its creation; while it is sustained, you are its preservation;

सर्वदा ‘always’ with अन्ते is rendered ‘at the end of every age’: each dissolution, not one alone.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe three cosmic works are given to her one after another: she protects the world, consumes it at every end, and in each phase takes the form of that phase — creation in creating, preservation in protecting. Dissolution follows in 1.77.

Traditional interpretationThe three works ordinarily assigned to Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra are all hers; the speaker is Brahmā himself, the creator, who here says that creation is her form. The tradition reads this as the Devī Māhātmya’s central claim: the gods act through her power.

1.77Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तथा संहृतिरूपान्ते जगतोऽस्य जगन्मये।
महाविद्या महामाया महामेधा महास्मृतिः ॥ ७७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तथा | संहृतिरूपा | अन्ते | जगतः | अस्य | जगन्मयेमहाविद्या | महामाया | महामेधा | महास्मृतिः

3IAST

As recitedtathā saṃhṛtirūpānte jagato'sya jaganmaye |
mahāvidyā mahāmāyā mahāmedhā mahāsmṛtiḥ ||

Separatedtathā | saṃhṛtirūpā | ante | jagataḥ | asya | jaganmaye
mahāvidyā | mahāmāyā | mahāmedhā | mahāsmṛtiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तथाtathāso too, likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
संहृतिरूपाsaṃhṛtirūpāhaving the form of dissolutionf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of त्वम् (1.76)
अन्तेanteat the endm. loc. sg. of अन्त; locative of time
जगतःjagataḥof the worldn. gen. sg. of जगत्; depends on अन्ते
अस्यasyaof thispronoun इदम्, n. gen. sg.; qualifies जगतः
