महामायाmahāmāyāthe great illusion-powerf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 1.55); predicate
महामेधाmahāmedhāthe great intelligencef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
महास्मृतिःmahāsmṛtiḥthe great memory (tradition)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संहृतिरूपान्ते saṃhṛtirūpānte → संहृतिरूपा + अन्ते
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • जगतोऽस्य jagato'sya → जगतः + अस्य
    visarga sandhi with pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • जगन्मये jaganmaye → जगत् + मये
    consonant sandhi before a suffix (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    The t of जगत् becomes n before the m of the suffix -maya.
  • महाविद्या mahāvidyā → महत् + विद्या
    stem form in a compound · महत् becomes महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya (described in words; no sūtra from the list applies)
    The same replacement gives महामाया, महामेधा, महास्मृतिः.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • संहृतिरूपा saṃhṛtirūpā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: संहृति + रूप · Vigraha: संहृतिः रूपं यस्याः सा saṃhṛtiḥ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose form is withdrawal”; in the verse: at the end you take the form of dissolution — completing creation and preservation of 1.76
  • महाविद्या mahāvidyā — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + विद्या · Vigraha: महती चासौ विद्या च mahatī cāsau vidyā ca
    Literally “great knowledge”; in the verse: the knowledge that frees (1.57)
  • महामेधा mahāmedhā — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + मेधा · Vigraha: महती चासौ मेधा च mahatī cāsau medhā ca
    Literally “great intelligence”; in the verse: the power of understanding and retaining
  • महास्मृतिः mahāsmṛtiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + स्मृति · Vigraha: महती चासौ स्मृतिः च mahatī cāsau smṛtiḥ ca
    Literally “great memory”; in the verse: memory, and also the remembered tradition (smṛti)

C. Morphology

  • जगन्मये jaganmaye — The suffix -मय (mayaṭ) means ‘consisting of’; feminine जगन्मयी, vocative जगन्मये (ī shortened to e in the vocative of feminines in -ī).
  • संहृतिरूपा saṃhṛtirūpā — संहृति ‘drawing together, withdrawal’ is सम् + √हृ + ktin: the dissolution pictured as the world gathered back in.

D. Anvaya — prose order

जगन्मये, तथा अस्य जगतः अन्ते (त्वं) संहृतिरूपा। (त्वं) महाविद्या, महामाया, महामेधा, महास्मृतिः, …

jaganmaye, tathā asya jagataḥ ante (tvaṃ) saṃhṛtirūpā | (tvaṃ) mahāvidyā, mahāmāyā, mahāmedhā, mahāsmṛtiḥ, …

The first line completes the series of 1.76 (सृष्टिरूपा … स्थितिरूपा … संहृतिरूपा); the second begins a list of nominatives predicated of the Goddess, continued in 1.78.

6Translation

Literalso too, at the end of this world, the form of dissolution, O you who consist of the world. (You are) the great knowledge, the great illusion, the great intelligence, the great memory,

Readableand at the end of this world, O you whose being is the world, you are its dissolution. You are the Great Knowledge and the Great Illusion, the Great Intelligence and the Great Memory,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe triad of 1.76 is completed: at the world’s end she takes the form of its dissolution. Then begins a litany of ‘great’ names — knowledge, illusion, intelligence, memory. Two printing errors of the digital text are corrected in this verse (सिंहृति → संहृति, महास्मृतः → महास्मृतिः), both with the independent witnesses; see corrections.json.

Traditional interpretationMahāvidyā and Mahāmāyā stand side by side, as liberation and bondage did in 1.57–58: the Goddess is both the knowledge that frees and the illusion that binds. ‘Great’ marks each as her cosmic form, not a human faculty.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 584, महाविद्या, and 215, महामाया, and the simple forms 538, मेधा, and 540, स्मृतिः.

1.78Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

महामोहा च भवती महादेवी महासुरी।
प्रकृतिस्त्वं च सर्वस्य गुणत्रयविभाविनी ॥ ७८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

महामोहा | च | भवती | महादेवी | महासुरीप्रकृतिः | त्वम् | च | सर्वस्य | गुणत्रयविभाविनी

3IAST

As recitedmahāmohā ca bhavatī mahādevī mahāsurī |
prakṛtistvaṃ ca sarvasya guṇatrayavibhāvinī ||

Separatedmahāmohā | ca | bhavatī | mahādevī | mahāsurī
prakṛtiḥ | tvam | ca | sarvasya | guṇatrayavibhāvinī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
महामोहाmahāmohāthe great delusionf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of भवती
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
भवतीbhavatīYour Ladyship, youhonorific pronoun भवत्, f. nom. sg. (भवती); subject
महादेवीmahādevīthe great Goddessf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
महासुरीmahāsurīthe great Asurī, the mighty one (variant: महेश्वरी ‘the great sovereign’)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
प्रकृतिःprakṛtiḥPrakṛti, primal naturef. nom. sg. of प्रकृति (i-stem); predicate
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
सर्वस्यsarvasyaof everythingpronominal adjective सर्व, n. gen. sg.; depends on प्रकृतिः
गुणत्रयविभाविनीguṇatrayavibhāvinīwho brings forth (differentiates) the three guṇasf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies प्रकृतिः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रकृतिस्त्वं prakṛtistvaṃ → प्रकृतिः + त्वम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • त्वं च tvaṃ ca → त्वम् + च
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • महासुरी mahāsurī → महा + असुरी
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. (The variant महेश्वरी would be महा + ईश्वरी with guṇa, आद्गुणः 6.1.87.)

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महामोहा mahāmohā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + मोह · Vigraha: महान् मोहः यस्याः सा mahān mohaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose delusion is great”; in the verse: she who is the great power of delusion
    Alternative: The feminine ending shows the compound describes the Goddess; it may also be read as a karmadhāraya personified in the feminine (‘the Great Delusion’). The sense is the same.
  • महादेवी mahādevī — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + देवी · Vigraha: महती चासौ देवी च mahatī cāsau devī ca
    Literally “great goddess”; in the verse: the supreme Goddess
  • महासुरी mahāsurī — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुरी · Vigraha: महती चासौ असुरी च mahatī cāsau asurī ca
    Literally “great Asurī”; in the verse: she whose power includes even the demonic; or, in the old sense of asura ‘mighty lord’, the great mighty one
    Alternative: The witnesses GRETIL and Freund read महेश्वरी, ‘great sovereign’ (महती चासौ ईश्वरी च, karmadhāraya). The Gita Press reading is kept; see flags.
  • गुणत्रयविभाविनी guṇatrayavibhāvinī — ṣaṣṭhī (upapada-type) tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (गुणत्रय)
    Members: गुणत्रय + विभाविनी · Vigraha: गुणानां त्रयम् गुणत्रयम्; तस्य विभाविनी guṇānāṃ trayam guṇatrayam; tasya vibhāvinī
    Literally “manifester of the triad of guṇas”; in the verse: she who unfolds sattva, rajas and tamas

C. Morphology

  • भवती bhavatī — The feminine of the honorific भवत् (‘Your Ladyship’); in Brahmā’s hymn it alternates with the familiar त्वम्, which follows in the next line.
  • गुणत्रयविभाविनी guṇatrayavibhāvinī — विभाविन् is an agent noun from the causative of वि + √भू (‘cause to appear distinctly’) with the kṛt affix -in; feminine -ī.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भवती च महामोहा, महादेवी, महासुरी। त्वं च सर्वस्य गुणत्रयविभाविनी प्रकृतिः।

bhavatī ca mahāmohā, mahādevī, mahāsurī | tvaṃ ca sarvasya guṇatrayavibhāvinī prakṛtiḥ |

The list of 1.77 continues with भवती as its expressed subject; a second clause names her as Prakṛti, with सर्वस्य and an epithet.

6Translation

LiteralAnd Your Ladyship (is) the great delusion, the great Goddess, the great Asurī; and you are the primal nature of all, who brings forth the three guṇas.

Readableand Great Delusion, Great Goddess and mighty Asurī. You are the primal Nature of all things, unfolding the three qualities.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe litany continues: she is the great delusion as well as the great knowledge of 1.77, the great Goddess, and — in the Gita Press reading — the great Asurī. She is also Prakṛti, the material source of everything, from which the three guṇas unfold.

Traditional interpretationThe Sāṃkhya term prakṛti is taken over: matter and its three qualities (sattva, rajas, tamas) are not an inert principle opposed to spirit but the Goddess herself. The Gita Press pāṭha’s liturgical scheme, in which the three caritas are presided over by Mahākālī, Mahālakṣmī and Mahāsarasvatī, is often explained through these guṇas; that scheme is tradition, not stated in this verse.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 209, महादेवी; 397, मूलप्रकृतिः; and 763, त्रिगुणात्मिका.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Variant reading (corrections.json, status ‘Textual Verification Required’): the Gita Press text reads महादेवी महासुरी; GRETIL (MkP 81.58) and Freund read महादेवी महेश्वरी. The Gita Press reading is kept and analysed; it must be checked against the printed book.

1.79Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कालरात्रिर्महारात्रिर्मोहरात्रिश्च दारुणा।
त्वं श्रीस्त्वमीश्वरी त्वं ह्रीस्त्वं बुद्धिर्बोधलक्षणा ॥ ७९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कालरात्रिः | महारात्रिः | मोहरात्रिः | च | दारुणात्वम् | श्रीः | त्वम् | ईश्वरी | त्वम् | ह्रीः | त्वम् | बुद्धिः | बोधलक्षणा

3IAST

As recitedkālarātrirmahārātrirmoharātriśca dāruṇā |
tvaṃ śrīstvamīśvarī tvaṃ hrīstvaṃ buddhirbodhalakṣaṇā ||

Separatedkālarātriḥ | mahārātriḥ | moharātriḥ | ca | dāruṇā
tvam | śrīḥ | tvam | īśvarī | tvam | hrīḥ | tvam | buddhiḥ | bodhalakṣaṇā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कालरात्रिःkālarātriḥthe Night of Time (of dissolution)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
महारात्रिःmahārātriḥthe Great Nightf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
मोहरात्रिःmoharātriḥthe Night of Delusionf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
दारुणाdāruṇādreadfulf. nom. sg. of दारुण; qualifies मोहरात्रिः (or all three nights)
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
श्रीःśrīḥŚrī, fortune and splendourf. nom. sg. of श्री (root-noun in ī); predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
ईश्वरीīśvarīthe sovereignf. nom. sg. of ईश्वरी (ईश्वर + ṅīp); predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
ह्रीःhrīḥmodesty, a sense of shamef. nom. sg. of ह्री (root-noun in ī); predicate
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.
बुद्धिःbuddhiḥunderstanding, intellectf. nom. sg. of बुद्धि (i-stem); predicate
बोधलक्षणाbodhalakṣaṇāwhose mark is awarenessf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies बुद्धिः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कालरात्रिर्महारात्रिर्मोहरात्रिश्च kālarātrirmahārātrirmoharātriśca → कालरात्रिः + महारात्रिः + मोहरात्रिः + च
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga after i before the voiced m appears as r (twice); before c it becomes s and then ś.
  • श्रीस्त्वमीश्वरी śrīstvamīśvarī → श्रीः + त्वम् + ईश्वरी
    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; त्वम् keeps its m before the vowel of ईश्वरी.
  • ह्रीस्त्वं hrīstvaṃ → ह्रीः + त्वम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • बुद्धिर्बोधलक्षणा buddhirbodhalakṣaṇā → बुद्धिः + बोधलक्षणा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i before the voiced b appears as r.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कालरात्रिः kālarātriḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: काल + रात्रि · Vigraha: कालस्य रात्रिः kālasya rātriḥ
    Literally “the night of Time”; in the verse: the night of dissolution brought by all-devouring Time
    Alternative: Also a karmadhāraya, काली चासौ रात्रिः च ‘the black night’; Time-as-death fits the dissolution of 1.76–77 better.
  • महारात्रिः mahārātriḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + रात्रि · Vigraha: महती चासौ रात्रिः च mahatī cāsau rātriḥ ca
    Literally “the great night”; in the verse: the great night of cosmic dissolution
  • मोहरात्रिः moharātriḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मोह + रात्रि · Vigraha: मोहस्य रात्रिः mohasya rātriḥ
    Literally “the night of delusion”; in the verse: the darkness of delusion in which beings are held
    Alternative: Or a karmadhāraya of identity, मोहः एव रात्रिः ‘the night that is delusion’.
  • बोधलक्षणा bodhalakṣaṇā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: बोध + लक्षण · Vigraha: बोधः लक्षणं यस्याः सा bodhaḥ lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose mark is awakening”; in the verse: intelligence known by its power to understand and to awaken

C. Morphology

  • श्रीः śrīḥ — Root-nouns in ī keep the visarga in the nominative singular (श्रीः, ह्रीः, धीः), unlike the derived feminines in ī (देवी, ईश्वरी) which have no ending.
  • दारुणा dāruṇā — Feminine of the adjective दारुण ‘harsh, dreadful’, agreeing with the feminine रात्रि compounds.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(त्वं) कालरात्रिः, महारात्रिः, दारुणा मोहरात्रिः च। त्वं श्रीः, त्वम् ईश्वरी, त्वं ह्रीः, त्वं बोधलक्षणा बुद्धिः।

(tvaṃ) kālarātriḥ, mahārātriḥ, dāruṇā moharātriḥ ca | tvaṃ śrīḥ, tvam īśvarī, tvaṃ hrīḥ, tvaṃ bodhalakṣaṇā buddhiḥ |

Three ‘night’ names with one adjective, then four identity statements each with त्वम्.

6Translation

Literal(You are) the Night of Time, the Great Night, and the dreadful Night of Delusion. You are Śrī, you are the Sovereign, you are Modesty, you are Understanding, whose mark is awareness.

ReadableYou are the Night of Time, the Great Night, the terrible Night of Delusion. You are Fortune, you are the Sovereign, you are Modesty, you are Understanding, whose very mark is awakening.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā names her as three nights — of Time, the Great Night, and the dreadful night of delusion — and then as Fortune, Sovereignty, Modesty and Understanding. The dark and the auspicious are named in one breath. बोध ‘awakening’ in the last word echoes the purpose of the hymn, the awakening (विबोधन) of Viṣṇu (1.70).

Traditional interpretationThe three nights are commonly explained as nights of dissolution and of delusion, though explanations of each differ and the text does not define them. Kālarātri is also one of the nine Durgās: the Devī Kavaca recited before the Gita Press pāṭha names her seventh (सप्तमं कालरात्रीति). Here ह्री is the virtue of modesty, paired with लज्जा in 1.80, not a seed-syllable.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 271, ईश्वरी, and describes her as 491, कालरात्र्यादिशक्त्यौघवृता, ‘surrounded by hosts of powers beginning with Kālarātri’ — there Kālarātri is one of her attendant powers, here one of her own names.

1.80Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

लज्जा पुष्टिस्तथा तुष्टिस्त्वं शान्तिः क्षान्तिरेव च।
खड्गिनी शूलिनी घोरा गदिनी चक्रिणी तथा ॥ ८० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

लज्जा | पुष्टिः | तथा | तुष्टिः | त्वम् | शान्तिः | क्षान्तिः | एव | चखड्गिनी | शूलिनी | घोरा | गदिनी | चक्रिणी | तथा

3IAST

As recitedlajjā puṣṭistathā tuṣṭistvaṃ śāntiḥ kṣāntireva ca |
khaḍginī śūlinī ghorā gadinī cakriṇī tathā ||

Separatedlajjā | puṣṭiḥ | tathā | tuṣṭiḥ | tvam | śāntiḥ | kṣāntiḥ | eva | ca
khaḍginī | śūlinī | ghorā | gadinī | cakriṇī | tathā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
लज्जाlajjāmodesty, the sense of shame that restrainsf. nom. sg. of लज्जा (ā-stem); predicate of an understood असि with त्वम्
पुष्टिःpuṣṭiḥnourishment, thrivingf. nom. sg. of पुष्टि (i-stem; √पुष् + ktin)
तथाtathālikewise, andindeclinable (adverb of manner, here connective)
तुष्टिःtuṣṭiḥcontentmentf. nom. sg. of तुष्टि (i-stem; √तुष् + ktin)
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject (the verb असि is understood throughout the hymn)
शान्तिःśāntiḥpeace, tranquillityf. nom. sg. of शान्ति (i-stem; √शम् + ktin)
क्षान्तिःkṣāntiḥforbearance, patiencef. nom. sg. of क्षान्ति (i-stem; √क्षम् + ktin)
एवevaindeed, tooindeclinable (emphatic particle)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
खड्गिनीkhaḍginībearing the swordf. nom. sg. of खड्गिन् (possessive taddhita -इन् on खड्ग ‘sword’; feminine in -ई)
शूलिनीśūlinībearing the trident (or spear)f. nom. sg. of शूलिन् (possessive -इन् on शूल)
घोराghorāterrible, dreadfulf. nom. sg. of the adjective घोर
गदिनीgadinībearing the macef. nom. sg. of गदिन् (possessive -इन् on गदा; the stem-final ā drops before the suffix)
चक्रिणीcakriṇībearing the discusf. nom. sg. of चक्रिन् (possessive -इन् on चक्र)
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, connective)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पुष्टिस्तथा puṣṭistathā → पुष्टिः + तथा
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. The same change gives तुष्टिस्त्वं.
  • त्वं शान्तिः tvaṃ śāntiḥ → त्वम् + शान्तिः
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.
  • क्षान्तिरेव kṣāntireva → क्षान्तिः + एव
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before a vowel, appears as r.

C. Morphology

  • खड्गिनी khaḍginī — Possessive taddhita -इन् (‘having’) on खड्ग gives खड्गिन् ‘sword-bearer’; the feminine adds -ई. The verse builds five such forms in a row (खड्गिनी, शूलिनी, गदिनी, चक्रिणी, and in 1.81 शङ्खिनी, चापिनी).
  • चक्रिणी cakriṇī — The n of the suffix turns retroflex (ṇ) because r stands earlier in the same word with nothing to block it; compare गदिनी and शूलिनी, where there is no r.
  • क्षान्तिः kṣāntiḥ — Action noun in -ति (ktin) from √क्षम् ‘to endure’; the nasal of the root appears as न् before त. शान्तिः from √शम् is formed the same way.

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वं लज्जा पुष्टिः तथा तुष्टिः, त्वं शान्तिः क्षान्तिः एव च (असि)। (त्वं) खड्गिनी शूलिनी घोरा गदिनी तथा चक्रिणी (असि)।

tvaṃ lajjā puṣṭiḥ tathā tuṣṭiḥ, tvaṃ śāntiḥ kṣāntiḥ eva ca (asi) | (tvaṃ) khaḍginī śūlinī ghorā gadinī tathā cakriṇī (asi) |

A list of predicate nominatives with त्वम् and an understood असि, continuing the list of 1.77–79: first five virtues, then a run of feminine possessive adjectives naming her weapons.

6Translation

Literal(You are) modesty, nourishment and likewise contentment; you (are) peace and forbearance too. (You are) sword-bearing, trident-bearing, terrible, mace-bearing and likewise discus-bearing;

ReadableYou are modesty, nourishment and contentment; you are peace, and patience too. You bear the sword and the trident; you are terrible; you carry the mace and the discus;

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā’s list turns in mid-verse from inner qualities to weapons. Modesty, nourishment, contentment, peace and forbearance are the gentlest powers of a person; the next line arms the same Goddess and calls her घोरा, ‘terrible’. The hymn makes no division between the two.

Traditional interpretationSeveral of these qualities return in the yā devī hymn of chapter 5 as forms in which the Devī abides in all beings: क्षान्ति (5.38-40), लज्जा (5.44-46), शान्ति (5.47-49) and तुष्टि (5.68-70). The weapons named in 1.80–81 match the dhyāna of Mahākālī that the Gita Press pāṭha sets before the first carita (sword, discus, mace, arrows, bow, iron club, trident, bhuśuṇḍī, a head, conch); the hymn names all of them except the head. The dhyāna is liturgical tradition, not part of the Purāṇa.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same names as single epithets: 443 तुष्टिः, 444 पुष्टिः, 447 शान्तिः and 740 लज्जा. The overlap is in vocabulary for the Goddess’s powers; the two texts are separate works.

1.81Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शङ्खिनी चापिनी बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघायुधा।
सौम्या सौम्यतराशेषसौम्येभ्यस्त्वतिसुन्दरी ॥ ८१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शङ्खिनी | चापिनी | बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघायुधासौम्या | सौम्यतरा | अशेषसौम्येभ्यः | तु | अतिसुन्दरी

3IAST

As recitedśaṅkhinī cāpinī bāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāyudhā |
saumyā saumyatarāśeṣasaumyebhyastvatisundarī ||

Separatedśaṅkhinī | cāpinī | bāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāyudhā
saumyā | saumyatarā | aśeṣasaumyebhyaḥ | tu | atisundarī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शङ्खिनीśaṅkhinībearing the conchf. nom. sg. of शङ्खिन् (possessive -इन् on शङ्ख)
चापिनीcāpinībearing the bowf. nom. sg. of चापिन् (possessive -इन् on चाप)
बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघायुधाbāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāyudhāwhose weapons are arrows, the bhuśuṇḍī and the iron clubf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
सौम्याsaumyāgentle, benign (lit. ‘moon-like’, from सोम)f. nom. sg. of the adjective सौम्य (taddhita from सोम)
सौम्यतराsaumyatarāmore gentlef. nom. sg. of सौम्यतर, comparative (taddhita -तर) of सौम्य; governs the ablative अशेषसौम्येभ्यः
अशेषसौम्येभ्यःaśeṣasaumyebhyaḥthan all gentle thingsn. abl. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of comparison
तुtuand, moreoverindeclinable (particle, here connective)
अतिसुन्दरीatisundarīexceedingly beautifulf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सौम्यतराशेष saumyatarāśeṣa → सौम्यतरा + अशेष(सौम्येभ्यः)
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. Two words are fused, so the line looks like one long compound; it is not one.
  • सौम्येभ्यस्त्वतिसुन्दरी saumyebhyastvatisundarī → अशेषसौम्येभ्यः + तु + अतिसुन्दरी
    visarga and vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Visarga before t becomes s; then u before a becomes v (तु + अति → त्वति).
  • परिघायुधा parighāyudhā → परिघ + आयुधा
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघायुधा bāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāyudhā — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघ)
    Members: बाण + भुशुण्डी + परिघ + आयुध · Vigraha: बाणाः च भुशुण्डी च परिघः च बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघाः; ते आयुधानि यस्याः सा bāṇāḥ ca bhuśuṇḍī ca parighaḥ ca bāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāḥ; te āyudhāni yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose weapons are arrows, bhuśuṇḍī and iron club”; in the verse: armed with arrows, the bhuśuṇḍī and the iron club; भुशुण्डी is a missile weapon whose exact form is not certain
  • अशेषसौम्येभ्यः aśeṣasaumyebhyaḥ — karmadhāraya (अशेष itself a nañ-bahuvrīhi: न विद्यते शेषः यस्य तत्)
    Members: अशेष + सौम्य · Vigraha: अशेषाणि सौम्यानि, तेभ्यः aśeṣāṇi saumyāni, tebhyaḥ
    Literally “than gentle things without remainder”; in the verse: than every gentle thing there is
  • अतिसुन्दरी atisundarī — prādi tatpuruṣa (the prefix अति ‘beyond, exceedingly’ as first member)
    Members: अति + सुन्दरी · Vigraha: अतिशयेन सुन्दरी atiśayena sundarī
    Literally “beautiful beyond measure”; in the verse: surpassingly beautiful

C. Morphology

  • सौम्यतरा saumyatarā — Comparative in -तर (taddhita) on सौम्य, feminine -तरा. The thing surpassed stands in the ablative: सौम्यतरा अशेषसौम्येभ्यः ‘gentler than all gentle things’.
  • सौम्या saumyā — Taddhita from सोम ‘the moon (Soma)’ with vṛddhi of the first syllable: ‘of the moon’s nature’, hence cool, mild, benign — the opposite of घोरा in 1.80.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(त्वं) शङ्खिनी चापिनी बाणभुशुण्डीपरिघायुधा (असि)। सौम्या, अशेषसौम्येभ्यः सौम्यतरा, अतिसुन्दरी तु (असि)।

(tvaṃ) śaṅkhinī cāpinī bāṇabhuśuṇḍīparighāyudhā (asi) | saumyā, aśeṣasaumyebhyaḥ saumyatarā, atisundarī tu (asi) |

The weapon-list of 1.80 ends; the second line sets a gentle triad against it. The ablative अशेषसौम्येभ्यः is taken with the comparative सौम्यतरा, which requires one. It can also be read with अतिसुन्दरी (‘more beautiful than all gentle things’); the comparative is the stronger claim on it.

6Translation

Literalconch-bearing, bow-bearing, having arrows, bhuśuṇḍī and iron club as weapons; gentle, more gentle than all gentle things, and exceedingly beautiful.

Readableyou bear the conch and the bow, and your weapons are arrows, the bhuśuṇḍī and the iron club. Yet you are gentle — gentler than every gentle thing — and beautiful beyond measure.

‘Yet’ renders the contrast the verse makes by placing सौम्या straight after the weapons; the Sanskrit has only तु ‘and, moreover’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe arming of the Goddess is completed — conch, bow, arrows, bhuśuṇḍī and iron club — and in the same breath she is called gentle, gentler than all gentle things, surpassingly beautiful. The terrible one of 1.80 (घोरा) and the gentle one here (सौम्या) are one.

Traditional interpretationThe pairing of the fierce (घोर) and the gentle (सौम्य) is basic to the Śākta picture of the Goddess: the same Devī is worshipped in terrible and benign forms. This hymn states the pairing outright before the stories of chapters 2–13 show it in action.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has सौम्या as name 910. The adjective is common; the shared word shows a shared idiom of praise, not a borrowing.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the hymn does not ask the reader to choose between a gentle and a fierce divinity. Protection and tenderness are offered as aspects of one care.

1.82Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

परापराणां परमा त्वमेव परमेश्वरी।
यच्च किंचित्क्वचिद्वस्तु सदसद्वाखिलात्मिके ॥ ८२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

परापराणाम् | परमा | त्वम् | एव | परमेश्वरीयत् | च | किंचित् | क्वचित् | वस्तु | सदसत् | वा | अखिलात्मिके

3IAST

As recitedparāparāṇāṃ paramā tvameva parameśvarī |
yacca kiṃcitkvacidvastu sadasadvākhilātmike ||

Separatedparāparāṇām | paramā | tvam | eva | parameśvarī
yat | ca | kiṃcit | kvacit | vastu | sadasat | vā | akhilātmike

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
परापराणाम्parāparāṇāmof (all things) higher and lowerm./n. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); partitive genitive with परमा
परमाparamāthe highest, supremef. nom. sg. of the superlative adjective परम
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.
एवevaalone, indeedindeclinable (restrictive particle)
परमेश्वरीparameśvarīthe supreme Sovereign, the supreme Īśvarīf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
यत्yatwhatever, whichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; correlative of तस्य in 1.83
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
किंचित्kiṃcitany (thing) at allindefinite pronoun, n. nom. sg. (किम् + the particle चित्); with यत्: ‘whatever’
क्वचित्kvacitanywhereindeclinable (indefinite adverb of place: क्व + चित्)
वस्तुvastuthing, entityn. nom. sg. of वस्तु (u-stem)
सदसत्sadasatexistent or non-existentn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वस्तु
वाvāor, whetherindeclinable (disjunctive particle)
अखिलात्मिकेakhilātmikeO Self of all, O you whose nature is everythingf. voc. sg. of अखिलात्मिका; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यच्च yacca → यत् + च
    consonant sandhi (palatal assimilation) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Final t before c becomes c.
  • क्वचिद्वस्तु kvacidvastu → क्वचित् + वस्तु
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t before the voiced v becomes d. The same change gives सदसद्वा. In किंचित्क्वचित्, t before the unvoiced k stays t (खरि च, 8.4.55).
  • सदसद्वाखिलात्मिके sadasadvākhilātmike → सदसत् + वा + अखिलात्मिके
    consonant and vowel sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    t → d before v; then ā + a → ā (वा + अखिल → वाखिल).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • परापराणाम् parāparāṇām — itaretara dvandva
    Members: पर + अपर · Vigraha: पराणि च अपराणि च परापराणि, तेषाम् parāṇi ca aparāṇi ca parāparāṇi, teṣām
    Literally “of the higher and the lower”; in the verse: of all things, high and low; the Goddess is the highest even among the highest
    Alternative: पर and अपर can also be taken as the transcendent and the immanent (the supreme and the manifest), so ‘supreme over both the transcendent and the manifest’. The compound is the same dvandva either way.
  • परमेश्वरी parameśvarī — karmadhāraya
    Members: परमा + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: परमा चासौ ईश्वरी च paramā cāsau īśvarī ca
    Literally “the supreme sovereign lady”; in the verse: the supreme Īśvarī, the Goddess as ruler of all; internal sandhi a + ī → e (आद्गुणः, 6.1.87), the feminine first member taking its masculine form
  • सदसत् sadasat — samāhāra dvandva (neuter singular), here used as an adjective of वस्तु
    Members: सत् + असत् · Vigraha: सत् च असत् च sat ca asat ca
    Literally “being-and-non-being”; in the verse: whatever is, or is not; with वा the two are alternatives: ‘existent or non-existent’
  • अखिलात्मिके akhilātmike — bahuvrīhi (with the samāsānta suffix -क; feminine -इका)
    Members: अखिल + आत्मन् · Vigraha: अखिलम् आत्मा यस्याः सा अखिलात्मिका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ akhilam ātmā yasyāḥ sā akhilātmikā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “O you whose self is the whole”; in the verse: O you who are the self of everything — the same formation as स्वरात्मिका (1.73) and मात्रात्मिका (1.74)

C. Morphology

  • किंचित् kiṃcit — The interrogative किम् with the particle चित् becomes an indefinite: ‘anything’. With the relative यत् it generalises: यत् किंचित् ‘whatever at all’. क्वचित् (क्व ‘where?’ + चित्) is the adverb ‘anywhere’.
  • अखिलात्मिके akhilātmike — Vocative singular of an ā-stem feminine: the final ā becomes e (अखिलात्मिका → अखिलात्मिके), as in देवि from देवी with shortening.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अखिलात्मिके, त्वम् एव परापराणां परमा परमेश्वरी (असि)। यत् च किंचित् सत् असत् वा वस्तु क्वचित् (अस्ति) —

(he) akhilātmike, tvam eva parāparāṇāṃ paramā parameśvarī (asi) | yat ca kiṃcit sat asat vā vastu kvacit (asti) —

The first line is a complete statement. The second opens a relative clause (यत् … वस्तु) whose correlative तस्य सर्वस्य comes in 1.83.

6Translation

LiteralOf the higher and the lower the highest, you alone are the supreme Īśvarī. And whatever thing (there is) anywhere, existent or non-existent, O Self of all —

ReadableHighest of all things high and low, you alone are the supreme Sovereign. And whatever thing there is, anywhere, whether it is or is not, O Soul of all —

The second line is completed by 1.83 (‘of all that, you are the power’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā moves from listing names to a claim about everything: she is the highest over high and low alike, and the second line begins to say that whatever exists, or does not exist, belongs to her. The sentence finishes in 1.83.

Traditional interpretationसत् and असत् are often explained in Indian thought as the manifest (the effect) and the unmanifest (the cause). Read so, the verse leaves nothing, manifest or latent, outside the Goddess.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 790 परापरा, 396 परमेश्वरी and 661 सदसद्रूपधारिणी (‘bearing the forms of being and non-being’). The concepts are the same; the formulations belong to each text.

1.83Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्य सर्वस्य या शक्तिः सा त्वं किं स्तूयसे तदा।
यया त्वया जगत्स्रष्टा जगत्पात्यत्ति यो जगत् ॥ ८३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्य | सर्वस्य | या | शक्तिः | सा | त्वम् | किम् | स्तूयसे | तदायया | त्वया | जगत्स्रष्टा | जगत् | पाति | अत्ति | यः | जगत्

3IAST

As recitedtasya sarvasya yā śaktiḥ sā tvaṃ kiṃ stūyase tadā |
yayā tvayā jagatsraṣṭā jagatpātyatti yo jagat ||

Separatedtasya | sarvasya | yā | śaktiḥ | sā | tvam | kim | stūyase | tadā
yayā | tvayā | jagatsraṣṭā | jagat | pāti | atti | yaḥ | jagat

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तस्यtasyaof thatpronoun तद्, n. gen. sg.; correlative of यत् in 1.82
सर्वस्यsarvasyaof allpronominal adjective सर्व, n. gen. sg.; agrees with तस्य
याyāwhichrelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.
शक्तिःśaktiḥpower, energyf. nom. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem; √शक् + ktin)
साsāthat (power)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative of या
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.
किम्kimhow? (rhetorical)interrogative किम्, used adverbially: ‘how, in what way’
स्तूयसेstūyaseare you praised, can you be praised√स्तु (adādi, 2U, ‘to praise’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana
तदाtadāthen, in that caseindeclinable (adverb of time, here inferential)
ययाyayāby whomrelative pronoun यद्, f. instr. sg.; agent of नीतः in 1.84
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; in apposition to यया
जगत्स्रष्टाjagatsraṣṭācreator of the worldm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate in the relative clause of यः
जगत्jagatthe worldn. acc. sg. of जगत्; object of पाति
पातिpātiprotects√पा (adādi, 2P, ‘to protect’), laṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अत्तिattidevours√अद् (adādi, 2P, ‘to eat’), laṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
यःyaḥhe whorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.; correlative of सः in 1.84
जगत्jagatthe worldn. acc. sg.; object of अत्ति

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सा त्वं किं स्तूयसे sā tvaṃ kiṃ stūyase → सा + त्वम् + किम् + स्तूयसे
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra, twice.
  • जगत्पात्यत्ति jagatpātyatti → जगत् + पाति + अत्ति
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before a becomes y (पाति + अत्ति → पात्यत्ति). The t of जगत् stays unvoiced before p (खरि च, 8.4.55); the words are only written together.
  • यो जगत् yo jagat → यः + जगत्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • जगत्स्रष्टा jagatsraṣṭā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + स्रष्टृ · Vigraha: जगतः स्रष्टा jagataḥ sraṣṭā
    Literally “the world’s emitter”; in the verse: the creator of the world
    Alternative: It can also be read as two words, जगत् (acc.) + स्रष्टा, with the agent noun taking an object like a verb, which keeps the line’s three जगत् parallel (creates / protects / devours the world). The sense is the same; the printed text joins all three, so print decides nothing.

C. Morphology

  • स्तूयसे stūyase — Passive present of √स्तु: the root vowel lengthens before the passive marker -य- (स्तू-य-), with the ātmanepada second-person ending -से. ‘Are you praised?’ — here rhetorical: ‘how can you be praised?’
  • अत्ति atti — √अद् (adādi) adds the ending directly to the root: अद् + ति → अत्ति, with d unvoiced before t.
  • जगत्स्रष्टा jagatsraṣṭā — स्रष्टृ ‘creator’ is the agent noun (tṛc) of √सृज्; nom. sg. स्रष्टा. The root’s vowel and r change places (सृज् → स्रष्) before the suffix.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तस्य सर्वस्य या शक्तिः, सा त्वम् (असि)। तदा (त्वं) किं स्तूयसे? यया त्वया, यः जगत्स्रष्टा (यः) जगत् पाति (यः) जगत् अत्ति, (सः अपि निद्रावशं नीतः) —

tasya sarvasya yā śaktiḥ, sā tvam (asi) | tadā (tvaṃ) kiṃ stūyase? yayā tvayā, yaḥ jagatsraṣṭā (yaḥ) jagat pāti (yaḥ) jagat atti, (saḥ api nidrāvaśaṃ nītaḥ) —

Line one completes 1.82 with the correlative pair या … सा. Line two opens a long relative clause: यया त्वया is the agent of नीतः in 1.84, and यः … जगत् describes the one who was put to sleep.

6Translation

LiteralOf all that, the power which (it has) — that (power) you (are). How then are you to be praised? By whom — by you — he who (is) the creator of the world, (who) protects the world, (who) devours the world,

Readableof all that, you are the power. How then can anyone praise you? You, by whom he who creates the world, sustains the world and devours the world

The sentence continues into 1.84: ‘— even he has been brought under the sway of sleep’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe argument of the hymn is made plain: if she is the power (शक्ति) of everything that is, then every act — praise included — is already hers, and no praise can reach her. The second line names the one she has overcome: the god who creates, preserves and consumes the world, i.e. the sleeping Viṣṇu.

Traditional interpretationIn Śākta thought śakti is the power by which any being acts, inseparable from it. The verse applies the idea to the gods themselves: even the agent of creation, preservation and destruction acts through her, and when she withdraws (as sleep) he does not act at all.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) gives the three acts to the Goddess directly: 264 सृष्टिकर्त्री (creator), 266 गोप्त्री (protector), 268 संहारिणी (destroyer). The Devī Māhātmya keeps them with a god and makes her the power behind him.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the hymn admits its own limit. To say ‘how can you be praised?’ in the middle of praise is a way of keeping words honest about what they cannot hold.

1.84Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सोऽपि निद्रावशं नीतः कस्त्वां स्तोतुमिहेश्वरः।
विष्णुः शरीरग्रहणमहमीशान एव च ॥ ८४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सः | अपि | निद्रावशम् | नीतः | कः | त्वाम् | स्तोतुम् | इह | ईश्वरःविष्णुः | शरीरग्रहणम् | अहम् | ईशानः | एव | च

3IAST

As recitedso'pi nidrāvaśaṃ nītaḥ kastvāṃ stotumiheśvaraḥ |
viṣṇuḥ śarīragrahaṇamahamīśāna eva ca ||

Separatedsaḥ | api | nidrāvaśam | nītaḥ | kaḥ | tvām | stotum | iha | īśvaraḥ
viṣṇuḥ | śarīragrahaṇam | aham | īśānaḥ | eva | ca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; correlative of यः in 1.83
अपिapievenindeclinable (emphatic particle)
निद्रावशम्nidrāvaśaminto the power of sleepm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); goal of नीतः
नीतःnītaḥhas been led, broughtpast passive participle (kta) of √नी (bhvādi, 1U, ‘to lead’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
कःkaḥwho?interrogative किम्, m. nom. sg.
त्वाम्tvāmyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोतुम्
स्तोतुम्stotumto praiseinfinitive (tumun) of √स्तु
इहihahere, in this worldindeclinable (adverb of place)
ईश्वरःīśvaraḥable, capable (lit. ‘master’)m. nom. sg. of ईश्वर; predicate of कः, construed with the infinitive
विष्णुःviṣṇuḥViṣṇum. nom. sg. of विष्णु (u-stem); first subject of कारिताः in 1.85
शरीरग्रहणम्śarīragrahaṇamthe taking on of a bodyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object retained with the passive causative कारिताः (1.85)
अहम्ahamI (Brahmā)first-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.
ईशानःīśānaḥĪśāna (Śiva)m. nom. sg. of ईशान (originally a present participle of √ईश् ‘to rule’)
एवevatoo, alsoindeclinable (particle)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सोऽपि so'pi → सः + अपि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • कस्त्वां kastvāṃ → कः + त्वाम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • स्तोतुमिहेश्वरः stotumiheśvaraḥ → स्तोतुम् + इह + ईश्वरः
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e (इह + ईश्वरः → इहेश्वरः); the m of स्तोतुम् joins the following vowel unchanged.
  • ईशान एव īśāna eva → ईशानः + एव
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -aḥ before a vowel other than a: the visarga becomes y, which is dropped, leaving the vowels apart.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • निद्रावशम् nidrāvaśam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: निद्रा + वश · Vigraha: निद्रायाः वशः, तम् nidrāyāḥ vaśaḥ, tam
    Literally “the control of sleep”; in the verse: the sway of sleep; वशं नी ‘bring under control’ is an idiom
  • शरीरग्रहणम् śarīragrahaṇam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शरीर + ग्रहण · Vigraha: शरीरस्य ग्रहणम् śarīrasya grahaṇam
    Literally “the taking of a body”; in the verse: embodiment, assuming bodily form

C. Morphology

  • नीतः nītaḥ — Past passive participle of √नी used as a finite verb (‘has been led’). With नी the destination stays in the accusative in the passive (निद्रावशम्), while the person led becomes the subject (सः).
  • स्तोतुम् stotum — Infinitive of purpose with ईश्वरः ‘able’: कः … स्तोतुम् ईश्वरः ‘who is able to praise?’
  • ईश्वरः īśvaraḥ — Literally ‘master, lord’; with an infinitive it means ‘having power to’. The word picks up परमेश्वरी of 1.82: she is the Īśvarī, and no one is ईश्वर to praise her.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सः अपि निद्रावशं नीतः। इह त्वां स्तोतुं कः ईश्वरः? विष्णुः, अहम्, ईशानः एव च शरीरग्रहणं (त्वया कारिताः) —

saḥ api nidrāvaśaṃ nītaḥ | iha tvāṃ stotuṃ kaḥ īśvaraḥ? viṣṇuḥ, aham, īśānaḥ eva ca śarīragrahaṇaṃ (tvayā kāritāḥ) —

The first line completes the relative clause of 1.83 (यया त्वया … सः अपि … नीतः) and asks a rhetorical question. The second line names three subjects whose verb, कारिताः, comes in 1.85.

6Translation

Literal— even he has been led into the control of sleep. Who here is able to praise you? Viṣṇu, I and Īśāna too, (have been made by you to undertake) the taking of bodies —

Readable— even he has been brought under the sway of sleep. Who here has the power to praise you? Viṣṇu, I myself and Īśāna

The second line continues into 1.85: ‘have been made by you to take on bodies’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe creator, preserver and consumer of the world lies asleep by her power, so no one is fit to praise her. Brahmā then names the three gods — Viṣṇu, himself and Īśāna (Śiva) — and in the next verse says that their very bodies are her doing.

Traditional interpretationŚākta readers take 1.84–85 as a foundation for the Goddess’s priority over the three gods: the three who create, preserve and dissolve are themselves made to take form by her. The speaker is Brahmā, one of the three, which gives the admission its weight.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the most capable figures in the story are shown to depend on a power they did not make. The hymn turns the gods’ own testimony into a lesson in humility.

1.85Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कारितास्ते यतोऽतस्त्वां कः स्तोतुं शक्तिमान् भवेत्।
सा त्वमित्थं प्रभावैः स्वैरुदारैर्देवि संस्तुता ॥ ८५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कारिताः | ते | यतः | अतः | त्वाम् | कः | स्तोतुम् | शक्तिमान् | भवेत्सा | त्वम् | इत्थम् | प्रभावैः | स्वैः | उदारैः | देवि | संस्तुता

3IAST

As recitedkāritāste yato'tastvāṃ kaḥ stotuṃ śaktimān bhavet |
sā tvamitthaṃ prabhāvaiḥ svairudārairdevi saṃstutā ||

Separatedkāritāḥ | te | yataḥ | ataḥ | tvām | kaḥ | stotum | śaktimān | bhavet
sā | tvam | ittham | prabhāvaiḥ | svaiḥ | udāraiḥ | devi | saṃstutā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कारिताःkāritāḥhave been made (to undertake)past passive participle (kta) of the causative of √कृ (कारय-), m. nom. pl.; predicate of विष्णुः, अहम्, ईशानः (1.84), with शरीरग्रहणम् as retained object
तेteby youenclitic form of युष्मद् (gen./dat. sg.), here in the sense of the agent of कारिताः
यतःyataḥsince, becauseindeclinable (relative adverb: यद् + -तस्); correlative of अतः
अतःataḥthereforeindeclinable (demonstrative adverb: इदम् + -तस्)
त्वाम्tvāmyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोतुम्
कःkaḥwho?interrogative किम्, m. nom. sg.
स्तोतुम्stotumto praiseinfinitive (tumun) of √स्तु
शक्तिमान्śaktimāncapable, having the powerm. nom. sg. of शक्तिमत् (possessive taddhita -मत् on शक्ति)
भवेत्bhavetcould be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), vidhiliṅ (optative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
साsāshe, that (one)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; in apposition to त्वम्: ‘you, such as you are’
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of the imperative मोहय in 1.86
इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
प्रभावैःprabhāvaiḥby (the telling of) your powersm. instr. pl. of प्रभाव
स्वैःsvaiḥyour ownreflexive possessive adjective स्व, m. instr. pl.; agrees with प्रभावैः
उदारैःudāraiḥexalted, noblem. instr. pl. of the adjective उदार; agrees with प्रभावैः
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी (final ī shortened)
संस्तुताsaṃstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्तु, f. nom. sg.; agrees with सा त्वम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कारितास्ते kāritāste → कारिताः + ते
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. The same change gives अतस्त्वां.
  • यतोऽतस्त्वां yato'tastvāṃ → यतः + अतः + त्वाम्
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o and the following a is elided (avagraha): यतोऽतः. Then अतः before t becomes अतस्.
  • स्वैरुदारैर्देवि svairudārairdevi → स्वैः + उदारैः + देवि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā (here ai), the visarga appears as r before a vowel and before a voiced consonant.
  • त्वमित्थं प्रभावैः tvamitthaṃ prabhāvaiḥ → त्वम् + इत्थम् + प्रभावैः
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m joins the vowel of इत्थम्; the m of इत्थम् becomes anusvāra before p.

C. Morphology

  • कारिताः kāritāḥ — Causative (णिच्) of √कृ, कारयति ‘makes (someone) do’, in the passive participle. In the passive of a causative the person made to act becomes the subject (विष्णुः, अहम्, ईशानः) and the thing done stays accusative (शरीरग्रहणम्). The plural masculine covers all three.
  • ते te — The enclitic ते is formally genitive or dative singular of युष्मद्. Purāṇic and epic verse use it with past participles for the agent, ‘by you’; a genitive of the agent with the participle is the grammatical explanation. Reading ते as the plural pronoun ‘they (were made)’ is also possible, but it sits awkwardly with अहम्.
  • भवेत् bhavet — Optative of possibility in a rhetorical question: कः … शक्तिमान् भवेत् ‘who could be able?’
  • शक्तिमान् śaktimān — Possessive -मत् on शक्ति; nom. sg. masculine -मान्. The word echoes शक्तिः of 1.83: she is the शक्ति of all, so who is शक्तिमान् to praise her?

D. Anvaya — prose order

(विष्णुः अहम् ईशानः च शरीरग्रहणं) ते यतः कारिताः, अतः त्वां स्तोतुं कः शक्तिमान् भवेत्? (हे) देवि, सा त्वम् इत्थं स्वैः उदारैः प्रभावैः संस्तुता —

(viṣṇuḥ aham īśānaḥ ca śarīragrahaṇaṃ) te yataḥ kāritāḥ, ataḥ tvāṃ stotuṃ kaḥ śaktimān bhavet? (he) devi, sā tvam itthaṃ svaiḥ udāraiḥ prabhāvaiḥ saṃstutā —

The first line completes the sentence begun in 1.84 with a यतः … अतः (‘since … therefore’) pair. The second line opens a new sentence: सा त्वम् … संस्तुता is the subject of the imperatives in 1.86.

6Translation

Literalsince (we) have been made by you (to take bodies), therefore who could be able to praise you? You, (such as you are), thus praised, O Devī, by (the telling of) your own exalted powers,

Readablehave been made by you to take on bodily form — so who could have the power to praise you? O Devī, praised in this way for your own exalted powers,

The first half finishes 1.84; the second half begins the request that 1.86 states.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā ends the praise with its reason: the three gods owe even their bodies to her, so none of them can praise her adequately. The second line turns from praise to petition: ‘you, thus praised …’ leads straight into the request of 1.86.

Traditional interpretationThe hymn closes in the classical manner of a stotra: an admission that praise falls short, then the request. Here the praise has been nothing but a recital of her own powers (प्रभावैः स्वैः) — she is praised with what is already hers.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: Brahmā asks only after acknowledging what he is. The order — recognition first, request second — is the shape of the whole hymn.

1.86Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मोहयैतौ दुराधर्षावसुरौ मधुकैटभौ।
प्रबोधं च जगत्स्वामी नीयतामच्युतो लघु ॥ ८६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मोहय | एतौ | दुराधर्षौ | असुरौ | मधुकैटभौप्रबोधम् | च | जगत्स्वामी | नीयताम् | अच्युतः | लघु

3IAST

As recitedmohayaitau durādharṣāvasurau madhukaiṭabhau |
prabodhaṃ ca jagatsvāmī nīyatāmacyuto laghu ||

Separatedmohaya | etau | durādharṣau | asurau | madhukaiṭabhau
prabodham | ca | jagatsvāmī | nīyatām | acyutaḥ | laghu

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मोहयmohayadelude, bewildercausative of √मुह् (divādi, 4P, ‘to be confused’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एतौetauthese twopronoun एतद्, m. acc. du.
दुराधर्षौdurādharṣauhard to assail, invinciblem. acc. du. of दुराधर्ष (दुर् + आ + √धृष् + kṛt -अ in the sense ‘hard to be done’)
असुरौasurauthe two asurasm. acc. du. of असुर
मधुकैटभौmadhukaiṭabhauMadhu and Kaiṭabham. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to असुरौ
प्रबोधम्prabodhamto waking, to wakefulnessm. acc. sg. of प्रबोध (प्र + √बुध्); goal retained with the passive नीयताम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
जगत्स्वामीjagatsvāmīthe lord of the worldm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to अच्युतः
नीयताम्nīyatāmlet (him) be brought√नी (bhvādi, 1U, ‘to lead’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
अच्युतःacyutaḥAcyuta, the Unfallen (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of नीयताम्
लघुlaghuquicklyn. acc. sg. of the adjective लघु ‘light, swift’, used adverbially

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मोहयैतौ mohayaitau → मोहय + एतौ
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.
  • दुराधर्षावसुरौ durādharṣāvasurau → दुराधर्षौ + असुरौ
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv.
  • नीयतामच्युतो लघु nīyatāmacyuto laghu → नीयताम् + अच्युतः + लघु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced l becomes -o. The final m of नीयताम् joins the following vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मधुकैटभौ madhukaiṭabhau — itaretara dvandva
    Members: मधु + कैटभ · Vigraha: मधुः च कैटभः च madhuḥ ca kaiṭabhaḥ ca
    Literally “Madhu and Kaiṭabha”; in the verse: the two asuras, named as a pair; the dual counts both
  • जगत्स्वामी jagatsvāmī — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + स्वामिन् · Vigraha: जगतः स्वामी jagataḥ svāmī
    Literally “owner of the world”; in the verse: lord of the world, i.e. Viṣṇu
  • अच्युतः acyutaḥ — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: न + च्युत · Vigraha: न च्युतः na cyutaḥ
    Literally “not fallen”; in the verse: the Unfailing One, a name of Viṣṇu

C. Morphology

  • मोहय mohaya — Causative stem मोहय- (√मुह् with guṇa + णिच्): ‘cause to be bewildered’. The same root gives महामोहा (1.78) and मोह in the king’s question (1.44) and Medhas’s answer (1.53, 1.55).
  • नीयताम् nīyatām — Passive imperative: नी + -य- + ātmanepada ending -ताम्. With नी the person led becomes the subject (अच्युतः) and the destination stays accusative (प्रबोधम्).
  • दुराधर्षौ durādharṣau — दुर्- ‘hard to’ with the action noun of आ + √धृष् ‘to assail’: ‘hard to attack’. Grammarians form such words with a special kṛt affix after दुर्, सु and ईषत्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे देवि) एतौ दुराधर्षौ असुरौ मधुकैटभौ मोहय। जगत्स्वामी अच्युतः च लघु प्रबोधं नीयताम्।

(he devi) etau durādharṣau asurau madhukaiṭabhau mohaya | jagatsvāmī acyutaḥ ca laghu prabodhaṃ nīyatām |

Two requests: an active imperative to the Goddess (मोहय) and a passive imperative about Viṣṇu (नीयताम्), joined by च.

6Translation

Literaldelude these two hard-to-assail asuras, Madhu and Kaiṭabha; and let Acyuta, lord of the world, be brought quickly to waking,

Readablebewilder these two invincible asuras, Madhu and Kaiṭabha, and let Acyuta, the lord of the world, be woken quickly,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe request at last: two things, one for each side. Delude the asuras; wake Viṣṇu. Both are acts of the same power — the Goddess as Mahāmāyā clouds the asuras’ minds and, as Yoganidrā, releases the god.

Traditional interpretationThe dhyāna of Mahākālī in the Gita Press pāṭha sums up this scene: she is the one whom the lotus-born (Brahmā) praised, when Hari slept, for the slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha (यामस्तौत्स्वपिते हरौ कमलजो हन्तुं मधुं कैटभम्). The dhyāna is liturgical, but it is drawn from these verses.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the same power is asked both to confuse and to awaken. The text does not treat illusion and clarity as the work of rival powers.

1.87Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

बोधश्च क्रियतामस्य हन्तुमेतौ महासुरौ ॥ ८७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

बोधः | च | क्रियताम् | अस्य | हन्तुम् | एतौ | महासुरौ

3IAST

As recitedbodhaśca kriyatāmasya hantumetau mahāsurau ||

Separatedbodhaḥ | ca | kriyatām | asya | hantum | etau | mahāsurau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
बोधःbodhaḥawareness, wakefulnessm. nom. sg. of बोध (√बुध्); subject of क्रियताम्
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
क्रियताम्kriyatāmlet (it) be made√कृ (tanādi, 8U), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
अस्यasyahis (Viṣṇu’s)pronoun इदम्, m. gen. sg.
हन्तुम्hantumto killinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi, 2P)
एतौetauthese twopronoun एतद्, m. acc. du.
महासुरौmahāsuraugreat asurasm. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); object of हन्तुम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • बोधश्च bodhaśca → बोधः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.
  • क्रियतामस्य kriyatāmasya → क्रियताम् + अस्य
    no change (final m before a vowel) · final m joins a following vowel
    Final m before a vowel is not changed to anusvāra; it is simply written with the vowel. The same in हन्तुमेतौ.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरौ mahāsurau — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तौ च तौ असुरौ च mahāntau ca tau asurau ca
    Literally “great asuras”; in the verse: the two mighty asuras; महत् takes the form महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya, and महा + असुर gives महासुर (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101)

C. Morphology

  • क्रियताम् kriyatām — Passive imperative of √कृ: the root takes the form क्रि- before the passive -य- (क्रियते ‘is done’), with the ending -ताम्.
  • हन्तुम् hantum — Infinitive of purpose: ‘(so that he may) kill’. The objects एतौ महासुरौ are accusative, governed by the infinitive.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अस्य बोधः च एतौ महासुरौ हन्तुं क्रियताम्।

asya bodhaḥ ca etau mahāsurau hantuṃ kriyatām |

A second passive imperative, parallel to नीयताम् in 1.86, with an infinitive of purpose.

6Translation

Literaland let his awareness be made, to kill these two great asuras.

Readableand let him be roused to slay these two great asuras.

बोधः क्रियताम् (‘let awareness be made’) is rendered ‘let him be roused’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last line of Brahmā’s hymn repeats the second request with a purpose added: wake him so that he may kill the two. प्रबोध (1.86) and बोध here may simply be the same waking said twice, or बोध may mean awareness — knowing what must be done; the text does not decide.

Traditional interpretationThe hymn of Brahmā (1.73–87) is the first of the four great hymns of the Devī Māhātmya; the others come in chapters 4, 5 and 11. In the Śākta tradition it is recited as a hymn to the Goddess as Yoganidrā, the sleep of Viṣṇu.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the hymn ends with a practical request. Its long praise serves an urgent need, and it says so plainly.

1.88Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ ८८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word
