ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P, ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before a vowel, appears as r.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe seer said:

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningBrahmā’s hymn (1.73–87) is over, and the narrator returns: Medhas, telling King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi how the Goddess first appeared, in answer to the king’s question of 1.60–62.

1.89Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवं स्तुता तदा देवी तामसी तत्र वेधसा ॥ ८९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | स्तुता | तदा | देवी | तामसी | तत्र | वेधसा

3IAST

As recitedevaṃ stutā tadā devī tāmasī tatra vedhasā ||

Separatedevam | stutā | tadā | devī | tāmasī | tatra | vedhasā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
स्तुताstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्तु, f. nom. sg.; agrees with देवी
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); subject of तस्थौ in 1.91
तामसीtāmasīshe who is of tamas, the dark power (of sleep)f. nom. sg. of तामस (taddhita from तमस्, with vṛddhi; feminine -ई); in apposition to देवी
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
वेधसाvedhasāby Vedhas, the Ordainer (Brahmā)m. instr. sg. of वेधस् (s-stem); agent of स्तुता

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं स्तुता evaṃ stutā → एवम् + स्तुता
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • तामसी tāmasī — Taddhita in -अ with vṛddhi of the first vowel (तमस् → तामस ‘belonging to tamas’); the feminine takes -ई. Tamas is darkness, inertia — the quality of sleep.
  • वेधसा vedhasā — Instrumental of वेधस् ‘disposer, ordainer’, an old name of Brahmā; s-stems keep the s before vowel endings (वेधसा, वेधसः).

D. Anvaya — prose order

एवं तदा तत्र वेधसा स्तुता तामसी देवी —

evaṃ tadā tatra vedhasā stutā tāmasī devī —

The subject with its participle and agent; the main verbs (निर्गम्य … तस्थौ) come in 1.90–91.

6Translation

LiteralThus praised then there by Vedhas, the Devī Tāmasī —

ReadablePraised in this way by Brahmā, the Ordainer, the Goddess who is the dark power of sleep

तामसी ‘of tamas’ is rendered ‘the dark power of sleep’ because in this scene she is Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā (1.66, 1.69); the sentence runs on to 1.91.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn has its effect. Medhas names the Goddess here तामसी, ‘of tamas’: she is the dark, heavy power of sleep that holds Viṣṇu, the Yoganidrā Brahmā has just praised.

Traditional interpretationThe Gita Press pāṭha assigns the first carita to Mahākālī as presiding deity (its viniyoga and dhyāna); that assignment is liturgical tradition. तामसी is the word in the Purāṇa’s own text that the assignment most naturally connects with: the first manifestation is the Goddess under the guṇa of tamas.

1.90Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विष्णोः प्रबोधनार्थाय निहन्तुं मधुकैटभौ।
नेत्रास्यनासिकाबाहुहृदयेभ्यस्तथोरसः ॥ ९० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विष्णोः | प्रबोधनार्थाय | निहन्तुम् | मधुकैटभौनेत्रास्यनासिकाबाहुहृदयेभ्यः | तथा | उरसः

3IAST

As recitedviṣṇoḥ prabodhanārthāya nihantuṃ madhukaiṭabhau |
netrāsyanāsikābāhuhṛdayebhyastathorasaḥ ||

Separatedviṣṇoḥ | prabodhanārthāya | nihantum | madhukaiṭabhau
netrāsyanāsikābāhuhṛdayebhyaḥ | tathā | urasaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विष्णोःviṣṇoḥof Viṣṇum. gen. sg. of विष्णु (u-stem); objective genitive with प्रबोधन-
प्रबोधनार्थायprabodhanārthāyafor the purpose of awakeningm. dat. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); dative of purpose
निहन्तुम्nihantumto slayinfinitive (tumun) of नि + √हन् (adādi, 2P)
मधुकैटभौmadhukaiṭabhauMadhu and Kaiṭabham. acc. du.; itaretara dvandva (as in 1.86); object of निहन्तुम्
नेत्रास्यनासिकाबाहुहृदयेभ्यःnetrāsyanāsikābāhuhṛdayebhyaḥfrom (his) eyes, mouth, nose, arms and heartn. abl. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of separation with निर्गम्य (1.91)
तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective)
उरसःurasaḥfrom (his) chestn. abl. sg. of उरस् (s-stem)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हृदयेभ्यस्तथोरसः hṛdayebhyastathorasaḥ → हृदयेभ्यः + तथा + उरसः
    visarga and vowel sandhi (guṇa) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga before t becomes s; then ā + u → o (तथा + उरसः → तथोरसः).
  • नेत्रास्य netrāsya → नेत्र + आस्य
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • प्रबोधनार्थाय prabodhanārthāya → प्रबोधन + अर्थाय
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रबोधनार्थाय prabodhanārthāya — caturthī tatpuruṣa (an अर्थ-compound, which grammarians treat as always compounded)
    Members: प्रबोधन + अर्थ · Vigraha: प्रबोधनाय इदम् (प्रबोधनार्थम्), तस्मै prabodhanāya idam (prabodhanārtham), tasmai
    Literally “for the purpose of awakening”; in the verse: so that (Viṣṇu) might wake
    Alternative: Also analysed as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, प्रबोधनस्य अर्थः, ‘the purpose of waking’, put in the dative. The sense is the same; the caturthī analysis is the grammarians’ usual one for अर्थ in this use. The same word-form, विबोधनार्थाय, stands in 1.70.
  • नेत्रास्यनासिकाबाहुहृदयेभ्यः netrāsyanāsikābāhuhṛdayebhyaḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: नेत्र + आस्य + नासिका + बाहु + हृदय · Vigraha: नेत्रे च आस्यं च नासिका च बाहू च हृदयं च, तेभ्यः netre ca āsyaṃ ca nāsikā ca bāhū ca hṛdayaṃ ca, tebhyaḥ
    Literally “from eyes, mouth, nose, arms and heart”; in the verse: from every seat of sleep in Viṣṇu’s body; उरस् (chest) follows outside the compound

C. Morphology

  • निहन्तुम् nihantum — Infinitive of नि + √हन्: the prefix नि- intensifies (‘strike down, slay’). It stands beside a dative of purpose (प्रबोधनार्थाय) with the same function: two purposes, waking and slaying.
  • उरसः urasaḥ — Ablative singular of the s-stem उरस् ‘chest’. It is left outside the dvandva, joined by तथा — a common way of completing a list in verse.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सा देवी) विष्णोः प्रबोधनार्थाय मधुकैटभौ निहन्तुं (च) नेत्रास्यनासिकाबाहुहृदयेभ्यः तथा उरसः (निर्गम्य) —

(sā devī) viṣṇoḥ prabodhanārthāya madhukaiṭabhau nihantuṃ (ca) netrāsyanāsikābāhuhṛdayebhyaḥ tathā urasaḥ (nirgamya) —

Two expressions of purpose and two ablatives of separation; the absolutive निर्गम्य that governs the ablatives comes at the start of 1.91.

6Translation

Literalfor the sake of awakening Viṣṇu, to slay Madhu and Kaiṭabha, from (his) eyes, mouth, nose, arms and heart, and likewise from (his) chest

Readableso that Viṣṇu might wake and slay Madhu and Kaiṭabha, came forth from his eyes, mouth, nose, arms and heart, and from his breast,

‘Came forth’ is supplied from निर्गम्य at the start of 1.91; ‘his’ (Viṣṇu’s) is understood.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess grants both requests of the hymn. She withdraws from Viṣṇu’s body, limb by limb — eyes, mouth, nose, arms, heart, chest — so that he wakes, and wakes in order to kill the two asuras.

Traditional interpretationBrahmā had praised her as dwelling in Hari’s eyes (हरिनेत्रकृतालयाम्, 1.70); the eyes stand first here as she leaves. Sleep is pictured as a presence in the body that departs by every organ.

1.91Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

निर्गम्य दर्शने तस्थौ ब्रह्मणोऽव्यक्तजन्मनः।
उत्तस्थौ च जगन्नाथस्तया मुक्तो जनार्दनः ॥ ९१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

निर्गम्य | दर्शने | तस्थौ | ब्रह्मणः | अव्यक्तजन्मनःउत्तस्थौ | च | जगन्नाथः | तया | मुक्तः | जनार्दनः

3IAST

As recitednirgamya darśane tasthau brahmaṇo'vyaktajanmanaḥ |
uttasthau ca jagannāthastayā mukto janārdanaḥ ||

Separatednirgamya | darśane | tasthau | brahmaṇaḥ | avyaktajanmanaḥ
uttasthau | ca | jagannāthaḥ | tayā | muktaḥ | janārdanaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
निर्गम्यnirgamyahaving come outabsolutive (ल्यप्) of निर् + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P, ‘to go’)
दर्शनेdarśanein the sight (of), within viewn. loc. sg. of दर्शन
तस्थौtasthaustood√स्था (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
ब्रह्मणःbrahmaṇaḥof Brahmām. gen. sg. of ब्रह्मन् (n-stem); with दर्शने
अव्यक्तजन्मनःavyaktajanmanaḥwhose birth is unmanifestm. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ब्रह्मणः
उत्तस्थौuttasthaurose upउद् + √स्था, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
जगन्नाथःjagannāthaḥthe lord of the worldm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to जनार्दनः
तयाtayāby herpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; agent of मुक्तः
मुक्तःmuktaḥreleasedpast passive participle (kta) of √मुच् (tudādi, 6U, ‘to release’), m. nom. sg.
जनार्दनःjanārdanaḥJanārdana (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg.; a name of Viṣṇu; subject of उत्तस्थौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ब्रह्मणोऽव्यक्तजन्मनः brahmaṇo'vyaktajanmanaḥ → ब्रह्मणः + अव्यक्तजन्मनः
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • जगन्नाथस्तया jagannāthastayā → जगन्नाथः + तया
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • मुक्तो जनार्दनः mukto janārdanaḥ → मुक्तः + जनार्दनः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced j becomes -o.
  • जगन्नाथ jagannātha → जगत् + नाथ
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal n becomes n. The rule is optional (जगद्नाथ is possible), but the nasal form is usual.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अव्यक्तजन्मनः avyaktajanmanaḥ — bahuvrīhi (अव्यक्त itself a nañ tatpuruṣa: न व्यक्तम्)
    Members: अव्यक्त + जन्मन् · Vigraha: अव्यक्तं जन्म यस्य सः, तस्य avyaktaṃ janma yasya saḥ, tasya
    Literally “of him whose birth is unmanifest”; in the verse: of Brahmā, whose origin is beyond perception
    Alternative: Also read as a vyadhikaraṇa bahuvrīhi, अव्यक्तात् जन्म यस्य सः, ‘whose birth is from the Unmanifest (avyakta, primal nature)’. Both readings are found for this epithet of Brahmā; the first keeps the members in the same case and is the simpler analysis.
  • जगन्नाथः jagannāthaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + नाथ · Vigraha: जगतः नाथः jagataḥ nāthaḥ
    Literally “protector of the world”; in the verse: lord of the world, Viṣṇu

C. Morphology

  • निर्गम्य nirgamya — Absolutive with a prefix takes -य (ल्यप्) instead of -त्वा. With √गम् the root nasal may stay (निर्गम्य) or drop (निर्गत्य).
  • उत्तस्थौ uttasthau — Perfect of √स्था: the reduplicated syllable is त — when a root begins with a sibilant + stop, the stop is repeated, without aspiration (थ् → त्): तस्थौ, with the ending -औ. With उद्, the d becomes t before t (खरि च, 8.4.55): उत्तस्थौ ‘rose’.
  • मुक्तः muktaḥ — Past participle of √मुच्: च् becomes क् before त (मुच् + त → मुक्त). With the instrumental तया: ‘released by her’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सा) निर्गम्य अव्यक्तजन्मनः ब्रह्मणः दर्शने तस्थौ। तया मुक्तः जगन्नाथः जनार्दनः च उत्तस्थौ।

(sā) nirgamya avyaktajanmanaḥ brahmaṇaḥ darśane tasthau | tayā muktaḥ jagannāthaḥ janārdanaḥ ca uttasthau |

Two clauses joined by च, each ending in the perfect of √स्था — तस्थौ (she stood) and उत्तस्थौ (he rose).

6Translation

Literalhaving come out, (she) stood in the sight of Brahmā, whose birth is unmanifest. And Janārdana, lord of the world, released by her, rose up

Readableand stood before the eyes of Brahmā, whose birth is beyond knowing. Released by her, Janārdana, lord of the world, rose

‘Came forth’ was given in 1.90; the verse continues into 1.92 (‘from his serpent couch on the one ocean’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo movements answer each other: she comes out and stands where Brahmā can see her (तस्थौ), and Viṣṇu, freed, rises (उत्तस्थौ). The Goddess praised in the hymn is now visible to the one who praised her.

Traditional interpretationMedhas had said that the Goddess, though eternal, is spoken of as ‘born’ whenever she appears for the gods’ work (1.65–66). This is the first such appearance: not a birth, but a coming forth from where she already was.

1.92Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एकार्णवेऽहिशयनात्ततः स ददृशे च तौ।
मधुकैटभौ दुरात्मानावतिवीर्यपराक्रमौ ॥ ९२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एकार्णवे | अहिशयनात् | ततः | सः | ददृशे | च | तौमधुकैटभौ | दुरात्मानौ | अतिवीर्यपराक्रमौ

3IAST

As recitedekārṇave'hiśayanāttataḥ sa dadṛśe ca tau |
madhukaiṭabhau durātmānāvativīryaparākramau ||

Separatedekārṇave | ahiśayanāt | tataḥ | saḥ | dadṛśe | ca | tau
madhukaiṭabhau | durātmānau | ativīryaparākramau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एकार्णवेekārṇaveon the single ocean (the flood at the end of an age)m. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
अहिशयनात्ahiśayanātfrom the serpent-couchn. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of separation with उत्तस्थौ (1.91)
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb: तद् + -तस्)
सःsaḥhe (Viṣṇu)pronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.
ददृशेdadṛśesaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P, ‘to see’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, with an ātmanepada ending (ārṣa use; see Morph)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. acc. du.; object of ददृशे
मधुकैटभौmadhukaiṭabhauMadhu and Kaiṭabham. acc. du.; itaretara dvandva (as in 1.86); in apposition to तौ
दुरात्मानौdurātmānauevil-mindedm. acc. du. of दुरात्मन्; compound (see Samāsa)
अतिवीर्यपराक्रमौativīryaparākramauof surpassing strength and valourm. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एकार्णवेऽहिशयनात् ekārṇave'hiśayanāt → एकार्णवे + अहिशयनात्
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).
  • शयनात्ततः śayanāttataḥ → अहिशयनात् + ततः
    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)
    Final t before the unvoiced t stays t; the two words are only written together.
  • ततः स ददृशे tataḥ sa dadṛśe → ततः + सः + ददृशे
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant. ततः before s keeps its visarga (the optional s-assimilation is not used).
  • दुरात्मानावति durātmānāvati → दुरात्मानौ + अति(वीर्यपराक्रमौ)
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • एकार्णवे ekārṇave — karmadhāraya
    Members: एक + अर्णव · Vigraha: एकः अर्णवः, तस्मिन् ekaḥ arṇavaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the one ocean”; in the verse: on the single ocean of the dissolution, when the world is all water (एकार्णवीकृते, 1.66)
  • अहिशयनात् ahiśayanāt — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: ‘the serpent, which is the couch’)
    Members: अहि + शयन · Vigraha: अहिः एव शयनम्, तस्मात् ahiḥ eva śayanam, tasmāt
    Literally “from the serpent-bed”; in the verse: from Śeṣa, the serpent on which Viṣṇu sleeps (आस्तीर्य शेषम्, 1.67)
    Alternative: Also possible as a saptamī tatpuruṣa with शयन as an action noun, अहौ शयनम् ‘lying on the serpent’: ‘from (his) lying on the serpent’. The karmadhāraya fits the ablative of place better.
  • दुरात्मानौ durātmānau — bahuvrīhi (prādi first member दुर्)
    Members: दुर् + आत्मन् · Vigraha: दुष्टः आत्मा ययोः तौ duṣṭaḥ ātmā yayoḥ tau
    Literally “whose selves are bad”; in the verse: evil-hearted, wicked
  • अतिवीर्यपराक्रमौ ativīryaparākramau — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (वीर्यपराक्रम) and the prefix अति
    Members: अति + वीर्य + पराक्रम · Vigraha: वीर्यं च पराक्रमः च वीर्यपराक्रमौ; अतिशयितौ वीर्यपराक्रमौ ययोः तौ vīryaṃ ca parākramaḥ ca vīryaparākramau; atiśayitau vīryaparākramau yayoḥ tau
    Literally “whose strength and valour are surpassing”; in the verse: of exceeding might and prowess

C. Morphology

  • ददृशे dadṛśe — Perfect of √दृश् with reduplication (द-दृश्-) and the ātmanepada ending -ए. √दृश् is parasmaipada (active perfect ददर्श), and ददृशे normally means ‘was seen’. Here the subject is सः and the object तौ is accusative, so the sense must be active, ‘he saw’ — an epic (ārṣa) use of the middle ending.
  • दुरात्मानौ durātmānau — Accusative dual of the n-stem आत्मन् at the end of a bahuvrīhi: -आनौ (strong stem).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः) एकार्णवे अहिशयनात् (उत्तस्थौ)। ततः सः दुरात्मानौ अतिवीर्यपराक्रमौ तौ मधुकैटभौ च ददृशे —

(saḥ) ekārṇave ahiśayanāt (uttasthau) | tataḥ saḥ durātmānau ativīryaparākramau tau madhukaiṭabhau ca dadṛśe —

The first two words complete 1.91 (‘rose … from the serpent-couch on the one ocean’). Then a new clause: subject सः, verb ददृशे, and the object तौ with its appositions running into the second line.

6Translation

Literalfrom the serpent-couch on the single ocean. Then he saw those two, Madhu and Kaiṭabha, evil-minded, of surpassing strength and valour,

Readablefrom his serpent couch on the one ocean. Then he saw the two, Madhu and Kaiṭabha, evil-hearted and of surpassing strength and prowess,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViṣṇu rises from Śeṣa on the cosmic waters and, for the first time, sees the two asuras who were about to kill Brahmā. The verse sets the scene of the fight: one ocean, no land.

Traditional interpretationThe pāda मधुकैटभौ दुरात्मानौ has nine syllables, one more than the anuṣṭubh pāda; every witness reads it so (see corrections.json, metre notes). The text is kept as it stands.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Metre: the pāda मधुकैटभौ दुरात्मानौ is hypermetric (nine syllables) in every witness; no emendation is made. ददृशे is taken as active (‘saw’) with an ātmanepada ending, as the accusative तौ requires.

1.93Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

क्रोधरक्तेक्षणावत्तुं ब्रह्माणं जनितोद्यमौ।
समुत्थाय ततस्ताभ्यां युयुधे भगवान् हरिः ॥ ९३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

क्रोधरक्तेक्षणौ | अत्तुम् | ब्रह्माणम् | जनितोद्यमौसमुत्थाय | ततः | ताभ्याम् | युयुधे | भगवान् | हरिः

3IAST

As recitedkrodharaktekṣaṇāvattuṃ brahmāṇaṃ janitodyamau |
samutthāya tatastābhyāṃ yuyudhe bhagavān hariḥ ||

Separatedkrodharaktekṣaṇau | attum | brahmāṇam | janitodyamau
samutthāya | tataḥ | tābhyām | yuyudhe | bhagavān | hariḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
क्रोधरक्तेक्षणौkrodharaktekṣaṇauwhose eyes were red with angerm. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तौ (1.92)
अत्तुम्attumto devourinfinitive (tumun) of √अद् (adādi, 2P, ‘to eat’)
ब्रह्माणम्brahmāṇamBrahmām. acc. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; object of अत्तुम्
जनितोद्यमौjanitodyamauwho had set about the attemptm. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तौ
समुत्थायsamutthāyahaving risen upabsolutive (ल्यप्) of सम् + उद् + √स्था
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb)
ताभ्याम्tābhyāmwith those twopronoun तद्, m. instr. du.; instrumental of accompaniment with युयुधे (‘fought with’)
युयुधेyuyudhefought√युध् (divādi, 4Ā, ‘to fight’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
भगवान्bhagavānthe Blessed Onem. nom. sg. of भगवत् (possessive -वत् on भग)
हरिःhariḥHari (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg. of हरि (i-stem); subject of युयुधे

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • क्रोधरक्तेक्षणावत्तुं krodharaktekṣaṇāvattuṃ → क्रोधरक्तेक्षणौ + अत्तुम्
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv; the m of अत्तुम् becomes anusvāra before b (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • ततस्ताभ्यां tatastābhyāṃ → ततः + ताभ्याम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • रक्तेक्षणा raktekṣaṇā → रक्त + ईक्षणौ
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e. Likewise जनित + उद्यम → जनितोद्यम (a + u → o).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • क्रोधरक्तेक्षणौ krodharaktekṣaṇau — bahuvrīhi (its first part क्रोधरक्त is a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa: क्रोधेन रक्ते)
    Members: क्रोध + रक्त + ईक्षण · Vigraha: क्रोधेन रक्ते ईक्षणे ययोः तौ krodhena rakte īkṣaṇe yayoḥ tau
    Literally “whose eyes are red with anger”; in the verse: glaring with rage
  • जनितोद्यमौ janitodyamau — bahuvrīhi
    Members: जनित + उद्यम · Vigraha: जनितः उद्यमः याभ्यां तौ janitaḥ udyamaḥ yābhyāṃ tau
    Literally “by whom an effort had been produced”; in the verse: having set about it, ready to strike

C. Morphology

  • युयुधे yuyudhe — Perfect ātmanepada of √युध्, reduplicated यु-युध्-; √युध् is conjugated in the middle (युध्यते). With the instrumental ताभ्याम् it means ‘fought with them’.
  • समुत्थाय samutthāya — सम् + उद् + √स्था with ल्यप्: after उद् the s of स्था drops (उद् + स्था → उत्था), giving उत्थाय ‘having risen’.
  • अत्तुम् attum — Infinitive of √अद्: अद् + तुम् with d unvoiced before t. In 1.68 the two were ready ‘to kill’ Brahmā (हन्तुम्); here they are ready ‘to devour’ him.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तौ) क्रोधरक्तेक्षणौ ब्रह्माणम् अत्तुं जनितोद्यमौ (ददृशे)। ततः भगवान् हरिः समुत्थाय ताभ्यां युयुधे।

(tau) krodharaktekṣaṇau brahmāṇam attuṃ janitodyamau (dadṛśe) | tataḥ bhagavān hariḥ samutthāya tābhyāṃ yuyudhe |

The first line still describes the object of ददृशे (1.92); the second is a new sentence with an absolutive (समुत्थाय) and the main verb युयुधे.

6Translation

Literalwith eyes red with anger, having set about devouring Brahmā. Having risen up, then, the Blessed Hari fought with the two

Readabletheir eyes red with rage, bent on devouring Brahmā. Rising up, the Lord Hari then fought with them

The fight’s duration and manner follow in 1.94.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe danger is at its height: the asuras, eyes red with fury, are about to eat Brahmā. Viṣṇu stands up and fights them himself.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the hymn’s two requests are now visible as events — the god is awake and acts. The Goddess has not fought; she has made the fight possible.

1.94Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पञ्चवर्षसहस्राणि बाहुप्रहरणो विभुः।
तावप्यतिबलोन्मत्तौ महामायाविमोहितौ ॥ ९४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

पञ्च | वर्षसहस्राणि | बाहुप्रहरणः | विभुःतौ | अपि | अतिबलोन्मत्तौ | महामायाविमोहितौ

3IAST

As recitedpañcavarṣasahasrāṇi bāhupraharaṇo vibhuḥ |
tāvapyatibalonmattau mahāmāyāvimohitau ||

Separatedpañca | varṣasahasrāṇi | bāhupraharaṇaḥ | vibhuḥ
tau | api | atibalonmattau | mahāmāyāvimohitau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पञ्चpañcafivenumeral पञ्चन्, n. acc. pl. (numerals five to nineteen have no gender distinction); qualifies वर्षसहस्राणि
वर्षसहस्राणिvarṣasahasrāṇithousands of yearsn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); accusative of duration
बाहुप्रहरणःbāhupraharaṇaḥwith his arms as weaponsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies विभुः
विभुःvibhuḥthe all-pervading Lordm. nom. sg. of विभु (u-stem); in apposition to हरिः (1.93)
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. nom. du.; subject of उक्तवन्तौ (1.95)
अपिapiand, in turnindeclinable (particle)
अतिबलोन्मत्तौatibalonmattaumaddened by their excessive strengthm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa)
महामायाविमोहितौmahāmāyāvimohitaudeluded by Mahāmāyām. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • बाहुप्रहरणो विभुः bāhupraharaṇo vibhuḥ → बाहुप्रहरणः + विभुः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.
  • तावप्यति tāvapyati → तौ + अपि + अति(बलोन्मत्तौ)
    vowel sandhi (āv and yaṇ) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    au before a becomes āv (तौ + अपि → तावपि); then i before a becomes y (अपि + अति → अप्यति).
  • बलोन्मत्तौ balonmattau → बल + उन्मत्तौ
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वर्षसहस्राणि varṣasahasrāṇi — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: वर्ष + सहस्र · Vigraha: वर्षाणां सहस्राणि varṣāṇāṃ sahasrāṇi
    Literally “thousands of years”; in the verse: with पञ्च: five thousand years
    Alternative: The printed text writes पञ्चवर्षसहस्राणि as one; it could be taken as a single numeral compound (dvigu type). A dvigu proper is a collective singular, so with the plural ending the numeral is more naturally a separate word qualifying वर्षसहस्राणि, as here; the sense is unchanged.
  • बाहुप्रहरणः bāhupraharaṇaḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: बाहु + प्रहरण · Vigraha: बाहू एव प्रहरणे यस्य सः bāhū eva praharaṇe yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose weapons are his arms”; in the verse: fighting with his bare arms
  • अतिबलोन्मत्तौ atibalonmattau — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, with a prādi tatpuruṣa (अतिबल) as first member
    Members: अतिबल + उन्मत्त · Vigraha: अतिबलेन उन्मत्तौ (अतिबलम् = अतिशयितं बलम्) atibalena unmattau (atibalam = atiśayitaṃ balam)
    Literally “maddened by excessive strength”; in the verse: drunk with their own power
    Alternative: Also possible: अतिबलौ च उन्मत्तौ च, ‘exceedingly strong and maddened’ (karmadhāraya of two adjectives). The instrumental reading gives the cause of their madness, which the story bears out.
  • महामायाविमोहितौ mahāmāyāvimohitau — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: महामाया + विमोहित · Vigraha: महामायया विमोहितौ mahāmāyayā vimohitau
    Literally “deluded by the Great Māyā”; in the verse: bewildered by Mahāmāyā, as Brahmā had asked (मोहयैतौ, 1.86)

C. Morphology

  • वर्षसहस्राणि varṣasahasrāṇi — Accusative of extent of time: ‘for (five) thousands of years’.
  • महामायाविमोहितौ mahāmāyāvimohitau — विमोहित is the past participle of the causative of वि + √मुह् (‘made to be bewildered’); the causative agent stands in the compound’s first member, महामाया.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सः) विभुः बाहुप्रहरणः पञ्च वर्षसहस्राणि (ताभ्यां युयुधे)। अतिबलोन्मत्तौ महामायाविमोहितौ तौ अपि (केशवम् उक्तवन्तौ) —

(saḥ) vibhuḥ bāhupraharaṇaḥ pañca varṣasahasrāṇi (tābhyāṃ yuyudhe) | atibalonmattau mahāmāyāvimohitau tau api (keśavam uktavantau) —

The first line completes 1.93 (‘fought … for five thousand years’); the second gives the subject of a new sentence whose verb, उक्तवन्तौ, comes in 1.95.

6Translation

Literalfor five thousands of years, the all-pervading one, with his arms as weapons. And those two, maddened by excessive strength, deluded by Mahāmāyā,

Readablefor five thousand years, the all-pervading Lord fighting with his bare arms. Then the two, drunk with their own great strength and deluded by Mahāmāyā,

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe battle lasts five thousand years, Viṣṇu fighting with his arms alone, and is not decided by force. It turns when Mahāmāyā clouds the asuras’ minds — the first of Brahmā’s requests (1.86) fulfilled.

Traditional interpretationThe compound महामायाविमोहितौ takes up Medhas’s teaching: Mahāmāyā draws even the minds of the wise into delusion (1.55–56). The asuras are her first example in the stories.

1.95Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

उक्तवन्तौ वरोऽस्मत्तो व्रियतामिति केशवम् ॥ ९५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

उक्तवन्तौ | वरः | अस्मत्तः | व्रियताम् | इति | केशवम्

3IAST

As reciteduktavantau varo'smatto vriyatāmiti keśavam ||

Separateduktavantau | varaḥ | asmattaḥ | vriyatām | iti | keśavam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
उक्तवन्तौuktavantausaidpast active participle (ktavatu) of √वच्, m. nom. du.; used as the finite verb, subject तौ (1.94)
वरःvaraḥa boonm. nom. sg. of वर; subject of the passive व्रियताम्
अस्मत्तःasmattaḥfrom usfirst-person pronoun अस्मद् with the suffix -तस् (ablative sense), indeclinable form
व्रियताम्vriyatāmlet (it) be chosen√वृ (‘to choose’; वृङ्, kryādi 9Ā), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (quotative particle)
केशवम्keśavamto Keśava (Viṣṇu)m. acc. sg. of केशव; the person addressed, accusative with a verb of speaking

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वरोऽस्मत्तो varo'smatto → वरः + अस्मत्तः
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • वरोऽस्मत्तो व्रियताम् varo'smatto vriyatām → अस्मत्तः + व्रियताम्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.

C. Morphology

  • उक्तवन्तौ uktavantau — Past active participle in -वत् (ktavatu) of √वच्, with samprasāraṇa (वच् → उक्-): उक्तवत् ‘having said’. In narrative it does the work of a finite past verb: ‘(they) said’. Dual, for the two asuras.
  • अस्मत्तः asmattaḥ — The suffix -तस् added to a pronoun stem gives an ablative adverb: अस्मत्तः ‘from us’, त्वत्तः ‘from you’ (1.62).
  • व्रियताम् vriyatām — Passive imperative of √वृ ‘to choose’: the root becomes व्रि- before the passive -य-. ‘Let a boon be chosen from us’ is the courteous form of ‘choose a boon’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(तौ) ‘अस्मत्तः वरः व्रियताम्’ इति केशवम् उक्तवन्तौ।

(tau) ‘asmattaḥ varaḥ vriyatām’ iti keśavam uktavantau |

The subject तौ is in 1.94; the quoted words are closed by इति, and केशवम् is the person addressed.

6Translation

Literalsaid to Keśava: ‘Let a boon be chosen from us.’

Readablesaid to Keśava, “Choose a boon from us.”

The passive ‘let a boon be chosen’ is rendered as an active imperative.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe delusion shows itself as pride: after five thousand years of combat the asuras, pleased with their opponent, offer him a boon — as though they were the gods and he the petitioner.

Traditional interpretationThe reversal is the point the tradition draws: a boon is a superior’s gift. In offering one to Viṣṇu the asuras act out the confusion that Mahāmāyā has worked in them (1.94).

1.96Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

श्रीभगवानुवाच ॥ ९६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

श्रीभगवान् | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedśrībhagavānuvāca ||

Separatedśrībhagavān | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
श्रीभगवान्śrībhagavānthe Blessed Lord (Viṣṇu)m. nom. sg. of भगवत् with the honorific prefix श्री; subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P, ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi

No sandhi joins the words of this mantra.

C. Morphology

  • श्रीभगवान् śrībhagavān — Nominative singular of भगवत् ‘possessing fortune, blessed’ (भग + possessive -वत्): the strong stem -वन्त्, with the final consonants reduced and the vowel lengthened, gives -वान्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

श्रीभगवान् उवाच।

śrībhagavān uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe Blessed Lord said:

ReadableThe Blessed Lord said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViṣṇu answers the asuras’ offer. Inside Medhas’s narration the text quotes the god directly, under the same label the Bhagavad Gītā uses for Kṛṣṇa’s speeches.

1.97Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भवेतामद्य मे तुष्टौ मम वध्यावुभावपि ॥ ९७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भवेताम् | अद्य | मे | तुष्टौ | मम | वध्यौ | उभौ | अपि

3IAST

As recitedbhavetāmadya me tuṣṭau mama vadhyāvubhāvapi ||

Separatedbhavetām | adya | me | tuṣṭau | mama | vadhyau | ubhau | api

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भवेताम्bhavetāmmay (you two) be√भू (bhvādi, 1P), vidhiliṅ (optative), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, parasmaipada; third person because its subject is the noun उभौ
अद्यadyatoday, nowindeclinable (adverb of time)
मेmewith meenclitic form of अस्मद्, gen. sg.; construed with तुष्टौ
तुष्टौtuṣṭaupleased, satisfiedpast participle (kta) of √तुष् (divādi, 4P, ‘to be content’), m. nom. du.
ममmamaby mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; genitive of the agent with the gerundive वध्यौ
वध्यौvadhyauto be slaingerundive (kṛtya, -य) from वध (the slaying-stem of √हन्), m. nom. du.; predicate of भवेताम्
उभौubhaubothpronominal adjective उभ, m. nom. du.; subject
अपिapiboth … indeedindeclinable (particle; with उभौ, ‘the two of you alike’)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भवेतामद्य bhavetāmadya → भवेताम् + अद्य
    no change (final m before a vowel) · final m joins a following vowel
    Final m before a vowel is written with it, not as anusvāra.
  • वध्यावुभावपि vadhyāvubhāvapi → वध्यौ + उभौ + अपि
    vowel sandhi (āv) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv, twice: वध्यौ + उभौ → वध्यावुभौ; उभौ + अपि → उभावपि.

C. Morphology

  • भवेताम् bhavetām — Optative third-person dual. The subject is the pronominal adjective उभौ ‘both’, so the verb is third person even though Viṣṇu is speaking to the two asuras (‘may both (of you) be …’); an understood भवन्तौ ‘your honours’ would give the same person. The optative expresses a wish that is also a demand: it is the boon he names.
  • वध्यौ vadhyau — Gerundive ‘to be killed, fit to be killed’, from वध, the form √हन् takes in several derivatives. With a gerundive the agent may stand in the genitive: मम वध्यौ ‘to be killed by me’.
  • तुष्टौ tuṣṭau — Past participle of √तुष् in an active sense (‘satisfied’), with the genitive मे ‘with me’. It states the condition the asuras themselves have just declared.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(यतः युवाम्) अद्य मे तुष्टौ, (अतः) उभौ अपि मम वध्यौ भवेताम्।

(yataḥ yuvām) adya me tuṣṭau, (ataḥ) ubhau api mama vadhyau bhavetām |

A single optative clause; तुष्टौ is circumstantial (‘since you are pleased’), and the anvaya spells out the implied reason. Alternatively भवेताम् can be supplied twice: ‘may you be pleased with me; may you both be slain by me’.

6Translation

Literal(Since you are) pleased with me today, may you both be (persons) to be slain by me.

ReadableIf you are pleased with me, then let both of you be slain by me today.

The participle तुष्टौ is rendered as a condition (‘if you are pleased’); the Sanskrit states it without a conjunction.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViṣṇu accepts the offer and asks the one thing that ends the war: that both asuras be killable by him. Their own boon becomes the instrument of their death.

Traditional interpretationThe motif of a boon turned against the giver recurs in the Purāṇas. Here it carries the chapter’s teaching: the asuras are undone not by Viṣṇu’s strength, which five thousand years did not make decisive, but by the delusion of Mahāmāyā.

1.98Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

किमन्येन वरेणात्र एतावद्धि वृतं मम ॥ ९८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

किम् | अन्येन | वरेण | अत्र | एतावत् | हि | वृतम् | मम

3IAST

As recitedkimanyena vareṇātra etāvaddhi vṛtaṃ mama ||

Separatedkim | anyena | vareṇa | atra | etāvat | hi | vṛtam | mama

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
किम्kimwhat (is the use)?interrogative किम्, n. nom. sg.; with the instrumental: ‘what is gained by …?’
अन्येनanyenaanother, any otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. instr. sg.; agrees with वरेण
वरेणvareṇaboonm. instr. sg. of वर; instrumental with किम् (‘what need of …’)
अत्रatrahere, in this matterindeclinable (adverb of place)
एतावत्etāvatthis much, just thispronominal adjective एतावत्, n. nom. sg.
हिhiindeed, forindeclinable (emphatic/causal particle)
वृतम्vṛtamchosenpast passive participle (kta) of √वृ (‘to choose’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of एतावत्
ममmamaby me, my (choice)first-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; agent in the genitive with वृतम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वरेणात्र vareṇātra → वरेण + अत्र
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • वरेणात्र एतावद्धि vareṇātra etāvaddhi → अत्र + एतावत्
    hiatus (metrical licence) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88) not applied
    a + e should give ai (अत्रैतावत्); the text leaves the vowels apart at the pāda break. Declared in sandhi_exceptions.
  • एतावद्धि etāvaddhi → एतावत् + हि
    consonant sandhi (voicing and aspiration) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39), झयो होऽन्यतरस्याम् (8.4.62)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced h, and the h then takes the aspiration of the stop: d + h → ddh.

C. Morphology

  • किम् kim — किम् with an instrumental of the thing is an idiom for ‘what is the use of …?’: किम् अन्येन वरेण ‘what (good is) another boon?’
  • वृतम् vṛtam — Past participle of √वृ ‘to choose’ (वृ + त), neuter to agree with एतावत्. With the genitive मम it reads ‘chosen by me’ or simply ‘my choice’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अत्र अन्येन वरेण किम्? एतावत् हि मम वृतम्।

atra anyena vareṇa kim? etāvat hi mama vṛtam |

A rhetorical question and a statement; हि gives the reason (‘for this much is my choice’).

Left unjoined: अत्र | एतावत् — The vowels stand apart at the pāda break (वरेणात्र | एतावद्धि): epic verse often leaves sandhi unapplied between the first and second pādas, and joining (अत्रैतावत्) would take a syllable from the line. A metrical licence, printed so in the Gita Press text.

6Translation

LiteralWhat (use is there) here of another boon? For this much (is) chosen by me.

ReadableWhat need have I of any other boon? This alone is my choice.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViṣṇu closes off any bargaining: he wants nothing else. The asuras, bound by their offer, cannot refuse.

Traditional interpretationThe hiatus अत्र | एतावत् falls at the pāda break and is printed so in the Gita Press text; it is a licence of epic verse, not an error, and is kept.

1.99Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ ९९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P, ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before a vowel, appears as r.

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas takes up the narrative again after Viṣṇu’s two lines (1.97–98) and tells how the asuras tried to escape the boon they had granted.

1.100Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वञ्चिताभ्यामिति तदा सर्वमापोमयं जगत् ॥ १०० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वञ्चिताभ्याम् | इति | तदा | सर्वम् | आपोमयम् | जगत्

3IAST

As recitedvañcitābhyāmiti tadā sarvamāpomayaṃ jagat ||

Separatedvañcitābhyām | iti | tadā | sarvam | āpomayam | jagat

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
वञ्चिताभ्याम्vañcitābhyāmby the two (who had been) deceivedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative √वञ्च् (वञ्चय- ‘to deceive’), m. instr. du.; agent of गदितः (1.101)
इतिitithus, in this wayindeclinable (particle, here ‘so, thus’ referring back to Viṣṇu’s words)
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
सर्वम्sarvamthe whole, allpronominal adjective सर्व, n. acc. sg.; qualifies जगत्
आपोमयम्āpomayammade of water, all watern. acc. sg. of आपोमय (आपः ‘waters’ + taddhita -मय ‘consisting of’); predicate adjective with जगत्
जगत्jagatthe worldn. acc. sg. of जगत्; object of विलोक्य (1.101)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वमापोमयं जगत् sarvamāpomayaṃ jagat → सर्वम् + आपोमयम् + जगत्
    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The m of सर्वम् joins the following vowel; the m of आपोमयम् becomes anusvāra before j.
  • आपोमयं āpomayaṃ → आपः + मयम्
    visarga sandhi inside a derived word · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    The nominative plural आपः keeps its ending before the suffix -मय, and -aḥ before m becomes -o, as between words.

C. Morphology

  • आपोमयम् āpomayam — -मय ‘consisting of, full of’ is normally added to a stem (अप् would give अम्मय or अप्मय). आपोमय is built on the plural आपः itself — an old formation also found in Vedic prose. ‘The whole world (was) water’.
  • वञ्चिताभ्याम् vañcitābhyām — Past participle of वञ्चय- (causative of √वञ्च्, ‘cause to go astray’, i.e. deceive), instrumental dual: the two asuras, ‘deceived’ by the boon they had granted.

D. Anvaya — prose order

इति वञ्चिताभ्यां (ताभ्यां) तदा सर्वं जगत् आपोमयं (विलोक्य) —

iti vañcitābhyāṃ (tābhyāṃ) tadā sarvaṃ jagat āpomayaṃ (vilokya) —

A participial agent phrase and the object of an absolutive; the absolutive विलोक्य and the passive verb गदितः come in 1.101.

6Translation

LiteralBy the two, thus deceived, then — the whole world (being) made of water —

ReadableThus deceived, the two then looked and saw the whole world turned to water,

‘Looked and saw’ is supplied from विलोक्य at the start of 1.101; the instrumental agent is rendered as the subject.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asuras realise they have been caught by their own word. Looking about for a way out, they see that the whole world is water — the cosmic flood on which Viṣṇu slept (1.66–67).

Traditional interpretationWho deceived them is left implicit: by Viṣṇu’s taking them at their word, and behind that by Mahāmāyā, who had bewildered them (1.94). The narrative makes the deception the Goddess’s work, done through the god.

1.101Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

विलोक्य ताभ्यां गदितो भगवान् कमलेक्षणः।
आवां जहि न यत्रोर्वी सलिलेन परिप्लुता ॥ १०१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विलोक्य | ताभ्याम् | गदितः | भगवान् | कमलेक्षणःआवाम् | जहि | न | यत्र | उर्वी | सलिलेन | परिप्लुता

3IAST

As recitedvilokya tābhyāṃ gadito bhagavān kamalekṣaṇaḥ |
āvāṃ jahi na yatrorvī salilena pariplutā ||

Separatedvilokya | tābhyām | gaditaḥ | bhagavān | kamalekṣaṇaḥ
āvām | jahi | na | yatra | urvī | salilena | pariplutā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विलोक्यvilokyahaving looked at, having seenabsolutive (ल्यप्) of वि + √लोक् (‘to look’)
ताभ्याम्tābhyāmby the twopronoun तद्, m. instr. du.; agent of गदितः
गदितःgaditaḥwas addressed, was toldpast passive participle (kta) of √गद् (bhvādi, 1P, ‘to speak’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
भगवान्bhagavānthe Blessed Onem. nom. sg. of भगवत्; subject of गदितः
कमलेक्षणःkamalekṣaṇaḥthe lotus-eyedm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भगवान्
आवाम्āvāmus twofirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. du.; object of जहि
जहिjahislay!√हन् (adādi, 2P, ‘to strike, kill’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
यत्रyatrawhereindeclinable (relative adverb of place)
उर्वीurvīthe earth (‘the broad one’)f. nom. sg. of उर्वी (feminine of उरु ‘wide’)
सलिलेनsalilenawith watern. instr. sg. of सलिल
परिप्लुताpariplutāflooded, overflowedpast passive participle (kta) of परि + √प्लु (bhvādi, 1Ā, ‘to float, flow’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with उर्वी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ताभ्यां गदितो भगवान् tābhyāṃ gadito bhagavān → ताभ्याम् + गदितः + भगवान्
    anusvāra and visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Final m before g becomes anusvāra; -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.
  • यत्रोर्वी yatrorvī → यत्र + उर्वी
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • कमलेक्षणः kamalekṣaṇaḥ → कमल + ईक्षणः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कमलेक्षणः kamalekṣaṇaḥ — bahuvrīhi (with an implied comparison, ‘like’)
    Members: कमल + ईक्षण · Vigraha: कमले इव ईक्षणे यस्य सः kamale iva īkṣaṇe yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose eyes are like lotuses”; in the verse: the lotus-eyed Viṣṇu

C. Morphology

  • जहि jahi — Imperative second singular of √हन्: before the ending -हि the root is replaced by ज- (ज + हि → जहि). The asuras still speak as if commanding.
  • गदितः gaditaḥ — Past passive participle of √गद् with the connecting vowel -इ-: ‘(was) spoken to’. The person addressed is the subject (भगवान्), the speakers the agent (ताभ्याम्).
  • विलोक्य vilokya — Absolutive in -य after the prefix वि-. Its agent is the instrumental ताभ्याम्, the agent of the passive main verb — a regular construction.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सर्वम् आपोमयं जगत्) विलोक्य ताभ्यां कमलेक्षणः भगवान् गदितः — ‘यत्र उर्वी सलिलेन परिप्लुता न (अस्ति, तत्र) आवां जहि।’

(sarvam āpomayaṃ jagat) vilokya tābhyāṃ kamalekṣaṇaḥ bhagavān gaditaḥ — ‘yatra urvī salilena pariplutā na (asti, tatra) āvāṃ jahi | ’

The first line completes 1.100 with a passive main verb (गदितः); the second is the asuras’ words, a command with a relative clause of place (यत्र … न).

6Translation

Literalhaving looked, the lotus-eyed Blessed One was addressed by the two: ‘Slay us where the earth is not flooded with water.’

Readableand they said to the lotus-eyed Lord, “Slay us where the earth is not flooded with water.”

The passive (‘was addressed by the two’) is rendered actively.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe asuras try to slip their promise: since the whole world is water, a place ‘where the earth is not flooded’ seems not to exist. They grant the boon on a condition they think cannot be met.

Traditional interpretationThe Purāṇa recension (the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa as edited in GRETIL) has further lines after this verse that the Gita Press text does not carry; this edition follows the Gita Press 700 and adds nothing (see corrections.json, recension notes).

2Padaccheda — the words separated

Direct textual meaningA second ‘the seer said’ only two verses after the first (1.99): the Gita Press pāṭha marks the return to narration after the asuras’ quoted words. Medhas tells the end of the fight.

1.103Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तथेत्युक्त्वा भगवता शङ्खचक्रगदाभृता।
कृत्वा चक्रेण वै च्छिन्ने जघने शिरसी तयोः ॥ १०३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तथा | इति | उक्त्वा | भगवता | शङ्खचक्रगदाभृताकृत्वा | चक्रेण | वै | छिन्ने | जघने | शिरसी | तयोः

3IAST

As recitedtathetyuktvā bhagavatā śaṅkhacakragadābhṛtā |
kṛtvā cakreṇa vai cchinne jaghane śirasī tayoḥ ||

Separatedtathā | iti | uktvā | bhagavatā | śaṅkhacakragadābhṛtā
kṛtvā | cakreṇa | vai | chinne | jaghane | śirasī | tayoḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तथाtathāso (be it)indeclinable (adverb of manner; here assent)
इतिitithus (closing the quotation)indeclinable (quotative particle)
उक्त्वाuktvāhaving saidabsolutive (ktvā) of √वच्
भगवताbhagavatāby the Blessed Onem. instr. sg. of भगवत्; agent of छिन्ने and of the absolutives उक्त्वा, कृत्वा
शङ्खचक्रगदाभृताśaṅkhacakragadābhṛtāwho bears the conch, discus and macem. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भगवता
कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving placedabsolutive (ktvā) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U, ‘to make, do, put’)
चक्रेणcakreṇawith the discusn. instr. sg. of चक्र; instrument of छिन्ने
वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
छिन्नेchinnewere cut offpast passive participle (kta) of √छिद् (rudhādi, 7U, ‘to cut’), n. nom. du.; agrees with शिरसी; used as the finite verb
जघनेjaghaneon (his) loins, on his lapn. loc. sg. of जघन ‘hip, loins’; place where the heads were laid
शिरसीśirasīthe two headsn. nom./acc. du. of शिरस् (s-stem); object of कृत्वा and subject of छिन्ने
तयोःtayoḥof the twopronoun तद्, m. gen. du.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तथेत्युक्त्वा tathetyuktvā → तथा + इति + उक्त्वा
    vowel sandhi (guṇa and yaṇ) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + i → e (तथा + इति → तथेति); then i before u becomes y (इति + उक्त्वा → इत्युक्त्वा).
  • वै च्छिन्ने vai cchinne → वै + छिन्ने
    consonant sandhi (doubling of ch) · छे च (6.1.73), पदान्ताद्वा (6.1.76)
    After a word ending in a long vowel, initial ch may be written cch. The Gita Press writes the added c at the head of the next word.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शङ्खचक्रगदाभृता śaṅkhacakragadābhṛtā — upapada tatpuruṣa containing an itaretara dvandva (शङ्खचक्रगदा)
    Members: शङ्ख + चक्र + गदा + भृत् · Vigraha: शङ्खः च चक्रं च गदा च शङ्खचक्रगदाः; ताः बिभर्ति इति शङ्खचक्रगदाभृत्, तेन śaṅkhaḥ ca cakraṃ ca gadā ca śaṅkhacakragadāḥ; tāḥ bibharti iti śaṅkhacakragadābhṛt, tena
    Literally “by the bearer of conch, discus and mace”; in the verse: by Viṣṇu, named by his emblems; -भृत् is √भृ ‘to bear’ with the zero kṛt affix (kvip) and an added t

C. Morphology

  • छिन्ने chinne — Past participle of √छिद्: after the root’s final d the t of the suffix becomes n and the d becomes n too (छिद् + त → छिन्न). Neuter dual -ए, agreeing with शिरसी: ‘the two heads were cut off’.
  • उक्त्वा uktvā — Absolutive of √वच् with samprasāraṇa (व → उ) and k for c: उक्त्वा ‘having said’. With कृत्वा it gives two steps before the main act.
  • शिरसी śirasī — Neuter dual of शिरस्: nominative and accusative are the same (शिरसी), so the one word serves both as the object of कृत्वा (‘having placed their heads’) and the subject of छिन्ने (‘the heads were cut off’). A neuter dual in -ī (like छिन्ने in -e) is pragṛhya and would stand unjoined before a vowel (ईदूदेद्द्विवचनं प्रगृह्यम्, 1.1.11); here a consonant follows, so no question of sandhi arises.

D. Anvaya — prose order

‘तथा’ इति उक्त्वा, शङ्खचक्रगदाभृता भगवता तयोः शिरसी जघने कृत्वा चक्रेण वै छिन्ने।

‘tathā’ iti uktvā, śaṅkhacakragadābhṛtā bhagavatā tayoḥ śirasī jaghane kṛtvā cakreṇa vai chinne |

A passive sentence: the subject शिरसी with its verb छिन्ने, the agent भगवता, and two absolutives (उक्त्वा, कृत्वा) sharing that agent. शिरसी does double duty as the object of कृत्वा.

6Translation

LiteralHaving said ‘So (be it)’, by the Blessed One, bearer of conch, discus and mace, having placed (them) on (his) loins, the two heads of the two were indeed cut off with the discus.

ReadableSaying “So be it,” the Lord who bears the conch, the discus and the mace laid them on his lap and cut off their two heads with his discus.

The passive is rendered actively. जघन, properly the hips or loins, is rendered ‘lap’: the one place above the water.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningViṣṇu meets the condition exactly: the only ground not covered by water is his own body. He lays their heads on his lap and cuts them off with the discus. The asuras die by the terms of their own boon.

Traditional interpretationThe narrative turns the asuras’ cleverness against them, as the boon itself was turned (1.97). The cause, as the chapter has insisted, is Mahāmāyā’s delusion rather than force: five thousand years of fighting did not end the war, a confusion of mind did.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the asuras look for a loophole in the world and find none outside the one they are fighting. The story leaves no place that is not within the Lord’s reach.

1.104Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवमेषा समुत्पन्ना ब्रह्मणा संस्तुता स्वयम्।
प्रभावमस्या देव्यास्तु भूयः शृणु वदामि ते ॥ १०४ ॥ ऐं ॐ

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | एषा | समुत्पन्ना | ब्रह्मणा | संस्तुता | स्वयम्प्रभावम् | अस्याः | देव्याः | तु | भूयः | शृणु | वदामि | ते

3IAST

As recitedevameṣā samutpannā brahmaṇā saṃstutā svayam |
prabhāvamasyā devyāstu bhūyaḥ śṛṇu vadāmi te ||

Separatedevam | eṣā | samutpannā | brahmaṇā | saṃstutā | svayam
prabhāvam | asyāḥ | devyāḥ | tu | bhūyaḥ | śṛṇu | vadāmi | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एषाeṣāshe, this (Goddess)pronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
समुत्पन्नाsamutpannāarose, appearedpast participle (kta) of सम् + उद् + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā, ‘to go’), f. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
ब्रह्मणाbrahmaṇāby Brahmām. instr. sg. of ब्रह्मन्; agent of संस्तुता
संस्तुताsaṃstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्तु, f. nom. sg.
स्वयम्svayamhimself, in personindeclinable (reflexive adverb); here emphasising ब्रह्मणा
प्रभावम्prabhāvamthe power, the glorym. acc. sg. of प्रभाव; object of शृणु
अस्याःasyāḥof thispronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.; agrees with देव्याः
देव्याःdevyāḥof the Goddessf. gen. sg. of देवी
तुtunow, butindeclinable (particle marking a new topic)
भूयःbhūyaḥfurther, againindeclinable (adverb; comparative of बहु in form)
शृणुśṛṇuhear!√श्रु (svādi, 5P, ‘to hear’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
वदामिvadāmiI (shall) tell√वद् (bhvādi, 1P, ‘to speak’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youenclitic form of युष्मद्, dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अस्या देव्यास्तु asyā devyāstu → अस्याः + देव्याः + तु
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound loses its visarga (अस्या देव्याः); before t the visarga becomes s (देव्यास्तु).
  • भूयः शृणु bhūyaḥ śṛṇu → भूयः + शृणु
    visarga sandhi (optional assimilation not applied) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before ś the visarga may stay or become ś; the text keeps the visarga.

C. Morphology

  • समुत्पन्ना samutpannā — Past participle of सम् + उद् + √पद्: d + t → nn (पद् + त → पन्न). Used as a finite verb: ‘(she) arose’. The word picks up उत्पन्ना of 1.66.
  • शृणु śṛṇu — Imperative of √श्रु (svādi): the root becomes शृ- before the class sign -नु, which becomes -णु after ṛ; the ending -हि is dropped after the u of the class sign.
  • वदामि vadāmi — Present tense used for the immediate future: ‘I am going to tell you’. Medhas announces the next story (chapters 2–4).

D. Anvaya — prose order

एवम् एषा (देवी) स्वयं ब्रह्मणा संस्तुता समुत्पन्ना। अस्याः देव्याः प्रभावं तु भूयः शृणु, (अहं) ते वदामि।

evam eṣā (devī) svayaṃ brahmaṇā saṃstutā samutpannā | asyāḥ devyāḥ prabhāvaṃ tu bhūyaḥ śṛṇu, (ahaṃ) te vadāmi |

A closing summary (एवम् … समुत्पन्ना), then the transition to the next story: an imperative and a first-person present.

6Translation

LiteralThus she arose, praised by Brahmā himself. But hear further the power of this Goddess; I tell (it) to you.

ReadableSo it was that she appeared, praised by Brahmā himself. Now hear more of this Goddess’s power; I shall tell you.

स्वयम् is taken with ब्रह्मणा (‘by Brahmā himself’). It can also be taken with the subject: ‘she appeared of her own accord’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas closes the first story and opens the next. The ring is complete: he had said that the eternal Goddess is called ‘born’ (उत्पन्ना) when she appears for the gods’ purpose (1.65–66); now he says एवम् एषा समुत्पन्ना — ‘this is how she arose’. शृणु and ते are singular: he speaks to the king.

Traditional interpretationThis is the last mantra of chapter 1. In the Gita Press pāṭha it is followed by the bījas ऐं ॐ, closing the sampuṭa that ॐ ऐं opened in 1.1; they belong to the recitation, not to the Purāṇa. The colophon names the chapter मधुकैटभवधः, the Slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, the first carita, which the pāṭha assigns to Mahākālī.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has a name for a goddess praised by the gods, 83 ब्रह्मोपेन्द्रमहेन्द्रादिदेवसंस्तुतवैभवा, ‘whose glory is praised by Brahmā, Upendra, Mahendra and the other gods’. The motif is shared; the Devī Māhātmya has just told one such praise in full.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: स्वयम् may qualify ब्रह्मणा (‘by Brahmā himself’, chosen) or the Goddess (‘she arose of her own accord’); both readings are grammatical.

Colophon

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये मधुकैटभवधो नाम प्रथमोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye madhukaiṭabhavadho nāma prathamo'dhyāyaḥ||

Chapter conclusion

Narrative summary

Two men who have lost everything, a king to his ministers and a merchant to his family, find that losing things has not loosened their hold on them. Medhas names the power behind that hold — Mahāmāyā — and, to show who she is, tells the first of the three stories: Brahmā’s praise of Yoganidrā, her withdrawal from the sleeping Viṣṇu, and the death of Madhu and Kaiṭabha. The chapter ends with a promise: more of her power is still to be told (1.104).

Principal vocabulary

SanskritIASTMeaning
महामायाmahāmāyāthe Great Māyā: the Goddess as the power that veils, binds and releases
योगनिद्राyoganidrāyogic sleep: the Goddess as Viṣṇu’s sleep between ages
ममत्वम्mamatvam‘mine-ness’, possessive attachment
मोहःmohaḥdelusion, bewilderment
विद्याvidyāknowledge that liberates
स्तुतिःstutiḥpraise, hymn
स्वाहाsvāhāthe offering-call to the gods; the Goddess named as it
स्वधाsvadhāthe offering-call to the ancestors
कालरात्रिःkālarātriḥthe night of time, of dissolution
तामसीtāmasīthe dark one, the Goddess of tamas
मन्वन्तरम्manvantaramthe age of a Manu
आश्रमःāśramaḥa sage’s hermitage

Important grammatical constructions

  • The perfect and the aorist as the narrative past (उवाच, बभूव, अभूत्, अद्राक्षीत्).
  • The speaker in the genitive with verbs of hearing (निशामय … गदतो मम, 1.2).
  • Long periodic sentences carried across several ślokas (1.12–16, 1.22–24, 1.51–55); the anvaya joins them.
  • Passive of a causative with an instrumental agent (तया सम्मोह्यते जगत्, 1.55).
  • The gerundive of obligation with न (विस्मयः न कार्यः, 1.54).
  • Hymn style: त्वम् with a predicate nominative, repeated in series (त्वं स्वाहा त्वं स्वधा …, 1.73), feminine vocatives, and compounds of praise.
  • Pūrvarūpa (-e/-o + a → avagraha) and -aḥ → -o are the commonest sandhis of the chapter.

Śākta insights

  • Attachment is a power, not only a fault: the wise are bound by the same Mahāmāyā as the birds (1.49–55).
  • The power that binds is the power that frees: she is vidyā and the cause of liberation, and also the cause of bondage (1.57–58).
  • The Goddess is eternal and unborn, yet is said to ‘arise’ whenever she appears for the gods’ purposes (1.64–65).
  • Even Viṣṇu, the sustainer, is subject to her: his waking is her withdrawal (1.88–90).
  • Brahmā’s hymn names her as everything that is — creation, sustenance and dissolution (1.75–77) — and as both gentle and terrible (1.80–82).

Śrīvidyā connections

The chapter shares names with the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) — Mahāmāyā (215), Viṣṇumāyā (339), Kālarātri and others noted on the verses — and both texts treat the Goddess as the power behind Viṣṇu and Brahmā. These are shared Śākta ideas, not evidence that one text depends on the other; no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine is stated in this chapter.

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 1.55 — Mahāmāyā deludes the world and draws even the wise into delusion.
  • 1.57 — She is the supreme knowledge, the eternal cause of liberation.
  • 1.64 — Eternal, the world her form: the definition of the Goddess in the frame story.
  • 1.73 — त्वं स्वाहा त्वं स्वधा — the opening of Brahmā’s hymn.
  • 1.75 — You are Sandhyā and Sāvitrī, the supreme mother; by you this universe is borne and created (continued in 1.76: protected, and consumed at the end).
  • 1.82 — Whatever thing exists anywhere, real or unreal — with 1.83: the power of all of it is you; how then can you be praised?

Chapter completion report

Chapter1 — The Slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha (मधुकैटभवधः)
Source editionŚrīdurgāsaptaśatī, Gita Press, Gorakhpur (Hindi translation by Paṇḍit Rāmanārāyaṇadatta Śāstrī “Rāma”)
Verse range1.1–1.104 (Gita Press mantra numbers)
Verse entries104 entries for 104 mantras — uvāca 14, half-ślokas 24, ślokas 66; the counts equal the chapter's colophon
Analysed104 of 104 entries
NumberingGita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 81) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.
Corrections to the digital text1.77: तथा सिंहृतिरूपान्ते → तथा संहृतिरूपान्ते
1.77: महास्मृतः॥ → महास्मृतिः॥
1.54: संसारस्थितिकारिणा*। → संसारस्थितिकारिणा।
Variant readings (kept as printed)1.78: महादेवी महासुरी / महादेवी महेश्वरी (Textual Verification Required)
Uncertain readings or interpretations1.5, 1.22, 1.27, 1.43, 1.71, 1.74, 1.78, 1.92, 1.104
Sandhi patterns
  • -aḥ before voiced consonants → -o, and -aḥ before a → -o’ (the commonest junctions).
  • Pūrvarūpa: -e/-o + a- → avagraha (कथ्यतेऽष्टमः, सोऽचिन्तयत्).
  • Visarga → ś/s before c/t (हेतुश्च, कस्त्वम्).
  • n + l → ṃ̐l (भवाँल्लुब्धैः, 1.27); t + n → nn (तन्नात्र, 1.54); savarṇa-dīrgha and guṇa across words.
  • One declared hiatus: अत्र | एतावत् at the pāda break of 1.98.
Samāsa constructions
  • Ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas throughout the narrative (सूर्यतनयः, क्षितिमण्डले).
  • Bahuvrīhis of epithet (महाभागः, चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः).
  • Upapada tatpuruṣas with agent nouns (संसारस्थितिकारिणा, शङ्खचक्रगदाभृता).
  • Karmadhārayas of divine names (महामाया, योगनिद्रा, महाविद्या), and dvandvas in the hymn’s lists.
Grammatical observations
  • Several sentences run across verses; every such anvaya says so.
  • ददृशे (1.92) is read as active ‘saw’, an ārṣa use of the ātmanepada ending.
  • पञ्च is separated from वर्षसहस्राणि in the padaccheda (1.94): a dvigu would be singular.
  • The hypermetric pāda of 1.92 is kept as every witness has it.
Philosophical themes
  • Mamatā and moha
  • Mahāmāyā as bondage and liberation
  • Yoganidrā and the waking of Viṣṇu
  • Stuti as the turning point
Open questions
  • 1.78: महासुरी (Gita Press) / महेश्वरी (GRETIL, Freund) — check the printed book.
  • Interpretive doubts are flagged on 1.5, 1.22, 1.27, 1.43, 1.71, 1.74, 1.92 and 1.104.
